# Causes investigated, and what happened

## Open

- **#1** *(goal)* PARTLY SOLVED, NOT DONE. The @LH sign-extension fix makes the OPS 901/201/301 transitions COMPLETE -- the phases load, FTRMGPOV posts, the DK holds and the IOQE pool exhaustion are gone -- but the machine then CRASHES: 'invalid instruction 0xc6c6 at 0x48da', NIA=648da, BSR=12, in EVERY post-fix run.
  - evidence: I MISSED THIS BY NOT CHECKING THE STOP REASON. Stop reasons: s1 226611108, s2 226797227, s3 226277495, s4 226249685, s5 227645136, s6 225895363, s7 226493598 steps -- all 'invalid instruction 0xc6c6 at 0x48da'. The PRE-fix runs w1 and v91a end on 'interrupted (SIGINT)', the harness timeout, because the machine was parked in FPMIDLE and never crashed. So the runs I read as 'ended at 403 s on the timeout' had in fact CRASHED at a fixed ~226 million steps, and s6/s7 did not run their requested wall time for the same reason. VERIFIED BY THE USER INTERACTIVELY with retest-crt2.sh --v 36 and real MEDS: the OPS 201 load completes -- 250 blocks from 1/5/0/5 exactly as #PFCMGPT predicts, then 41 from 2/5/0/0, and the pair repeats -- and the run then stops with the same invalid instruction. The reported POLL FAIL is the CONSEQUENCE: the emulator halted, so nothing polls the display units. 0x648da lies inside NO load block of any phase -- never-loaded memory, c6c6 fill on both sides -- so the CPU branched into territory that was never loaded. (0x048da IS inside loaded regions of phases 4,5,6,7,8,10,12,15 but also holds a c6c6 padding word.) WHAT THE FIX DID ACHIEVE, and it is real: the transition completes and the phases load correctly where before the machine parked forever in FPMIDLE. The crash is the NEXT defect. CANDIDATE: 818df88's 'The sector replaces the high-order bit of a 16-bit address (sect.2.2.9)' -- PSW1=c8dae0c1 carries address field c8da and BSR=12, giving NIA=648da. METHOD NOTE: my success criterion was the ledger's own '6/5/0/0 appears', which is necessary and NOT sufficient; a run must also be checked for how it ENDED.

- **#44** *(flight-sw)* LEADING HYPOTHESIS: phase 8's overlay never completes because FIOMGCV's checksum-verify-and-reprotect PCT is never dispatched. No protection error is needed -- if the PCT does not run, the overlay status table never records the load blocks as SUMMED/VERIFIED/PROTECTED, so FCMMGPOV's tail is never reached and FTRMGPOV never posts the event.
  - evidence: FIOMGCV is the MM/GTG CHECKSUM PROCESSOR (0x1a134, 328 hw), called from FIOMGMTR (0x1a4c2) which sits on the completion path FPMIHPC2 case 9 -> FIOMGMTR -> FIOMGCMP (0x1a0ea). Its own header: 'CALLED WHENEVER CHECKSUM GENERATION AND SUBSEQUENT VERIFICATION IS REQUIRED ... RUNS UNDER A PCT WITH INTERRUPTS ENABLED ... RETURN IS TO FPMIHPC2 AFTER A CHECKSUM PROCESSOR PCT HAS BEEN PLACED ON THE RUN QUEUE ... AN UPDATED OVERLAY STATUS TABLE IDENTIFYING THE SEGMENT(S)(/LOAD BLOCK(S)) SUMMED,VERIFIED,PROTECTED'. So verification IS on the critical path between the transfer and the event -- correcting an earlier claim here that the overlay path only GENERATES checksums; FCMMGBOV's generation is the GTG TRANSMITTER side (gated on 'IF (CHI,R7,EQ,1) THIS GPC THE TRANSMITTER ?'), not a substitute for verification. WHY IT FITS: the data arrives perfectly (30/30 load blocks byte-identical AND passing their own checksums), the CPU is 92-100% idle in FPMIDLE through every hold with wait=0%, and the event is never posted. A queued PCT that is never dispatched looks exactly like that, and needs no protection error. TEST: landmark FIOMGCV/FIOMGMTR/FIOMGCMP and see whether they fire for phase 3 and not for phase 8 (run z5).
  - addrs `1a134-1a27b,1a4c2-1a54a,1a0ea-1a133,1935c-1935c` &middot; csects `FIOMGCV,FIOMGMTR,FIOMGCMP,FCMMGPOV,FTRMGPOV,FCMMGBOV` &middot; phases `3,8` &middot; env `YAGPC_LANDMARKS` &middot; doc `FIOMGCV.asm header` &middot; run `z5`
- **#49** *(emulator)* LEADING CANDIDATE FOR THE LAST LINK: bus 24 has NO MODEL ATTACHED, yet it carries more traffic than any modelled bus -- 4,281 transactions against 804 on each DK bus -- with a 0.51 ms median. Its requests complete almost instantly because nothing is there to take time, so FCOS re-dispatches it at a rate the real vehicle could not sustain, and FIOPDISP keeps it at the head of the active IOQE list ~48% of every DK hold. Bus 6's entry, queued behind it, is monitored roughly once per 1052 ms.
  - evidence: Bus ownership: DEU 6-9, MMU 18, MTU 20-22 (mtumodel.c MTU_BUS_FIRST/LAST). Bus 24 belongs to none of them. Transaction counts over v91a: bus 24 = 4281, bus 20 = 1248, bus 22 = 1248, buses 6/7/8 = 804/804/806, bus 21 = 181. Head writers during 388-400 s (z6, WATCHHW on TCVTMIOA 0x146 with the writing NIA): FIOPDISP at 1bd6f (FIOPSMSC+17) writes 9068 121 times and 905a 50 times; FIOPDISP at 1bd30 (FIORBTST+978) writes 9068 77 times and 90b2 44; FIOCMPLT at 1b559 (FIOCMPL2+100) clears to 0000 134 times. IOQE 9068 decodes (TFIOQ DSECT) to TIOQMNTM=00000000 80 = BUS 24. WHY THIS IS OURS AND NOT THE FLIGHT SOFTWARE'S: FIOPDISP inserting a newly dispatched request at the head is the flight software behaving correctly on the traffic it is given; what it is given is a bus with no subsystem, whose transactions cost almost nothing. Precedent in this ledger: entry 11's 40 RECV timeouts were all BCE20, 'a bus with no model attached', so unmodelled buses demonstrably do generate transactions here. TEST: give bus 24 realistic wire time, or stop dispatching an absent subsystem, and see whether the head rotates fast enough for the DK completions to keep up and the 25-entry pool to survive. CAUTION: this is a hypothesis with the right shape, NOT a measurement, and eight candidates for this link have already been refuted.
  - addrs `146-146,9068-9068,1bd6f-1bd6f,1bd30-1bd30,1b559-1b559` &middot; csects `FIOPDISP,FIOCMPLT,FIOMNTR` &middot; procs `BCE6,BCE24,MSC` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/mtumodel.c,src/iop.c` &middot; tsec `388-400` &middot; run `v91a,z6`
- **#55** *(method)* THE HEADLESS RESULT DOES NOT MATCH THE INTERACTIVE ONE. With the in-process DEU model all three transitions complete cleanly and DK polling continues; with real MEDS the user reports loading activity on MM1 READY followed by POLL FAIL and no menus, on BOTH OPS 901 PRO and OPS 201 PRO.
  - evidence: Headless (s1-s5): loads complete, FTRMGPOV posts, zero sentinel faults, DK transaction rate FLAT across the transition -- ~23 per 5 s at t=370 through t=404 in both 901 and 201 -- and the last DK transaction in every run lands at the run's final instant, so polling had not stopped. THREE CANDIDATE EXPLANATIONS, none yet ruled out. (1) BINARY TIMESTAMP: the @LH fix was built at 17:13; a run started before that has the old behaviour, and POLL FAIL is precisely its signature -- Clock 2 dies at t~397, the CPU parks in FPMIDLE, all bus dispatch stops. Not yet confirmed either way. (2) APPARATUS: deumodel.c's own header says it answers 'synchronously, in the same call, with no socket, no drops and no pacing', built to decide 'with a peripheral that cannot lose anything and answers instantly, does the load finish?' -- so real poll timing, datagram loss and the MEDS protocol are invisible to it BY CONSTRUCTION, and 'polling continues' here is a statement about a peripheral that cannot fail to be polled. (3) A DOWNSTREAM DISPLAY FAULT unrelated to this defect; there is already an open item about MEDS2-port.py rendering a clock but no menu. THE DISCRIMINATOR the user can supply cheaply: whether the MM1 READY activity runs to COMPLETION -- 250 blocks for OPS 201, 34 for 901 -- or stops partway. Completing separates 'the load failed' from 'the load succeeded and the display path failed after it', which need different investigations. ALSO UNMEASURED HERE: survival well past the transition. The headless runs end 1-3 s after it; s6 reached only 13 s past because it shared the machine; s7 runs 1150 wall seconds alone for ~700 s of post-transition time.
  - procs `BCE6,BCE7,BCE8` &middot; phases `5,6,18` &middot; env `DEUMF,DEU2` &middot; files `src/deumodel.c,pass-run/retest-crt2.sh` &middot; run `s1,s2,s3,s4,s5,s6,s7`
- **#96** *(emulator)* INHERITED MISPRINT, still present upstream: the intercomputer-bus port table gives GPC 2 and GPC 3 THE SAME PORT, so five GPCs share four ICC buses. yaGPC2 reproduces it deliberately, and that choice was made when only one computer ran -- where the ICC port cannot matter because there is nobody to talk to. With --gpcs naming both 2 and 3 it now means two computers literally sharing an intercomputer bus, each hearing the other's transmissions as its own.
  - evidence: Reported upstream as ColanderCombo/nsts-sim-gpc#34 on 2026-09-12, asking which correction Don wants (IP3=6926 with IP4/IP5 shifted, or IP3=6928); 6928-6930 are unused in bus.civet. The same issue also reports _NSP2_data, which repeats _NSP1_data's port 6960 AND its nom 'NSP1 to PCMMU' -- that one is unambiguous and is what made the IP3 line worth reporting rather than second-guessing. INTERIM MEASURE IN yaGPC2, not a guess at the upstream fix: the table is used verbatim except when a vehicle carries BOTH GPC 2 and GPC 3, where GPC 3 alone moves to offset 28 and a line on stderr says so and names the issue. GPCs 1, 2, 4 and 5 keep their upstream ports, so any run without the colliding pair is unchanged and a run with it diverges by exactly one computer. YAGPC_IP_PORTS_VERBATIM=1 keeps the collision and still reports it. Revisit when Don answers.
  - procs `BCE24` &middot; files `src/bcenet_transport.c` &middot; symptom `intercomputer bus,ICC,shared port,upstream typo,IP2 IP3`
- **#111** *(goal)* THE FIRST FAIL-TO-SYNC. The set now forms and is then VOTED APART: FCMCSYNC admits the neighbour and writes a non-empty CZ2B_CS, and FCMSFAIL -- the sync failure processor -- clears it again. This is the state the multi-GPC plan named as the goal of Stage 4's discovery, reached for the first time.
  - evidence: Run cs4, watching CZ2B_CS at 0x2406 across a 900 s two-computer run, with every store identified against DASS_G2: 'gpc=1 addr=02406 val=00000008 nia=18be9' where 018BE9 is FCMCSYNC+005B, a BAL to FCMSMASK -- so the NORMAL SSIP SYNCHRONIZATION PROGRAM, whose stated job is to allow new GPCs to join the common set, admitted one and rebuilt the masks. Then 'gpc=1 addr=02406 val=00000000 was=0008 nia=19501', and 019501 is FCMSFAIL+0075. The earlier zero at nia=180b4 is FCMASYNC+0062, STH R0,X'0012'(R2), the initialisation. So the sequence is: FCMASYNC initialises the mask to zero, FCMCSYNC admits GPC2 and the mask becomes 0x0008, FCMSFAIL clears it. GPC2 never writes its own copy at all. CONSEQUENCE FOR WHAT MATTERS NEXT: the 3.85 ms sync timeout is now load-bearing for the first time -- until this run nothing ever waited on a neighbour, so neither the simulated-time barrier nor the sync path had ever been under a real deadline. The question is no longer whether a set can form but WHICH of the five sync programs times out and why: FCMSFAIL is reached through FCMSFINT from FCMCSYNC, FCMTSYNC, FCMSSYNC or FCMISYNC, and which one it is decides whether this is a barrier tuning problem or a modelling gap.
  - addrs `18be9-18be9,19501-19501,180b4-180b4,02406-02407` &middot; csects `FCMCSYNC,FCMSFAIL,FCMSFINT,FCMASYNC,FCMSMASK` &middot; tape `v44` &middot; env `YAGPC_BARRIER_US` &middot; symptom `fail to sync,common set voted apart,CZ2B_CS cleared,3.85 ms timeout` &middot; run `cs4`
- **#112** *(goal)* THE FAILING SYNC IS FCMISYNC, THE I/O-COMPLETE SYNC. Of the eleven sync-failure parameter lists built in a 900 s two-computer run, EIGHT come from FCMISYNC -- four on each machine -- with one each from FCMASYNC and one from FCMCSYNC. So the set is not torn apart by the initial handshake or by the common-set sync, both of which succeed; it is torn apart by the barrier taken at every I/O COMPLETION.
  - evidence: Traced FCMSFINT, the routine that builds FCMSFAIL's parameter list, at 0x19674-0x19692, and read the link register at entry. Callers, decoded from R7: 0x180b1 = FCMASYNC+005F (once per machine), 0x18be4 = FCMCSYNC+0056 (once, GPC1), 0x18f7d = FCMISYNC+00E9 (FOUR TIMES ON EACH MACHINE). THE DECODING IS VERIFIED, not assumed: in the same traces 'BAL 7,X''a846''' at 0x180ed produced R7=80ef6031 with execution resuming at 0x180ef, and 'BAL 1,*+X''0081''' at 0x18064 produced R1=80666031 with the return at 0x18066 -- in both cases nibbles 1-3 of the link register are the low three of the return address, which is the rule applied here. Program spans from DASS_G2: FCMCSYNC 18b8e..18c85, FCMISYNC 18e94..18fbd, FCMSSYNC 19716..197af, FCMTSYNC 19856..1989e. WHY THIS POINTS SOMEWHERE SPECIFIC: an I/O completion sync fails when the two computers do not agree on when an I/O finished, and I/O completion timing is the part of this emulator with the largest known divergence from the hardware -- ledger #22 measured DK completion latency of 1052 ms that is entirely dispatch delay rather than bus work, and #49 records bus 24 carrying more traffic than any modelled bus because nothing is attached to it. Those are now candidates with a consequence rather than curiosities.
  - addrs `19674-19692,18f7d-18f7d,18be4-18be4,180b1-180b1` &middot; csects `FCMISYNC,FCMSFINT,FCMSFAIL,FCMCSYNC,FCMASYNC` &middot; procs `BCE24,BCE6` &middot; symptom `fail to sync,FCMISYNC,I/O complete sync,common set voted apart` &middot; run `sf1`
- **#113** *(goal)* THE SET LIVES 4.33 MILLISECONDS. GPC2 is admitted to the common set and voted out again one sync-timeout later: CZ2B_CS goes to 0x0008 at t=435.2097 s and back to zero at t=435.2141 s, a gap of 4.33 ms against FCOS's 3.85 ms timeout. So the FIRST barrier after admission fails, every time, and it is the I/O-complete sync (#112). GIVING GPC2 ITS OWN DISPLAY DOES NOT FIX IT -- tested, and the window is unchanged at 4.41 ms.
  - evidence: Run cs4, and run rs15 with --deu-bus 2:7 so GPC2 commands a display of its own on DK2: both show the same admit-then-clear pair within 4.4 ms, and mmu2 ends at 59 commands / 432 blocks in both. So the hypothesis that the two computers fail the I/O sync because one drives a display and the other drives nothing is REFUTED -- do not retest it by handing GPC2 a unit. THE BARRIER IS NOT THE CAUSE EITHER: the simulated-time barrier reports 475,004 holds and ZERO ABANDONED at delta 200 us across that run, so the machines' simulated clocks are within 200 us of each other throughout and cannot be 3.85 ms apart. TWO THINGS THIS LEAVES. The admission is real and repeatable, so whatever the computers disagree about at an I/O completion is a property of the I/O model rather than of the clocks: ledger #22's finding that a DK hold is 1052 ms of pure dispatch latency with the BCE finishing in 0.3 ms, and #49's bus 24 carrying more transactions than any modelled bus with nothing attached, are the two candidates with the right shape. Neither has been tested against a two-computer set.
  - addrs `02406-02407` &middot; csects `FCMISYNC,FCMCSYNC,FCMSFAIL` &middot; procs `BCE6,BCE24` &middot; env `YAGPC_BARRIER_US` &middot; symptom `set lives 4.33 ms,admitted then voted out,I/O complete sync,3.85 ms timeout` &middot; run `cs4,rs15`
- **#129** *(goal)* WITH BOTH FIXES THE PAIR HOLDS UNTIL THE OPS REQUEST, BOTH COMPUTERS THEN LOAD FROM MASS MEMORY, AND THE SET BREAKS AT THE OPS TRANSITION'S SYNCHRONISING SVC. GPC1, whose keyboard received OPS 201 PRO, raises the SVC-sync code within ~1.5 s and votes GPC2 out 4.7 ms later; GPC2 reaches the same handshake 2.2-2.4 s after GPC1. Both votes are cleared again 26-30 s later and SSIP handshakes continue in lockstep to the end of the run. GPC2 never commands its display unit, and both mass-memory units lose 250-395 thousand words during the load, against 720 in a single-computer run. Whether either computer actually reaches OPS 2 is not yet established.
  - evidence: Four 900 s runs op2-b25-0..3, YAGPC_BARRIER_US=25, GPC1 loads at 70 s and GPC2 at 230 s wall, NBAT for configuration 2 from 360 s at 15 s steps, OPS,2,0,1,PRO at 600 s -- all four behaved identically. MID- RUN (10:37, about 540 s shared): 0 votes in all four, I/O-complete handshakes exactly matched (e.g. 1691/1691). AT THE OPS REQUEST (shared times): keystroke delivered at 611.5-613.9; GPC1 -> 100 SVC at 612.95 (run 0); GPC1 back to null +4.712 ms and 'GPC1 fail-vote 00000000 -> 00000008'; 'GPC2 fail-vote 00000000 -> 00000001' about 100 ms later; GPC2 -> 100 SVC at 615.31, i.e. 2.36 s after GPC1 (2.20, 2.20, 2.20 s in runs 1-3); votes cleared GPC2 at 639.1 and GPC1 at 642.5 (similar in all runs); no SSIP gap over 400 ms anywhere and the last SSIP identical on both machines at run end. These are the first SVC-sync codes (100) seen in any two-computer run; no timer sync (101) ever. LOAD: mmu1 83 commands, 1,010 blocks, wordsLost 249-267k; mmu2 81 commands, 1,458 blocks, wordsLost 371-396k; vehicle reported 16-20 clashing commands on bus 18 and 11-13 on bus 19 (81,918 on bus 19 in run 2). DISPLAYS: GPC1's unit on bus 6 took ~4,950 commands; the unit on bus 7 assigned to GPC2 took 0, ipled false. CORRECTION TO A HYPOTHESIS OFFERED IN CONVERSATION: the 2.2 s lag is close to 14 x 160 ms and TFICC has TICCSYTM 'CS SYNC TIME - 14TH CYCLE', but FCMCSYNC.asm:247-249 and FPMMTURM.asm:554-560,689-696 show that field is a GMT stamp saved at CSYNC and used for MTU time deltas, not a 14-cycle schedule -- no support. RELEVANT: FCMSFAIL.asm:426-428 treats a failure during an OPS transition specially ('PRIME ICCED BY ARC_GPC AT END OF OPS TRANS'), so a vote at the transition followed by a clear may be partly designed behaviour. NEXT: watch CZ2V_CURRENT_OPS 0x23FC-0x23FE and CZ2B_CS/RS 0x2406-0x2407 on both machines, trace the redundant-set mask per I/O sync and every vote's caller, and take a bus census. MEASURED 2026-09-13, runs ts-b25-0..2 (YAGPC_WATCHHW=2894-2897 on CZ2V_REC_TSIP, IBM long float decoded): THE TWO CLOCKS AGREE. On receiving the reconfiguration message both computers store TSIP = CLOCKTIME + 3.020 at #CARGREC+0069 and the values are IDENTICAL -- 22158545.906300 on both in run 0, 22158550.786300 in run 1, 22158550.546300 in run 2. So a GMT disagreement, and with it the hypothesis that the shared timing unit tells each computer a different time, is refuted for this gap; the patch written on that hypothesis was reverted unbuilt. WHAT DIFFERS: at the following SSIP GPC1's AIESIP takes special case 2 'GPC RECONFIGURATION. ADJUST TSIP BY 20 MSEC' (A4AIESIP+012E), CZ2V_REC_TSIP = CLOCKTIME + 0.020, moving its TSIP 2.04 s EARLIER (22158543.866300 in run 0; 22158548.746300 run 1; 22158548.346300 run 2) -- and GPC2's AIESIP NEVER stores TSIP at all, so GPC2 keeps the +3.020. The TSIP gaps 2.04, 2.04 and 2.20 s plus one SSIP match the SVC-sync gaps observed in the same runs, 2.20, 2.20 and 2.36 s. MECHANISM IN THE SOURCE: ARCGPC.hal:481-487 sends ICC message type 2 when self is in the original RS and then waits DO UNTIL RUNTIME > CZ2V_REC_TSIP; AIESIP.hal:1094-1105 processes the ICC buffers of every GPC in its common-set mask INCLUDING ITS OWN, unless AIEB_CS_MASK(1..5) equals AIEB_SIP_RS(12..16), when it processes only its own. So GPC1 acts on the type-2 message from its own buffer, and GPC2 should act on it from its copy of GPC1's -- it does not. Open: whether GPC2 is on the self-only branch, or GPC1's message never reaches GPC2's copy (runs im-b25-* log the ICC message buffers by sender).  WHY GPC2 NEVER ADJUSTS, runs am-b25-0..2 (YAGPC_ICCMSG=09f6,0cf3,42bd, which logs transfers carrying ARC_ICC_MSG headers and the reader that takes their last word; TSIP watched on both). Identical in all three, in log order: GPC1 sends type 1 (09F6) on bus 1 in transfer 2184; GPC1's ARGREC sets TSIP = CLOCKTIME+3.02 = 22160383.3863 from its OWN buffer; GPC2 reads transfer 2184 in full; GPC2's ARGREC sets the identical 22160383.3863; GPC1 sends type 2 (0CF3 03FC) in transfer 2190; GPC1's AIESIP case 2 sets TSIP = 22160381.3463 from its own buffer; GPC1 enters the SVC sync 19 ms later and votes GPC2 out; ONLY THEN does GPC2 finish reading transfer 2190; GPC2 votes; GPC2's CS is gone, so its AIESIP no longer processes GPC1's buffer and never adjusts. THE LAG IS ONE SSIP CYCLE: wall time from GPC1's send of a transfer to GPC2 taking its last word was 160.5 ms for transfer 2184 and 159.0 ms for 2190, against an SSIP period of 160 ms. AIESIP.hal:56-60 -- phase 2 'FCOS IS DOING ICC I/O', buffers processed in phase 3 -- so GPC2 processes the PREVIOUS cycle's copy of GPC1's buffer while GPC1 processes its own current one. SUSPECT: iccmodel keeps a receiver's unread words until its next listen, so one missed listen leaves a permanent one-transfer backlog; a real bus holds nothing. Runs lag-b25-* measure the lag of every transfer and the words queued at each new command.
  - addrs `023fc-02407,00166-00169` &middot; csects `FCMSSYNC,FCMSFAIL,DM6OPS,ARCGPC,FCMCSYNC,FPMMTURM` &middot; env `YAGPC_BARRIER_US,YAGPC_SYNCORDER,YAGPC_DEUKEYS` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c,src/vehicle.c` &middot; symptom `OPS transition,SVC sync,GPC2 late to SVC sync,vote then cleared,mass memory words lost,GPC2 no display,redundant set formation` &middot; run `op2-b25-0,op2-b25-1,op2-b25-2,op2-b25-3`
- **#134** *(goal)* THE REDUNDANT SET NOW FORMS AT THE OPS TRANSITION AND BREAKS ABOUT A SECOND LATER, WHEN THE TWO COMPUTERS REACH A BARRIER WITH DIFFERENT CODES: GPC2 SIGNALS I/O COMPLETE WHILE GPC1 SIGNALS A SYNCHRONISING SVC. That is an I/O that finishes on one member at a point where the other has already moved on. It comes at the start of the OPS 2 overlay load, and the leading suspect is the device models: in a redundant set every member issues the same I/O, only the bus commander's transmitter is enabled, and the others LISTEN on that bus and receive the same words -- but the mass-memory model is a single conversation, so a listening member would pull words out of the commander's queue.
  - evidence: Runs lm-ops-2/3/4 (Listen Mode build, 720 s). After OPS 201 PRO both computers enter the SVC sync (100) 122-136 times and the timer sync (101) 10 times in lockstep within about one second (shared 614-615 s) -- the first redundant-set syncs. Then, identically in all three, e.g. lm-ops-2: GPC2 -> 001 I/O done at -4.860 ms, GPC1 -> 100 SVC at -4.670 ms, both hold their code ~4.7 ms, and both vote; lm-ops-4: after 18 lockstep SVC/timer handshakes, GPC2 -> 001 at -5.135 ms and GPC1 -> 100 at -4.240 ms. Afterwards only the SSIP handshake continues, 6-7 per second on both, and the votes clear 17-38 s later. Mass memory in those runs: mmu1 510/1051/510 blocks, mmu2 1551/749/1551, wordsLost up to 353,473 against 720 in a single-computer load; vehicle clash reports on buses 18 and 19. GPC2's display unit on bus 7 took 0 commands. OVERLAY FLOW (ARCGPC.hal:546-690): for MEM_SRC 1-4 (GPC-to-GPC overlay for MFB and PGM, then mass-memory overlay for MFB and PGM) every target GPC calls ARC_MEM_OVL when ARC_SRC_GPC(MEM_SRC) is set, so both computers issue the mass-memory I/O. HYPOTHESIS, not yet shown: a listening member's receive on bus 18/19 takes words from the same single- conversation model queue as the commander's, which both loses words and desynchronises the two computers' I/O completions. Runs dv-b25-* identify the IOQE that completes on each computer at the failing barrier and take a per-computer bus census.  MEASURED, runs dv-b25-0..2 (FIOCMPLT/FIOMGSNC calls into FCMISYNC traced with R1 = IOQE, FCMSSYNC entry traced with R7, all on the shared clock via SYNCORDER-JOIN): THE BARRIER THAT FAILS IS NOT A MASS-MEMORY I/O -- the mass-memory hypothesis is not the proximate cause here. Identical in all three runs: both computers SSIP at -5.7 ms and return to null; GPC2 takes the I/O completion of IOQE7, the ICC SSIP transfer, at -4.743 ms (FIOCMPLT+65, code 0880) and raises 001; GPC1 enters FCMSSYNC at -4.569 ms (R7 a8b4c031, return ...8B4) and raises 100; vote; GPC1's own IOQE7 completion arrives at +0.215 ms and GPC2 enters FCMSSYNC from the same caller at +0.788 ms (run 1: -4.851/-4.671/+0.113/+0.711 ms; run 2: -0.212/-0.002 ms). So both computers do the same two things -- the ICC I/O completion and the synchronising SVC -- in OPPOSITE ORDER, because GPC1's ICC completion reaches FCOS about 5 ms after GPC2's. Bus census: bus 18 GPC1 1.57-2.72 M against GPC2 0.40-0.47 M transactions; bus 19 GPC2 1.49-2.15 M; clash reports 9-171,781 on bus 18 and up to 83,481 on bus 19 -- GPC1 drives most of the vehicle, the prime suspect for a slower completion. On real hardware both ICC transfers take the same ~4 ms of wire time (124 words at 33 us), which would line their completions up. Next: time each ICC BCE's finish against the completion's arrival at FIOCMPLT on both machines.
  - csects `ARCGPC,FCMISYNC,FCMSSYNC,FIOCMPLT` &middot; procs `BCE18,BCE19` &middot; env `YAGPC_SYNCORDER,YAGPC_BUSCENSUS,YAGPC_RANGETRACE` &middot; files `src/mmumodel.c,src/run.c,src/vehicle.c` &middot; symptom `redundant set formed,fail to sync after formation,I/O done vs SVC,overlay load,mass memory contention,listener steals words,bus fan-out` &middot; run `lm-ops-2,lm-ops-3,lm-ops-4`
- **#136** *(emulator)* A LISTENING COMPUTER TAKES THE COMMANDER'S WORDS FROM A SHARED DEVICE MODEL, SO THE COMMANDER ALONE GETS A BAD TRANSACTION AND FAILS ITSELF OUT OF THE REDUNDANT SET. The timing unit (buses 20-22) and mass memory (18, 19) models each keep one reply cursor, and in a redundant set every member reads: the transmitter-enabled commander sends the command, the others listen. A listener's receive calls the same model and consumes the words. The commander counts an I/O error only it had; at the second, FIOERRLC's fail-to-sync rule ('ONE GPC WITH ERRORS' and it is self) calls FCMSFAIL and the computer leaves the set.
  - evidence: Runs b24-short-0 and b24-short-1 (MSC bit on; FCMISYNC/FCMSFINT/FCMSFAIL/FIOCMPLT entry traces, REPEAT, DISP, BWTRACE 1,2,24). IDENTICAL in both: IOQE 905a (monitor mask acc=00000a00 = buses 20 and 22; #48 names 905a bus 20) completes with the IPR code (FIOCMPLT R3=0808) on both computers twice, each raising 010 in lockstep, FCMSFINT from FCMISYNC+00E9 (18f7d) on both; the ICC transfer 8c66 then completes in lockstep; 0.19-0.24 ms later GPC1 ALONE enters FCMSFAIL from 0x19dc7 = FIOERRLC+0313 (DASS_G2 map 19ab4..1a05d) with R3=905a0000, and GPC2 only votes back at its FCMISYNC timeout 4.3 ms later (FCMSFINT from FCMISYNC+00B2). SOURCE FIOERRLC.asm, FAIL TO SYNC PROCESSING: 'IF (CHI,R5,GE,2) IF THIS ERROR EQ OR OVER THRESHOLD ... IF ... (CHI,R6,EQ,1) ONE GPC WITH ERRORS ... IF (CR,R5,EQ,R6) IF SELF HAS ERROR ... LHI R0,X''8000'' MASK FOR FORCE F-T-S ... CALL FCMSFAIL', with the comment 'PMU BUSSES DOES NOT HAVE LISTENERS. FAILED/POWERED DOWN PMU CAN RESULT IN NON-UNIVERSAL I/O ERROR' -- i.e. every other bus HAS listeners and a non-universal error is taken as the computer's own fault. MODEL CODE: mtumodel.c 'uint16_t reply[MTU_WORDS]; int replyHead, replyCount;' consumed by every RECV_WORD whoever calls; its own header already says 'FIOPRMPG's commander reads the MTU with #MIN 0,6 and its listener with #RDLI 6'. Transmission is correctly gated: #CMDI, #CMD and the #MIN/#MOUT command word all test regXmitEna (iop_bce_instr.c), so listeners send nothing -- the defect is the receive side only. Mass memory shows the same single cursor (queueHead) and, in b24-short-0, wordsLost 27,269 (mmu1) and 558,513 (mmu2) against 720 in a single- computer load. FIX UNDER TEST (listener fan-out build): per-bus replies and per-reader cursors in the timing unit; a per-listener tap in the mass memory with every word due at the commander's due time on the SHARED clock; listeners first shown the commander's command word marked command sync; busMarksSync extended to buses 18-22 when more than one computer exists; the commander's path and counters unchanged. Runs fo- regress, fo-short-0, fo-std-0. ROLES ON BUSES 20-22, run fc-short-0 (build before the fan-out; YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE_PE=20,21,22, arms attributed by log position after the OPS request, where the two local clocks do not overlap): GPC1 arms with its transmitter ENABLED on buses 20 (1,268), 21 (77) and 22 (340), and 2 with it disabled on 22; GPC2 arms with its transmitter ENABLED on bus 22 (700) and 2 with it disabled on 20. So in one transaction each computer commands one bus of the pair and listens on the other -- matching the NBAT string assignment (strings 1,2 to GPC1, string 3 to GPC2) -- and the model's SINGLE REPLY FOR ALL THREE BUSES was being rewritten by the other computer's command on the other bus as well as consumed by the listener. The vote recurred as before: GPC1 FCMSFAIL from 19dc7 (FIOERRLC+0313) with R3=905a0000 at shared 466.26 s, 268 SVC syncs before it. The fan-out build keys the timing unit's reply by BUS as well as by reader. RESULT OF THE FAN-OUT BUILD. Regression gate fo-regress (single GPC): EVERY EVENT COUNTER EXACT; periodic deu 1849/376/569/368/575, mtu 7897. Two-GPC short run fo-short-0 (OPS at 450 s): the set formed at 465.59 s and held to 467.03 s -- 217 SVC and 34 timer syncs in lockstep, against 134 and 16 (0.67 s) before -- and the bus 20/22 transaction (905a) that ended the previous build no longer diverges. The break is now IOQE 90e8: GPC1 completes it clean (FIOCMPLT code 0880), GPC2 with the IPR code (0808), GPC2 raises 010 twice (FCMSFINT from FCMISYNC+00E9 on both) and fails ITSELF from FIOERRLC+0313 with R3=90e8 -- the same rule, the other computer, a different transaction -- and GPC1 then times out in FCMSSYNC (FCMSFINT from 1979a) and votes. Every completion before it matches across computers (9084, 9068, 905a). Also seen: mtu commands 315,061 (about ten times earlier runs), mmu1 listeners 13,592 words delivered and 21,750 gone past unread, mmu2 listeners 7 and 31,252, and 71,548 second- computer commands mid-transfer on bus 18 -- which bus 90e8 serves, and whether both computers really command bus 18 at once, are being traced in runs fe-short-0/1 (gpc-labelled REPEAT and RECV ARM traces).  REPRODUCED ON THE LONG SCHEDULE, fo-std-0 (OPS at 600 s): set formed 615.35 s, 217/218 SVC and 34 timer syncs, GPC2 self-FTS from 19dc7 with R3=90e8, votes 616.785/616.79, cleared 631.93/632.41 -- the same sequence as fo-short-0. A STANDING ASYMMETRY BEFORE THE SET FORMS (FIOCMPLT codes per computer, shared 400-460 s, the same in the old and fan-out builds): GPC1 completes IOQE 905a (buses 20/22) WITH ERROR 371 and 375 times in those 60 s -- about six a second, every cycle -- where GPC2 errors on it 2 and 0 times; GPC1 also errors on 90b2 (bus 18) 92/126 and 90d6 32/124 times, GPC2 on 90b2 once. A simplex computer tolerates that; a redundant set does not, since FIOERRLC fails whichever member alone has an error. Which bus of the pair fails for GPC1, and why, is traced in fe-short-0/1.
  - addrs `19dc7-19dc7,19ab4-1a05d,905a-905a,18f7d-18f7d,18f46-18f4f` &middot; csects `FIOERRLC,FCMSFAIL,FCMSFINT,FCMISYNC,FIOPRMPG` &middot; procs `BCE18,BCE19,BCE20,BCE21,BCE22` &middot; env `YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE_PE,YAGPC_REPEATTRACE,YAGPC_SYNCORDER` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/mmumodel.c,src/run.c,src/iop_bce_instr.c` &middot; symptom `redundant set breaks,self fail to sync,non-universal I/O error,listener steals words,bus fan-out,single-conversation device model,IPR 010` &middot; doc `FIOERRLC.asm FAIL TO SYNC PROCESSING; FCMISYNC.asm` &middot; run `b24-short-0,b24-short-1,b24-std-0`
- **#137** *(emulator)* THE NEXT BREAK IS DISPLAY BUS DK1: A LISTENING COMPUTER IS TOLD THERE IS NO PERIPHERAL ON A BUS WHOSE DISPLAY UNIT ANOTHER COMPUTER COMMANDS, SO ITS RECEIVE TIMES OUT AND IT FAILS ITSELF OUT OF THE SET. Separately, the timing unit model hands its last 7-word reply to reads addressed to OTHER devices on buses 20-22, which we do not model, so those reads get 7 stale words and time out -- per computer, since each now has its own cursor, where an absent device should give every computer the same silence.
  - evidence: Runs fe-short-0 and fe-short-1 (listener fan-out build 976489e53 plus gpc-labelled REPEAT/RECV ARM traces, YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE_PE=6-9,18-22 from 300 s), IDENTICAL to the microsecond: first self-FTS GPC2 at shared 467.0248 s, IOQE 90e8; GPC1 completes 90e8 clean (0880) at -1.12 ms and GPC2 with the IPR code (0808) at -1.11 ms; the FIOMCKIO arm before each has monitor mask 02000000 = BUS 6; in the 60 ms before, GPC1 arms a 16-word receive on BCE6 with its transmitter ENABLED and GPC2 the same receive with it DISABLED -- commander and listener. Router: a display unit is served only to deu_owned_by(br, owner, busID); anyone else gets bus_no_peripheral, including a listener. After formation GPC2 logs BCE6 time-outs 'left=16 gotAny=0 waited=5.00 ms mto=5.00 ms'. BUS 20/22, run fe-short-0 time-outs from shared 400 s: before the set forms GPC1 BCE20 x204 and BCE22 x204, all 'left=25 gotAny=1 waited=0.03 ms' on its count=32 receives (204 each) -- seven words then silence, which is the MTU model's 7-word reply read by a 32-word receive for another device; after formation GPC1 BCE20 x117, BCE21 x9, GPC2 BCE22 x12. These are the 905a errors GPC1 logs about six a second (#136). Also GPC1 BCE7/BCE8 time out x135 before and x109 after formation (16-word receives, gotAny=0): the display units on those buses are not GPC1's. FIX UNDER TEST (DK-listener build, runs dk-regress, dk-short-0, dk-std-0): deumodel_service_as gives every computer other than the one commanding the display unit its own copy of the unit's reply, first showing it the commander's command word marked command sync; the router lets a non- owner READ a display bus through that path when more than one computer exists (a non-owner's transmit still meets an empty bus), names the owner to the model, and marks buses 6-9 for Listen Mode alongside 1-5 and 18-22. The timing unit now silences its reply on a bus when a command for another device goes out there, so an absent device answers every computer the same way. RESULT AND A REFUTATION. The DK-listener build (runs dk-regress, dk-short-0, dk-std-0) passes the regression gate exactly but does NOT move the break: both two-GPC runs still end at IOQE 90e8 (bus 6) with GPC2 completing 0808 and failing itself, although GPC2's BCE6 receive is now armed in Listen Mode (listen=1) and the display unit delivered 1,392 words to listeners. THE IUAR HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED: run iu-short-0 (RECV ARM trace with iuar=) shows GPC2's BCE6 listener armed with iuar=10, the display unit's address (FIODEUAD, BCEEQU), as GPC1's commander is -- probably left from when GPC2 commanded DK1 itself. WHAT THE TIMING SAYS INSTEAD: GPC1's commander arms its #MIN receive at 467.018493 s and GPC2's listener arms its #RDS at 467.018560 s, 67 us LATER. FIODEUPG's commander (FIODEUCP) sends '#MOUT 0,6' (seven time words), the listen command, '#DLYI 10', '#DLYI FIODELAY' (12) and then '#MIN 0,15'; the listener (FIODEULP) waits '#DLYI 30 DELAY TO LET MOUTC/CMDI EXECUTE' before '#RDS 15,0'. On the wire the seven MOUT words alone take ~231 us, putting the #MIN command after the listener's 495 us delay; our transmits take no wire time, so the command -- and its echo -- arrives while the listener is still delaying. SEPARATELY, EXEC_WIX'S LISTEN-COMMAND DECODE WAS ONE BIT OFF (IUA (data & 0x01f00000) >> 20 is 4 for the listen command 400AFE, subsystem and index 5 and 127 for 10 and 254); corrected per the BCE POO, though PASS does not use #WIX here -- FIOWAIT is #WAT and the MSC starts the listener directly. AND THE LOCAL-STORE PCO DECODE ignores the BCE number the flight software puts in bits 23-27 (FCMTBCD, FIOSTIUA, FIOMGTSK all 'SLL 4' it in); a decode that adds it to bits 7-11 is under test in runs ls-regress, ls-short-0, ls-std-0, judged on its own results since it is not the cause of this break.  RESOLVED FOR BUS 6 BY THE WIRE HOLD. Per-word pacing through the DMA queue (YAGPC_BUS_WORD_US on buses 6-9, runs wt-*) was the wrong model: command words, which are not queued, overtook the queued #MOUT words, and display traffic fell ten-fold (16 polls by 157 s wall against 147 by 70 s) -- those runs were stopped. The wire hold (YAGPC_WIRE_HOLD_BUSES) sends every word at once and holds the BCE's NEXT instruction until its words would have cleared the wire at 33 us each. Regression gate wh-regress with the hold on buses 6-9: EVERY EVENT COUNTER EXACT, periodic unchanged (deu 1849/376/569/368/575). Two-GPC runs with it (wh-dk-0, and wh-dkls-0 with the local-store decode as well -- identical): set formed 465.59 s and held to 467.50 s, 297 SVC and 43 timer syncs in lockstep against 217 and 34 without it, and IOQE 90e8 (bus 6) no longer diverges. The next break is GPC2's self-FTS from FIOERRLC+0313 with R3=90c4. With the hold on the ICC buses too (wh-dkicc-0, buses 1-9) it is WORSE: the set breaks at 465.30 s after 147 SVC syncs, GPC2 self-FTS on 905a -- so the hold is not for buses 1-5. THE LOCAL-STORE DECODE IS NEUTRAL for the two-computer break (ls-short-0 and ls-std-0 end exactly as before: 90e8, 467.025 s and 616.79 s) and passes the regression gate (ls-regress), so it stays on its evidence from the flight source.
  - addrs `90e8-90e8` &middot; csects `FIOERRLC,FCMSFAIL` &middot; procs `BCE6,BCE7,BCE8,BCE20,BCE22` &middot; env `YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE_PE,YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE_FROM,YAGPC_REPEATTRACE` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/deumodel.c,src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `redundant set breaks,self fail to sync,DK bus listener,no peripheral,display unit ownership,stale reply,absent device` &middot; run `fe-short-0,fe-short-1`
- **#148** *(emulator)* AFTER THE #145 FIX, A RARE FOUR-GPC LOSS REMAINS: AN IPR-HANDSHAKE SLIP WITHIN THE BARRIER. One computer issues the I/O-problem-report sync code ~20 us after its peers; after FCMISYNC's T3 hold and FCMNOISE delay it re-reads DIA, finds a neighbour already back at null, and fails it (FCMSFINT from FCMISYNC+0117); the others then drop the late computer. Open -- NOT a barrier-retuning target without new evidence (see #125).
  - evidence: eve16 (fix 273563b7f), 02:42:27, shared 6021.07 s, ~4880 s after OPS 2, host quiet (latprobe max 0.15 ms, no disturbance), no splits, no FIOMSCTO. IPR published G1 .069539, G4 .069539, G3 .069542, G2 .069562 (20 us late). GPC2 FCMSFAIL at .069777, R7=18fab (FCMISYNC+0117, the return after BAL FCMSFINT following the FCMIPRDL noise loop), R4=8 = its N+1 = GPC3; CAM lit 23 first. G3 null .069817; GPC2's view of it stamped pub2=.0697925, 15 us after GPC2's FCMSFAIL -- inside the 25 us barrier, so thread order can let GPC2's DIA read see it first. G1/G3/G4 second IPR .070170-.070209, G2's .070863; GPC1/3/4 FCMSFAIL from FCMSSYNC+0086 (R7=1979c) at .075050. Constants: FCMIT3 F'188' (min ISYNC duration, us), FCMNOISE F'10' (us), FCMCBLKS.asm:683-687. #125 swept YAGPC_BARRIER_US (25 us still failed, 5 us starved MM loading) and says do not retune against pulse width. RATE: the only such episode in eve16's whole run -- one in ~14,600 s after OPS 2; the remaining three GPCs held the set for the last 3 h 12 min. SECOND EPISODE, 2026-09-16 13:02 (see #149): a 4-GPC run with YAGPC_SYNCTRACE, GPC1 the victim. Its last code was 111 null at t=794931.477193; 265 us later GPC2, GPC3 and GPC4 all sent 010 IPR and GPC1 sent none at all; the fail lamp lit in the same millisecond and GPC1 never transmitted again, holding null with its CPU busy for the next 3 h. IPR rate that run: 5096 in 400 s, 1274 per computer.
  - addrs `18fab,1979c,1948c,18ece-18f4f` &middot; csects `FCMISYNC,FCMSFINT,FCMSFAIL,FCMSSYNC` &middot; procs `CPU` &middot; config `G2` &middot; env `YAGPC_BARRIER_US,YAGPC_SYNCORDER,YAGPC_RANGETRACE` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c,src/discretes.c` &middot; symptom `IPR slip,late IPR,fail to sync,GPC voted out,handshake slip,barrier 25 us,CAM 23` &middot; tsec `6021.07` &middot; run `eve16`
- **#154** *(flight-sw)* WHAT GPC/CRT DOES TO A COMPUTER OUTSIDE THE REDUNDANT SET -- READ FROM THE SOURCE, NOT YET SEEN IN OUR MODEL. DM1KEY case 4 tests CDMB_RSALL for the target GPC and, if that computer is NOT in the set, sets CDMV_MAT_DEU_ONLINE for the named DK bus to 0 before calling DIM_ICC_COLLECTOR to send the assignment on. Read alone that says the key takes the display away from the set that was driving it and hands it to a computer that may do nothing with it, with no interlock -- and that doing it to your last displays strands the crew station. A DIRECT TEST ON 2026-09-16 DID NOT SHOW THAT: with GPC4 deliberately out of the set (RUN, OPS 0, out of sync per SPEC 6), GPC/CRT 4 1 EXEC changed nothing at all -- CRT1 stayed with GPC1, kept being driven, and SPEC 6 still showed GPC1 on CRT1 and GPC2 on CRT2. So in OUR model the entry is close to a NO-OP, which is consistent with everything it does travelling by ICC (#107 broken) -- the target never hears it has a display and the set never hears about the offline marking. THE SOURCE READING STANDS; THE HAZARD IS NOT CONFIRMED HERE, and an earlier version of this entry claimed it was. Whatever killed both displays in the run before is therefore STILL UNEXPLAINED and must not be attributed to this line without new evidence.
  - evidence: Source read 2026-09-16 from OI340600/SSSRC/DM1KEY.hal 047800AA-048000AA. REFUTING TEST the same evening, four-GPC run (--gpcs 1,2,3,4 --bce-network, port base 6900), deliberate and deterministic: with the set up in OPS 201, GPC4 was moded STBY then RUN so it left the set and re-initialized into OPS 0, then GPC/CRT 4 1 EXEC was entered. Result: no change -- CRT1 remained on GPC1 and remained driven; SPEC 6 PRO reported GPC4 out of sync, in RUN, OPS 0, with GPC1 driving CRT1 and GPC2 driving CRT2. THE EARLIER OBSERVATION THIS ENTRY WAS BUILT ON, that GPC/CRT 3 1 EXEC followed by GPC/CRT 3 2 EXEC left both CRTs in POLL FAIL with zero polls and zero fills over 20 s, is real and logged, but its cause is now open: the state then differed in that GPC3 had been VOTED OUT and taken through STBY/RUN/OPS 201 PRO first, and it is not known which keyboard each entry was made from, which decides which GPC processed it and whether any effect could be local rather than ICC. NEXT STEP: rerun the refuting test with YAGPC_ICCTRACE to see whether the GPC/CRT ICC message reaches the target at all, which separates 'the key is a no-op here' from 'the key works and the receiver ignores it'.
  - csects `DM1KEY,DIM_ICC_COLLECTOR,CDMB_RSALL,CDMV_MAT_DEU_ONLINE` &middot; procs `DK6,DK7,BCE6,BCE7` &middot; tape `v44boot` &middot; files `SSSRC/DM1KEY.hal` &middot; symptom `GPC/CRT does nothing,GPC/CRT no-op,POLL FAIL,both CRTs dead,locked out of the crew station,DEU offline,no poll replies,keystrokes ignored,SPEC 6 shows wrong CRT owner` &middot; doc `OI340600 SSSRC/DM1KEY.hal case 4 (GPC_CRT_KEY)`
- **#156** *(method)* OUR OWN NBAT MAY BE WHY A COMPUTER THAT LEAVES THE SET CANNOT COME BACK. examples/4gpc-startup.script gives GPC3 and GPC4 ONE BUS EACH -- an FC string -- and nothing else: no CRT, no mass memory, no payload string, no launch data bus. Inside the redundant set that is survivable, because they take mass-memory and display data as LISTENERS on the bus GPC1 or GPC2 commands. Outside it they have no mass memory bus of their own and no CRT. BUT THE MASS-MEMORY HALF OF THAT ARGUMENT IS WRONG AND IS RETRACTED: an overlay is loaded GPC-TO-GPC BY PREFERENCE over the launch data buses, with mass memory only as a fallback, so a computer without an MM assignment is not stuck for that reason -- see the GPC-to-GPC overlay entry. What remains of this entry is the CRT half and the question of whether the flight configuration is the right one at all. Every symptom recorded in #155 -- an out-of-set computer doing no I/O but its own ICC, settling into OPS 0 after being briefly readmitted, being unreachable from any keyboard -- follows from that configuration without needing an emulator defect at all. THIS MAY BE CORRECT FLIGHT BEHAVIOUR AND A BAD CONFIGURATION ON OUR PART, and it must be ruled out before more emulator hunting.
  - evidence: Read 2026-09-16 from examples/4gpc-startup.script against CD0001.dfg and ARFDPSCO.hal. The script's NBAT is ITEM 1 +2 (CZ2V_MC_REQ = memory configuration 2); ITEM 2 +1, 3 +2, 4 +3, 5 +4 (GPC SEL, so CZ2B_GRT_GPC_SET for MC 2 names GPCs 1-4); ITEM 7 +1, 8 +2, 9 +3, 10 +4 (FC strings 1-4); ITEM 11 +1 (payload string); ITEM 12 +1, 13 +2 (CRT1, CRT2); ITEM 18 +1, 19 +2 (MM1, MM2). Items 14-17 are never entered, so CRT3, CRT4 and both launch data buses stay 0. CD0001.dfg declares ITEM 7-19 as I1 range 0-5 and ARFDPSCO.hal:011000BA stores them as CZ2V_STRNG_MC$(ARF_NO-6), whose display grouping is 5 + 4 + 4 -- strings 1-4 plus PL, then CRT 1-4, then the launch data buses and MM1/MM2. Corroborating measurement from the live run (#155): the two computers outside the set logged 188 I/O-complete syncs against 187 ICC commands in 30 s -- one completion per ICC command and nothing else -- while the two inside logged ~2720, i.e. about 2530 other transactions. TO TEST: assign GPC3 and GPC4 a CRT (ITEM 14/15) and a mass memory in the NBAT, then repeat the deterministic repro (set up in OPS 201, one computer RUN -> STBY -> RUN, then GPC/CRT it a display). If it reloads and rejoins, the emulator was never at fault. PROVENANCE, TRACED 2026-09-16: THE ITEM NUMBERING IS DOCUMENTED, THE VALUES ARE OURS. #99 cites TD115 Rev B sec 5 pp.5-3/5-4 for the NBAT's structure -- the MEM/BUS CONFIG table on GPC MEMORY, ITEM 1 plus an MC number to choose which NBAT is edited, ITEMs 2-6 the target set, CRT 1-4 at ITEMs 12-15 in the figure -- and that an NBAT takes effect on an OPS transition or recall, not when typed. The VALUES were traced back through examples/4gpc-startup.script, the commit that introduced crewscript.py, and HANDOFF-SYNC-MARGIN.md (which only transcribes them) to discretePanel/temp.txt, an instruction sheet headed 'INSTRUCTIONS: GPCs 1, 2, 3, 4 with 2 CRTs' that annotates each line with its intent ('CRT 1 -> GPC1 (keep CRT 1 on GPC1)', 'mass memory 1 -> GPC1') and CITES NOTHING. So the arrangement was composed to suit our two-CRT four-GPC setup; it is not a flight configuration. ALREADY KNOWN TO MISBEHAVE: #119 found on a single-GPC control run that the OPS load came from MM1 DESPITE ITEM 19 ASSIGNING MM2 -- only the IPL and GPC MEMORY load used MM2 -- and recorded that the NBAT's mass-memory assignment is not taking effect the way the multi-computer work assumed, an assumption never tested. That is precisely the item this entry turns on. THE AUTHORITATIVE VALUES ARE IN THE LOCAL IBIBLIO MIRROR AND HAVE NEVER BEEN CONSULTED: ~/Desktop/sandroid.org/public_html/apollo/Shuttle/ holds a Post Insertion checklist per flight (the book that configures the GPCs and transitions to OPS 201 after ascent, e.g. STS-114, STS-119), plus the DPS Dictionary in several revisions, the DPS Console Handbook and the DPS Workbook. Read the Post Insertion GPC configuration pages, compare with ours, and correct the script -- a self-contained job needing no simulation. THE REAL TABLE, FOUND 2026-09-16 in STS-134 Post Insertion (local mirror, Shuttle/Post Insertion/, pdf p.14-16 = book pages 1-2..1-4). Its MEMORY CONFIGURATION TABLE gives, by column CONFIG 1 / 2 / 3 / 4(5) for OPS G1,G6 / G2 / G3 / SM -- GPC SEL 1,2,3,4 / 1,2 / 1,2,3,4 / 4; STR 1-4 = 1,2,3,4 / 1,2,1,2 / 1,2,3,4 / 0,0,0,0; P/L = 1 / 1 / 0 / 4; CRT 1-4 = 1,2,3,0 / 1,2,0,2 / 1,2,3,0 / 4,4,0,4; L 1-2 = 1,2 / 0,0 / 0,0 / 4,4; MM 1-2 = 1,2 / 1,2 / 1,2 / 4,4. SO OPS 2 ON ORBIT IS A TWO-GPC SET: GPC SEL is 1,2, the procedure says in as many words 'Assign strings 1,3 to one GPC and strings 2,4 to other GPC', the post-insertion DUAL G2 table gives the target-set field as 12000, and step 10 RECONFIG GPCs then modes the others to HALT with one left as the SM GPC on config 4(5). The nominal OPS 201 NBAT is therefore ITEM 1 +2; ITEM 2 +1, 3 +2, 4 +0, 5 +0; ITEM 7 +1, 8 +2, 9 +1, 10 +2; ITEM 11 +0; ITEM 12 +1, 13 +2, 14 +0, 15 +2; ITEM 16 +0, 17 +0; ITEM 18 +1, 19 +2. OUR SCRIPT IS NOT THAT AND NOT CONFIG 2 AT ALL: strings one per GPC across four GPCs, CRT1->1, CRT2->2, MM1->1, MM2->2, launch buses unassigned, which is CONFIG 3 -- THE ENTRY CONFIGURATION -- except that we also give P/L to GPC1 where CONFIG 3 says 0. We then request it as MC 2. So our NBAT is a hybrid that never flies: the four-GPC ENTRY bus layout loaded into the two-GPC ON-ORBIT memory configuration. CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PROJECT GOAL: a four-computer redundant set is real, but in OPS 1 (ascent, CONFIG 1) and OPS 3 (entry, CONFIG 3), NOT in OPS 2. If four computers in a genuine set is the target, G3 is the faithful configuration; if a faithful OPS 201 is the target, it should be two computers. Either choice is defensible; the present mixture is not, and every sync finding recorded against this workload was measured on it.
  - csects `ARFDPSCO,DM6OPS,ARCGPC,ARC_GPC_RECONFIG,DM1KEY` &middot; tape `v44boot` &middot; files `yaShuttle/discretePanel/examples/4gpc-startup.script,SSSRC/ARFDPSCO.hal,SSSRC/CD0001.dfg` &middot; symptom `GPC will not rejoin,settles into OPS 0,no mass memory bus,no CRT assigned,out-of-set GPC does no I/O,cannot reload overlay,NBAT incomplete,wrong memory configuration,four GPC set in OPS 2,entry layout in orbit config` &middot; doc `STS-134 Post Insertion, MEMORY CONFIGURATION TABLE and CONFIG GPCs FOR OPS 2, book pages 1-2..1-4 (local mirror Shuttle/Post Insertion/); TD115 Rev B sec 5 for the item numbering via #99`
- **#162** *(emulator)* Three syscall-reduction optimisations are IN THE TREE, GATE-VERIFIED FUNCTIONALLY, BUT THEIR SPEED BENEFIT IS UNMEASURED: sendmmsg batching in bcenet_transport.c, a memoised getenv (new src/envcache.c) replacing all 165 literal getenv call sites, and recvmmsg batching in discretes_poll's drain. Do NOT assume they help; one of them probably does not.
  - evidence: Gate (PORT_BASE=27000 headless-gpcmem.sh 420) passes with every scored counter exact: mmu1 commands 56, blocksRead 431, wordsOut 220731, wordsTaken 220011, wordsLost 720; deu formatFills 8; one 'MODE: IPL'; keystrokes at poll=247. The saturated four-GPC A/B that would have priced them was abandoned because the host kernel was in the state described in #161. WHAT THE PROFILE SAID (saturated 4-GPC, perf -F 999, cpu_core samples -- note this is a HYBRID CPU and a default perf report shows only the ~500 cpu_atom samples, not the 11K cpu_core ones): network syscalls and their kernel stack 31.3%, emulation cpu 12.8%, iop/bus 11.4%, libc/misc 9.7%, timing model 4.7%, locking 2.6%. Top symbols __fdget 5.24%, vehicle_barrier_wait 4.94%, instr_decode 4.83%, __libc_recv 4.38%, pthread_enable_asynccancel 3.88%, getenv 3.23%, __sys_recvfrom 2.68%, __strcmp_avx2 2.28%. THE getenv MEMO IS THE ONE WITH A CLEAR CASE (3.23% plus a share of __strcmp_avx2, and nothing in the tree calls setenv/putenv so the answers cannot change). THE recvmmsg ONE IS DOUBTFUL and should be priced before being believed: discretes_poll is gated to one drain per 250 us and the socket carries only about 2200 messages/s per GPC, so roughly 95% of drains find an EMPTY socket, where recvmmsg costs exactly the same one syscall as recv. Ledger #91 is why: the inter-GPC sync codes no longer travel by socket at all -- they go into the neighbour's register synchronously via discretes_apply_external_pair -- so the datagrams are for external monitors only.
  - env `YAGPC_PACETRACE,YAGPC_BARRIER_US` &middot; files `src/bcenet_transport.c,src/discretes.c,src/envcache.c,src/envcache.h,Makefile` &middot; symptom `core utilization,optimization unmeasured`
- **#163** *(emulator)* THE SIMULATED-TIME BARRIER IS THE LARGEST SINGLE CONSUMER OF THREAD TIME IN A SATURATED FOUR-GPC RUN, and is the best remaining lead for the 3%-per-step optimisation goal -- bigger than anything the instruction path still offers. Raising the barrier delta from the 25 us simulatePASS forces towards the code's own 200 us default is the obvious experiment, and 200 us is still 19x inside FCOS's 3.85 ms sync timeout.
  - evidence: From a 540 s --gpcs 1-4 --crts 3 run: 'vehicle: simulated-time barrier held 44047583 times, 544.370 s total, 0 abandoned (delta 25 us); 42688492 released while spinning, 385893 sleeps'. 544.4 s of hold against 4 threads x 540 s = 2160 thread-seconds is 25% of all thread time, and 97% of the holds ended in the SPIN phase, which burns a core rather than yielding it. The profile attributes 4.94% of cycles to vehicle_barrier_wait itself, but that UNDERSTATES the cost, because the spin's work is charged to what it calls -- barrier_slowest and the clock read that shows up as [vdso] 1.85%. CAUTION ON THE OBVIOUS OBJECTION: the four machines' reported rates differ (0.707/0.803/0.897/0.987) even though the barrier ties them to within 25 us; that is not a contradiction and not evidence the barrier is loose. Each machine's barOffsetUs is set when it JOINS, and rate is sim/wall measured from that machine's own birth, so machines that IPLed at different moments report different rates while covering the same simulated ground. For the same reason STEP COUNTS ARE NOT A THROUGHPUT METRIC -- the same run gave 191M/247M/304M/369M steps across the four -- and rate from YAGPC_PACETRACE is.
  - csects `FCMCSYNC,FCMTSYNC,FCMSSYNC,FCMISYNC` &middot; env `YAGPC_BARRIER_US,YAGPC_PACETRACE` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c,src/rtpacer.c` &middot; symptom `core utilization,barrier spin,25% of thread time`
- **#204** *(emulator)* A RESTORED FIVE-GPC ASCENT VEHICLE STILL LOSES SYNC OCCASIONALLY: 1 of 3 parallel 45-minute holds drew a vote at t=679 s, GPC3 failing itself and being voted out by all four others within 100 ms.  Nothing in the I/O accounting explains it -- no BCE receive timeout, no error termination, no display-bus contention, and the other two vehicles from the SAME snapshot ran the same 45 minutes clean.
  - evidence: THREE SETS OF EVIDENCE, 2026-09-22/23.  (1) ORIGINALLY: three restores of ops101-5gpc-3crt-gpc5crt3 held 45 min each IN PARALLEL; 7700 and 7900 clean, 7500 NOT -- cam.log 'voting: 33 ON (bus) [t=678.9]' then 13, 43 and 23 at t=679.0, GPC3 against ITSELF first and the others following.  All five machines ran to the end, so GPC3 kept running after being voted out.  Pacing 83.9/84.0/84.8 percent, fifteen emulated GPCs on twenty cores.  (2) A REPEAT OF THE SAME THREE IN PARALLEL, instrumented, was CLEAN 3 of 3.  (3) A SINGLE INTERACTIVE VEHICLE on 2026-09-23 (the narrated ascent script, five GPCs, three CRTs, ONE vehicle on the host) DID vote: 33 ON at t=377.6, then 23 and 13 at t=377.7 -- about six seconds after GPC3 was taken to RUN and joined the set, during the IPL of GPC4.  GPC1 and GPC2 WITHDREW their votes on their own at t=710.8, 333 s later; GPC3's vote against itself persisted.  That run had no sync instrumentation.  SO CONTENTION IS NOT REQUIRED: one vehicle alone reproduced it, which refutes the reading that three-up host contention was the trigger.  (4) THE SAME SCRIPT RUN AGAIN WITH THE INSTRUMENTATION ARMED (YAGPC_SYNC_HISTORY=8192, YAGPC_LANDMARKS=1948c, YAGPC_LANDMARKS_REGS=1) ran 2 h 50 min with ZERO votes and zero FCMSFAIL, so the ring was never dumped.  That is the longest clean five-GPC run on record and it passed the t=377 point that failed before.  WHAT THE TWO FAILURES SHARE: the victim votes against ITSELF first, the others follow within 100 ms, and the victim keeps running.  WHAT THEY DO NOT SHARE: one was steady-state on a restored vehicle at t=679, the other six seconds after a computer joined the set at t=377.  NEXT: keep the landmark armed on every long run until one fires; the dump is on the landmark's FIRST hit, which is the self-failing computer, which is the one wanted.
  - procs `CPU` &middot; config `G1` &middot; symptom `fail vote,GPC3 voted out,self vote,five GPC,OPS 101,restored vehicle,1 in 3` &middot; tsec `678-680` &middot; run `ascent5-7500,ascent5-7700,ascent5-7900,ascent5p-7500,ascent5p-7700,ascent5p-7900,narrated-0923`
- **#210** *(flight-sw)* The three MTU ACCUM times on the TIME display (SPEC 2 PRO) freeze and carry a down arrow in the five-computer ascent configuration because THE MTU IS NEVER READ AT ALL.  Not a stale value, not a redundancy-management latch that an ITEM could clear: the read command is not issued.  The timing unit answers a command of iua 10, function 126, 39 words (MTU_READ_CMD 0x024C26) on buses 20, 21 and 22, one bus per accumulator.  In a one-computer OPS 0 vehicle each bus carries 342 of those reads in a few minutes and all three accumulators track GPC time to the millisecond with no arrow.  In a five-computer vehicle in OPS 1 the same buses carry 66,930 commands in 71 seconds -- about 940 a second, 21 distinct device/function pairs, the flight-critical traffic of the ascent -- and NOT ONE of them is an MTU read.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-24.  Single GPC, OPS 0, YAGPC_CMDTRACE=20,21,22: bus 20 2,720 commands of which 342 func=126, bus 21 684 of which 342, bus 22 2,719 of which 342; the TIME display read back whole (NSTS_ANNOUNCE_ROWS=all) shows MTU ACCUM 1, 2 and 3 all equal to GPC time and advancing, e.g. 267/15:07:05.168, for eight minutes, on the commanding computer AND on a listening one in a two-computer run.  Five GPCs restored from examples/5gpc-3crt-subtitled.snapshot (OPS 1, CRT1 and CRT2 in 1011/LAUNCH TRAJ 1, CRT3 in 0001/GPC MEMORY under GPC5): bus 20 commanded by GPC1, 21 by GPC2, 22 by GPC3, 66,930/66,484/66,930 commands and zero func=126 on any of them; no receive timeouts on those buses at all, so nothing is failing -- the read is simply not being issued.  GPC4 and GPC5 command no timing bus and only listen, which is why the owner sees the same frozen values on CRT3/GPC5 as on CRT1/GPC1, and why ITEM 34, 35 and 36 (and their +1 through +5 forms) change nothing.  OPEN, and the question is which side is wrong: whether PASS reads the MTU only in OPS 0 and a frozen accumulator in ascent is CORRECT, or whether our I/O is starving the read on a bus carrying 940 transactions a second.  The two variables are confounded in what has been measured so far -- the one-computer runs are OPS 0 and the five-computer run is OPS 1 -- so the discriminating test is a multi-computer vehicle held in OPS 0 with the same trace.  THE OPEN QUESTION IS ANSWERED, AND IT IS NOT CORRECT BEHAVIOUR.  The MTU reading code is resident in EVERY memory configuration: FIOPRMPG, FPMMTURM, FPMMTUFX, FPMFXMTU, FPMUPMTU and FIOMTUTG all appear in csects-G2, G3, G8, G9, G16, P9, S2 and SSW alike.  Unlike AIG_DEU_LOADER, which really is confined to the three configurations its own gate names (#208/#209), nothing about the MTU reader is OPS-restricted, so a frozen accumulator in OPS 9 or OPS 1 is not the flight software declining to read -- the code to read is there.  MEASURED ON THE MODEL'S OWN COUNTER, which is not subject to the command trace's budget: a five-computer vehicle restored into OPS 1 and left alone for three and a half minutes reports mtu: {"commands":542581,"timeReads":0,"wordsOut":0} -- over half a million commands on those buses and NOT ONE read, so not one word ever leaves the timing unit.  AND IT IS NOT GOING SOMEWHERE ELSE: receive timeouts in that run are confined to BCE 6, 7 and 8, the display buses, 696 per computer; no other bus times out, so there is no unanswered MTU read on a bus this model does not own.  THE OWNER'S OWN TEST SEPARATES THE VARIABLES BETTER THAN MINE DID: with the redundant set in OPS 9 and GPC5 in OPS 0, ALL the accumulators are frozen INCLUDING GPC5's; taking the SET to OPS 0 starts all of them counting, including GPC5's; putting the set back to OPS 9 freezes all of them again.  GPC5's own OPS is irrelevant because GPC5 commands no timing bus -- it listens -- so what it displays is whatever the commanders last read.  The OPS that matters is the redundant set's, and the failure is therefore in whatever decides to issue the read, not in redundancy management, not in the display, and not in GPC5.  He also reports that in OPS 0 the down arrows REMAIN while the values count, and that all four fields look like copies of the GPC time, which is the next thing to establish: whether the accumulators are genuinely accepted in OPS 0 or merely back-filled from the GPC's clock.  NARROWED FURTHER, AND ONE CAUSE REFUTED.  It is not the number of computers nor which of them commands a bus: THREE computers held in OPS 0, each commanding one timing bus -- the same structure a five-computer vehicle has -- read the unit 1,137 times in seven minutes (mtu: commands 6795, timeReads 1137, wordsOut 7959).  One computer in OPS 0 reads it 342 times.  Five computers in G1 read it not at all, twice measured: 542,581 commands/0 reads and 598,067 commands/0 reads.  So the determinant is the MEMORY CONFIGURATION, SSW against G1, and nothing else that has been varied.  REFUTED, do not retry: that the unit is commfaulted by the commands it does not answer.  Our model replies to exactly one command, MTU_READ_CMD, and is silent for every other command sent to its own IUA 10 -- and there are such commands, measured, immediately after an IPL: func 132 and 138 of 33 words and func 136 of 128 words at t=44.4 to 44.7, each timing out its BCE.  Making the model answer them (an experiment behind YAGPC_MTU_ANSWER_ALL, since reverted) cut the timing-bus timeouts from 5 to 2 and changed nothing else: the G1 vehicle still read the unit 0 times in 483,431 commands.  Those functions are probably the NSP's, which shares IUA 10 on buses 20 and 22 per FIONSPPG, and not the timing unit's at all.  ALSO ESTABLISHED: our reply passes FPMLIMCK field by field (FPMMTURM.asm 066100-069600) -- GMT days 0x267 within 0x0001..0x0399, hours 0x15 <= 0x23, minutes <= 0x59, and seconds packed two bits left so that 59 s reads exactly as the limit 0x0164, which is what our bcd_pack produces; MET all zero passes its own tests.  So the data is not being rejected for being out of limits.  AND THE BUS PROGRAM HAS AN EXPLICIT BYPASS: FIOPRMPG's FIOMTUR1 begins #LBR/#CMDI and then FIOBY51 #BU FIORK51 '*OVERLAID BY BCE BYPASS CODE', so a bypassed transaction falls straight to FIOWAIT and the #MIN 0,6 that reads the unit never executes.  That is the shape of what is happening; what applies the bypass in G1 and not in SSW is the open question.  WHEN IT STOPS, TRACED THROUGH A WHOLE ACID TEST (YAGPC_CMDTRACE=20,21,22, five computers, IPL to ascent).  The read runs steadily at about one per second per bus -- 93 to 96 reads per 30 s across the three -- from t=60 all the way to t=690, through all five IPLs.  It then STOPS BUS BY BUS as the crew enters the NBAT: FC2 and FC3 fall silent at about t=705, which is 'ITEM 1 +9 EXEC', the item that selects the configuration the table is being written for; FC1 carries on alone until about t=765, just after 'ITEM 11 +0'.  From t=780 onwards there is not one read, through 'OPS 9 0 1 PRO' at t=798 and the later OPS 1 transition, and none afterwards for the rest of the run.  So the loss is not caused by the OPS transition itself: it happens WHILE THE NBAT IS BEING ENTERED, before any PRO, and the transition merely never restores it.  Each bus has its own commander by then -- FC1 GPC1, FC2 GPC2, FC3 GPC3 -- and all three keep carrying heavy traffic afterwards (66,930 commands on FC1 in 71 s), so the buses are alive and commanded; only this transaction is gone.  CAUTION FOR WHOEVER PICKS THIS UP: two earlier readings in this entry were artefacts of reading a log while its run was still going, and one was an ordering mistake -- the yaGPC2 log interleaves five computers, so the LAST LINE mentioning a bus is not its LATEST event.  Bin by time, do not take the last match.  ROOT CAUSE, READ OUT OF MEMORY: THE READ IS BYPASSED, NOT UNSCHEDULED.  The CSECT tables give FIOPRMPG's layout exactly -- it is at 117872 in BOTH csects-SSW and csects-G9, with contents FIOMTUR1 +0, FIOBY51 +4, FIORK51 +8, FIOMTUR2 +10, FIOBY52 +14, FIOMTUR3 +20, FIOBY53 +24 -- so the three bypass slots can be read straight out of any capture.  In a vehicle whose reads WORK (one GPC, OPS 0) they hold f001 cc78, f001 cc82, f001 cc8c: #BU to FIORK51/52/53, which reaches the common code and its #MIN 0,6.  In a vehicle whose reads are DEAD (the five-computer OPS 1 capture, examples/5gpc-3crt-subtitled.snapshot) ALL THREE hold c022 c000 -- #DLYI 34 then #DLYI 0.  That is precisely the 'OVERLAID BY BCE BYPASS CODE' the source names at FIOPRMPG.asm 008100: with the branch replaced by delays the program falls through to FIOWAIT, ends as commander, and never reads the unit.  So FCOS has deliberately removed the transaction, which is what it does to a COMMFAULTED device -- the arrows on the display are that same judgement.  AND THAT INVALIDATES THE REFUTATION RECORDED ABOVE: the experiment that answered every command sent to the unit's IUA was run against a RESTORED vehicle, and a restore brings back the bypassed program with the rest of memory, so the test could not have worked whatever the truth was.  It is being re-run from a fresh IPL.  Whoever reads this: an experiment about how the flight software CONFIGURES itself cannot be run from a snapshot of a machine that already configured itself.  STATUS: the MECHANISM is settled and the TRIGGER is not.  What makes MTU1-3 freeze with a down arrow is that FCOS has overlaid all three read transactions with delays; that is read directly out of memory and is not in doubt.  What is not yet known is what makes FCOS do it.  The obvious candidate -- a commfault from the commands our model leaves unanswered at its own IUA just after the IPL -- could not be tested properly: the only quick vehicle is a restored one, which carries the bypass in its memory image, and a fresh acid test with the answering experiment on never reached the NBAT, because answering every command on three flight-critical buses adds a quarter of a million words of traffic and collapses the run's pace.  An instrument that changes the measurement, again.  NEXT, and cheaper than another acid test: watch FIOBY51 at 117876 in a fresh five-computer run by capturing twice, once before the NBAT is typed and once after, which shows the overlay being written and brackets it to a single item; and read the I/O error log, CZ2V_IO_ERR_LOG in CZ2_COMMON, to see whether device 22 is logged at all.  Both need the run to reach t=700 without the experiment, which it does comfortably when the machine is free.
  MEASURED 2026-09-24, AND IT IS NOT THE NBAT BY ITSELF.  A single
computer that types the WHOLE ascent NBAT -- the seventeen items of
examples/5gpc-3crt-subtitled.script, ITEM 1 +9 through ITEM 19 +2 -- never
bypasses anything.  FIOBY51/52/53 (117876/117886/117896) are untouched from the
moment GPCIPL's memory fill leaves them: YAGPC_WATCHHW=1cc70-1ccb0, a range
covering the listener slots as well, fires only during that fill at t=122 s and
never again, and MTU reads continue on all three buses across the items and
past them -- iua 10 func 126, 62 or 63 per bus in every 60 s bin from t=240 to
the run's end at t=545, with the items typed at t=400 to t=496.  Run:
headless-gpcmem.sh, TAPE=OI340700-v44boot.mmv, DEUMF=1,
YAGPC_DEUKEYS_SIMTIME=1, the items as eighteen DEUKEYS batches, all eighteen
confirmed delivered.  So the trigger needs PEERS rather than the NBAT: what
bypasses the read depends on there being other computers on those buses, which
is also why one computer in OPS 0 has never shown the fault.
  THE WRITER IS FCMBCEMD, AND THE DOWN ARROW IS THE SAME EVENT.  FIOPRMPG
carries TWO elements per accumulator -- a commander (FIOMTUR1-3, bypass slots
FIOBY51/52/53) and a listener (FIOMTUL1-3, slots FIOBY51L/52L/53L at
117912/117920/117928) -- and both are named in FCMRTBLE, 'NSP AND MTU RESTORE
TABLES'.  The frozen value c022 c000 identifies which routine wrote it:
FCMBCEMD builds its bypass as TBCECNT OR FCMDLYI, and FCMDLYI is X'C000C000'
(FCMCBLKS.asm 025800), while the only other writer, FCMRSTFC, uses a delay of
FCMDLA=75.  The overlay is therefore the generic element bypass, not the
bypass of the NSP that is not in use.  FIOERRLC calls FCMBCEMD at its line
075200 after the SECOND counted error on a comfaultable transaction, and the
next block of that same routine is '*** DETERMINE IF DOWNARROW IS TO BE
DISPLAYED', which calls FIODWNAR.  The frozen accumulator and the arrow beside
it are not two symptoms to be explained separately: they are one act of the
I/O error logic, and the arrow is evidence that an I/O ERROR was counted.
  AND THERE IS A RESTORE PATH, which is what a correct reconfiguration uses:
FCMBUSCM request type 15, 'RESTORE MTU AND NSP BCE ELEMENTS' (SVC 36), calls
FCMRSTFC, which rewrites the commander AND the listener slot of all eight
NSP/MTU entries from FCMRTBLE.  So a bypass that stays is either an error that
keeps being counted or a type-15 request that is never made.
  THE TRIGGER, MEASURED 2026-09-24, AND IT IS THE OPS TRANSITION, NOT THE
NBAT.  TWO computers reproduce it, which makes the fixture cheap: simulatePASS
--gpcs 1,2 --crts 2 with examples/2gpc-ops201.script on OI340700-v44boot.mmv,
about four minutes to the event.  With YAGPC_WATCHHW=1cc70-1ccb0 both machines
write all six slots, and the times place it exactly: the crew types OPS 2 0 1
PRO at panel t=228.5, FCOS RESTORES the six slots at t=241.72 (f001 cc82 and
the rest, the ordinary #BU), and then BYPASSES all six at t=242.365, 242.551
and 242.953 -- one accumulator every 0.4 s, and within each pair the commander
slot and its listener slot 14 microseconds apart.  MTU reads run at 93 per 30 s
up to t=240, fall to 4 in the bin containing the bypass and are ZERO for the
remaining 650 s of the run: nothing ever restores them.
  THE CALLER IS FIOERRLC.  Every one of the six stores is from nia 181f5 =
FCMBCEMD+243, and YAGPC_NIARING=1024 with YAGPC_RINGTRIG=1cc74:c022 gives the
path into it: ...19d9d 19d9e ... 19ddc then 18102, which is FIOERRLC+745
through FIOERRLC+808 (FIOSSM07+67..+130) falling into FCMBCEMD+0.  That is the
CALL FCMBCEMD at FIOERRLC.asm line 075200, reached only after the SECOND
counted error on a comfaultable transaction, and the next block of that same
routine is the one that displays the down arrow.  The 14-microsecond pairing is
explained by FCMBCEMD's FIOBCML, its 'COMMANDER/LISTENER ENTRY', which bypasses
the commander element and then computes the listener element's address from
TBCECFDS + TBMTLIST and bypasses that too -- one request, two overlays, each
with its own TBCECNT, which is why the commander gets c022 (34) and the
listener c000 (0).
  SO THE FLIGHT SOFTWARE IS BEHAVING CORRECTLY AND OUR I/O IS NOT.  FCOS is
being told, twice, that the MTU read failed.  External authority agrees the
elements should be live: the STS-134 DASS GNC9 dump (mafgen/DASS_G9.ASC, the
FIOPRMPG block at 01CC70) has FIOBY51/52/53 = F001 CC78 / F001 CC82 / F001 CC8C
and FIOBY51L/52L/53L = F001 CC9C / F001 CCA4 / F001 CCAC, every one of them the
live branch, so a bypassed MTU element is not something the build ships with.
The remaining question is narrow and is an EMULATOR question, not a flight
software one: what makes the MTU read transaction fail on all three buses, on
both computers, immediately after an OPS transition that reassigns the
flight-critical buses, when the same transaction succeeds indefinitely before
it.  Next: YAGPC_ERRTERM_TRACE=20,21,22 on the same two-computer fixture.
  WHY PEERS ARE NEEDED, FROM THE SOURCE.  FIOERRLC branches on FIOGPCWE,
the number of GPCs reporting the same error: 'IF (CHI,R5,NE,FIOSRBID),ANDIF,
(CHI,R6,EQ,1),ANDIF,(CHI,R5,NE,FIOPMUDV)' takes the SELF FAIL-TO-SYNC path when
exactly ONE computer has the error, and only the ELSE branch -- '*** PROCESS
COMFAULTABLE TRANSACTIONS' -- bypasses the element and displays the arrow.  One
computer therefore can never bypass anything, however badly its I/O fails, and
two can.  That is exactly the measured difference between the one-computer run
(no bypass, reads for ever) and the two-computer run (all six slots bypassed),
and it is why the fault has only ever been seen with a set.
  AND THE ELEMENT IT BLAMES IS READ OFF A RUNNING BCE.  FIOERRLC names the
failing element at line 054600, 'PC R6,R6  READ BASE REG FROM BCE' (command
skeleton FIOBARC3 = X'22098000', bank 2 word 3 of that BCE's page), and then
searches the BMT for the entry whose TBCEBUF equals it.  Element 0x0d's BMT
entry at 0x9f0c reads 1b00 9c7e 1018 cc74 2236: buffer 0x9c7e, which is the
#LBR operand of FIOMTUR1 in the DASS dump, and bypass slot 0xcc74, which is
FIOBY51.  So FCOS really did blame MTU1.  IT NEVER FAILED.  With
YAGPC_ERRTERM_TRACE=all over a whole two-computer run, 996 error terminations
were recorded -- BCE8 518, BCE20 201, BCE22 198, BCE14-17/21/23 twelve each --
and NOT ONE has a program address inside FIOPRMPG (117872-118001); the only two
in that range are BCE24 at t=42 s, before PASS owns the memory.  The MTU read
transaction is answered right up to the moment it is bypassed (last read
t=242.385, bypass t=242.69).  What FCOS is reacting to is an error condition on
the flight-critical buses as a whole, and it then sweeps BCEs 14 to 23 -- the
counter at 0x8d81 walks 0x0e to 0x17 on each pass -- reading each one's base
register.  Because the BCE it reads has NOT error-terminated it is still
running its chain, so the register names whichever element is current: on bus
20 two consecutive sweeps 20 ms apart blamed element 0x05 (buffer 0x3d14) and
then element 0x0d (buffer 0x9c7e, the MTU).  The blame is effectively
arbitrary, and the MTU's turn comes round twice.
  ROOT CAUSE.  Our vehicle has no flight-critical MDMs.  In OPS 0 that costs
little; after a GNC OPS transition the FC chains are full of transactions to
devices that never answer, every computer sees the same errors, FIOGPCWE is
therefore greater than one, and FIOERRLC's comfaultable path runs continuously
over all ten flight-critical buses.  Each pass bypasses whatever element the
BCE's base register happens to name, and the timing unit's read -- commander
and listener together, through FCMBCEMD's FIOBCML -- is one of the elements it
names.  The frozen MTU ACCUM times and their down arrows are a SECONDARY effect
of the unmodelled flight-critical hardware, not a defect of the timing unit
model or of the MTU bus programs.  A fix therefore has to make the
flight-critical transactions stop failing (a stub MDM that answers with the
expected word count is the smallest version) rather than anything MTU-specific;
patching the timing unit alone would only move the arrow to another element.
  AND THE MTU IS SIX BYPASSES OUT OF A HUNDRED AND TWENTY-ONE.  The
prediction that the timing unit is collateral is measurable from a capture
already in the tree, without a run.  Take the twenty CSECTs that csects-SSW
marks type BCE -- the bus programs -- and compare a redundant-set member
against GPC5 in examples/5gpc-3crt-subtitled.snapshot halfword by halfword,
counting the places where GPC5 holds a live #BU (f0xx) and the set member holds
a #DLYI (c0xx).  There are 121 of them, in TWELVE different bus programs:
FIOMFEPG 34, FIOHFEPG 29, FIOGNIPG 16, FIONSPPG 12, FIOSRBPG 8, FIOPRMPG 6,
FIOGPSPG 6, FIOMUWP9 4, FIOPDSPG 2, FIODDUPG 2, FIOMDMPG 1, FIOSMFPG 1.  The
six in FIOPRMPG are the MTU's three commander and three listener slots; the
other 115 are the forward and aft MDMs, the GN&C inputs, the NSP, the SRBs, the
GPS, the DDUs.  FCOS has commfaulted essentially the whole flight-critical I/O
of every computer in the set, because in our vehicle essentially none of it
answers, and the TIME display's three frozen accumulators are simply the part
of that a crew member can see.  Anything MTU-specific would be treating one
symptom out of twelve.  REPRODUCTION, four minutes: cd yaShuttle/discretePanel
&& DISPLAY=:48 YAGPC_WATCHHW=1cc70-1ccb0 python3 -u simulatePASS.py --gpcs 1,2
--crts 2 --size 384 --no-wait-user --tape ~/workspace/pass-run/OI340700-v44boot.mmv
--script examples/2gpc-ops201.script --port-base 7900 --logs OUT --duration 400
-- the six slots go from f001 to c0xx about 14 s after OPS 2 0 1 PRO.
  PROVED BY ANSWERING THE TRAFFIC, AND THE NAIVE FIX IS NOT A FIX.
YAGPC_FC_STUB (src/mtumodel.c, default OFF) answers every command on buses
20-22, not just the timing unit's, with the number of ZERO words the command
asked for -- the count is the command's own low nine bits plus one, so no
device table is needed.  Same fixture, same script, same tape: WITH IT ON the
six MTU slots are NEVER bypassed and the reads continue to the end of the run
(t=318 s and still going), where the control stops dead at t=242.4.  That is
the causal claim demonstrated rather than argued: the timing unit freezes
because the traffic AROUND it is unanswered, and answering that traffic --
without touching the MTU model, the MTU bus programs or anything else -- keeps
it alive.
  BUT THE SET BREAKS.  The control runs record no votes at all; the stub run
records two at t=254.7, 'voting: 21 ON (bus)' and 'voting: 12 ON (bus)', each
computer failing the other, and the MTU read rate then decays from 121 per 30 s
to 38.  Zeros are not what an MDM sends, and PASS acts on what it is handed.
So the stub settles the diagnosis and must not be mistaken for the repair: a
real fix has to model the flight-critical devices well enough that the data
passes the flight software's own checks, which is a vehicle-wide piece of work
and a fidelity decision, not a patch.  Keep the flag for re-proving #210
cheaply; do not turn it on for a simulation anyone is watching.
  THE BLAME MECHANISM IS NOT THE DEFECT -- MY OWN HYPOTHESIS, REFUTED.
The suspicion above, that FCOS names an innocent element because it reads the
base register off a BCE that has not stopped, is WRONG, and three measurements
settle it.  (a) YAGPC_BASEREAD_TRACE (new, src/iop.c, default off) prints every
CPU read of a BCE's base register -- bank 2 word 3, the FIOBARC3 = X'22098000'
command FIOERRLC issues at 054600 -- with that BCE's STAT1, STAT4 and program
counter.  Over a whole two-computer run 1,243 such reads were taken and 1,236
of them found the BCE STOPPED, go=0 busy=0: the identification is honest, and
the BCE really is parked at the element that failed.  Our scheduler is right to
do that -- iop_exec_slice refuses to step a processor whose Busy/Wait bit is
clear, and the BCE Principles of Operation 3.4.7 says a time-out 'will also
error terminate to the Wait State'.  (b) NOT ONE of those reads returned 09c7e,
the timing unit's buffer, so the MTU element was never individually named.
(c) FIOBMTAD never held the MTU's BMT entry address either: that entry is at
09ef8 in our own G2 memory, read out of a capture of this fixture -- buf=9c7e,
flag=1015, adrs=cc74, and the halfword at +3 is 2236, whose high byte 22 is
exactly the delay in the c022 overlay -- and the string '9ef8' does not appear
anywhere in the run's log.
  WHAT FCMBCEMD IS ACTUALLY DOING IS A WHOLE-STRING BYPASS.  The NIA ring taken
at the instant of the overlay is entirely inside FCMBCEMD and is a LOOP --
181df 181e1 181e2 181e3 181e5 181ee 181f0 181f1 181f3 181f5 (the store) 181f6
181f8 181f9 181fb 181fc 181fe 18200 18206 18207 and round again, about three
and a half times in the 64 addresses the ring holds.  FCMBCEMD's single-element
bypass sets R5 to 1 and runs that loop ONCE; a loop means it is walking a
TABLE of BMT entries, which is its bus-or-string path.  So the timing unit is
not being blamed for someone else's failure: FCOS is bypassing everything on
the affected strings, which is what it is supposed to do to a string whose
devices have stopped answering, and the MTU's six slots are six of the 121 that
go with it.
  CONCLUSION.  There is no element-identification defect to fix, and no
MTU-specific defect either.  The only thing wrong in the vehicle is that the
flight-critical devices are not modelled, which YAGPC_FC_STUB demonstrates from
the other direction by keeping every MTU slot live for as long as the traffic
