That does not generalise: discretePanel/mdmrt votes FOUR times with NO bus having only one computer in error (20 and 22 have all four, 21 has three, 23 has three), and discretePanel/learn2 votes NINE times with no such bus either (14-17 all four).  So a bus held by one computer is neither necessary for a vote nor demonstrated to cause one; it appeared in one run of three and I generalised from it immediately after correcting myself for generalising from one run.  THE MEASUREMENT IS ALSO TOO COARSE TO ANSWER THE QUESTION: FIOGPCWE is evaluated at an INSTANT, and this aggregates over a whole 1000 s run, so two computers erring on the same bus minutes apart count as 'universal' here while being FIOGPCWE==1 at each moment.  Binning by time needs the per-computer clock alignment used earlier in this entry (a shared wire event), and that has not been done for the vote windows.  WHAT IS STILL SOLID: the counts themselves, the identification of the failing elements, and that FIOGPCWE is per bus rather than per element (FIOCMPLT FIOICCF, 'MASK OF BCES WITH ERROR THIS GPC', 'SLL R3,0(R7) SHIFT LEFT BY BCE #').  WHAT IS NOT: any claim about WHY the set votes when the timing unit is alive.  THE 'ASYMMETRY IS NOT WHAT VOTES' CONCLUSION ABOVE IS OVERTURNED BY #225.  It rested on aggregating error terminations over a whole 1000 s run and finding no bus held by exactly one computer -- while noting, and then ignoring, that FIOGPCWE is evaluated at an INSTANT.  Read directly out of PASS's own store (YAGPC_WATCHHW on 08d83, FIOCBLKS+445), FIOGPCWE is written as 1 -- the self-fail condition -- 328 times in a single run, with all four computers reaching it within a tenth of a second of each other at panel t=818.6-818.7.  The self-fail path IS entered; the aggregate simply could not see it.  Use #225's method, not error-count aggregation, for anything about the votes.
  - addrs `1d5b6,1d636,1d67c,1d57a,1d820,1d8ac,1e8b0,1e8bc,1e908,1e91a,1e942,1e8fc,1ccae,1cc74` &middot; csects `FIOIMUC1,FIOIMUPG,FIOPRMPG,FIONSPPG,FCMRTBLE,FCMBCEMD,FIOERRLC` &middot; procs `BCE20,BCE21,BCE22,MTU` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS,YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE,YAGPC_ERRTERM_TRACE` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/fcbustable.h` &middot; symptom `MTU ACCUM blank with M,string commfaulted,IMU not modelled,universal error,14-word read` &middot; tsec `760` &middot; doc `fc-bus-survey table: 524c0d FIOIMUC1 14 words` &middot; run `listener2,listener,acid-wire`
- **#229** *(emulator)* THE TIMING UNIT MIGHT BE BETTER SERVED OVER THE NETWORK DATABUS THAN BY AN IN-PROCESS FUNCTION CALL, and the evidence is that every listener defect found in it so far was the in-process model failing to reproduce what bcenet_framer.c already does. mtumodel is reached by a direct call: the router claims buses 14-23, calls mtumodel_service_as and RETURNS, never falling through to the framer, so none of that traffic is multicast and no out-of-process device can see or answer it. The framer is installed as the BASE servicer and the in-process models sit in front of it.
  - evidence: Owner's suggestion, 2026-09-26, and it is supported by the shape of this work.  THREE defects fixed in one night were all the in-process model diverging from framer semantics: #214, the wire pacing indexing on the reply cursor rather than on words delivered; #218, a listener one transfer behind losing its data, whose fix explicitly imitates 'CLEAR ALWAYS, ECHO ALWAYS -- bcenet_framer.c's rule on every network bus, and the only listener delivery in this tree that demonstrably works across several computers'; and #219, buses the model had grown to own never being added to the Listen-Mode mask.  The framer also stamps each word with a 'due' time on the SHARED simulated clock, which mmumodel has done since it was written and mtumodel did not until the wire pacing was added -- the very change that introduced #214.  THE OBJECTION FROM LATENCY IS WEAKER THAN IT LOOKS: when a word becomes visible is governed by its simulated due time, not by when the datagram physically arrives, so UDP does not by itself perturb simulated timing.  The real cost is wall-clock throughput -- bcenet_transport.c notes each sendto is about 8 us and these buses carry roughly 940 commands a second each across eight buses -- and wall headroom is what this vehicle is measurably short of (#228).  HOW TO DECIDE IT WITHOUT A REWRITE: narrow mtumodel_owns_bus to yield per COMMAND rather than per bus, so anything the model does not answer falls through to the framer.  One run can then carry some timing-unit traffic in-process and some over the wire, and YAGPC_BARSPREAD_US plus the vote count say which is better behaved.  That narrowing is also the prerequisite for any out-of-process skeleton peripheral on buses 14-23 (a GPS on FIOGPSRD at IUA 10, for one), which today would be answered by the model or by nobody.  AND IT IS NOT A PREFERENCE BUT A REQUIREMENT, which the owner pointed out and which changes the status of this entry.  Any device that is not a GPC thread inside yaGPC2 can only reach a bus through bcenet, so every peripheral this simulator might grow -- the GPS, the IMU, the PSP, the nose wheel steering, and the forward and aft MDMs themselves -- is an external process by definition and must be served over UDP.  The in-process model is therefore TRANSITIONAL by construction, and the open question is when it moves rather than whether.  A CONSEQUENCE FOR THE WORK ALREADY DONE: YAGPC_FC_MDM and YAGPC_FC_SENSORS grow the in-process model to answer MORE devices -- the NSP power discretes, the MDM BITE, the channel reads -- and every one of those is work a real device model would do over the databus.  That path established the mechanism of #210 and #216 and was worth it for that, but it is accumulating behaviour that will have to be moved rather than kept, so its value is diagnostic and not a foundation.  THE SEAM IS THE PER-COMMAND NARROWING described above: it lets the two coexist, in-process for what is modelled today and fall-through to the framer for everything else, so devices migrate one at a time instead of in a single rewrite.  That makes it worth doing sooner than the measurement question alone would justify.  A PREMISE CHECKED AND FOUND FALSE, 2026-09-26: it was argued that routing the flight-critical replies through the framer would make listener delivery SYMMETRIC BY CONSTRUCTION and so cure the FIOERRLC self-fails (#240). It would not. bcenet_framer.c gives every computer its OWN recvQueue (queue_push per framer in drain_bus, fanned out under fan_lock), and each is drained only by that computer s own GPC_SVC_RECV_WORD -- exactly the per-reader-cursor shape mtumodel.c has. What the framer really differs in is that a whole datagram lands at one instant with no simulated-time pacing, which is "atomic release plus a fast wire" -- and run wire10 already approximated that and still lost one computer (#242). So #229 remains a REQUIREMENT for out-of-process peripherals, but it is not a fix for the loss of sync and must not be pursued as one.
  - procs `BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17,BCE20,BCE21,BCE22,BCE23` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_BARSPREAD_US` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/run.c,src/bcenet_framer.c,src/bcenet_transport.c,src/mmumodel.c` &middot; symptom `in-process model,no multicast,listener delivery,skeleton peripheral cannot answer` &middot; doc `bcenet_framer.c listener rule; mmumodel tap_push due times`
- **#248** *(emulator)* THE COMMANDER RECEIVES ITS FIRST REPLY WORD AT THE INSTANT IT ISSUES THE COMMAND, WHILE EVERY LISTENER IS PACED -- so the commander finishes each reply about one word (33 us) ahead of the listeners, issues the next command, and cuts off whichever listener polled a little late. This would resolve #217 s open contradiction (the commander reclaiming the bus in a median 561 us when a paced 21-word reply needs 693) and it is a steady source of listener-only Sync Errors whose victim is set by poll timing.
  - evidence: In every YAGPC_MTU_RING dump examined, the commander s first data word carries the SAME timestamp as its command, and later words follow at ~33 us: trial fix-a bus 15, GPC2 C 6082a5 @55.929845 and GPC2 m (sent=1) @55.929845, while GPC3 got its first word @55.929884 and GPC4 @55.929897; trial ex-a bus 20, GPC1 C 526420 / 525000 / 526c7f each followed by GPC1 m at the identical time. Readiness is judged only in RECV_POLL (sharedUs >= wireUs + k*33) and every IOP caller polls first (mia_data_available before mia_get_data), so the first word must be passing that test at t+0. Both wireUs (stamped from m->sharedUs at the command) and the test itself (m->sharedUs at the poll) read the shared scalar #244 measured being overwritten under 4 percent of calls. NOT YET PROVEN which it is; YAGPC_MTU_LOCKED=1, which sets the caller s own clock under a lock before serving, is being tried together with YAGPC_MTU_ECHO_EXPIRE=1 (trials exl-a/b/c), and the ring will show whether the commander s first word moves to +33 us. ALSO: with echo expiry alone (trial ex-a) the timing unit survived PARTIALLY on the flown configuration for the first time -- FIOBY53 live on GPC1, GPC2 and GPC4 -- though GPC3 still self-failed, that time from FIONSPPG+0036 on bus 20 (left=31), a listener that took the echo of 525000 but never its data word before the commander s next command 165 us later.
  - csects `FIONSPPG,FIOMFE38` &middot; env `YAGPC_MTU_LOCKED,YAGPC_MTU_ECHO_EXPIRE,YAGPC_MTU_RING` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `commander ahead of listeners,left=31,sync error` &middot; run `fix-a,ex-a`
- **#255** *(method)* THE SYNC MARGINS TABLE, FIRST CUT: every timing assumption the flight software's sync points make about what a computer sees of its neighbours, read from the source BEFORE a failure instead of after one. Both of 2026-09-27's causes (#253, #254) violated a margin that is written down here. To be completed for FCMISYNC, FCMASYNC, FPMIHPC2 and the BCE listen programs, and each row set against the emulator's delivery rule and a MEASURED jitter.
  - evidence: CONSTANTS (FCMCBLKS, microseconds): FCMNOISE 10 (discrete rise/noise), FCMMISKW 200 (maximum interrupt skew), FCMIT5 87 and FCMST5 87 (second look, ISYNC and SSYNC), FCMIT3 188, FCMIPRT3 248 (ISYNC with IPR), FCMCTT3 103 (CSYNC/TSYNC), FCMST3 182 (minimum time a code is held), FCMSYNTO 440, FCMSNTO2 3850 (sync time-out), FPM40MSC 40000. SSYNC (FCMSSYNC): first read in step -> FCMTRACE -> wait to first-read time + 87 -> second read -> hold own code to first-read time + 182 -> null. Not in step: opens the PC2/I-O interrupt window (SSM PC2IOENA/PC2IODIS, FCMSSYNC+0046) once per pass of a 19-instruction loop, restarts the time-out on any leading edge. REQUIREMENTS: (a) spread of first-success times between computers < 182-87 = 95 us less one loop pass; (b) a neighbour that takes an interrupt in the window must be SEEN to have dropped its code within 87 us of the last arrival's first read, so line delay x 2 + one pass < 87. TSYNC (FCMTSYNC) and CSYNC (FCMCSYNC): NO second look after a delay -- two reads FCMNOISE = 10 us apart, both must agree, then hold to FCMCTTIM + 103; they rely on the interrupt itself being simultaneous, with FCMMISKW 200 the stated bound. So a timer interrupt delivered to the computers more than about 100 us apart in simulated time breaks TSYNC by construction. CSYNC ALSO STORES, for comparison by the neighbours through the ICC buffer (TICCDIA1, TICCDIB1): discrete input A, discrete input B (PC 081c, NOT gated by vehicle_sync_read_gate) and BCE busy/wait status 4 (PC 1004) masked to the mass-memory buses -- two values that depend on the instant they are read and on the bus models' timing. EMULATOR RULES AS OF 2026-09-27: discrete input A read is gated and stamped (exact in simulated time); discrete input B and status 4 are not; bus words are delivered when the reader's thread looks (mtumodel paces by wire time and carries untaken commands, but is not gated).
  - csects `FCMSSYNC,FCMTSYNC,FCMCSYNC,FCMISYNC,FCMCBLKS,FPMIHPC2` &middot; files `SSSRC/FCMSSYNC.asm,SSSRC/FCMTSYNC.asm,SSSRC/FCMCSYNC.asm,SSSRC/FCMCBLKS.asm` &middot; symptom `margin,timing assumption,second look,interrupt window,FCMNOISE,FCMMISKW`

## Confirmed

- **#2** *(iop)* OVERTURNED BY THE DOCUMENT, and the reason correcting it 'breaks' the machine is now understood: it does not expose a SECOND defect, it exposes THE SAME ONE. exec_RAW must include the MSC's own bit (PROC_ALL). Our PROC_ALL_BCE ends FIOMDLY's delays early, which keeps the machine moving and makes the ~1053 ms holds RARE; with the correct delay they happen constantly.
  - evidence: POO IBM-6246556A, @RAW PROGRAMMING NOTES: 'Since processor 0 corresponds to the MSC, (which is busy during instruction execution), the MSC execution of an @RAW instruction will correspond to a loop until the maximum repeat count is reached.' nsts-sim-gpc 818df88 agrees, citing POO II-80. RUN r1, @RAW corrected, with YAGPC_IOQEDEPTH: the IOQE free pool falls 7 -> 2 -> 0 at t=232-233 and then RECOVERS (1 at 234, 5 at 240, 15 at 261), so the t~231 fault is the pool briefly touching zero, not a fatal second defect. And the DK holds carry THE SAME QUANTUM as the transition window -- 1055, 1053, 2101, 4264, 2164, 6361 ms at t=226.6 through 237.2 -- 9 holds all over 300 ms, against 5 in a whole 963 s baseline run. So the hold pathology is present in BOTH configurations and is independent of @RAW; our bug merely suppresses its frequency by cutting FIOMDLY's delays short. THERE ARE NOT TWO DEFECTS. Fixing the holds is the whole job, and the mask correction becomes safe once they are fixed. The earlier reading in this entry -- 'a second defect underneath' -- was wrong.
  - csects `FIOMDLY,FIOMCKIO,FIOMNTR` &middot; instrs `@RAW,@LI` &middot; procs `MSC` &middot; env `YAGPC_RAW_MSCBIT,YAGPC_IOP_UPSTREAM` &middot; files `src/iop_msc_instr.c` &middot; symptom `I/O engine stops,keys=0` &middot; tsec `230-400` &middot; doc `IBM-6246556A @RAW PROGRAMMING NOTES; nsts-sim-gpc 818df88` &middot; run `v53b,x1,x2,u1,u2,r1`
- **#20** *(method)* pass-910.mmv is NOT an original tape -- every .mmv is one we built, so it cannot serve as a control for 'the real machine did X'.

- **#21** *(method)* The authoritative hardware manuals are in the local ibiblio mirror and were unused for most of this work.

- **#22** *(iop)* ANSWERED. The ~1053 ms DK hold is ENTIRELY COMPLETION LATENCY, not bus work. The BCE finishes its bus program in about 0.3 ms in every case, long hold and short alike; the hold is the time between the BCE executing #WAT and FCOS learning about it.
  - evidence: ANSWERED IN PART. The hold is ENTIRELY COMPLETION LATENCY, not bus work: in w3 a 1052.64 ms hold and a 0.59 ms hold have identical BCE behaviour -- busy set by the SIO at +0.2 ms, #WAT clearing it at +0.29 ms -- so the bus program finishes in a third of a millisecond every time and the BCE is never the bottleneck. FOUR EXPLANATIONS OF THE LATENCY ARE NOW REFUTED, do not re-adopt: (1) BCE message timeouts, entry 11; (2) the @RAW accumulator mask, entry 2, retested and worse the second time; (3) the MSC parking on an 8.65 s repeat count, entry 39 -- measured median gap between MSC arms is 33.7 ms and only 3 of 7932 exceed 100 ms; (4) queue depth, refuted by ordering -- the first 414 ms hold is at t=390.87 and the pool sentinel fault is at t=396.99, six seconds later, so the holds PRECEDE the pool drain and cause it rather than the reverse. STILL OPEN: what makes the FIRST hold, at t~390.87, immediately after the OPS key at t~390.7 and BEFORE the first overlay entry at t=391.29. One unexplained correlation worth checking properly: in v91a that first hold ends at 391.135 and the MMU's READY->1 for its 26-block read is at 391.131, 4 ms earlier -- but the same correlation does NOT hold for the later holds, so it is suggestive only.
  - addrs `152-152,14d-14d,19b-19c,9068-9068,90b2-9273,80ce-8100` &middot; csects `FIOSVC,FIOPDISP,FIOCMPLT,FIOMNTR,FPMSVCEP,FIOCBLKS,FCMBUSCM,FPMIHPC2` &middot; instrs `#WAT,@RAW` &middot; procs `BCE6,BCE7,BCE8,MSC` &middot; phases `8` &middot; tape `v36` &middot; env `YAGPC_BWTRACE,YAGPC_WATCHHW,DEUMF` &middot; files `src/iop.c,src/iop_msc_instr.c` &middot; symptom `pool overflow,Clock 2 dead` &middot; tsec `391-399` &middot; run `v91a,w3,w4`
- **#23** *(emulator)* ANSWERED. The phase-8 overlay's unprotect walk starts at 0x1010e because 1010e lies 22 halfwords inside a PROTECTED load block based at 100f8 that FOUR phases share at different lengths: phase 4 LB9 100f8..140f5 (16382 hw), phase 6 LB11 100f8..140f3 (16380), phase 15 LB39 100f8..1017d (134), and phase 8 LB11 100f8..10129 (only 50 hw). All four are protected.
  - evidence: Decoded from #PFCMGPT (0x1ccf2) in w1's memory dump. The address had been unexplained since before this session because the load-block table was never read; it is not an arbitrary offset but an interior address of a shared, protected, phase-dependent-length load block.

On its FIRST use it established the DK/MM correlation from a question (overlap 6 18) not anticipated when the run started, which no pre-chosen trace could have answered. It also made the overclaim visible quickly: the same data showed the flooding MECHANISM was unsupported.

- **#29** *(method)* PHASE 8 LOADS PERFECTLY, BY THE FLIGHT SOFTWARE'S OWN TEST. All 30 load blocks are byte-identical in memory at the destinations #PFCMGPT gives, and all 30 PASS THEIR OWN CHECKSUM there. The transfer was never the problem; the OVERLAY is what never completes.
  - evidence: Run w1. (1) #PFCMGPT (0x1ccf2) decoded from the memory dump gives phase 8 thirty load blocks, mm=0x2a80 = ADDR 52400; phase 18's mm=0x2e00 = 6/5/0/0, confirming the goal criterion from the machine itself. (2) Walking the 238 tape blocks read, each load block padded to a tape-block boundary, consumes exactly 121,856 halfwords with ZERO remainder, and all 30 load blocks are BYTE-IDENTICAL in memory at their table destinations -- 30/30. (3) Each load block ends with a 2-halfword fullword checksum slot that its length INCLUDES (mmbstamp.py: 'length: int  # hw, INCLUDING the 2-hw checksum tail'), and in memory all 30 slots equal a 16-bit sum of their own body -- 30/30 -- which is FCMINSSL's own criterion, the sum-and-compare whose third failure is a deliberate wait state. So the load is correct by the machine's standard, not merely against the tape image. (4) The 30 blocks merge into 20 CONTIGUOUS REGIONS, disjoint, spanning 002ac..38975 with 119,070 hw of gap; splits inside a region are administrative -- the 16384-hw checksum cap (LB_CAP_HW) or a change in the store-protect flag -- not gaps in memory. THE TAPE CARRIES NO METADATA: the ~19 blocks of overhead are padding to block boundaries. EVERY EARLIER CLAIM OF MISSING OR MODIFIED DATA IS RETRACTED -- '24 unaccounted', '42 mismatches', '19 modified after loading', 'the missing block is a header' -- all were artifacts of inferring layout from byte searches and extrapolating a uniform 512-hw pitch across load-block boundaries. THE LESSON: read the machine's own table. THE OVERLAY still never completes: FTRMGPOV (1935c) is reached exactly ONCE in 963 s, for phase 3, while FCMMGPOV (192ce) is entered twice.

During a hold bus 6 emits ZERO wire events -- not even the per-revolution RECV_POLL that bus 18 emits while a receive is pending -- so BCE6 is not in a receive wait and is not executing. Running as GPC 1 with MF=PL, every OPS 901 PRO is refused 'NO TARGETS IN RUN', which reads like a missing overlay and is nothing of the kind. CONFIRMED BY LOG: v90a and v91a print 'bus 6 major function 1 (GNC)'; v92a-v99a and both key sweeps print 'major function 0 (PL)'. YAGPC_DEU_EXTRA_PRELOADED is the second, independently confirmed: v90a/v91a show ipled=true on ALL FOUR DEUs including the bus-9 unit with commands=0 (never loaded by the GPC, so it can only have been preloaded), and without it buses 7/8/9 come up unloaded and get BITEd (v97a: 476 BITE on bus 8) -- with it restored, v99a reproduced v91a's DK signature exactly (bus6==bus7, 30929/30929/30929 at t=240 vs v91a's 30607, bus10=16/bus11=32 at t=180, bus24=99750) yet still did not transition, which is what isolated DEUMF as a SEPARATE second requirement. FIVE OTHER EXPLANATIONS WERE TRIED AND ALL REFUTED -- do not re-adopt any: (1) wall-clock key-gate drift; (2) DEU count alone (v97a had four and failed); (3) the ITEM key's poll number (214/215/216/217 byte-identical); (4) the OPS key's poll number (556/557/558/560, simt 388.36-390.26, bracketing v91a's 389.64, all failed); (5) a source regression -- a bisect showed c23856793 AND c23856793-minus-BWTRACE both reproduce the failing behaviour exactly, so no source change this session caused it. The runs are deterministic: v91a and v98a match to the microsecond through all eight IPL reads at t=136.49 s. VALIDATE A RUN BY: 'major function 1 (GNC)', four DEUs all ipled=true, and a 'mmu1: read' later than the key.

So the second overlay never reaches CALL FPMRLPCT, which sits before FIOSTEVT and FTRMGPOV. YAGPC_PGMTRACE names the killer in the same run: 'PGMCHK code=0007 at=1be4b lastProt=080d6 t=398.039549' -- store-protect violation, 1be4b = FIOSVC+13 (csect 1be3e), lastProt=080d6 inside FPMSVCEP (080ce). That is the deliberate TIOQNXT sentinel of entry 22: the IOQE pool drains, FIOSVC walks off the end onto 080ce, and the store-protect check kills Clock 2 mid-overlay. CONSEQUENCE: entry 22 (the ~1053 ms DK bus holds) is ON THE CRITICAL PATH for the goal, not a side issue. NOTE a landmark on 080ce does NOT detect this -- 080ce is a VALUE FIOSVC walks onto, not code the CPU executes; the arming address to watch is the program-check handler FPMIHPGM (1ad5c), which does fire.

- **#41** *(iop)* THE LONG DK HOLDS HAPPEN EXACTLY WHILE MASS MEMORY IS TRANSFERRING. DK completion latency is gated by MM activity: while the MMU is busy, a DK transaction that has already finished its bus program waits, and it is released when the MM transfer ends.
  - evidence: Two independent runs, identical results. v91a and w3, bus 6, 804 busy intervals each: LONG holds (>300 ms) overlapping an MMU-busy window 5 of 5; SHORT holds 2 of 799. The MMU-busy windows are taken from the model's own READY->0 / READY->1 pairs. Hold durations are identical across the two runs (414.1, 1052.6, 1054.7, 2101.1, 4263.8 ms), so this is deterministic. Combined with entry 22 -- the BCE executes #WAT at +0.29 ms in every hold, long or short -- the picture is: the bus program finishes at once, and the COMPLETION is withheld for as long as mass memory is moving data. THIS IS THE CAUSE OF THE FIRST HOLD, which was the last thing left open: the first hold starts at t=390.72/390.87, inside the MMU-busy window for the 26-block phase-3 read (390.480-391.131 in v91a), and ends 4 ms after that read's READY->1. NOTE ON AN EARLIER RETRACTION: 'the MMU floods the servicer' was proposed and retracted in the same turn on the argument that service calls cost wall time rather than simulated time. That argument does not address this correlation and the retraction was premature. WHAT IS NOT YET ESTABLISHED: the mechanism inside our code. Candidates are the shared DMA request path (iop.c, the queue feeding iop_watch_store's 'dma' writes), and the MSC monitoring only the TOP ACTIVE IOQE (FIOMNTR0 takes its mask from TCVTMIOA) so that an MM transaction at the head of the list withholds attention from the DK buses for its whole duration -- which is NOT the general queue-depth idea refuted separately, since it depends on the MM entry being at the head rather than on how many entries exist.

- **#42** *(method)* YAGPC_POISON (range fill at a simulated time, ap101.c) answers 'did the loader WRITE here' where content comparison structurally cannot -- but it CANNOT be pointed at an overlay's destination, because an overlay's destination is LIVE MEMORY and poisoning it kills the machine before the transfer happens.
  - evidence: Run z1 wrote 0xdead into 14,848 halfwords across 23 ranges at t=386 -- the computed destinations of phase 8's 24 'not found anywhere' blocks plus its 5 fill blocks. The poison took (14,844 still 0xdead at the t=387 snapshot, 4 overwritten by normal activity). But the run produced NO transition at all, zero mass-memory reads after the key, so the headline result -- 100% of the poison still intact at t=403 -- means nothing: there was no transfer to overwrite it. Poisoning 14.8K halfwords of running code and data is what broke it; that is what an overlay destination IS. THE NON-INVASIVE FORM WORKS INSTEAD: YAGPC_WATCHHW already logs IOP DMA writes tagged with pe=, so watching a destination range during the transfer distinguishes the same three cases -- written with tape bytes, written with transformed bytes, never written -- without modifying anything (run z2). KEEP THE HOOK: poison is still the right instrument for a destination that is NOT live before the write, and it is the only way to test a block whose correct content is zeros or fill, where comparing content answers the same either way.
  - phases `8` &middot; env `YAGPC_POISON,YAGPC_WATCHHW` &middot; files `src/ap101.c` &middot; symptom `no transition` &middot; run `z1,z2`
- **#45** *(iop)* THE DK COMPLETION LATENCY IS QUEUE POSITION, NOT QUEUE DEPTH. FIOMNTR0 takes the MSC's monitor mask from the TOP ACTIVE IOQE (TCVTMIOA), so the MSC watches ONE request at a time; during a 1052 ms hold it checks for bus 6's completion exactly ONCE.
  - evidence: Run z4, YAGPC_REPEATTRACE extended to print the accumulator (which IS the monitor mask). Inside the 392.251..393.303 hold, 108 MSC arms: pc=1cb6c acc=ffffffff x72 (FIOMDLY's delay, all buses + MSC, not a monitor), and pc=1cb70 -- the I/O-complete check -- acc=00000080 x27 watching bus 24, acc=00000800 x8 watching bus 20, and acc=02000000 x1 watching BUS 6. Same shape in the other holds (bus 6 once, bus 7 once, bus 8 once). So bus 6's completion is looked at about once per 1052 ms while the MSC spends its passes on buses 24 and 20. THIS IS DISTINCT FROM THE REFUTED QUEUE-DEPTH IDEA: it does not depend on how many IOQEs exist, only on which one is at the HEAD, so it is untouched by the ordering argument (holds precede the pool drain) and by y1 (hold quantum invariant to MM pacing). OPEN: whether monitoring one IOQE at a time is FCOS's design working as intended -- in which case our IOQE ordering or the rate at which the head advances is what differs -- or an artifact of how TCVTMIOA is maintained here.
  - addrs `1cb6c-1cb70,152-152` &middot; csects `FIOMNTR,FIOMCNTL` &middot; procs `MSC,BCE6,BCE20,BCE24` &middot; env `YAGPC_REPEATTRACE` &middot; tsec `390-400` &middot; run `z4`
- **#46** *(flight-sw)* PHASE 8'S OVERLAY STOPS AT AN EXACT GATE: FIOMGMTR never sets TIOQLTMM, so every monitor pass takes the 'ANOTHER I/O TRANS REQUIRED' branch and calls FIOMGDSP instead of FIOMGCV. Verification, and therefore completion, is on the other side of that flag.
  - evidence: Run z5, landmarks on FIOMGCV (1a134), FIOMGMTR (1a4c2), FIOMGCMP (1a0ea). PHASE 3: ovp-entry 394.141, monitor passes at 394.398/394.598/394.798/394.998/395.198, cksum-verify on the FIFTH pass at 395.198, mm-complete 395.337, overlay-complete 395.338. PHASE 8: ovp-entry 395.339, monitor passes every 200 ms from 396.078 through 398.678 and beyond, and cksum-verify NEVER fires, mm-complete never fires, overlay-complete never fires. FIOMGMTR.asm's structure: IF (TB,TIOQFLG1,TIOQLTMM,Z) 'ANOTHER I/O TRANS REQUIRED?' -> ... IF (TB,TIOQFLG1,X'0200',O) 'TERMINATE TRANSACTION?' -> SB TIOQFLG1,TIOQLTMM, ELSE CALL FIOMGDSP 'DISPATCH/START NEXT I/O TRANS'; the ELSE of the outer test is where CALL FIOMGCV lives. WHY TIOQLTMM IS NEVER SET: phase 8 needs TWO reads (123 blocks then 115, because its allocation crosses a track boundary). Read 1 is dispatched at 396.079 and finishes about 399.2. FIOMGDSP then needs a free IOQE to dispatch read 2 and the 25-entry pool is drained by the DK holds, so read 2 does not go until 401.712 -- after the store-protect program checks at 400.881 and 401.238 have killed Clock 2. Phase 3 needs only ONE read (26 blocks) and so reaches the terminate test on its first completion. THIS EXPLAINS WHY PHASE 3 SURVIVES AND PHASE 8 DOES NOT, which nothing before did.

- **#48** *(flight-sw)* THE IOQE CHAIN READ DIRECTLY OUT OF MEMORY. The head-hogging entry is a BUS 24 request, not mass memory; the DEU fill backlog is five IOQEs held by ONE PCT; and the MM transaction's LTMM flag IS eventually set -- so the TIOQLTMM gate is a SYMPTOM, not the defect. The fix must stop the pool draining, not change the gate.

Entries seen at the head during the holds: 9068 mask=00000080 = BUS 24 (head ~48% of every hold), 905a = bus 20, 90c4/90fa/9130/9178 = bus 7, 910c = bus 8, 9166 = bus 6, 90b2 = bus 18. THREE FINDINGS: (1) the entry that dominates the head is bus 24, not the mass-memory transaction -- so the DK holds are not the MM transfer holding the head. (2) PCT 83a8 owns FIVE simultaneous IOQEs -- buses 6, 7, 7, 7 and 8 -- which is AIG_DEU_LOADER's fill backlog seen directly in the queue rather than inferred from free-pool depth. (3) 910c's TIOQNXT is 80ce, the FPMSVCEP sentinel, so it is the chain tail and FIOSVC walking one past it lands exactly on the store-protect fault we see. (4) MOST IMPORTANT: 90b2, the MM IOQE, has flg1=0c00 at t=403 -- MM-TAPE-POSITION and TIOQLTMM BOTH SET. The 'last MM transaction' gate that FIOMGCV waits behind WAS eventually satisfied; FIOMGMTR would have taken the verification branch on its next pass. The machine had already died. So entry 'phase 8 stops at the TIOQLTMM gate' describes a symptom: the gate closes in time only if the pool survives long enough for FIOMGDSP to dispatch read 2 promptly.

- **#50** *(emulator)* WE OVERRIDE THE PER-BUS MESSAGE TIMEOUTS THE FLIGHT SOFTWARE PROGRAMS. FCMINIOP loads every BCE's MTO explicitly, and src/iop.c's RECV_TIMEOUT_FLOOR_US = 2000.0 forces a 2 ms floor over any bus whose programmed value is shorter -- which is every bus except 6-9, 12-13 and 18-19. Upstream has already corrected this: 'The receive time-out floor is zero: the loaded time out governs.'

Our floor turns bus 24's 49.5 us into 2 ms (40x) and the 33 us buses into 2 ms (60x). CONFIRMED UPSTREAM: nsts-sim-gpc commit 818df88 'gpc: addressing, timer and bus-rate corrections in the CPU and the IOP' lists 'The receive time-out floor is zero: the loaded time out governs' alongside 'A MIA presents one received word per 33 us bus word time' and '@RAW keeps accumulator bit 0 (POO II-80)'. THAT LAST ITEM MATTERS FOR ENTRY 2: upstream KEEPS the MSC bit, citing the Principles of Operation II-80, which is the change entry 2 records as refuted twice here. Tested in ISOLATION it is catastrophic (run x2). Upstream lands it as part of a COORDINATED SET with receive pacing, a zero timeout floor, and #LBR@/#BU@ taking an 18-bit value from one fullword per BCE at ADDRESS + 2 x BCE#. So entry 2's refutation may be an artifact of changing one item of a set. DO NOT retry the mask alone; port the set.

- **#54** *(iop)* THE HOLDS ARE THE INTERVAL BETWEEN SUCCESSIVE FIOMCKIO CHECKS OF THAT BUS. The MSC checks a bus once, with count=0 ('JUST CHECK IT'), at the same instant FIOPDISP marks it busy -- BEFORE the BCE has run -- so the check fails, and the MSC does not look at that bus again for the whole hold.
  - evidence: Run r2, @RAW corrected so FIOMDLY's delays are honoured, YAGPC_REPEATTRACE printing the accumulator (the monitor mask). Checks of bus 6 are the pc=1cb70 arms with acc=02000000, and they fall at t=227.5180, 228.5706, 230.6718 and 234.9357 -- intervals of 1.053 s, 2.101 s and 4.264 s, EXACTLY the measured hold durations. Every one arms count=0. The first lands at 227.5180, the same instant TCVTBCEB's bus-6 bit is set by FIOPDISP, while the BCE does not execute #WAT until +0.29 ms (entry 22), so the check tests a BCE that has not started and fails. THE LONG SLEEPS COMPOUND IT: at t=232.9512 FIOMDLY arms count=60136 = 1984.5 ms, and 232.951 + 1.985 = 234.936, exactly where the next hold begins -- while the MSC sleeps on one entry's time-to-go nothing else is watched. FIOMNTR2's design is to DELAY until the monitor window and THEN check ('@BNN FIOMDLY *BEFORE MONITOR WINDOW. GO DELAY.' / '@B FIOMCKIO'), so a check arriving immediately with count=0 means TCVTMTTG is reporting the request already due. THAT IS THE NEXT QUESTION: why TCVTMTTG says a just-dispatched request is already past its monitor window. Note this is visible ONLY with @RAW corrected -- our PROC_ALL_BCE bug cuts FIOMDLY's delays short, which hides the whole structure.

- **#56** *(tape)* The 0xc6c6 crash at 0x648da is an UNFILLED ZCON TABLE, not a CPU addressing defect. The SCAL at 0x44839 (d0ff 39de: AM=1 indexed, B2=3 so no base, X=1, IA=1, I=1, disp=0x1de) fetches a fullword indirect address pointer from 0x001de, which is the CSECT #ZDCDDG9. In our image that fullword is c6c6c6c6 -- IPL fill, never written. Decoded as a pointer, c6c6 gives XC=0, C=1, CB=1, CD=0, BSR=12, DSR=6, so C=1/CB=1 replaces the PSW BSR with 12 and XC=0 post-indexes by R1's high half (0x0214): 0xc6c6+0x0214 = 0xc8da, high bit set, sector 12 -> 0x648da, which is fill, hence 'invalid instruction 0xc6c6'. Every digit of the crash is accounted for. THE EMULATOR IS CORRECT HERE; the data it read is not.
  - evidence: All 8 DASS dumps (G2,G3,G8,G9,G16,P9,S2,SSW) agree and have real ZCONs exactly where ours has c6c6 fill. Holes in ours: 0x1d6-0x1e5 (8 fullwords) and 0x1f6-0x1f9 (2). DASS values: 1d6=de62 0e30 (BSR3), 1d8/1da/1dc=ddcc 0e30, 1de=83aa 0e60 (BSR6 -> DCD_DG9 at 0x303aa), 1e0/1e2=c074 0e40 (BSR4), 1e4=8022 0e40, 1f6=888c 0e90 (BSR9), 1f8=80f8 0e20 (BSR2). Neighbours 0x1c0-0x1d5 and 0x1e6-0x1f5 are IDENTICAL to DASS in our image, so the table is loaded but incomplete. Every REAL ZCON here carries XC=1 (no post-indexing) -- only the c6c6 fill has XC=0, which is why the wild branch post-indexes at all, and why unfilled ZCONs at DIFFERENT table slots all land on the SAME 0x648da: the target depends only on the index register, not on which slot was read. That answers the user's objection that OPS 201 PRO fails identically without going through DCD_DG9 -- it need only hit SOME ZCON in the hole. SEPARATE DISCREPANCY, not part of this: 0x1e8 is 117c in ours but 88be in all 8 DASS dumps (both 0e20).
  - addrs `1de,1d6-1e5,1f6-1f9,1e8,44839,648da,303aa` &middot; csects `#ZDCDDG9,#ZDCDDG1,DCD_DG9,#CDCDDOW` &middot; instrs `SCAL` &middot; procs `CPU` &middot; phases `8,18` &middot; config `G9,P9,S2` &middot; tape `v36` &middot; files `src/cpu.c,src/cpu_instr.c` &middot; symptom `invalid instruction 0xc6c6 at 0x48da,POLL FAIL,NIA=648da,BSR=12` &middot; doc `Shuttle GPC Software Model AP-101S.pdf p195 sec 9.7 STACK CALL` &middot; run `c2,c3`
- **#57** *(tape)* ROOT CAUSE of the 0xc6c6 crash: the ROOT LINK leaves every cross-phase Z-CON unresolved. A HAL/S call into a procedure that lives in an OVERLAY phase goes through a fullword indirect address pointer (a Z-CON) held in the RESIDENT root at a fixed low address. The compiler emits the cell as 8000 0E00 -- offset 0 with the sector bit set, XC=1 C=1 CB=1 BSR=0 -- and only the LINKER can finish it, because only the linker knows which sector the overlay's code landed in. Our root link does not: the #C... targets are not in the root's own link, so the cells stay unrelocated and reach the volume as IPL fill C6C6C6C6. 18 Z-CONs are affected, all in 0x1d6-0x243.
  - evidence: /tmp/claude-1000/c80boot/PHASE02.lib (the root image staged for the tape) shows the UNRELOCATED form directly: 8000 0e00 at 0x1d6,1d8,1da,1dc,1de,1e0,1e2,1e4,1ec,1ee,1f0,1f2,1f6,1f8 -- exactly the cells that are fill on the tape. phase/PHASE02.fcm shows the same signature at 0x1f0,1f2,1f6,1f8,204,20e, and link/P15.fcm at 0x1d6. By contrast the WHOLE-MEMORY links (link/G9.fcm, G9-r.fcm, G9-oracle.fcm and every other link/*.fcm) get it RIGHT -- not one of them has fill at 0x1de -- because they link all phases together. NOT a loader defect: the hole is in the .mmv itself (OI340700-v36boot.mmv, slot 768, load block dest=0x1a8 len=160), and 83aa0e60 / de620e30 appear NOWHERE on the tape. NOT an overlay-writes-it-later defect: #PFCMGPT says only phase 10's LB0 (00000..03c1f) covers 0x1de, and OPS 901 loads phases 3, 8 and 18 -- phase 18's 8 load blocks touch nothing below 0x1000, nor do phase 4's (002ac/005a2/00602). So no phase loaded in the run rewrites the cell; it has to be right in the root image.
  - addrs `1d6-1e5,1f6-1f9,234-243,1de` &middot; csects `#ZDCDDG1,#ZDCDDG9,#ZDCDDS4,#CDCDDOW,PHASE02` &middot; phases `2,10,18` &middot; config `G9,P9,S2` &middot; tape `v36,v37` &middot; files `tools/patch_root_zcons.py` &middot; symptom `invalid instruction 0xc6c6 at 0x48da,8000 0e00 unrelocated zcon` &middot; run `c2,c3`

The 18 Z-CONs are only the currently-FATAL subset; the largest holes are #PCDHMMU (179 refs), #PCDCSPC (29), #PCVWMMU (12), FIOTBLBF (12), FIOSWDCT (12), #PCDIMMU (9). CONTRAST: the whole-memory links in link/ have only 22 unresolved (FIOG9OSW x8, FIOMUWB2 x8, six FSVC*) and get all 18 Z-CONs right, because every phase is in the same link. Check which tree a claim came from before trusting it.

OPS 201 on v39: ZERO DMAPROT lines. - **#71** *(method)* The zero-operand fingerprint (ours==0000, DASS non-zero, matching neighbours) UNDERCOUNTS unresolved relocations: an unresolved external's operand holds the symbol's OFFSET within its section, which is often non-zero (0030, 0050, 0014), and adjacent unresolved cells defeat the neighbour test. - **#82** *(method)* HAL/S-FC's decimal-to-IBM-float conversion is NOT correctly rounded: the exact decimal expansion of a representable value sometimes compiles one unit low, and the 8-digit literal the dump prints sometimes compiles one unit high. Reconstructing a SCALAR INITIAL from memory therefore needs a literal chosen by COMPILING candidates, not by formula.
