OPS 2 in MF 2 targets exactly GPCs 1 and 2, which is the smallest redundant set the tape can be asked for and the right first experiment for --gpcs 1,2.
  - evidence: Read out of the source, not guessed. CZ2COMMO.hal:434 CZ2B_GRT_GPC_SET ARRAY(10) BIT(16) INITIAL(F000,C000,F000,1400,1400,4400,8400,C000,F000,0000) and CZ2COMMO.hal:527 CZ2V_GRT_TAB (MC,POVL,MFOVL,MF_VALUE,OPS_NBR,MAX_MODE) INITIAL(1,4,3,2,1,6 / 2,5,3,2,2,2 / 3,6,3,2,3,5 / 4,15,14,3,2,2 / 0,16,14,3,4,2 / 6,12,9,1,9,1 / 0,13,9,3,9,1 / 8,7,3,2,8,1 / 9,8,3,2,9,1 / 0,0,0,0,0,0). Only bits 1-5 of the set word are the GPCs: DM6OPS.hal:508 'TARGET_GPC$(12 TO 16) = TSET$(1 TO 5)', so the bit 6 that appears in 1400/4400/8400 is NOT a sixth computer and is dropped. Decoding bits 1-5: index 1 F000={1,2,3,4} MF2 OPS1 maxmode6; 2 C000={1,2} MF2 OPS2 maxmode2; 3 F000={1,2,3,4} MF2 OPS3 maxmode5; 4 1400={4} MF3 OPS2; 5 1400={4} MF3 OPS4; 6 4400={2} MF1 OPS9; 7 8400={1} MF3 OPS9; 8 C000={1,2} MF2 OPS8; 9 F000={1,2,3,4} MF2 OPS9; 10 empty. Rows 5, 7 and 10 can never be selected at all -- DM6OPS.hal:604 requires CZ2V_GRT_MC(GRTS)=GRTS and those three carry MC=0. The single-GPC decode agrees independently with headless-gpcmem.sh's own note that entries 4 and 5 name GPC 4, 6 names GPC 2 and 7 names GPC 1.
  - csects `DM6OPS,CZ2COMMO,ARCGPC` &middot; files `SSSRC/CZ2COMMO.hal,SSSRC/DM6OPS.hal` &middot; symptom `redundant set,target set,GRT,which OPS`
- **#94** *(emulator)* Two computers loading from the two DIFFERENT mass memory units at the same time works exactly as the vehicle should: it is the concurrent case the plan named as the sharper test, and it passes.
  - evidence: Run mm1, --gpcs 1,2 --mmu-model 1:tape --mmu-model 2:tape, GPC1 sourcing MM1 and GPC2 sourcing MM2, both IPLing within the same second. Both read the full bootstrap -- 'GPC2 ... from MM2 (BCE 19) (72 blocks, 36864 halfwords)' and 'GPC1 ... from MM1 (BCE 18) (72 blocks, 36864 halfwords)' -- and mmu2 reports blocksRead=72 wordsOut=36864 wordsTaken=36864 wordsLost=0. No bus-19 contention is reported at all. The contention that IS reported, 72 clashes on bus 18, belongs entirely to the spurious second IPL in the companion entry, where both machines wrongly went to MM1.
  - procs `BCE18,BCE19` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `concurrent mass memory,MM1 and MM2,two GPCs`
- **#99** *(emulator)* A second computer gets no crew input because DK bus assignment is CREW CONFIGURATION, not something the emulator or the panel switches decide. The first PASS GPC moded to RUN automatically takes IDPs 1 to 3 when nothing else is commanding them, so GPC1 takes the displays and GPC2 is left uncommanded -- which is correct behaviour, not a defect. Reassignment is done from the NBAT on the GPC MEMORY display, or with the GPC/CRT key.
  - evidence: USA005350 Rev B sec 3.2.15.1, p.3-59: 'any PASS GPC moded to RUN automatically takes control of IDPs 1 to 3 if no DK buses are being commanded by the CS, as in the case of the first GPC to be IPLd' -- exactly what run s4e showed, GPC1 commanding and GPC2 silent. Same section p.3-57: the GPC/CRT key reassigns DK buses between GPCs, and p.3-59: 'PASS GPCs can only control three IDPs at a time', a fourth gives '>3 DEU'. STRIKING CONFIRMATION OF OUR OWN MEASUREMENT, p.3-58: 'no check is made to ensure against multiple commanders on the same DK bus ... Dual commanders should be avoided, as it can result in PASS GPCs failing-to-sync.' The real vehicle has the same failure mode we measured as 477 abandoned transfers. THE NBAT, TD115 Rev B sec 5, pp.5-3/5-4: the MEM/BUS CONFIG table on the left of GPC MEMORY is the NBAT; ITEM 1 plus an MC number selects which NBAT to edit; ITEMs 2-6 are the TARGET SET, one slot per GPC, the GPC number in its slot and 0 to remove; the lower items assign bus commanders, with CRT 1-4 at ITEMs 12-15 in the figure. An NBAT takes effect on an OPS transition or an OPS/mode recall (OPS XXX PRO), not when it is typed. NOTE THE TIE TO #92: the GRT is the onboard default target set for an OPS; the NBAT is the crew's editable copy of the same thing.
  - csects `FCMCSYNC,FCMASYNC,GPCIPL` &middot; procs `DK1,DK2` &middot; env `--deu-bus,YAGPC_DEUKEYS,YAGPC_SYNCTRACE` &middot; files `src/run.c` &middot; symptom `second GPC silent,no keystroke,DK bus assignment,NBAT,GPC/CRT key,dual commanders` &middot; doc `USA005350 Rev B sec 3.2.15.1 pp.3-57..3-59; TD115 Rev B sec 5 pp.5-3..5-4`
- **#100** *(flight-sw)* The GPC STATUS matrix on panel O1 is driven by a HARDWARE REGISTER per computer, not by a software report: five fail-vote discretes, one computer's ROW of the 5x5 matrix, saying which computers THAT one has voted out. yaGPC2 and nsts-sim-gpc both already implement the register and the instructions that write it; NEITHER publishes it anywhere, which is why nothing could drive a lamp panel.
  - evidence: Traced from the panel back to the register. INCL80/CAMEMACS.hal defines SETCAMEX(SN,MASK) as SVC CMRSVC=25 (INCL80/CMSMACS.hal) request 2 op 1 -- the same SVC as SET/RESET/READ DISCRETE (SETDMACS req 1 op 3, RSTDMACS req 1 op 4, RDISMACS req 1 op 2), so the CAM rides the discrete-output path. SSSRC/FCMSFCAM.asm is the MSC program that does it: @LF TCVTRESC / @RFD 0 / @LF TCVTSETC / @SFD 0, then zeroes both masks and branches to FIOMNTR. SSSRC/FCMSFAIL.asm fills those masks as it votes (lines 470-500) and also maintains a SOFTWARE CAM status in TICCSTAT, bits X'00F8' -- which is CZ2B_STATUS$(gpc;9 TO 13) in HAL/S, absolute GPC numbering, shared over the ICC and used by the SPEC display rather than by the lamps. In yaGPC2 the register is iop.msc.regFailDisc with exec_SFD/exec_RFD in src/iop_msc_instr.c; in nsts-sim-gpc it is gpc/iop_msc_instr.coffee '@SFD'/'@RFD' and iop.coffee's FAILDSC dump field. THE BITS ARE ROTATED: FCMSFAIL's FCMCVTM procedure (line 551) rotates an absolute 5-bit mask of failed GPCs by the computer's own id -- 'CHANGE OUTPUT MASK FOR EACH GPC' -- before it reaches the hardware, exactly as the sync lines are rotated. THE EXACT BIT ORDER IS NOT CONFIRMED: it has been read out of the assembly and not yet seen in a run, because no sync failure has been produced.
  - csects `FCMSFCAM,FCMSFAIL,FCMCVTM,FCMSVOTE` &middot; instrs `@SFD,@RFD,@LFD` &middot; procs `MSC` &middot; files `src/iop_msc_instr.c,src/discretes.c` &middot; symptom `GPC STATUS matrix,CAM,fail vote,panel O1 lamps,voting.py`
- **#101** *(goal)* THE OPS TRANSITION NOW HAPPENS; what is still missing is GPC2 joining. The first two attempts failed on the IDP's MAJOR FUNCTION, not on the tape: DM6OPS resolves an OPS request through the GRT keyed on the REQUESTING major function, and with the switch left at its default of PL no row matches an OPS 2 request at all. With YAGPC_DEUMF=1 (GNC) the transition runs.
  - evidence: Three runs, same script, only the variable named changing. rs1 (OI340600 tape, DEUMF default) and rs2 (v44, DEUMF default) are identical to each other: mmu1 ends at 56 commands / 431-432 blocks, the plain GPC-MEMORY figure, and not one mass-memory command follows the request -- so THE TAPE IS REFUTED, do not retest. rs3 (v44, DEUMF=1 GNC): mmu1 ends at 71 commands / 749 blocks / 383559 wordsOut, i.e. 318 BLOCKS READ AFTER THE OPS REQUEST. The mapping, from headless-gpcmem.sh's own note which had found this trap before: the GRT's MF_VALUE is 1=PL, 2=GNC, 3=SM, while YAGPC_DEUMF is the SWITCH position 0=PL 1=GNC 2=SM 3=ILLEGAL, so DEUMF = MF_VALUE - 1. Every multi-GPC GRT row is MF_VALUE 2, which is GNC, which is DEUMF=1. NOTE FOR ANYONE REASONING FROM THE DISPLAY: a working GPC MEMORY page proves NOTHING about the major function, because GPC MEMORY is the OPS 0 page and shows in any of them; the major function only gates the GRT match for the OPS request itself. WHAT REMAINS: GPC2 still read only its IPL (mmu2 47 commands / 160 blocks) and still emits 000 once and nothing more. It never reached GPC MEMORY, because it never got an ITEM 1 EXEC -- it has no DK bus, GPC1 having taken IDPs 1 to 3 on being moded to RUN first. NEXT, UNTESTED: assign a CRT to GPC 2 with the NBAT's CRT items (the GPC MEMORY figure puts CRT 1-4 at ITEMs 12-15) so GPC2 commands the display unit already attached to it on bus 7, then give that unit its own keystroke batch.
  - csects `DM6OPS,ARCGPC,FCMASYNC` &middot; procs `DK1,DK2,BCE18,BCE19` &middot; tape `v44` &middot; env `YAGPC_DEUMF,YAGPC_DEUKEYS` &middot; symptom `no OPS transition,redundant set,NBAT,OPS 201 PRO,second GPC silent` &middot; run `rs1,rs2,rs3`
- **#102** *(goal)* TWO COMPUTERS NOW BOTH LOAD PASS AND BOTH TALK ON THE SYNC LINES. The blocker was that DISPLAY-KEYBOARD COMMAND IS NOT STATIC: every GPC's bootstrap talks to DK1, so two computers IPLing at once both drive bus 6, and a fixed --deu-bus owner silently starved whichever one did not own it. Command now follows the BFC CRT SELECT discrete, the crew control that actually hands the buses over, and the computers are IPLed one at a time as the crew does it.
  - evidence: Measured with a DK trace tagged by computer. Both IPLing at once: 'DK gpc=1 bus=6' 531 commands AND 'DK gpc=2 bus=6' 340 commands in the same 100 s -- GPC2 WAS trying, and our ownership check answered it with no-peripheral, so it sat in GPCIPL's STMWAIT at NIA=0x1df8 forever. With no CRT selected at all it issues ZERO DK commands and parks at 0x1cfc instead, so 'no DEU selected' is not a default-load escape either. SEQUENCED, with GPC1 claiming the buses to 300 s and GPC2 from 310 s: 'vehicle: GPC1 commands the display buses' / 'GPC1 releases' / 'GPC2 commands', two keystroke batches delivered (poll=207 at wall 100 s, poll=408 at wall 400 s), and BOTH mass memories end at 56 commands / 432 blocks / 221243 wordsOut -- IDENTICAL loads. THE SYNC LINES THEN CARRY BOTH WAYS: GPC1 out 4774 nulls and 4773 SSIPs, GPC2 out 2893 and 2892 (it started later), and each RECEIVES exactly what the other sent -- GPC1 sees GPC2 at N+1 with 2893/2892, GPC2 sees GPC1 at N+4 with 4774/4773. GPC2's first 000->111 is the transition FCMCSYNC admits a common-set member on. WHAT IS STILL MISSING FOR OPS 2: display polling nearly stops once GPC2 holds the buses (410 polls in 900 s, almost all before 410 s), so the later NBAT keystrokes never found a poll to ride in on; and the NBAT was incomplete -- the PASS User's Guide OI32 Table 3.000-1 has ITEM 1+X for the MC, 2-6 for the target set and 7-19 for the BUS COMMANDERS (strings 7-10, PL 11, CRT 12-15, MM 18-19), and only the target set was ever being set.
  - procs `DK1` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c,src/vehicle.h,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `second GPC never loads,STMWAIT,DK1 contention,common set,both GPCs syncing` &middot; doc `PASS Users Guide OI32 sec 3.5.000 Table 3.000-1; USA005350 Rev B sec 3.2.15.1` &middot; run `rs6,rs7,rs8`
- **#105** *(flight-sw)* THE ADDRESSES OF THE SET MASKS, so this never has to be guessed at again. TCVTCSSM, the COMMON SET SYNC MASK, is the fullword at 0x000168; TCVTRSSM, the REDUNDANT SET SYNC MASK, is at 0x000166; both live in FCMPSA. CZ2B_CS and CZ2B_RS -- the application-visible copies in the GPC Status Table -- are at 0x002406 and 0x002407 for GPC 1, stepping 0x14 per computer, and CZ2VGST is pointed to by TCVTGST at 0x00015B.
  - evidence: Read out of the DASS dumps, which name every member and offset: PFS/mafgen/DASS_G2.ASC gives '000166-000167 FCMPSA +0166 TCVTRSSM DC F' and '000168-000169 FCMPSA +0168 TCVTCSSM DC F', and cross-checks them against their users -- 'FCMCSYNC+009D L R5,X'0168' TCVTCSSM' and 'FCMISYNC+000C L R5,X'0166' TCVTRSSM'. #PCZ2COM+0012 CZ2B_CS resolves to 002406 in ALL FIVE configurations checked (G2, G3, G8, G9, S2), so it is resident and the address is stable rather than configuration-specific. FIRST USE: watching 0x2406 across a 900 s two-computer run produced TEN store events in total -- GPC1 writing CZ2B_CS and CZ2B_RS once each, both ZERO, and GPC2 never writing either. The single zero is DM5NEW.hal:484, 'CLEAR COMMON SET MASK'; NO HAL/S code anywhere assigns CZ2B_CS a non-zero value, so the application copy is filled from FCOS and TCVTCSSM is the mask that actually decides membership.
  - addrs `00166-00169,0015b-0015b,02406-02407,023f4-023f4` &middot; csects `FCMPSA,FCMCSYNC,FCMISYNC,CZ2COMMO,DM5NEW` &middot; symptom `common set mask,redundant set mask,CZ2B_CS,TCVTCSSM,where is it` &middot; doc `PFS/mafgen/DASS_G2.ASC`
- **#108** *(flight-sw)* THE NEIGHBOUR BITS ARE VISIBLE TO THE FLIGHT SOFTWARE IN BOTH DIRECTIONS -- the overlay is not hiding them, so that is not why the set fails to form. Each computer's discrete input A carries the other's three-bit code at the correct rotated position AND the driven mask includes those bits, which is what decides whether the overlay passes them to the CPU. FCMASYNC therefore samples a DIA that really does show a live neighbour, and still builds an empty mask.
  - evidence: Printed the driven mask beside the decoded code, because the value alone only proves the bits reached the Discretes object -- the CPU reads the overlay, which passes a bit only if it is ALSO currently driven, and a neighbour present in the VALUE but absent from the DRIVEN mask would be invisible without the decoded code showing any difference. Run dia1: 'GPC2 <- N+4 (GPC1) 111 null A=87000111 driven=fc0c0111' and 'GPC1 <- N+1 (GPC2) 111 null A=20000888 driven=fc0c0888'. 0x111 is bits 23, 27 and 31 -- STBY/RUN/SYNC N+4 -- and 0x888 is bits 20, 24 and 28 -- STBY/RUN/SYNC N+1 -- each exactly right for its rotation, and each present in the driven mask as well as the value. SO THE REMAINING CANDIDATE IS TIMING, NOT VISIBILITY: FCMASYNC's stated purpose (FCMASYNC.asm:34-37) is 'TO DETECT OTHER ACTIVE GPCS IN THE COMMON SET AND TO DETERMINE THE TIME OF SSIP PROCESSING SO THAT THE COMMON SET MAY BE JOINED' -- it has to phase-lock onto the existing set's SSIP, not merely notice that somebody is alive. Our two machines run free SSIP cycles at the same RATE but no common phase, which is consistent with every count we have: GPC1 and GPC2 issue nulls and SSIPs in numbers proportional to how long each has been up, never in step.
  - csects `FCMASYNC,FCMSMASK` &middot; files `src/discretes.c,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `common set never forms,FCMASYNC empty mask,SSIP phase,driven mask` &middot; run `dia1`
- **#109** *(flight-sw)* THE SIP HANDSHAKE IS BIT-CORRECT AND WELL INSIDE ITS TIMING WINDOWS, so neither the code pattern nor the sampling window explains the failure to form a set. FCMASYNC's own constant is FCMSIPC = X'00000FF0' -- STBY and RUN set, SYNC clear, for all four neighbour positions -- which is exactly what our computers present to each other.
  - evidence: FCMCOLAG (FCMASYNC.asm:221-241) reads DIA, XORs it with FCMSIPC, waits 10 microseconds, reads and XORs again, ORs the two so any instability marks a neighbour non-SIP, folds the three four-bit groups together with two SLL 4s and inverts. FCMSIPC is DC X'00000FF0' at FCMASYNC.asm:258 and again at FCMCSYNC.asm:385. OUR BITS MATCH IT: a neighbour in SSIP shows A=...110 in its group (STBY and RUN set, SYNC clear) and in null A=...111, measured directly as 'GPC2 <- N+4 (GPC1) 110 SSIP A=87000110' against 0x111 for null. MEASURED TIMING, both comfortably inside what the algorithm needs: the SSIP PULSE is median 330 us wide (p10 283, p90 701), far longer than FCMCOLAG's 10 us stability check and longer than the 200 us simulated-time barrier, with only 0.5% narrower; and the SSIP INTERVAL is a very regular 160 ms (p10 158.6, p90 161.2, 6.3 per second) against FCMASYNC's 170 ms LOOP2 window, so only 1.8% of gaps could outlast the search. A hypothesis that the pulse was too narrow to sample, and a hypothesis that the gap was too long for the window, are both REFUTED by those numbers -- do not retest either.
  - csects `FCMASYNC,FCMCOLAG,FCMCSYNC` &middot; symptom `SIP sync code,FCMSIPC,pulse width,SSIP rate,common set never forms` &middot; run `dia1`
- **#115** *(emulator)* LEDGER #49 IS CONFIRMED AND IS WHY THE SET CANNOT HOLD. Buses with nothing attached complete instantly and get re-dispatched at a rate no vehicle could sustain, and the commanding computer's I/O completions are inflated by orders of magnitude as a result. That is the 70-to-1 FCMISYNC disparity: it is an EMULATOR ARTEFACT on the busy computer, not a property of the flight software or of the quiet one.
  - evidence: Per-computer, per-bus transaction census over a 900 s two- computer run (YAGPC_BUSCENSUS, run bc1). GPC1: bus 18 = 2,153,932; bus 24 = 2,036,905; bus 8 = 561,332; bus 7 = 561,010; bus 20 = 541,708; bus 22 = 480,765; bus 6 = 333,073; bus 1 = 12,396. GPC2: bus 19 = 1,022,695 and bus 6 = 11,316, and NOTHING ELSE AT ALL. Read that against what is actually attached: buses 7 and 8 are DK2 and DK3 with NO display unit on them in this configuration, and they still take 561,000 transactions each; bus 24 is the intercomputer bus and takes two million from GPC1 while GPC2 puts NOT ONE on it; bus 18 takes 2.15 million for a computer that read 749 blocks of tape. The pattern is exactly what #49 predicted from the other side -- 'its requests complete almost instantly because nothing is there to take time, so FCOS re-dispatches it at a rate the real vehicle could not sustain'. THE CONSEQUENCE IS NEW: every one of those completions is an I/O-complete interrupt, FCMISYNC synchronises the common set at each of them (FCMISYNC.asm:33-36), and a computer commanding six inflated buses can never meet a barrier with one commanding two. THE TEST #49 ASKED FOR IS NOW WORTH RUNNING AND HAS A PASS CRITERION: give the unattached buses realistic wire time, or stop dispatching an absent subsystem, and see whether the FCMISYNC counts converge and the set survives past 4.33 ms. csects FCMISYNC,FIOPDISP | procs BCE7,BCE8,BCE18,BCE20,BCE22,BCE24 | env YAGPC_BUSCENSUS | files src/vehicle.c,src/vehicle.h,src/iop.c | symptom unmodelled bus,inflated I/O,70 to 1,fail to sync,set voted apart | run bc1 THE PROPOSED TEST HAS NOW BEEN RUN, and the documented rate is not usable as it stands. YAGPC_BUS_WORD_US already exists for exactly this -- off by default, 33 us being the hardware figure from IBM-74-A31-016, 28 bits at 1 MHz plus a 5 us interword gap. Run bw1, two computers with it set to 33: BOTH machines stop at mmu 44 commands / 160 blocks, short of the 432 they reach with a free wire, so neither finishes loading and the question of whether the set survives cannot even be asked. That is the warning in bus_word_us()'s own header coming true -- FCMBOOT's block-gap timing is load-bearing and this changes every transmit, mass memory included. SO THE ORDER OF WORK IS FIXED BY THIS: the wire cannot be given its real cost until the emulator's own dispatch latency is dealt with, because the two are additive and the software's margins were sized for one and not the other. #22 -- a DK hold of 1052 ms that is entirely completion latency, with the BCE finishing its bus program in 0.3 ms every time -- is that latency, and it has to come first. An intermediate value between 0 and 33 may buy a working set without breaking the boot, but picking one is tuning against a defect rather than fixing it. csects FCMISYNC,FIOPDISP | procs BCE7,BCE8,BCE18,BCE20,BCE22,BCE24 | env YAGPC_BUSCENSUS | files src/vehicle.c,src/vehicle.h,src/iop.c | symptom unmodelled bus,inflated I/O,70 to 1,fail to sync,set voted apart | run bc1  SUPERSEDED IN PART BY #122. The bus-24 figure quoted above -- "bus 24 is the intercomputer bus and takes two million from GPC1 while GPC2 puts NOT ONE on it" -- was NOT dispatch inflation. The intercomputer wire was never connected to GPC1 (an initialisation-order defect; see #122), so those two million are router-level attempts into a disconnected bus and the ICC model recorded 0 transmits and 0 receives for the whole run. Do not cite bus 24 as evidence for this entry. The unattached-bus inflation on buses 7, 8, 14, 15, 20, 21 and 22 is untouched by that and still stands; so does the finding that YAGPC_BUS_WORD_US=33 breaks the boot. But the CONSEQUENCE claimed here -- "a computer commanding six inflated buses can never meet a barrier with one commanding two" -- is separately refuted by #117, which measured the barriers rather than the calls and found FOUR on each machine.
  - csects `FCMISYNC,FIOPDISP` &middot; procs `BCE7,BCE8,BCE18,BCE20,BCE22,BCE24` &middot; env `YAGPC_BUSCENSUS` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c,src/vehicle.h,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `unmodelled bus,inflated I/O,70 to 1,fail to sync,set voted apart` &middot; run `bc1`
- **#117** *(flight-sw)* ANSWERED, AND IT REFUTES #114's PREMISE. FCMISYNC's mask is selected by TIOQSYNT, a bit IN THE I/O REQUEST, not a property of the computer: TFIOQ.asm gives TIOQFLG2 at IOQE halfword offset 8 -- exactly the `TB 8(R1),X'0400'` at 0x18e9b -- with TIOQSYNT EQU X'0400' "SYNC TYPE (RS OR CS)", X'0000' = RS and X'0400' = CS. Measured over a 900 s two-computer run: the redundant-set mask is ZERO on BOTH machines and EVERY RS pass skips the spin, so the 34,428-against-454 entry disparity costs nothing at all. The number of passes that actually enter the barrier is FOUR ON EACH MACHINE -- perfectly symmetric. A count is not a barrier.
  - evidence: Run ms2, 900 s, --gpcs 1,2, YAGPC_RANGETRACE over FCMISYNC's two mask loads (0x18ea0 = TCVTRSSM, 0x18eab = TCVTCSSM) and its go/no-go branch (0x18eb0, where R3 != 0 means the spin is entered). GPC1: RS 34,428 passes, CS 4. GPC2: RS 454, CS 4. THE RS MASK IS 0x00000000 ON EVERY ONE OF THE 34,882 RS PASSES ON BOTH MACHINES, and the go/no-go shows entered=4 skipped=34,428 on GPC1 and entered=4 skipped=454 on GPC2. So all four real barriers on each machine are the CS ones, and they match each other exactly. THE CS MASKS ARE CORRECTLY ROTATED: GPC1 loads 0x00000888 (bits 20/24/28, the N+1 group, which is GPC2) and GPC2 loads 0x00000111 (bits 23/27/31, the N+4 group, which is GPC1). WHICH I/O ASKS FOR A CS SYNC: of the 25 pre-initialised IOQEs in FIOCBLKS.asm only IOQE7 carries TIOQSYNT -- TIOQFLG2 = X'0528' = TIOQSYNT + TIOQICCR + TIOQPRTR + TIOQOTYT, device id 3, priority 255 -- i.e. the INTERCOMPUTER exchange, which AIESIP drives (DIMICC.hal: applications hand messages to DIM_ICC_COLLECTOR and "AIESIP unloads it"). WHAT THIS DOES NOT SHOW: the four CS passes did NOT take FCMISYNC's fail path. 0x18f44 (BAL FCMSFINT) and 0x18f4d (BAL FCMSFAIL) NEVER EXECUTED on either machine. They left through 0x18efb, `BC 4,X'0053' BZ FCMISLPE`, which is the exit taken when the masked neighbour bits read ZERO. METHOD WARNING: I first traced 0x18f44-0x18faa and called it "the failure path"; 0x18f50 (FCMISLPE) is ALSO the normal exit's target, so lines in that range are not evidence of a failure. Only 0x18f44 and 0x18f4d are. So the fail-vote that the SYNC trace shows at 454 s does NOT come from FCMISYNC, and the next measurement is FCMSFINT (0x19674) and FCMSFAIL (0x1948c) entry with R7, whose nibbles 1-3 name the calling sync program.
  - addrs `18e94-18eb4,18efb-18efc,18f44-18f4e,00166-00169` &middot; csects `FCMISYNC,FCMSFINT,FCMSFAIL,FIOCMPLT,FIOCBLKS,AIESIP` &middot; env `YAGPC_RANGETRACE` &middot; files `src/run.c` &middot; symptom `I/O complete sync,mask selection,redundant set mask,which branch fails,TIOQSYNT,barrier count not entry count` &middot; run `ms2`
- **#119** *(goal)* GPC 2 ALONE CAN LOAD AND RUN OPS 2, on the very buses the two-computer test assigns it -- mass memory 2 and CRT 2, not GPC1's. So nothing about the second computer's identity blocks the transition, and the two-computer failure is entirely in set formation and maintenance.
  - evidence: Run one2b: --gpcs 2 alone, panel sourcing MM2, NBAT selecting MC 2 with target set {2} and assigning CRT 2 and MM2 to GPC 2, display units on BOTH DK1 (for GPCIPL's menu, which talks to DK1 and nothing else) and DK2. Verified rather than assumed: mmu2 ends at 59 commands / 432 blocks -- the IPL and GPC MEMORY load, from MM2 as selected -- and mmu1 at 22 commands / 608 blocks, the OPS 2 load; DKTRACE shows bus 6 commanded 3477 times, BUS 7 2261 TIMES and bus 8 4119. Bus 7 being commanded is the proof that the CRT 2 assignment took effect. TWO THINGS THE VERIFICATION CAUGHT, both mine. FIRST, THE OPS LOAD CAME FROM MM1 DESPITE ITEM 19 ASSIGNING MM2 -- only the IPL and GPC MEMORY load used MM2 -- so the NBAT's mass-memory assignment is NOT taking effect the way the two-computer work assumed, and that assumption was never tested. SECOND, AN EARLIER 'CONTROL' WAS NOT ONE: run one2 gave GPC 2 MM1 and CRT 1 via ITEM 12 and ITEM 18, i.e. GPC1's whole configuration with GPC2's identity, and its success proved less than was claimed for it.
  - csects `DM6OPS` &middot; procs `BCE6,BCE7,BCE8,BCE18,BCE19` &middot; tape `v44` &middot; symptom `GPC2 alone,OPS 2,control experiment,NBAT assignment` &middot; run `one2,one2b`
- **#120** *(method)* THE TWO-COMPUTER TEST SCRIPT ASSIGNED GPC 2 A DISPLAY THAT DOES NOT EXIST. rs9.sh types ITEM 13 +2, giving CRT 2 to GPC 2, while creating only --deu-model, a single unit on DK1. There is no unit on DK2 in that run at all, so in every two-computer result reported from it GPC 2's bus assignment pointed at nothing.
  - evidence: Compared the two scripts directly. rs9.sh: '--mtu-model --deu-model' and a keystroke sequence containing ITEM 1,3 PLUS 2 (CRT 2 to GPC 2) and ITEM 1,9 PLUS 2 (MM2 to GPC 2). one-ops2.sh, the corrected control, adds '--deu-bus 7' and DKTRACE then shows bus 7 commanded 2261 times -- so with a unit present the assignment is used, and without one it cannot be. WHAT THIS UNSETTLES: #114 attributed GPC2's low I/O to its being unable to reach the transition and therefore commanding nothing. Part of that is instead a TEST SETUP DEFECT -- it was given a CRT that is not modelled. The 70-to-1 I/O ratio should be re-measured with a display unit actually present on GPC2's assigned bus before it is used as evidence for anything.
  - files `src/run.c` &middot; symptom `test setup,assigned a nonexistent display,--deu-bus,70 to 1` &middot; run `rs9,one2b`
- **#124** *(emulator)* THE REGRESSION GATE MIXES A DETERMINISTIC CHECK WITH A THROUGHPUT SAMPLE, AND ONLY THE FIRST IS A GATE. Its EVENT-DRIVEN counters are bit-exact across runs and across builds; its PERIODIC counters cannot be, because the run is cut off after a fixed WALL duration and they simply count how much simulated time fitted inside it. README.md quotes both sets together as figures that "should match exactly", and the periodic half of that is not achievable and never was. An exact hit on them is luck, a small miss is weather, and neither is evidence about the tree.
  - evidence: Four runs of headless-gpcmem.sh 420 on 2026-09-13 across two different builds -- the committed ca5dc0452 and a tree adding the bus 1-5 ICC change -- separated by whether each counter moved. BIT-EXACT IN ALL FOUR: mmu commands 56, blocksRead 431, wordsOut 220731, wordsTaken 220011, wordsLost 720, position 3/3/0; deu formatFills 8, resets 1, modeStatus 6, ipled true, and abandoned/bite/dumps/headerless/unknown all 0. VARIED: deu commands 1848/1845/1844/1844, fills 375/375/374/374, timeFills 569/568/568/568, displayFills 367/367/366/366, medsXfers 328/327/327/327, polls 575/574/574/574, wordsIn and wordsOut with them, and EVERY mtu counter (commands 7890/7883/7869/7876). THE SPLIT IS EXACTLY EVENT-DRIVEN AGAINST PERIODIC. The mass memory reads 431 blocks because the PASS load IS 431 blocks -- a bounded sequence of events, so it reproduces perfectly. Display repaints and MTU time reads happen every N ms for as long as the run lasts, so their totals measure the wall time the run got. formatFills staying at 8 while fills drifts is the same point in miniature: a format load is an event, a repaint is weather. AND THE DETERMINISTIC PHASE IS LONGER THAN THAT SUGGESTS: the scripted keystroke lands at simt=133.704 in ALL FOUR RUNS, identically, so boot, load and keystroke are bit-exact and every bit of the variance is in the free-running tail between the keystroke and the 420 s wall cutoff. HOW TO USE IT. Compare the invariant set STRICTLY -- that is the regression check, and it caught nothing today, which is what exonerated the ICC work. Do NOT compare the periodic set against README.md's numbers: 1848 is one sample of a real-time process and is not uniquely correct, and a run returning 1852 would mean the host gave the emulator more throughput, not that anything regressed. If the periodic counters are wanted as a check, they have to be read at a fixed SIMULATED instant rather than after a fixed wall duration; the harness cuts off on wall time today. ONE REAL DEFECT CAME OUT OF CHASING THIS and is fixed: YAGPC_CMDTRACE did a getenv per command word, so a run with the variable merely PRESENT in the environment measured differently from one without it. CORRECTED 2026-09-13: this entry first said "the regression gate is not byte-for-byte reproducible" and treated that as a fault in the gate. That overstates it. The gate reproduces on everything that constitutes behaviour; what does not reproduce was never a correctness signal.
  - env `YAGPC_CMDTRACE,YAGPC_BUSCENSUS` &middot; files `README.md,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `regression gate,periodic counters,event counters,throughput not correctness,false regression,real-time pacing,wall cutoff` &middot; run `fx-regress,ic-regress,ic-regress2,ctl-regress`
- **#126** *(emulator)* NO COMPUTER IS SYSTEMATICALLY LATE TO THE I/O-COMPLETE HANDSHAKE, AND AT THE ONE THAT FAILS BOTH ARE THERE. A swarm of eight short two-computer runs, half with the start order swapped, shows the order of arrival at I/O-complete handshakes is a coin toss, while the computer that STARTED SECOND casts the first vote in 7 of 8 runs regardless of its ID. At the failing handshake both raise I/O-complete within 13-143 us of each other; one finishes normally after ~0.2 ms and the other holds its code for 4.65-4.66 ms -- the full timeout -- and votes. So the failure is one computer not RECOGNISING a partner that was demonstrably present, not a partner arriving late. This withdraws #125's 'GPC2 arrives late by more than a pulse width' and the pointer to #22 that rested on it.
  - evidence: Runs sw-f1-0/2/4/6 (GPC1 IPLs first) and sw-f2-1/3/5/7 (GPC2 first), 300 s each, eight at once on separate port bases, all valid: mmu1 and mmu2 both 432 blocks, ICC traffic on both buses, 2 fail-votes each. Instrument YAGPC_SYNCORDER (vehicle_route_out) stamps each computer's sync-code change with its time on the barrier's SHARED clock, barPubUs -- the only frame in which two machines' instants compare; an earlier table here aligned the two machines' own clocks by ASSUMING two events simultaneous, which is circular. I/O-COMPLETE ARRIVAL: GPC1 first 27, GPC2 first 26, median gap +3 to +13 us. FIRST VOTER: the second starter in 7 of 8 (GPC2 in three of four FIRST=1 runs, GPC1 in all four FIRST=2 runs). SSIP: the second starter raises SSIP a steady ~1.65 ms (initially ~2.0 ms) before the first starter in every one of ~3,600 handshakes, mirror-imaged when the order swaps (-1670 vs +1666 us), slope about -0.08 us/s -- NOT drifting toward the 3.85 ms timeout, and the SSIP handshakes continue in step after the vote, so this offset is not what breaks the pair. THE VOTE comes 0.01-2.56 s into joint running, after 1-15 handshakes. FAILING HANDSHAKE, e.g. sw-f1-0: GPC1 001 at 1279.899 ms, GPC2 001 at 1279.933, GPC1 back to 111 at 1280.111, GPC2 back to 111 at 1284.595; the voter's hold is 4.65-4.66 ms in seven runs. sw-f2-5 differs: GPC1 raises 010 IPR repeatedly while GPC2 raises 001, a genuine disagreement about the I/O. No timer-sync (101) or SVC-sync (100) code occurs in any run. OPEN HYPOTHESIS, not yet measured: a causality leak -- codes cross between machines instantly in wall time while their simulated clocks may differ by up to the barrier delta, so a reader that is BEHIND may see its partner's return to null within its own first instants at 001. Against it: the 25 us barrier run in #125 still failed. The next measurement is every machine's shared clock at every code change, comparing voter against partner at the failing handshake.
  - csects `FCMISYNC,FCMCSYNC,FCMSFAIL` &middot; env `YAGPC_SYNCORDER,YAGPC_SYNCTRACE,YAGPC_BARRIER_US` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c` &middot; symptom `fail to sync,arrival order,start order,second starter votes,SSIP phase offset,I/O complete sync,not recognised,causality` &middot; run `sw-f1-0,sw-f1-2,sw-f1-4,sw-f1-6,sw-f2-1,sw-f2-3,sw-f2-5,sw-f2-7`
- **#130** *(method)* AN ICC MESSAGE LOG WITH A LINE CAP REPORTED 'NO RECONFIGURATION MESSAGES' BECAUSE THE CAP WAS SPENT ON ROUTINE TRAFFIC IN THE FIRST MINUTE, NOT BECAUSE NONE WERE SENT. Do not read an empty filtered result from a capped log as evidence of absence; check where the log stopped first.
  - evidence: Runs im-b25-0..2 and pa-b25 with YAGPC_ICCMSG=1 (report every change of an SSIP transfer's message buffer, first 400 lines). A filter for the ARC_ICC_MSG headers 09F6/0CF3/42BD over those logs found 0 -- and every log holds exactly 400 ICCMSG lines, spanning only 44-61 s of wall time and transfers 1-266 of ~2,000, ending minutes before the OPS request at 600 s. The offsets themselves are right: the buffers carry recognisable headers, 11EE x174, 12ED x93 (the DST header AIESIP.hal:1617 filters), 05FA x88, 03FC x45, at TFICC TICCBUFF (data positions 74-123) with TICCVBLG at position 8. Fixed: YAGPC_ICCMSG now takes a list of headers and reports only transfers carrying one, and ICCMSG-READ reports the receiver that takes the last word of such a transfer, since sending and reading are separate facts. Also added SYNCORDER-JOIN, each machine's shared-clock offset at barrier join, after an AIESIP probe (pa-b25, pb-b25) chose its windows on the wrong clock and looked after the event -- its evaluation of the CS_MASK/SIP_RS branch is void; the one fact that stands is that AIEB_SIP_RS is stored once all run and holds 0000 on both computers.
  - env `YAGPC_ICCMSG,YAGPC_SYNCORDER,YAGPC_WATCHHW` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c,src/vehicle.c` &middot; symptom `capped log,false absence,instrument artefact,wrong clock window` &middot; run `im-b25-0,im-b25-1,im-b25-2,pa-b25,pb-b25`
- **#135** *(goal)* THE REDUNDANT SET'S I/O COMPLETIONS DIVERGE BECAUSE OUR @RAW IGNORES THE MSC'S OWN BIT, SO FIOMDLY'S DELAY LENGTH DEPENDS ON EACH COMPUTER'S OTHER BUS TRAFFIC. When the CPU starts the MSC for an I/O, FIOMNTR sleeps the request's time-to-go with '@LI -1 / @RAW' -- the MSC bit set, which the POO says loops to the full count -- then looks once and raises @INT. Every member runs the same sleep, so on the vehicle completions line up. Ours ends the sleep as soon as every BCE is waiting: at once on an idle IOP, the full count on a busy one. Setting the bit (YAGPC_RAW_MSCBIT=1) passes the single-GPC regression gate and moves the break past the ICC barrier to bus 24 (IOQE 9068).
  - evidence: Runs ms-short-0, ms-std-0, ms-std-1 (YAGPC_DISPTRACE, YAGPC_BWTRACE_PE=1,2, FIOCMPLT/FCMSSYNC entry traces, shared clock with each computer's LATEST SYNCORDER-JOIN offset -- GPC2 re-joins and its offset moved 156.999 -> 157.003 s). ms-short-0, ICC SSIP transfer: on each computer the CPU starts the ICC MSC program (MSCPC<-096e4, LOADMSCBUSY), BCEs 1/2 go busy, finish 0.3 ms later, the CPU loads MSCPC<-099b2 with LOADMSCBUSY (the MSC still busy, flagged CLOBBER on BOTH), and then GPC2's MSC reaches @INT 0.22 ms later while GPC1's takes 4.52 ms; GPC1's CPU meanwhile enters FCMSSYNC and raises 100 against GPC2's 001 -> mutual vote. ms-std-0 (vote at 583.57 s) shows the same on IOQE 9068: MSC started -0.242/-0.248 ms on both, GPC2 @INT +0.20 ms, GPC1 @INT +4.95 ms. Earlier runs bw-b25-0..2 had already shown the ICC BCEs finishing within 0.05 ms of each other on both computers, so the skew is not on the bus. SOURCE: FIOMCNTL.asm FIOMNTR2/FIOMDLY ('@LI -1 *TURN ON MSC BIT IN REPEAT MASK', '@RAW 0(1) *DELAY'), then FIOMCKIO '@LI 0 *JUST CHECK IT'; FIOSTMSC sets TCVTMTTG from TIOQICNT (remaining expected time, 16 us units, times FIOSTMK1 into 33 us). POO IBM-6246556A: the @RAW count resolution is 33 us (255 counts = 8.4 ms), and with bit 0 set the repeat runs to the maximum count. WITH THE BIT SET: regression gate mb-regress (single GPC, headless-gpcmem 420 s) -- EVERY EVENT COUNTER EXACT (mmu 56/431/220731/220011/720/3-3-0, deu formatFills 8, resets 1, modeStatus 6, ipled), periodic deu 1849/376/569/368/575, mtu 7890 -- where #2 recorded this change killing a single-GPC run at ~231 s before the later fixes. Two-GPC short run mb- short-0 (OPS at 450 s): 134 SVC and 16 timer syncs in lockstep, every completion before the break within 30 us across computers (905a, 8c66, the ICC); the break is IOQE 9068, bus 24: GPC1's completion 0.77 ms after 8c66, GPC2's 0.80 ms after GPC1's, and GPC2 votes GPC1 out BEFORE its own 9068 completion arrives -- not a 3.85 ms timeout -- while GPC1 then times out in FCMISYNC and votes GPC2.  WHAT 099B2 IS, from run mp-short-0 (YAGPC_BCEPCTRACE=096e0-09a40, YAGPC_REPEATTRACE, bit OFF): the MSC program the CPU loads at 099b2 is a two-instruction stub (099b2 f3c1 ccb8, 099b4 f001 cb46) that enters FIOMNTR; its next instruction is FIOMDLY's @RAW at 1cb6c, armed 'count=148 (4884.0 us) acc=ffffffff' -- the time-to-go sleep with the MSC bit in the mask. In that sample it exited after 153 us (busy=80000080), and the met-exit skipped FIOMCKIO's '@LI 0 *JUST CHECK IT', so the next @RAW at 1cb70 armed 'x=3ffff count=262143 (8650719.0 us)' -- the 0x3ffff arm #2 noted, explained: it is the skip after a met FIOMDLY, which the correct mask makes impossible (a repeat with bit 0 set can only time out). 096e4 is the ICC device's own start program (f309 99ce, f311 99d8 -- operands in the FIOSICCM/FIOSICLS BCE programs at 199ce/199d8). SECOND TWO-GPC RUN WITH THE BIT SET, mb-std-0 (900 s, OPS at 600): same outcome as mb-short-0 -- set formed at 602.39 s, 134 SVC and 16 timer syncs on both, mutual vote 603.06 s, votes cleared 622.33/638.65 s.
  - addrs `96e4-96e4,99b2-99b2,1cb2e-1cb70` &middot; csects `FIOMNTR,FIOMDLY,FIOMCKIO,FIOSTMSC,FIOPDISP,FCMSSYNC,FCMISYNC` &middot; instrs `@RAW,@LI,@INT,@WAT` &middot; procs `MSC,BCE1,BCE2,BCE24` &middot; env `YAGPC_RAW_MSCBIT,YAGPC_DISPTRACE,YAGPC_BWTRACE_PE,YAGPC_SYNCORDER` &middot; files `src/iop_msc_instr.c,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `redundant set breaks,I/O done vs SVC,completion skew,MSC delay,FIOMDLY ends early,fail to sync after formation,bus 24` &middot; doc `IBM-6246556A @RAW programming notes; FIOMCNTL.asm; FIOSTMSC.asm` &middot; run `bw-b25-0,bw-b25-1,bw-b25-2,ms-short-0,ms-std-0,ms-std-1,mb-regress,mb-short-0`
- **#151** *(flight-sw)* I/O RESET CANNOT CLEAR A FAIL-TO-SYNC VOTE OR THE CAM, and neither can an OPS mode recall on its own. DIR_IORESET, the program scheduled when the I/O RESET key is depressed, resets only strings and buses: FC strings 1-4, launch data buses 12 and 13, payload string 5, plus a PMU reset, a GPCF reset and an error counter. It never touches redundant-set membership or the fail discretes. From a good GPC it resets only the buses that GPC's set commands, and a voted-out GPC is no longer in that set; from the failed GPC it resets only what that GPC still commands, which after a vote-out is nothing. What clears the vote is a redundant-set re-formation: ARC_GPC_RECONFIG writes CZ2B_RS, distributes it over the ICC, calls SMR(ARC_SYNC) to form the RS, and then sets ARC_CAM to 0.
  - evidence: Read 2026-09-16 from OI340600/SSSRC/DIRIOR.hal, whose header states that it requests FCOS reset all strings and buses commanded by the requesting GPC or by the redundant set of which it is a member; DIR_BUS_MASK is built from CZ2B_BUS_MASK1/2 indexed by TFCMID and RECONFIG is called per bus, with mass memory buses 18 and 19 explicitly excluded. ARC_CAM=0 sits at ARCGPC.hal line 035210BL immediately after SMR(ARC_SYNC) at 035200BI. ARC_GPC_RECONFIG is scheduled from DM6OPS.hal:403 and :447 (OPS request and MODE RECALL from a DM1 keyboard), DM5NEW.hal:476, ARGREC.hal:146 and ARFDPSCO.hal:509. ITEM 47 on the GPC MEMORY display schedules it directly but is locked out with CDLK_K6 unless the entering GPC is in no redundant set and is in OPS 0, so it cannot be used from a set member. USER CONFIRMED EMPIRICALLY the same day: I/O RESET EXEC from both a good GPC and the failed GPC left the lockout in place, and OPS 201 PRO from two different keyboards did not clear it either.
  - csects `DIRIOR,DIR_IORESET,ARCGPC,ARC_GPC_RECONFIG,DM6OPS,DM5NEW,ARGREC,ARFDPSCO,FCMSFAIL,FCMCSYNC` &middot; tape `v44boot` &middot; files `SSSRC/DIRIOR.hal,SSSRC/ARCGPC.hal,SSSRC/DM6OPS.hal,SSSRC/ARFDPSCO.hal,SSSRC/CD0001.dfg` &middot; symptom `I/O RESET does nothing,I/O RESET flashing,CAM stays lit,fail-to-sync lockout will not clear,voted out GPC will not rejoin` &middot; doc `OI340600 SSSRC/DIRIOR.hal header; ARCGPC.hal FORM RS`
- **#153** *(emulator)* REDUNDANT-SET MEMBERS DO NOT PERFORM IDENTICAL I/O IN OUR MODEL, and each extra I/O is a synchronising SVC that one computer executes ALONE -- a handshake the other three must reach within FCOS's 3.85 ms or vote it out. This is the root cause of the IPR-handshake slip of #148/#149, and it is chronic rather than a rare timing accident. It is NOT host scheduling: the simulated-time barrier holds all four machines within 25 us of each other, so a computer that burns 3.85 ms of simulated time spinning in FCMSSYNC proves its peers executed ~3.8 ms of simulated work in the same window -- they were running, not descheduled. It is also not barrier tuning, which #125 already settled. New evidence for #137: the asymmetry is in peripheral I/O between set members and non-members sharing buses with no arbitration.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-16 over 208.6 s of healthy running immediately before the failure, from the YAGPC_SYNCTRACE log of a four-GPC --bce-network run. Codes issued per computer: timer 9126/9126/9126/9126, SSIP 1304/1304/1304/1304 and IPR 870/870/870/870 match EXACTLY across all four, while the I/O-derived codes do not -- SVC 33901 on GPC1 against 33887/33885/33887, and I/O complete 18581 against 18580/18579/18579. So the divergence is sourced specifically in I/O, not general drift: GPC1 runs about one extra synchronising SVC every 14 seconds. Join delay from the first computer issuing a code to the last joining it: SVC median 75 us, 99% 727 us, MAX 3645 us against a 3850 us deadline -- a 5 percent margin, with I/O complete max 2648, timer 2515 and SSIP 2285. One lone handshake every 14 s against a tail already touching the deadline makes a vote-out a matter of time, which matches hours of good running before a loss. The failing round itself: GPC1 alone issued 100 SVC at t=794931.472804, no peer joined, GPC1 dropped to 111 null at .477193 after 4.39 ms, and GPC2/3/4 raised 010 IPR at .477466-.477483 and voted. A CANDIDATE MECHANISM FOR THE TAIL, from the barrier statistics of a 22-minute four-GPC run (--gpcs 1,2,3,4 --bce-network, delta 25 us): 'simulated-time barrier held 158,907,466 times, 1090.851 s total, 0 abandoned; 154,462,371 released while spinning, 666,322 SLEEPS'. Nothing was abandoned, so the barrier kept its guarantee -- but two thirds of a million holds were NOT cleared by the 200 us spin and went to pthread_cond_timedwait with a BARRIER_SLEEP_SEC = 2 ms timeout. A machine asleep there is stopped for milliseconds, which is the scale of the measured join tail (SVC max 3645 us against the 3850 us deadline) and far above the 67-87 us median. This would explain why #125's sweep of YAGPC_BARRIER_US did not help: the damage would not be how far the machines DRIFT but what it costs one to be STOPPED while a handshake is open. TO TEST: correlate barrier sleeps against the join delays in a YAGPC_SYNCTRACE run, or instrument the sleep path to report when a machine sleeps with a non-null code on its own output lines. REEVALUATED 2026-09-16 LATE, AND THE UNITS WERE WRONG. discretes_synctrace prints yagpc_monotonic_seconds() -- WALL CLOCK -- while FCOS's 3.85 ms timeout is counted by a spin loop and is therefore SIMULATED time. Every join-delay figure above mixes the two, so the framing 'the tail reaches 3645 us against a 3850 us deadline, a 5% margin' IS NOT ESTABLISHED: a large wall-clock gap can be wholly benign, because a machine held at the barrier has its simulated clock stopped too and spends none of its budget. The same objection kills a barrier-sleep hypothesis raised the same evening (666,322 sleeps of up to 2 ms): a sleep STOPS simulated time and so cannot consume the timeout. BUT THE BARRIER'S OWN GUARANTEE NOW ARGUES THE OTHER WAY, and more strongly. Delta is 25 us and the run reported 0 ABANDONED over 22 minutes, so the bound never lapsed; therefore for a victim to burn 3.85 ms of SIMULATED time spinning in FCMSSYNC, its peers must have advanced ~3.83 ms of simulated time as well. They were executing real work and never reached the sync point. So the cause is EXECUTION-PATH DIVERGENCE, not timing, and the barrier is exonerated rather than implicated. THIS ALSO RETIRES THE 'SECOND MECHANISM': GPC3's failure at a 101 timer sync was set aside because timer counts matched exactly across all four computers, but equal TOTALS over 208 s do not make each ROUND symmetric, and extra I/O work shifts WHEN a machine reaches its next sync point. Divergence sourced in I/O will surface at whichever handshake comes next, SVC or timer. ONE CAUSE, NOT TWO. FIRST THING TO DO: put the machine's simulated time on the SYNC trace line beside the wall clock -- everything here is inference from a measurement in the wrong units, and with simulated time in the trace 'did the peers really execute 3.8 ms of work' becomes a direct observation instead of an argument.
  - csects `FCMSSYNC,FCMISYNC,FCMSFAIL,FCMCSYNC` &middot; procs `DK6,BCE6,discretes` &middot; tape `v44boot` &middot; env `YAGPC_SYNCTRACE,YAGPC_BARRIER_US` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/vehicle.c,src/bcenet_transport.c` &middot; symptom `lone SVC handshake,fail to sync after hours,IPR handshake slip,GPC voted out at random,join delay 3645 us` &middot; tsec `794700-794932`
- **#157** *(flight-sw)* AN OVERLAY IS LOADED GPC-TO-GPC BY PREFERENCE, OVER THE LAUNCH DATA BUSES, AND ONLY FROM MASS MEMORY AS A FALLBACK. ARCGPC's source selector is explicit: MEM_SRC=1 major-function overlay GPC TO GPC, 2 program overlay GPC TO GPC, 3 MF overlay FROM MM, 4 PG overlay FROM MM, and the search runs DO FOR MEM_SRC = 1 TO 4 -- GPC first, mass memory last. The transfer moves on the LAUNCH DATA BUSES 12 and 13, not the mass memory buses: for MEM_SRC < 3 the code sets ARC_BUS_DELTA = -6 with the comment 'LDB BUS 12,13 -- MM BUS 18,19', so a source GPC commands the LDB and plays the mass memory unit's part while the receiving computers listen. It is gated on ARC_DPS_STATUS$(3), the GPC/LDB item on the DPS UTILITY SPEC (ITEM 11, per the DR105712 comment), and the source GPC is GIVEN the bus on demand -- 'ASSIGN LDB BUS TO SOURCE GPCS IF REQUIRED', by ICC message type 3 -- which is why both CONFIG 2 and CONFIG 3 leave L1 and L2 unassigned in the NBAT. CONSEQUENCE: A COMPUTER WITH NO MASS MEMORY ASSIGNMENT IS NOT STUCK. It does not need one to be reloaded; it needs the LDB path and the ICC message that sets it up. Any reasoning of the form 'GPC3 has no MM in the NBAT, therefore it cannot reload an OPS overlay' is WRONG -- that argument was made and retracted the same evening.
  - evidence: Read 2026-09-16 from OI340600/SSSRC/ARCGPC.hal: the MEM_SRC legend at 054100BI-054400BI; the search loop 'DO FOR MEM_SRC = 1 TO 4' at 038369CV; ARC_SRC_GPC$(MEM_SRC) at 039220BY and GPC_OVERLAY_SOURCE(MEM_SRC,ARC_J) at 062100BY; bus selection at 055500BL-056500BI where ARC_BUS_ID is 18 or 19 and, for MEM_SRC<3 with ARC_DPS_STATUS$(3) ON, ARC_BUS_DELTA=-6 'LDB BUS 12,13 -- MM BUS 18,19'; the dynamic assignment at 058502BQ-058570BP, 'ASSIGN LDB BUS TO SOURCE GPCS IF REQUIRED', using GTG_OVERLAY_SOURCE and TFCM_LDB_CMDR and sending ICC_MSG_TYPE = 3 when the source GPC does not already command the bus. Corroborated by DM6OPS's MODE RECALL comment, 'NO MMU PRE POSITION WILL ACTUALLY TAKE PLACE SINCE AT LEAST ONE GPC MUST HAVE THE MF/OPS OVERLAYS'. NOT YET TESTED IN OUR MODEL: buses 12 and 13 are inside YAGPC_BUS_MAX = 24 and nothing treats them specially, so under --bce-network they would ride the framer like any other bus -- but whether one GPC's LDB transmission reaches the other computers' framers has never been checked, and neither has the ICC message that assigns the bus (see #107). THE CREW PROCEDURE HAS A NAME: FREEZE DRY (FD). A spare computer is loaded with a memory configuration it is not running, so that it HOLDS that OPS's overlays and can supply them to others -- the caching GPC. From STS-134 Post Insertion book page 1-2, step 3 'LOAD MC 2 INTO G2FD GPC': PL, GPC/CRT <FD GPC>/X EXEC; on X: PL GPC MEMORY; CONFIG - ITEM 45 +2 EXEC; GPC - ITEM 46 +<FD GPC> EXEC; STORE - ITEM 47 EXEC; 'Store complete when MC = 02'. The same book uses 'Retry GPC FREEZE DRY (step 3)' and names the roles G2, G2FD, FD and SM in its single-PASS-GPC-failure matrix. ARFDPSCO.hal gives the rules: ITEM 45 sets CDJV_FD_MC and is refused (CDLK_K6) unless CZ2V_GRT_MC$(ARF_I;) = ARF_I; ITEM 46 sets CDJV_FD_GPC and is refused unless CZ2B_RS$(TFCMID;) = HEX'0000' AND ARF_I = TFCMID -- so it must be typed on a computer that is NOT IN A REDUNDANT SET, NAMING ITSELF; ITEM 47 sets CZ2V_REC_XERR = 2 and schedules ARC_GPC_RECONFIG, and is itself locked out if the entering GPC is in a redundant set or not in OPS 0. CD0001.dfg declares them ITEM=(45,UE,I2,1,10), ITEM=(46,UE,I1,1,5), ITEM=(47,UX). SO THERE ARE TWO PROCEDURES, AND THEY FIT TOGETHER: freeze dry PUTS the overlays into a spare computer (crew action, ITEMs 45/46/47 on its own GPC MEMORY display, which it can only have if it has been given a CRT); and at the next OPS transition ARCGPC's MEM_SRC 1/2 search TAKES them from whichever GPC holds them, over LDB 12/13, in preference to mass memory.
  - csects `ARCGPC,ARC_MEM_OVL,ARC_OVERLAY_MASKS,GTG_OVERLAY_SOURCE,DM6OPS,DM2APP,DM5NEW,ARFDPSCO` &middot; procs `BCE12,BCE13,BCE18,BCE19,ICC,LDB` &middot; tape `v44boot` &middot; files `SSSRC/ARCGPC.hal,SSSRC/DM6OPS.hal,src/bcenet_transport.c,src/vehicle.c` &middot; symptom `cannot reload overlay,no mass memory bus,OPS overlay,GPC to GPC transfer,launch data bus,pre position,settles into OPS 0,freeze dry,FD GPC,ITEM 45,ITEM 46,ITEM 47` &middot; doc `STS-134 Post Insertion p.1-2 step 3 (LOAD MC INTO FD GPC) and the MEMORY CONFIGURATION TABLE p.1-3; OI340600 ARCGPC.hal MEM_SRC legend; ARFDPSCO.hal items 45-47`
- **#161** *(method)* PERFORMANCE NUMBERS TAKEN ON THE 20-CORE HOST ON 2026-09-17 ARE NOT TRUSTWORTHY, because its kernel had oopsed four times in AppArmor, whose hooks sit on every socket call in yaGPC2's hot path. Functional gate results from the same period ARE trustworthy -- they score deterministic event counts, not timings. WHAT CAUSED THE OOPS IS NOT ESTABLISHED: this entry first blamed the failing disk, and that link does not hold up.
  - evidence: dmesg: 'BUG: unable to handle page fault for address: ffff9b1601789053', RIP common_perm_cond+0x6d/0x200 (AppArmor), Oops #1 at uptime 660700, #2 692208, #3 778629, #4 865034 -- the SAME faulting address every time, each raised by systemd-tmpfiles doing statx. /proc/sys/kernel/tainted = 12417 = P(1) + D(128, the kernel died) + OE(4096) + unsigned(8192). WHY THE DISK EXPLANATION WAS WRONG, per the user 2026-09-17: AppArmor's labels and per-inode security blobs are KERNEL MEMORY, and the faulting address is a kernel pointer loaded from an object and dereferenced at +0x53 -- not file content off a platter. ext4 answers a corrupt on-disk inode with an I/O error, not a wild pointer. And systemd-tmpfiles manages about 134 paths of which 133 are on the ROOT volume (/var /run /etc /dev /tmp /root); only one names /home, the symlink onto the storage volume. What existed was a CORRELATION -- one machine with both a failing disk and an oopsing kernel -- and the mechanism joining them was invented, not measured. The recurrence at one address fits a persistent corrupted kernel object or an AppArmor use-after-free at least as well, and neither needs a disk fault. COUNTER-EXAMPLE, same day: the 2011 i7-3930K showed Xorg spinning with the identical signature (98% of a core, state R, wchan 0, ~8 voluntary context switches a second against ~456 when healthy) with ZERO oopses, taint 12289 lacking the D bit, and no disk fault -- so the X spin does not require the kernel fault it was attributed to. INDEPENDENTLY TRUE AND WORTH KEEPING: a saturated four-GPC profile puts aa_inet_msg_perm at 1.41% and apparmor_socket_recvmsg at 0.85% of cycles, both on the per-syscall path being optimised, so 3%-resolution work should not be done on a kernel in that state whatever put it there.
  - env `YAGPC_PACETRACE` &middot; files `src/bcenet_transport.c,src/discretes.c,src/envcache.c` &middot; symptom `Xorg 100%,kernel oops,apparmor,measurement contaminated`
- **#164** *(method)* MULTI-GPC IPL FAILS ON A SLOW HOST BY RUNNING OUT OF WALL-CLOCK TIME, NOT BY LOSING SYNC -- and the per-GPC procedure's own wait timeouts were the hard stop. A crew script's times are WALL CLOCK while the computers it drives run on SIMULATED time, so every fixed delay and every timeout shrinks in simulated terms in proportion to how far below rate 1.0 the host is.
  - evidence: On a 2011 i7-3930K (6 cores, no AVX2) a single GPC driving three displays runs at rate 0.18-0.30, about a quarter of real time, against 1.00 on the 20-core machine. Measured per-computer IPL cost here, from panelO6's own wait reports: 'wait crt 1 title GPCIPL' met after 41.2 s, 'wait gpc 1 mode-tb RUN' met after 163.8 s with nothing else competing; the second computer's GPCIPL menu took 65.5 s because the first was by then taking its share. Four computers plus the 14-item NBAT at +8 s each, OPS 301 and the IDP3 block come to over 1250 s, so 480 s reached 1 of 4 in RUN and 1200 s could not have finished either. THE HARD STOP was ipl-one-gpc.script's 'timeout 300' on the RUN talkback: the first computer needed 164 s of it unopposed, and each later computer is slower again, so the fourth would have been cut off and the script would have carried on issuing crew actions to a computer that never came up. Raised to 300/900, which costs a fast host nothing because a wait ends when its condition is met. WHAT IS NOT WRONG, checked across three runs on this host: zero BCE receive timeouts, zero error terminations, zero fail-votes, zero fail-to-sync. The emulator's own bus timeouts are safe on a slow host because iop.c:1991 compares against 'now = iop->cpu->elapsedTimeUs', which is SIMULATED time, not the wall clock. The computers that did come up ran clean.
  - env `YAGPC_PACETRACE` &middot; files `discretePanel/examples/ipl-one-gpc.script,discretePanel/examples/4gpc-g3-startup.script,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `multi-GPC will not IPL,1 of 4 in RUN,wait timeout,slow host`
- **#165** *(method)* WHY FOUR-GPC RUNS ONLY EVER GOT ONE OR TWO COMPUTERS INTO RUN, and it is not a sync failure: the per-GPC IPL procedure waits for the GPCIPL menu with 'wait crt N title GPCIPL', which is a STATE test, on a CRT that three of the four computers share in turn. The previous computer's menu is still on that screen, so from the second sharer onward the wait is met instantly, the script types ITEM 1 EXEC into a computer that has not yet put up its menu, the menu-selected load never starts, and that computer sits until its RUN wait expires.
  - evidence: crewscript.py _poll_screen: with a title, met = 'the text is CONTAINED in the page now on show' -- nothing requires the page to be NEW. panelO6's own log is explicit, from a 2100 s four-GPC run on 2026-09-17: 'wait met after 65.9 s: wait crt 2 title GPCIPL' for GPC2, which had a clean CRT2, then 'wait met after 0.0 s: wait crt 2 title GPCIPL  [crt2: GPC MEMORY 27 OPTION _ (1) 2 GPCIPL MENU]' for GPC3, matching GPC2's leftover page. 4gpc-g3-startup.script gives crt=2 to GPC2, GPC3 AND GPC4, and GPC3 and GPC4 are exactly the two that never came up, in three separate runs. THE FIX is the transition test the vocabulary already has: 'wait crt  new-screen' before the title wait. new-screen baselines the page when the wait begins and returns only when a DIFFERENT one arrives, so the stale page must go first. A LONGER FIXED DELAY WOULD NOT HAVE FIXED IT and would have hidden it on a fast host: the IDP reload gets a few seconds of WALL clock to clear the display, which was many display polls on the 20-core machine and about one simulated second at rate 0.24 -- the same wall-versus-simulated trap as #164. WHAT WAS NOT WRONG: across every run on this host, zero fail-votes, zero fail-to-sync, zero BCE receive timeouts, zero error terminations, and the three computers that were running showed instantaneous rates of 0.244/0.244/0.244, identical to three decimals, which is the simulated-time barrier holding them in step exactly as designed.
  - files `discretePanel/examples/ipl-one-gpc.script,discretePanel/crewscript.py` &middot; symptom `1 of 4 in RUN,2 of 4 in RUN,GPC3 never runs,GPC4 never runs,stale display,garbage on CRT2`
- **#168** *(emulator)* ON THIS HOST the emulation loop is MEMORY-LATENCY-BOUND, not work-bound, so removing instructions from it does not make it faster; only costs that STALL -- syscalls above all -- are worth attacking. CAUTION ON SCOPE: this is measured on a 2011 Sandy Bridge-E (i7-3930K, 256 KB L2 per core, no AVX2) and is NOT a permanent property of the emulator. A machine with a larger cache, better prefetch or more memory-level parallelism could shift the balance back towards work, so re-measure IPC and cache-miss rate on new hardware before concluding that instruction-path work is pointless there.
  - evidence: perf stat on the emulation thread of a one-GPC run, 2026-09-17, while it sat at 98.3% CPU in state R: IPC 0.82 against a core that can retire about 4; frontend stalled 58.58% of cycles; cache-misses 74.74% OF ALL LAST-LEVEL CACHE REFERENCES -- 205M misses in 20 s, about 10M trips to RAM per second, which at ~100 ns each is the whole time budget; branch-misses only 1.14%, so branches are NOT the problem. WHAT THIS PREDICTS AND WHAT WAS MEASURED, uninstrumented 1-GPC, 600 s, after the display-buffer fix in #167 brought run-to-run spread down to 0.2%: attend-only baseline 697010 steps/s; plus a 64 KB hw1->opcode decode table AND binding the timing row and override flag into InstrDesc, 701336; plus binding SIX per-instruction strcmps in instr_time_pre_n and decodef to a nmBits bitfield, 696912. Three builds spread across 697k-701k is ONE NUMBER, not a trend -- the +0.6% in the middle was given back by the next measurement, so nothing here should be banked as a gain. The profile attributed 3.19-3.61% to __strcmp_sse42 and 2.05% to instr_decode and removing both changed nothing, because that work filled slots the CPU was already idling through. CONTRAST #166: gating attend()'s clock read bought 105%, because a clock_gettime under the hpet clocksource is a SYSCALL to a memory-mapped timer -- a serialised stall that out-of-order execution cannot hide. THE CHANGES WERE KEPT ANYWAY, on the user's decision and for SIMPLIFICATION rather than speed: the decode table replaced a 256-bucket partition plus a candidate scan, cutting instr_decode to two lines and static footprint from 131.5 KB to 64 KB, and it is proved against the full scan for all 65536 halfwords (YAGPC_DECODE_SELFTEST: 0 mismatches).
  - files `src/cpu_instr.c,src/timing.c,src/instr.h` &middot; symptom `core utilization,optimization no effect,cache misses,IPC 0.82,stalled frontend`
- **#170** *(method)* WHERE REAL TIME RUNS OUT ON THE 2011 i7-3930K, and what costs what: ONE computer holds OPS 201 with about 20% of a core to spare; TWO is where it tips over. Going to OPS 201 costs a lone computer ~64% more than idling in OPS 0; forming a real set costs a further ~26% per computer on top. BOTH matter and the OPS is the larger -- do not attribute the whole cost to the sync machinery.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-17 after the day's optimisation work (340906 -> 730568 steps/s, 2.14x). The test is the THREAD STATE, which needs no instrumentation: a pacer that is keeping up SLEEPS (hrtimer_nanosleep) because it is ahead of the wall clock, while one that cannot is pinned in state R with wchan 0. 1 GPC / 1 CRT / OPS 0: 48% of a core, sleeping, keeps up. 1 GPC / 1 CRT / OPS 201: 78.8%, keeps up with less margin. 2 GPC / 2 CRT / OPS 201: 99% + 99%, both pinned, does not keep up -- but runs CLEAN (zero fail-votes, fail-to-sync, BCE receive timeouts, error terminations; both displays driven, zero transfers abandoned). 4 GPC / 2 CRT / OPS 201: 4 x 99%, loses about 5 minutes of simulated time over a run. THE FIRST COMPARISON MADE WAS CONFOUNDED and is corrected here: 1 GPC in OPS 0 against 2 GPCs in OPS 201 changes the computer count AND the OPS together, so it cannot say which caused the extra cost; the user caught it. The 1-GPC OPS 201 run separates them, and OPS 201 IS REACHED -- mass memory goes busy four times after the request, which is the computer loading OPS 2 overlays. A single computer can hold OPS 201 because set membership is DATA: DM6OPS takes the target set from the GPC Requirements Table indexed by the OPS requested, and a GRT entry naming only itself never syncs with anybody. Script: examples/.1gpc-ops201.script (diagnostic, hidden).
  - config `G2` &middot; symptom `real time,does not keep up,core utilization,OPS 201`
- **#173** *(emulator)* A vehicle restored from a snapshot is NOT a redundant set: the computers vote against each other, and once the votes clear they no longer transition in common -- OPS xxx PRO has to be entered separately on each GPC instead of one carrying the other. Restoring per-machine state is not sufficient to restore a SET.
  - evidence: A RESTORED VEHICLE LEFT ALONE IS CLEAN. 2-GPC 2-CRT OPS 201 restored from a snapshot and held ten minutes with no input at all: 0 votes, 0 fail-to-sync, 0 BCE receive timeouts, 0 error terminations, 0 barrier abandonments, 0 display peer holds, 0 panel-silent holds. Repeated with RUN -> STBY -> RUN played into GPC1: equally clean, which clears #175 as the cause. Both ran at roughly 97-99% of real time once the harness's own start-up delays are accounted for -- against the ~80% an interactive session showed, the difference being the manager, the GUI and a person driving it. So the earlier reading of this entry -- that a restored vehicle diverges of its own accord -- is NOT supported: it does not. WHAT THE CLEAN RUNS DID NOT TEST is the path the voting session actually used. They start simulatePASS fresh with --snapshot-resume, which builds a vehicle in a new process. The manager's Restore button does something else entirely: Launcher.stop(keep={'manager'}) and bring_up() INSIDE a running process, stopping six children, keeping one, and relaunching into the same port base, the same multicast groups and the same discrete buses. Every clean run has taken the easy path. That is now the hypothesis and the next experiment -- restore, then restore again in place through the session port, and watch the CAM across the second one. Earlier evidence on this entry (votes with zero wall-clock holds, so not slowness) stands and is what makes it worth chasing.
