- **#174** *(emulator)* A restored display shows FOREGROUND DATA ONLY: no background fixed format text, and the foreground periodically blanks or shows wrong data before correcting itself. The DEU/IDP picture is not part of a snapshot, so PASS's periodic field updates arrive against a display that was never loaded with the format they belong to.
  - evidence: Observed 2026-09-18 on a 2-GPC OPS 201/301 restore, and it is the expected consequence rather than a defect in the capture: --snapshot writes per-machine CPU/IOP state and main storage, and nothing else. The static format text arrives once, at DEU LOAD time, as a FORMAT FILL -- measured at 8 for a whole 420 s run (README.md:262-266), so it is an EVENT and not a periodic refresh; a capture taken afterwards has no record of it and a restore never replays it. The changing fields are written continuously (367 displayFills and 569 timeFills in the same run), which is why the foreground appears at all. This is the gap the rewritten plan calls Stage 2, and this observation is its Stage 0 input: what came back is the foreground, what did not is the format. Cheapest candidate fix first: DEUUnit.requestLoad (MEDS2.py:1826) per IDP, letting PASS repaint; if that does not restore the whole format, save DEUUnit state and replay it via _sendToMDUs in 509-halfword chunks as _clearMDUs (MEDS2.py:10305) already does with zeros.
  - procs `DK6,DK7,DK8` &middot; env `--snapshot,--resume` &middot; files `discretePanel/MEDS2.py,discretePanel/simulatePASS.py,src/deumodel.c` &middot; symptom `foreground only after restore,no background fixed data,format text missing,foreground blanks then corrects`
- **#175** *(emulator)* What happens when a RUNNING GPC is moded to STBY, and why the other computer votes against it. The flight software's own answer is FCMSWMON ('RUN MODE MONITOR'): output sync discretes 000 so the set sees a graceful departure rather than a failure, reset the READY talkback, then spin in FCMSTBYL with both interval counters pinned at maximum -- running nothing else -- until moded back to RUN, then re-enable interrupts and continue initialization. FCMSWMON is called only from FCMNINIT (HALT->STBY of an IPL'd GPC) and FCMLINIT; it tells the RUN->STBY case apart by TCVTSREF=2, which it sets itself on the way out. So the RUN->STBY case can only be reached by RE-ENTERING INITIALIZATION -- which suggests the hardware resets or interrupts on that edge, and would explain the documented 'forces OPS 0'. This emulator resets the CPU on HALT->STBY only. NOT YET CONFIRMED in the hardware documentation; the next step is the BCE/CPU Principles of Operation on the mode switch.
  - evidence: TRACED 2026-09-19 with YAGPC_SYNCTRACE, one RUN->STBY->RUN excursion of GPC2 on a restored 2-GPC OPS 201 vehicle, NO VOTES -- and the graceful-departure protocol visibly works. STBY selected at ~t=1556.8; GPC2 carried on cycling normally (110 SSIP / 111 null every ~160 ms) for seven cycles; at t=1558.029800 GPC2 put up 000 'dead/halt/standby' -- FPMZSYNC's all-zero pattern, ~1.2 s after the switch, which is the software-poll latency; GPC1 read GPC2 as 000 at t=1558.029785; the 000 stood 10.2 s with GPC2 emitting nothing else; on RUN at t=1568.228 GPC2 went 000 -> 111 null, the transition FCMCSYNC readmits a member on. So a SINGLE excursion is modelled correctly and draws no vote, as FPMZSYNC ('to prevent fail-to-syncs in other GPCs') and FCMISYNC ('removes them from the appropriate set') intend. The ~1.2 s latency also explains a short excursion moving nothing: back to RUN before the poll noticed. VOTES NEED SOMETHING MORE: in the morning session the first excursion was likewise clean and the votes (12 ON, 21 ON) arrived on the SECOND. Leading candidate, untested: a second STBY arriving while GPC2 is still being readmitted after the first. Earlier evidence on this entry: the CRT2 clock inference is withdrawn (GPC1 had taken over CRT2); the Principles of Operation list DI-1 STANDBY and DI-2 RUN with 'IOP response TBD' and hold the CPU in reset for DI-0 HALT only, so the emulator's switch modelling matches the hardware documentation.  ANSWERED BY THE FLIGHT SOFTWARE, 2026-09-28 (runs cyc1, cyc2: examples/stby-cycle.script, 20 excursions of GPC2 after the four-GPC start-up, yaGPC2-mtu8). THE FIRST EXCURSION -- GPC2 leaving the REDUNDANT SET in OPS 201 -- draws votes every time (cyc1 and cyc2 both); LATER EXCURSIONS, from the common set in OPS 0 after the forced OPS 0, draw none. What happens, in order: ARAGPCSW needs STBY on two consecutive samples (SRN 5482-6005), then ZSD (SVC 27, FPMZSYNC) puts up 000 -- seen at shared 464.338898 -- masks LDB1/2, forces OPS 0. The rest of the redundant set times out waiting for GPC2's I/O-complete code in FCMISYNC and enters FCMSFAIL with r0=20080001: bit 2 (0x2000) is the HALT/STANDBY ENTRY flag and mask 0x0008 is GPC2 -- the designed path. FCMSFAIL suppresses CAM processing ONLY for a target set drop (TBMWFLMK 0x0800); a halt/standby entry merely skips stopping the trace, so the fail votes (cam 12, 32, 42) ARE issued by design. GPC2 meanwhile times out in FCMSSYNC+0086 on its next synchronising SVC, removes the other three, finds itself alone in its redundant set and lights its own diagonal (cam 22) -- also the code's stated behaviour ('SELF IS ALONE IN R.S.'). So votes on a redundant-set member moded to STBY are NOT an emulator defect; ZSD's purpose is a correctly attributed removal, not a silent one. Also measured: every STBY->RUN rejoin goes through AIBGPCLO's secondary clock initialisation (landmark 1c429 hit once per excursion), so the excursion loop is a valid test of #259 from the second excursion on.
  - csects `FCMSWMON,FCMNINIT,FCMLINIT,FCMSTBYL  ARAGPCSW,FPMZSYNC,FCMSSYNC,FCMISYNC,FCMSFAIL` &middot; procs `CPU` &middot; env `YAGPC_MODETRACE,--discretes` &middot; files `src/run.c,PFS OI340600/SSSRC/FCMSWMON.asm,PFS OI340600/SSSRC/FCMNINIT.asm` &middot; symptom `mutual fail votes after RUN to STBY to RUN,GPC1 takes over GPC2 display bus,CRT2 title shows GPC 1,MODE talkback BP sometimes,short STBY excursion missed,cannot force OPS 0  votes` &middot; doc `PASS User's Guide OI32 Table 2-5 footnote; DPS Workbook USA005350 Rev B 2.x and 3.x` &middot; run `cyc1,cyc2`
- **#176** *(emulator)* A RESUMED MACHINE NEVER ANNOUNCES ITS OUTPUT DISCRETES, so nothing ever learns them. discretes_publish_out sends only the bits that CHANGED and returns early when none did; a resumed machine loads regDiscreteOut out of its snapshot, so by the time the software next touches it nothing has changed and the register is never put on the wire -- while every listener starts from nothing. The crew panel's gpc_out is 'None until heard', so its MODE talkback sits at barberpole for the whole run, and the peers, wired through outHook, never learn that computer's lines either.
  - evidence: Found by a crew script that waited for the talkback before doing anything else: 'WAIT TIMED OUT after 300 s: wait gpc 2 mode-tb RUN timeout 300'. The vehicle was healthy by every other measure -- it ran 2 h 16 min, 6,139,962,517 instructions on GPC1 and 6,171,541,426 on GPC2, 8196.5 s of simulated time, 4270 display fills to MEDS1, the barrier held 191,955,595 times with 0 abandoned -- and its MODE talkback never once read RUN. discretes.c: 'uint32_t changed = before ^ after; ... if (changed == 0) return;' and the outHook call sits after that return. THIS IS THE SAME PUBLISH-ON-CHANGE TRAP the restore invariants already name for cam.py, which only rebuilds its matrix because a fresh yaGPC2 has value[] zeroed and so its first publish IS a change -- a resumed machine is exactly the case where that reasoning fails, because the value it comes up holding is the one it means to keep. Note also that even when something later does change a bit, only the delta goes out, so a listener that never heard the base value can never reconstruct it. FIX: on a --resume, publish the restored register with ~value as the previous value, so every bit counts as changed and the whole word goes out as one SET and one RESET. Whether this also accounts for #173's votes is NOT established -- 2 h 16 min in this state produced no votes at all.
  - procs `CPU` &middot; env `--resume,--snapshot` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/discretes.c,discretePanel/panelO6.py` &middot; symptom `MODE talkback stays barberpole after a restore,wait gpc mode-tb RUN times out,talkback never RUN,peers never learn a restored computer's lines`
- **#177** *(flight-sw)* WHO CASTS THE MUTUAL FAIL VOTES WHEN A RUNNING GPC IS MODED TO STANDBY A SECOND TIME, 2-GPC set at OPS 201 (snapshot201, examples/mode-stby-twice.script). Deterministic -- 4 of 4 runs, same log lines. Both votes are FCOS SYNC-FAIL processing, called from FCMISYNC (I/O-completion sync), NOT the application CAM SVC (SVC 25 req 2 never issued, YAGPC_SVCTRACE over 0-200 s). GPC1: FCMSFAIL R0=a0080001 = common-set FTS, HALT/STANDBY ENTRY, fail GPC2 -- the orderly-departure path, and FCMSFAIL lights the CAM on it by design (only TARGET SET DROP suppresses). GPC2: FCMSFAIL R0=80100001 = common-set FTS, NOT a halt/standby entry, fail GPC1 -- GPC2 was already in STBY (FPMZSYNC had zeroed its sync code and ICC transmitter) yet kept executing and ran an I/O sync. THE FIRST EXCURSION CALLS FCMSFAIL ON NEITHER MACHINE: before it the pair did only 110/111 syncs; after the first RUN they also do 001 I/O-complete syncs, so the second STBY lands mid protected I/O, GPC1 shows 010 IPR 0.12 s after GPC2's 000, and both FTS. OPEN: (a) is a STBY GPC supposed to still be executing FCOS I/O syncs (PoO: STANDBY 'IOP response TBD'); (b) why the first excursion never removed GPC2 from GPC1's set at all.
  - evidence: Runs stby-twice-1..4 (before) and stby-twice-fix1 (after #179's fix). Trace: GPC2 out 000 t=1947.345356, GPC1 out 010 IPR 1947.467095, then FCMSFAIL entered on GPC1 (r7=8fab->18fab) and GPC2 (r7=8f4f->18f4f), both in FCMISYNC 18e94-18fbe; each starts MSC program FCMSFCAM (1cb2e) whose @SFD at 1cb34 sets the fail discrete (GPC1 mask 08, GPC2 mask 01). CSECT addresses from PFS latest.unlinkG2.results/signature.json matched against snapshot201/gpc2.mem.bin. NOT THE SAME DEFECT AS #179: with 4f0c47516 in place the excursion still votes mutually, identical FCMSFAIL arguments (r0=a0080001 on GPC1, 80100001 on GPC2) and the same MSC address -- so the stale sync-code copy was not what caused this, and #179 is ruled out as an explanation.  ANSWERED 2026-09-28: FLIGHT-SOFTWARE DESIGN, NOT TIMING AND NOT THE EMULATOR. Two computers to OPS 201 from IPL (no restore), build fc482e01a. Run s2a keeps the crew rule (examples/2gpc-stby-cycle.script: five stby-one.script excursions, 12 s either side, 60 s after the RUN talkback); run s2b uses this entry's 10 s spacing. BOTH give the same result: the mutual votes (cam 21 and 12) arrive on the FIRST excursion -- GPC2 FCMSFAIL r0=00100001 from FCMSSYNC (redundant-set FTS naming GPC1), GPC1 r0=a0080001 from FCMISYNC (common-set, halt/standby entry, GPC2) -- and every later excursion repeats the pair (GPC2 now 80100001, common set) with the lamps already lit; every rejoin clean (five in s2a, iccsyt landmark each time). FCMSFAIL's own comment explains the departing computer's vote: 'FOR 2-GPC CS, DON'T KNOW WHICH GPC IS BAD'; one vote each fails nobody. In a four-computer set the departing computer instead finds itself alone and lights only its own diagonal (runs cyc1, cyc2; #175). The earlier observation that the first excursion was clean came from a RESTORED vehicle (snapshot201), whose restores are unreliable (#180).
  - addrs `1948c,18e94-18fbe,1cb2e-1cb3b,1cb3c-1cb45,1b46c-1b47e` &middot; csects `FCMSFAIL,FCMISYNC,FCMSFCAM,FCMSVOTE,FCMSVC,FPMZSYNC,FIOCMPLT,FIOERRLC` &middot; instrs `@SFD,@RFD` &middot; procs `MSC,CPU,ICC` &middot; config `G2` &middot; tape `v44` &middot; env `YAGPC_SYNCTRACE,YAGPC_LANDMARKS,YAGPC_SVCTRACE` &middot; files `src/iop_msc_instr.c,src/run.c  discretePanel/examples/2gpc-stby-cycle.script` &middot; symptom `mutual fail votes 12 21,CAM,STBY,standby,IPR,fail to sync,second excursion` &middot; run `stby-twice-1,stby-twice-2,stby-twice-3,stby-twice-4  s2a,s2b`
- **#182** *(iop)* A BCE WHOSE MIA TRANSMITTER IS DISABLED STILL PUTS DATA WORDS ON THE BUS. exec_CMD gates the command word on regXmitEna, but exec_TDS, exec_TDLI, exec_MOUT and exec_MOUT_at queue their DMA reads ungated, and iop.c's DMA service calls mia_xmit_word with no check -- so only the command is withheld and the data streams out anyway. The BCE Principles of Operation (IBM-6246556A part 3) says the opposite twice: #MOUT 'if it finds either its transmitter disabled or its MIA busy at a time when it should be idle, the BCE will terminate the #MOUT, set its Program Exception Bit to 0 ... and enter the Wait State'; and 'A BCE in Listen Mode ... will not transmit either commands or data. The instructions #CMDI, #CMD, #MOUT, #TDS, #TDLI, #TDL ... [are] error terminat[ed] ... A BCE in Listen Mode should never be told to transmit data.' SEEN AS: after GPC/CRT 2 2 EXEC in OPS 0, GPC1 drops BCE7 and GPC2 never enables it, yet CRT2 keeps receiving 258-halfword fills and shows GPC2's content. DO NOT GATE THE DATA WITHOUT FIRST FINDING WHY THE NEW COMMANDER NEVER ENABLES ITS TRANSMITTER: gating alone would leave CRT2 with no commander at all, which is POLL FAIL -- what the owner saw after the same entry in OPS 201.
  - evidence: YAGPC_XMITENA_TRACE, run gpccrt: t=42.0 GPC1 holds buses 1,6,7,8,10-24; t=43.3 GPC2 holds 2,18,19,24 only; t=103.3 (the GPC/CRT entry) GPC1 drops bus 7; no GPC enables it afterwards. meds2.log shows IDP2 still taking fills to the end of the run, and panel.log shows CRT2 reading '0001/ / GPC MEMORY 2'. Only one code path writes regXmitEna (iop.c:2264, the MIA TRANSMITTER ENABLE/DISABLE PCO), so the trace is complete.
  - csects `FCMBMASK,FCMBUSCM` &middot; instrs `#MOUT,#TDS,#TDLI,#CMD` &middot; procs `BCE6,BCE7` &middot; env `YAGPC_XMITENA_TRACE` &middot; files `src/iop_bce_instr.c,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `listener transmits,dual commanders,POLL FAIL after GPC/CRT,display driven by a GPC with its transmitter disabled` &middot; doc `IBM-6246556A part 3, BCE PoO` &middot; run `gpccrt`
- **#187** *(method)* FAIL VOTES IN THE LAST SECONDS OF A RUN ARE THE SHUTDOWN, NOT A FAILURE.  simulatePASS stops the emulator with SIGINT and the machine threads die one at a time, so the survivors correctly vote the first one out and cam.py ends with lit lamps -- '44 34 14 24 ON' in a 4-GPC run, '21 12 ON' in a 2-GPC one.  Nothing is wrong with the vehicle.  Before this was understood, a clean run read as a failed one because cam.py's log carried NO TIME and the last four lines looked identical to a mid-run collapse.  cam.py now stamps every line ('voting: 12  ON (bus)  [t=330.5]', seconds since cam start, appended at the END so syncscore.py's voting:\\s+(\\d)(\\d) still matches).  RULE: read the stamp before treating a lit lamp as evidence -- compare it with the last CMD shared= in yaGPC2.log; if they agree within a second or two it is teardown.
  - evidence: Run 4gpc3 (4 GPCs, 2 CRTs, corrected script, stamped cam.py): votes 44/34/14/24 all at cam t=668.1, and the last command words from the machines still running are at shared t=668.794 (bus 6 gpc1, bus 7 gpc2) -- 0.7 s apart on two independent clocks, at the instant the run stops.  No lamp lit at any earlier point in a 700 s run.  Run 4gpc2, same script unstamped, ends the same way with the same four votes; run fixed (2 GPCs) ends with 21 and 12 while both machines commanded their buses to t=775.3 of a 780 s run.
  - procs `CPU` &middot; env `YAGPC_CMDTRACE` &middot; files `discretePanel/cam.py` &middot; symptom `fail vote at end of run,cam lamps lit at shutdown,GPC4 voted out,mutual fail vote 21 12,voted out at teardown` &middot; run `4gpc3,4gpc2,fixed`
- **#188** *(method)* A SCRIPT WITH 'wait user' CANNOT BE RUN UNATTENDED, AND FAILS SILENTLY WHEN IT IS.  examples/4gpc-startup-subtitled.script begins with 'wait user', which holds until someone CLICKS IN THE PANEL O6 WINDOW (crewscript.py Player._run, kind wait_user).  Started from a script-driven test with no one there, the whole run sits in HALT for its full duration and produces a log with four 'MODE: HALT' lines, no IPL, no commands and NO ERROR -- it looks like a vehicle that refused to start rather than a script waiting for a mouse.  The only notice is one line on simulatePASS's console, 'note: the script has a wait user', which goes to the console and not to any log that gets scored.  To run such a script unattended, copy it without that line: grep -v '^wait user$' examples/NAME.script > examples/.NAME-nowait.script.  THE COPY MUST LIVE IN examples/ -- nested 'script ...' lines resolve relative to the PARENT SCRIPT, so a copy in a scratch directory cannot find ipl-one-gpc.script.
  - evidence: Run 4gpc (4 GPCs, 2 CRTs, 620 s): all four GPCs held 'MODE: HALT; CPU held in reset' from start to finish, yaGPC2.log 4 KB with 0 command words, keyboard1.log and keyboard2.log 0 bytes, cam matrix all zeros, exit code 0.  The same script through a .4gpc-nowait.script copy (runs 4gpc2 and 4gpc3) IPLs all four and reaches OPS 201.
  - files `discretePanel/examples/4gpc-startup-subtitled.script,discretePanel/crewscript.py,discretePanel/simulatePASS.py` &middot; symptom `all GPCs stay in HALT,script does nothing,no IPL,empty keyboard log,run produces no commands` &middot; run `4gpc,4gpc2,4gpc3`
- **#189** *(flight-sw)* THE NBAT ENTRIES ARE NOT NO-OPS, BUT THE FIRST FEW LOOK LIKE IT.  Watching the databus setup it appears that nothing changes and that GPC2's buses are configured exactly as GPC1's already were.  What actually happens: the table is EMPTY before any entry (CONFIG 0(0), every bus item 0), and 'ITEM 1 + 2 EXEC' -- selecting memory configuration 2 -- FILLS IT FROM THE GRT.  The GRT's MC 2 target set is C000, GPCs {1,2} and no others (#92), so the defaults alternate the odd buses to GPC1 and the even ones to GPC2: STRING 1=1 2=2 3=1 4=2, CRT 1=1 2=2, MM 1=1 2=2.  The next keystrokes retype exactly that, which is why the display does not move.  THE ENTRIES THAT DO WORK ARE THE LATER ONES: ITEM 4 + 3 and ITEM 5 + 4 add GPC3 and GPC4 to a target set that defaults to two computers, and ITEM 9 + 3, ITEM 10 + 4 and ITEM 11 + 1 move STRING 3, STRING 4 and PL 1/2 off their two-GPC defaults.  Keep the whole block: on a re-IPL of a set whose buses have drifted, the entries that are redundant here are the ones doing the work.  AND THE SECOND HALF OF THE CONFUSION: an entry typed on CRT2 changes the NBAT on BOTH displays.  Crew keyboard input to a redundant set is a COMMON-SET INPUT -- every GPC in the set processes it and updates its own copy -- so GPC MEMORY 1 and GPC MEMORY 2 move together, CRT1 trailing CRT2 by about a second.  Looking back at CRT1 after typing on CRT2 finds it already agreeing, which reads as 'nothing changed' when in fact both changed.
  - evidence: Run nbat (4 GPCs, 2 CRTs, NSTS_ANNOUNCE_ROWS=all, every frame captured off the screen-announce port base+91, 637 frames).  CRT2, GPC MEMORY 2: t=372.9 '1 CONFIG 0( 0)', GPC slots blank, STRING/CRT/MM all 0.  t=384.4 after ITEM 1 + 2: '1 CONFIG 2(G2)', 'GPC 1 2 _ 0 0', STRING 1=1 2=2 3=1 4=2, CRT 1=1 2=2, MM 1=1 2=2 -- 22 lines changed in one keystroke.  EACH ENTRY LANDS ON ITS OWN FRAME, about 1.9 s apart, with no lag and no batching: t=402.1 'GPC 1 2 3 0 0' (ITEM 4 + 3), t=408.4 'GPC 1 2 3 4 0' (ITEM 5 + 4), t=426.1 STRING 3 1->3 (ITEM 9 + 3), t=433.3 STRING 4 2->4 (ITEM 10 + 4), t=440.1 PL 1/2 0->1 (ITEM 11 + 1).  A first pass here reported the last three as one batched change at t=440.1; that was a FILTER ARTEFACT -- the continuation rows render as '3 9 1 ...' and '4 10 2 ...' with no keyword in them, so a dedupe keyed on CONFIG|STRING|GPC|CRT|PL|MM could not see them change.  Read these frames unfiltered.  Final table before the OPS transition: CONFIG 2(G2), GPC 1 2 3 4 0, STRING 1=1 2=2 3=3 4=4, PL 1/2=1.  NOTE FOR ANYONE REPEATING THIS: the scratchpad is NOT in the announced frame (MEDS draws it from its own geometry), so keystrokes cannot be located that way -- find them by the table changing.  Same five changes on CRT1, 0.5-1.5 s later: 403.6, 409.3, 426.6, 434.3, 440.0 against CRT2's 402.1, 408.4, 426.1, 433.3, 440.1.
  - csects `CZ2COMMO,DM6OPS` &middot; procs `DK2` &middot; env `NSTS_ANNOUNCE_ROWS` &middot; files `discretePanel/MEDS2.py,discretePanel/examples/4gpc-startup-subtitled.script` &middot; symptom `NBAT does nothing,ITEM entries change nothing,GPC2 buses same as GPC1,databus setup no-op,MEM/BUS CONFIG unchanged` &middot; tsec `372-440` &middot; doc `TD115 Rev B sec 5 pp.5-3..5-4; GRT in CZ2COMMO.hal:434` &middot; run `nbat`
- **#192** *(method)* THE INSTRUMENT SUPPRESSES #190, SO CHOOSE ONE THAT WRITES NOTHING.  The 4-GPC 3-CRT vote fails 2 runs in 6 untraced (3 in 7 with the first), and NOT ONCE in eight runs carrying a trace that writes per event: one run with full YAGPC_SYNCTRACE (2,397,750 lines) and six with YAGPC_SYNCORDER (0 of 6) (about 616,000 lines a run, ~1000 a second) were all clean.  Six clean in a row is p=0.047 against the measured rate, and with the sync-trace run p=0.028.  The conclusion for anyone investigating this: an instrument that performs I/O per sync event changes the timing enough that the race stops happening, and the evidence it was built to collect is never produced.  USE THE RING: YAGPC_SYNC_HISTORY=N records the same events at a few stores each with no syscalls and prints them only when a computer reaches FCMSFAIL, which is the one moment they are wanted.  ALSO NOTE what is NOT a useful metric here: 'silence' on the sync lines.  In a healthy run each computer already has about 29,000 gaps longer than the 3.85 ms sync timeout and maxima of tens of seconds, because codes change in bursts during a handshake and then go quiet for as long as nothing is happening.  The timeout applies to an EXPECTED transition, not to any gap.
  - evidence: Rate with light instrumentation only (stamped cam + landmark 1948c): runs rate-1..6, 2 of 6 lost a computer, plus g3crt3 = 3 of 7.  With per-event tracing: g3sync (YAGPC_SYNCTRACE) clean; hist-1..5 (YAGPC_SYNC_HISTORY + YAGPC_SYNCORDER) all clean, 616,168 / 616,720 SYNCORDER lines in the first two.  Healthy-run gap census from hist-1, shared microseconds between one machine's successive codes: median 283.3 for every computer, p99 154414/153827/32920/10485, max 42.0/95.0/148.1/201.4 SECONDS, and 29693/29361/28690/28027 gaps over 3.85 ms -- the long ones span the phases before each machine IPLs.
  - env `YAGPC_SYNC_HISTORY,YAGPC_SYNCORDER,YAGPC_SYNCTRACE,YAGPC_LANDMARKS` &middot; files `src/discretes.c` &middot; symptom `heisenbug,trace hides the failure,race disappears under instrumentation` &middot; run `g3sync,hist-1,hist-2,hist-3,hist-4,hist-5,hist-6,rate-1,rate-6`
- **#203** *(flight-sw)* A GPC/CRT ASSIGNMENT DOES NOT SURVIVE A MAJOR-FUNCTION CHANGE.  Moving an IDP's MAJ FUNC switch re-evaluates that display against the NEW major function's NBAT and discards whatever GPC/CRT assigned by hand, so the display falls again to the lowest-numbered GPC in the new major function.  Not a defect -- it is USA005350's rule working -- but it decides the ORDER of any script that gives a display to a computer outside the redundant set: the GPC/CRT must come after the last MAJ FUNC change as well as after the OPS transition.
  - evidence: Measured on a running vehicle 2026-09-22 (run acid), four GPCs in a GNC OPS 1 set with GPC5 outside it.  CRT3 had been given to GPC5 with GPC/CRT 5 3 EXEC while IDP3's MAJ FUNC was SM.  Flipping IDP3 to PL handed CRT3 straight back to GPC1: the emulator's display-bus guard logged 'GPC1 now transmits on 1 display bus(es)' followed by 'GPC1 transmits on 2 display buses at once', and the owner confirmed the digit on the screen.  Re-keying GPC/CRT 5 3 EXEC returned it to GPC5 and GPC1 dropped back to one bus.  WHY PL BEHAVES THAT WAY: CZ2V_NOM_BUS row 6 (memory configuration 6, P9) is 0,0,0,0,0,2,2,0,2,0,0,2,2, whose 8th slot -- CRT3 -- is 0, and every common-set computer is in OPS 0 for PL, so GPC1 wins 'the lowest numbered GPC in that major function' (ledger #181).  ALSO ESTABLISHED IN THE SAME RUN: a GPC runs up to three major functions at once and each display shows whichever one its own MAJ FUNC switch selects -- GPC1 was seen driving CRT1 on GNC OPS 101 (1011 LAUNCH TRAJ 1) and CRT3 on SM OPS 0 (0001 GPC MEMORY) simultaneously, which is why one computer appeared to show two different screens.  A GPC in OPS 0 drives an SM display and a PL display equally well.
  - procs `DK6,DK7,DK8` &middot; symptom `GPC/CRT lost,MAJ FUNC change,display reassigned,wrong GPC driving CRT,two screens one GPC` &middot; doc `USA005350 Rev B DK Assignment; CZ2COMMO.hal CZ2V_NOM_BUS` &middot; run `acid`
- **#209** *(tape)* The DEU load module that PASS's own display loader reads is on NO volume we have.  AIG_DEU_LOADER reads 17 blocks -- 8704 halfwords, its AIGV_READ.DWDCT -- from CZ1V_MM_ADDR_TBL entry 5, and CZ1COM.hal declares that table INITIAL(9216,6144,858,8870,9223,9352,...), so entry 5 is block 9223, which is 4/4/0/7.  Neither our OI340700 build nor pass-910 has a block there.  The read therefore returns zeros -- mmumodel's do_read gives an absent block back as zeros and says nothing -- the read-with-check fails, and the loader retries for ever: 110 reads of that address in one 300 s run, never a format fill after any of them.  SEPARATELY, our tape build drops three regions pass-910 carries in the same neighbourhood: 9216 (table entry 1), 9339 (entry 12) and 9465 (entry 13), plus 9187 and 9440-9447.  Only entry 6, 9352, is present in both, and that is the critical formats -- DMACDFT1/2/3, three 8-block copies of SYS5, which is what GPCIPL actually loads a display from and why displays get their backgrounds at all.
  - evidence: Read straight out of the volumes, 2026-09-24.  OI340700-v44boot.mmv has 2,665 blocks; file 4 holds exactly three runs -- 8800-8854, 8960-9186 and 9352-9439 -- and CF_PAD 111e appears only in 9352-9375, which is three 8-block copies.  pass-910.mmv has 3,004 blocks and adds 9187, 9216, 9339, 9440-9447 and 9465, but still nothing at 9223.  The two addresses GPCIPL itself reads during an IPL, 9320 (17 blocks) and 9240 (8 blocks), are absent from BOTH volumes, so GPCIPL loads a display unit's control program out of zeros on every tape we own; it goes unnoticed because nothing here emulates the display unit's own processor, and the backgrounds come from 9352, which is real.  Traced from the MMU trace of a run in which a display asked to be loaded: position to 4/4/0, read 17 blocks from 4/4/0/7, 86 times, with no fill between them.  CONSEQUENCE: PASS's own DEU loader cannot complete on any volume we have, so a display powered up after the IPLs can only be loaded by IPLing a GPC onto it, which is what the scripts do.  CORRECTION, and it matters: THE 9223 IMAGE IS NOT PART FORMATS.  AIGDEU loads a display in two phases from two places.  Phase 1 is the DISPLAY CONTROL PROGRAM, read from entry 5 (9223) and sent as the eight fills AIGB_DEU_FILL_LOC names -- 0F49, 1145, 1341, 153D, 1739, 1935, 1FE4, 0002 -- which is the unit's own executable firmware, the background-top word and low memory; it then waits 300 ms and polls, requiring the unit to report INITIALIZATION PERFORMED with a good checksum, i.e. evidence that it RAN the new code.  Phase 2 is the formats, AIGB_DEU_DFT_LOC 0100, 02FC, 04F8, 06F4, 08F0, 0AEC, 0CE8, 0EE4, seven fills of 508 and one of 101, read from entry 6 (9352) -- which we have, and which is what draws a background.  So the missing 9223 image is ENTIRELY firmware we do not have and could not run; the backgrounds were never the gap.  What that means in practice is the opposite of discouraging: nothing here executes a display unit's processor, so the content at 9223 need not function, only exist and pass the read's check -- zeros would serve, with the model answering the verification poll.  That is a deliberate choice to put content of our own invention on a tape, not a recovery, and it is recorded here as a choice rather than taken.  DO NOT invent one without deciding that deliberately -- it would be tape content with no provenance.  WHY THE GPC REJECTS IT IS NOT THE BUS AND NOT A CHECKSUM WE CAN NAME. Measured: with YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE over the failing window there is NO receive timeout on the mass-memory BCE and no MMU fault of any kind -- the 8704 words are delivered cleanly, they are simply all zero, because an absent block reads back as zeros.  The rejection is in the flight software's own read service: AIGDEU's private data area, #DAIGDEU at 0444e-04721, comes back from a capture taken while it was failing with 9223 at 0449a and 8704 at 04497 -- its DBLOCK and DWDCT, which is what fixes the identification -- and a 9 beside them, which is AIGDEU's code for MMU1 I/O ERROR ON DCP READ, the one it sets when CDHV_RW_TSW is non-zero.  No fill is ever issued, so the failure is at or before that test and not in the fills (code 3).  What the check actually tests is unknown: MMURRDCK is a macro, the service that sets CDHV_RW_TSW is FCOS assembler, and CDHV_RW_TSW is declared in a COMPOOL template we do not hold -- only AIGDEU.hal mentions it in the whole source tree.  THE CONSEQUENCE FOR ANY SYNTHETIC IMAGE: all-zero content is REJECTED, measured, so a made-up image would have to satisfy whatever that check wants -- probably a block header or a checksum convention -- and that is not known yet.  'Write zeros there' is not a fix.  AND THE MISSING BLOCK TURNS OUT NOT TO BE FATAL, which corrects this entry's own conclusion.  The read of 9223 is ACCEPTED: measured in the SM common buffer itself, #PCVNMMU in the post-IPL configuration at 197410, whose CDHV_RW_BUFR header (CSMCOM.hal: DUMMY, DGO_ADJ(3), RW_TSW, RWCT, ERRCD, then the data) reads RW_TSW = 0000 0000 while the loader runs, and whose data area holds EXACTLY 8704 zeros followed by other content -- the read landing precisely where and as asked, all zero because the block is absent.  So the flight software does not reject zeros; it loads them.  Nothing here executes a display unit's control program, so a zeroed one costs nothing and the load completes (see #208).  WHAT REMAINS TRUE: our tapes carry no display control program at 9223, and none at the 9320 and 9240 GPCIPL reads either, so every display in this emulator runs on a control program of zeros.  That is a real gap against the flight software's own address table and worth closing if the image is ever recovered, but it is not what kept a display from being loaded, and no synthetic image is needed to make the loader work.
  - csects `AIGDEU,DEUCFLM,DMACDFT1,CZ1V_MM_ADDR_TBL` &middot; procs `IDP1,IDP3,MM1` &middot; tape `v44,pass-910` &middot; files `src/mmumodel.c,tapebuild/build.sh,tools/build_deucflm.py` &middot; symptom `no background,DEU load module,9223,block absent,read returns zeros,loader retries,MMURRDCK,AIG_DEU_LOADER` &middot; doc `CZ1COM.hal 113600-113800 (the address table); AIGDEU.hal 009100-009400`
- **#217** *(emulator)* OUR BCE RECLAIMS THE BUS BEFORE A REPLY COULD PHYSICALLY HAVE PASSED, for half of all 21-word reads. A MIA bus word takes about 33 us (1 Mbps, 28-bit words), which is what mtumodel's wire pacing charges, so a 21-word reply occupies the wire for 693 us. Measured on a five-computer vehicle, the median interval between one command and the next on the same bus, for a receive armed for 21 words, is 561 us -- and 50.0% of those intervals are shorter than the reply needs. A real bus could not do that: the commander would still be receiving. Our BCE advances its chain without accounting for the transfer's duration, so a paced model cannot deliver the reply in full and the receive is cut off by the next command's sync word -- Table 1.2's Sync Error.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26 from discretePanel/listener2 (five computers, whole ascent, YAGPC_FC_MDM=1, sensors OFF, YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE=20,22), by walking each computer's word stream on each bus and timing one command sync to the next, 27,122 intervals. By the length the receive was armed for: 1 word 0.1% too short, 3/4/6 words 0.0%, 7 words 4.9%, 21 WORDS 50.0% (median gap 561 us against 693 us needed), 32 words 0.0% (median gap 159 ms) and 36 words 0.0% (median 15 ms). So this is NOT a general 'long replies cannot be delivered' -- the 32- and 36-word reads have ample wire time in this run, and an earlier statement of this entry that implied otherwise was wrong. It is specific and it is worst at 21 words, which the bus survey gives as FIOFFIC1, X'005082C5', in FIOMFEPG. NOTE THE MTU'S OWN READ IS SEVEN WORDS and 4.9% of its intervals are too short, so the timing unit is mildly exposed to the same defect. WHAT THIS DOES NOT EXPLAIN: the left=32 listener failures seen with YAGPC_FC_SENSORS on (#216, FIOMFEG9 FIOBY27L/28L/29L) came from a different run with different traffic and cannot be attributed to this from the sensors-off data measured here. THE FIX DIRECTION, untested: either the BCE should not issue the next command until the previous transfer's wire time has elapsed, which is what the hardware does, or the pacing rate is wrong -- and the first is the physically correct one.  THE OBVIOUS FIX IS REFUTED -- DO NOT RETRY.  The wire hold already exists (YAGPC_WIRE_HOLD_BUSES, src/iop.c): the words go at once and in order and the BCE's NEXT instruction is held until they would have cleared the wire at 33 us each, which is exactly what this entry says the hardware does.  The router turns it on for buses 6-9 by default (run.c, 0xf<<6) and #137 records it GOOD there and WORSE on the ICC buses 1-5.  It had never been tried on the flight-critical buses.  Tried 2026-09-26 on the configuration that keeps the timing unit alive -- five computers, YAGPC_FC_MDM=1, YAGPC_FC_SENSORS=1, YAGPC_WIRE_HOLD_BUSES=6,7,8,9,14,15,16,17,20,21,22,23 (the env var REPLACES the router's default, so 6-9 were kept explicitly), captured at 945 s (discretePanel/hold): IT IS WORSE ON BOTH COUNTS.  The timing unit goes from all six slots live on GPC2-GPC5 to ALL SIX BYPASSED on GPC1, GPC2 and GPC3; only GPC4, just voted out, and GPC5, never in the set, keep theirs.  The set still breaks: four votes against GPC4 at t=824.2.  Traffic rises from 225,655 commands to 799,742, which is more failure and more retry.  So the MEASUREMENT stands -- half of all 21-word reads do have the bus reclaimed before the reply could pass -- but holding the BCE is not the remedy, and the remedy is not known.
  - csects `FIOFFIC1,FIOMFEPG,FIOPRMPG` &middot; procs `BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17,BCE20,BCE21,BCE22,BCE23` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE,YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_BUS_WORD_US` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/iop.c,src/iop_bce_instr.c` &middot; symptom `sync error,command sync mid-receive,wire reclaimed,transfer duration,left=21` &middot; doc `MIA 1 Mbps 28-bit word ~33 us; BCE PoO 4.1` &middot; run `listener2`
- **#219** *(emulator)* LISTENERS ON BUSES 14-17 WERE NEVER MARKED FOR LISTEN MODE, so they never waited for their command and were served once every 40 MILLISECONDS instead of once every 33 microseconds. run.c set the Listen-Mode mask to 0x3e | (0xf<<6) | (0x1f<<18) -- the ICC buses 1-5, the display units 6-9, the mass memory 18-19 and the timing unit 20-22 -- and its comment still said 'timing unit (20-22)'. The timing-unit model's range was extended to 14-23 when it took on the forward and aft MDMs, and THE MASK WAS NOT EXTENDED WITH IT. iop_bce_receive sets bce->recvAwaitCmd only where the bus is marked, so a listening BCE on 14-17 began timing out data the moment it armed, hit the 2 ms message time-out before its command ever arrived, was error-terminated and retried -- which is ledger #131-#133 exactly, the same defect that was found and fixed on the intercomputer buses. THE MODEL ECHOES ON THOSE BUSES IN THE DEFAULT BUILD TOO (with the answering off its echo condition count==0 is always true there), so this is not specific to YAGPC_FC_MDM.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26 on three five-computer runs that differ in nothing but the fix, all with YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE=14,15,16,17 (discretePanel/fa, /carry, /mask). Listener words taken on those buses 156, 190, then 3,671; median interval between one word and the next 40,078 us, 39,979 us, then 33.0 us -- which is MTU_BUS_WORD_US, the wire rate, and is what listeners on buses 20/22 already achieved (median 33.0 us, measured the same way). Receives with recvAwaitCmd set: 0 of 156, 0 of 190, then 1,707 of 3,671 -- Listen Mode honoured for the first time on those buses. Data words delivered to listeners 0, 36, then 2,057. Error terminations 186, 186, 162. THE FIX derives the mask from the model's own MTU_BUS_FIRST/MTU_BUS_LAST (mtumodel_bus_mask) rather than writing the range again in run.c, because writing it twice is what let the two drift apart. WHAT IT DOES NOT DO: the timing unit is still bypassed on all four set members and that run took four votes -- 162 errors is still far above FIOERRLC's threshold of two, so the string is commfaulted anyway by the devices this vehicle does not model (#216).  AND IT CANNOT BE SHIPPED ON BY DEFAULT.  The default-configuration gate with the mask extended unconditionally (five computers, whole acid test, 2400 s, YAGPC_FC_MDM unset; discretePanel/gate4) took FOUR VOTES at t=821.4, all four peers against GPC1, where the same gate with 20-22 alone has ZERO (gate2 and gate3) -- and bus traffic fell from about 11,000,000 commands to 3,160,776, so it is a real change to that configuration and not the intermittency of #215 (whose one occurrence was at t=1914 against GPC2, much later and after everything had settled).  The extension therefore travels with YAGPC_FC_MDM, like the echo rule, the wire pacing, the shared clock and the backlog.  The default mask is now PROVABLY unchanged: 0x3e | (0xf<<6) | (0x3<<18) | buses 20-22 is 0x7c03fe, which is bit for bit the 0x3e | (0xf<<6) | (0x1f<<18) it replaces; with the answering on it becomes 0xffc3fe, adding 14, 15, 16, 17 and 23.  A CONTRADICTION THIS LEAVES, and it is the next thing to try: with the answering OFF the model still ECHOES a commander's command on 14-17, because its echo condition there (count == 0) is always true -- so it marks command sync on buses whose listeners are told not to expect one.  Making the ECHO match the mask, rather than the mask match the echo, is the other way to resolve it and has not been measured.
  - procs `BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17,BCE20,BCE21,BCE22,BCE23` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE,YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/mtumodel.c,src/mtumodel.h,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `listener starved,40 ms between words,await=0,no data word,message time-out,retried` &middot; doc `BCE PoO 4.1 Listen Mode; ledger #131-#133, the same defect on the ICC buses` &middot; run `fa,carry,mask`
- **#220** *(emulator)* A DEVICE AT IUA 13 ON BUS 24 IS READ FOURTEEN THOUSAND TIMES A RUN AND NEVER ANSWERS, and nothing has ever looked at it. The command is 6d3a60 -- iua 13, function 29d, ONE word, issued by the commanding BCE -- and it accounts for 13,999 of the 14,078 unanswered receives in a five-computer ascent. Bus 24 is not one of the eight flight-critical buses, so every count this ledger has quoted for 'unanswered transfers' has been silently excluding it.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26 with YAGPC_FC_LEARN (which prints FCLEN lines, not LEARN -- worth knowing, a grep for the flag's name finds nothing) on a five-computer ascent with YAGPC_FC_MDM=1 and YAGPC_FC_SENSORS=1 (discretePanel/learn2).  14,078 unanswered receives in total, by bus: 24:14001, 14:25, 15:16, 16:16, 17:8, 12:5, 10:2, 11:2, 20:2, 8:1.  By IUA: 13:14001, 0:44, 12:12, 17:7, 10:5, 23:2, 24:2, 6:2, 18:1, 9:1, 15:1.  So the flight-critical buses are down to about SIXTY-FIVE unanswered receives in a whole run, from the 437 this work started with, and the overwhelming remainder is one device on a bus outside that set.  WHAT THE FLIGHT-CRITICAL REMAINDER IS: 44 of the 65 are LISTENER receives reporting cmd=000000 -- both bce->recvCmd and the per-computer lastBusCmd are zero, which for a listener means it never took a command sync, because the command was issued by a PEER and lastBusCmd only records what this computer sent.  Those are FIOMFEG9's FIOBY27L/28L/29L (32 words, 20 of them) and FIOHFE89's FIOBYLR family (1 word, 24 of them).  The named commander-side ones are few: 88401f at iua 17, 32 words, x5; 631c20 (FIOMDMRT) at iua 12, x8; 532aaa and 632aaa at iua 10 and 12, x4 each; b8401f at iua 23 and c0401f at iua 24, x2 each; 884005 at iua 17, 6 words, x2.
  - procs `BCE24,BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_FC_LEARN,YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS` &middot; files `src/iop.c,src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `unanswered receive,never answers,iua 13,bus 24,14000 a run` &middot; doc `FCLEN output of YAGPC_FC_LEARN` &middot; run `learn2`
- **#223** *(emulator)* THE LENGTH OF A READ DOES NOT EXIST WHEN THE COMMAND GOES OUT, AND DOES WHEN THE READER POLLS -- which disproves the note src/run.c is built on ('a bus program arms its receive and then issues the command, so the count is in the BCE by the time the model sees it'). A BCE finishes receive N, issues command N+1, and only THEN arms receive N+1, so at XMIT time the commanding BCE has nothing armed. Measured directly, and the margin is not small: sizing each answer when its reader first POLLS takes 63,267 answers from a real BCE against 199 from the static survey, where sizing at the command takes them all from the survey or not at all. SIZING LAZILY IS STILL A NET LOSS, though, because it breaks the one-transfer backlog (#218): mtu_carry_take runs at COMMAND time and moves what a reader still wants into its backlog, but under lazy sizing mdm is still zero then -- the reader has not polled -- so there is nothing to carry and a listener that polls after the next command loses the answer outright instead of receiving it late.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26 on the five-computer ascent, YAGPC_FC_MDM=1 YAGPC_FC_SENSORS=1 YAGPC_FC_LEARN=1, against the same fixture unchanged (discretePanel/learn2 against discretePanel/lazy).  THE SIZING ITSELF WORKS: 'mtu: 63267 answer(s) sized from the reader's own BCE, 199 from the bus-program survey' -- 99.7% from a real armed count, so the count really is there at poll time and really is not there at command time.  run.c hands the model the POLLING computer's count before every service call, not just the commander's, so each reader is also sized from its own BCE, which is more correct than giving every reader the commander's number.  THE OUTCOME IS WORSE: 116 error terminations on buses 14-17 against 65, 23 distinct failing elements against 15, 10 held by a single computer against 8, 116 unanswered receives against 67 -- and 82 of those 116 are listeners (cmd=000000).  That is the backlog interaction, and it is the tension to resolve: the carry needs a LENGTH at command time and the length only exists per-reader at poll time.  A three-line candidate not yet tried: have mtu_carry_take size an unsized pending answer from the survey fallback, so the backlog has something to move even when the reader has not polled.  DIAGNOSTIC: YAGPC_ARMED_TRACE=<seconds> prints why iop_bce_armed_words has nothing to give, from that time onward.  It takes a START TIME because a flat budget is spent entirely at t=42 during the IPL, where recvActive=0 is expected and says nothing -- which is what a first attempt measured and nearly reported.
  - procs `BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17,BCE20,BCE21,BCE22` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_ARMED_TRACE,YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS,YAGPC_FC_LEARN` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/iop.c,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `armed count -1 at command,available at poll,backlog starved,listener loses answer` &middot; doc `run.c's comment at mtumodel_set_armed_words, now disproven` &middot; run `learn2,lazy`
- **#225** *(sync)* FIOGPCWE REACHES 1 -- THE SELF-FAIL CONDITION -- 328 TIMES IN A RUN, AND ALL FOUR COMPUTERS REACH IT AT THE SAME INSTANT. Read out of PASS's own memory rather than inferred: FIOGPCWE is a halfword at 08d83 (FIOCBLKS+445, the same address in G9 and SSW), and watching it with YAGPC_WATCHHW=8d83-8d86 records the number FIOERRLC and FIOCMPLT actually compute at every decision, stamped with computer, NIA and time. Over one five-computer ascent the values written are 0 x1976, 1 x328, 2 x276, 3 x248 and 4 x1644 -- so the commfault path (more than one) dominates, but the SELF-FAIL path is entered 328 times, and the FIOGPCWE==1 stores cluster at panel t=818.6 to 818.7 with GPC1, GPC2, GPC3 and GPC4 all present. The store is at nia 1b762 = FIOCMPLT FIOPROT+116.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26 (discretePanel/gpcwe), five computers, YAGPC_FC_MDM=1 YAGPC_FC_SENSORS=1, YAGPC_WATCHHW=8d83-8d86, 6,968 CPU stores parsed of 6,968 seen, times converted to the panel clock with the per-computer reset-release offsets from panel.log.  THIS OVERTURNS THE CONCLUSION IN #216 THAT THE ASYMMETRY IS NOT WHAT VOTES.  That conclusion came from aggregating error terminations over a whole 1000 s run and finding no bus held by exactly one computer -- but FIOGPCWE is evaluated at an INSTANT, and the flaw was flagged at the time and then drawn from anyway.  Read instant by instant out of PASS's own store, the self-fail condition is met, repeatedly.  THE SHAPE SUGGESTS A PROPAGATION RACE rather than a genuine asymmetry: each computer's error reaches its peers through the ICC buffers, so a computer that evaluates FIOERRLC before its peers' bits arrive sees itself alone in error and takes the self-fail path -- which would explain why ALL FOUR reach FIOGPCWE==1 within a tenth of a second of each other while the per-bus aggregate shows every bus held by three or four computers.  NOT YET CONFIRMED: no store of X'8000' to FIOICCFL (08d86), FIOERRLC's 'MASK FOR FORCE F-T-S', was captured in this watch, so the link from FIOGPCWE==1 to FCMSFAIL is inferred from the source and not yet observed.  Watch 08d86 with a wider window, and correlate the FIOGPCWE==1 instants against the ICC buffers' error masks, to settle it.
  - addrs `08d83,08d84,08d86,1b762,1b765` &middot; csects `FIOCMPLT,FIOPROT,FIOERRLC,FCMSFAIL,FIOCBLKS` &middot; procs `BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17,BCE20,BCE21,BCE22,BCE23` &middot; config `G9,SSW` &middot; env `YAGPC_WATCHHW,YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS` &middot; files `src/iop.c,src/iccmodel.c,src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `fail to sync,FIOGPCWE==1,self fail,all four simultaneously,ICC propagation` &middot; tsec `818` &middot; doc `csects-G9: FIOGPCWE at FIOCBLKS+445; FIOCMPLT FIOICCF computes it per bus` &middot; run `gpcwe`
- **#226** *(sync)* EIGHT OF TEN SELF-FAILS COME FROM THE SYNC PATHS, NOT THE I/O ERROR PATH. FCMSFAIL has SIX call sites -- FCMCSYNC, FCMISYNC (twice), FCMSSYNC, FCMTSYNC, FIOCMPLT and FIOERRLC -- and only FIOERRLC's writes X'8000' to FIOICCFL, which is why an instrument watching that address saw nothing while computers were being voted out. Landmarked on FCMSFAIL's entry at 1948c with registers, a five-computer ascent gives TEN entries: FCMSSYNC (FCMSSELP+0) four times, FCMCSYNC (FCMCSMNL+19) four times, FIOERRLC (FIOSSM07+109) twice. FCMSSYNC is the SYNC TIMEOUT path -- its own comment is 'END OF SYNC TIMEOUT PROCESSING'. So the redundant set is lost to SYNC TIMING, and the I/O error path that this whole investigation has been aimed at accounts for two of ten.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26 (discretePanel/whofails), five computers, YAGPC_FC_MDM=1 YAGPC_FC_SENSORS=1, YAGPC_LANDMARKS=1948c:fcmsfail with YAGPC_LANDMARKS_REGS.  The landmark facility's own note says why it works: a routine's entry has its argument in R0 and its RETURN ADDRESS IN R7, so the two say what it was asked to do and who asked.  DECODING R7 TAKES THE PSW LAYOUT, and getting it wrong is easy -- PSW_DESC1 is 'ppppppppppppppppccrvf_usbbbbdddd', so the NIA is the TOP sixteen bits and, when its high bit is set, the address is (BSR << 15) | (nia16 & 0x7FFF) with BSR in bits 24-27.  Trying plain shifts resolves to nothing.  THE PATTERN: GPC1 and GPC2 call FCMSFAIL at the SAME shared instant with IDENTICAL r0 -- shared 717.4 r0=00020001 from FCMSSYNC then r0=80020001 from FCMCSYNC, shared 721.6/721.7 the same pair with r0=00040001 and 80040001, shared 759.0 r0=8bc40000 from FIOERRLC.  Bit 31 of r0 is CSFAIL, 'INDICATE COMMON SET', so each computer fails a SYNC check and then immediately a COMMON-SET check.  Two computers deciding simultaneously that they have failed to sync is not one computer detecting a fault in another.  WHAT THIS EXPLAINS: seven fixes tonight moved I/O error counts and never moved the vote count, because they were all aimed at FIOERRLC.  It also explains #215 -- a vote at t=1914 with ZERO I/O error terminations in the surrounding 1,150 s -- which was recorded as an unexplained anomaly and is simply a sync-path failure, invisible to every instrument pointed at I/O.
  - addrs `1948c,1979c,18c4b,19dc7,08d86` &middot; csects `FCMSFAIL,FCMSSYNC,FCMCSYNC,FCMISYNC,FCMTSYNC,FIOCMPLT,FIOERRLC,FCMSFINT` &middot; procs `CPU` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_LANDMARKS,YAGPC_LANDMARKS_REGS,YAGPC_SYNCTRACE,YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS` &middot; files `src/cpu.c,src/vehicle.c,src/iccmodel.c` &middot; symptom `fail to sync,self fail,sync timeout,common set fail,CSFAIL,two computers simultaneously` &middot; tsec `717-759` &middot; doc `FCMSSYNC 'END OF SYNC TIMEOUT PROCESSING'; FCMTSYNC 'IF-TIMEOUT, THEN CALL FCMSFAIL'; PSW_DESC1 in regmem.c` &middot; run `whofails`
- **#227** *(sync)* CAPPING THE IDLE CATCH-UP LUMP DOES NOT REDUCE THE SHARED-CLOCK SEPARATION, so the wait-state pacer is not what lets a machine overshoot the barrier. rtpacer_advance_idle may advance a waiting machine by up to IDLE_CATCHUP_MAX_NS (5 ms) of simulated time in ONE pass, and the barrier is checked between passes and never inside one -- 200 times the 25 us barrier delta and 27 times FCMST3's 182 us sync-code hold, so on its face one lump could leap a peer's pulse. Reducing the cap a hundredfold changes nothing measurable.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26, five-computer ascent, YAGPC_FC_MDM=1 YAGPC_FC_SENSORS=1 YAGPC_SYNC_HISTORY=8192, identical but for YAGPC_IDLE_CATCHUP_MS.  Separation between GPC1 and GPC2 on the shared clock at the same wall instant, ~15,900 samples each: at the 5 ms default, median 0.027 ms, p90 0.233, p99 0.736, max 2.153; at 0.05 ms, median 0.022, p90 0.225, p99 0.715, max 2.154.  Unchanged.  Self-fails went UP (14 FCMSFAIL entries against 8), which on one run each says only that the cap did not help.  Real-time rate did NOT collapse -- the repayment race that a small cap risks losing was won on this host -- so the cap is not harmful either, merely irrelevant.  THE SEPARATION IS REAL AND MATTERS: p99 0.7 ms and max 2.2 ms are far beyond the windows the flight software holds a sync code for (FCMCTT3 103 us, FCMST3 182 us, FCMIT3 188 us, #190), so a machine that far behind arrives after a pulse has been raised and dropped.  What is now excluded is the idle path as its source; the excursions must happen while a machine is EXECUTING, where this cap never applies.  METHOD NOTE, because it nearly produced a false result: the per-machine offsets must be taken from the sync history itself -- GPC1's 'out <code>' and GPC2's '<- GPC1 <code>' are one event, so the difference of their own sim clocks IS the offset (measured -112.093995 s over 1,671 pairs).  Panel reset-release stamps are rounded to 0.1 s and gave an offset 6 ms wrong, which read as a flat 6.000 ms median separation -- an artefact, not a finding.  THE REFUTATION ABOVE IS OVERTURNED -- IT WAS A FALSE NEGATIVE FROM A BAD INSTRUMENT.  #227 judged the cap by a 'separation' computed from what each machine last happened to LOG, and #228 showed that measure is dominated by logging sparsity rather than by divergence: the per-machine event-gap distribution (median 0.013 ms, p90 0.242) tracks the apparent separation (0.027, 0.233) almost exactly.  It could not see the quantity it was judging, so 'unchanged' meant nothing.  RE-TESTED 2026-09-26 with the barrier's own histogram -- every sample, per machine, no threshold, no cap, buckets summing to 100.0% -- on the same five-computer ascent, one variable: default 5 ms against YAGPC_IDLE_CATCHUP_MS=0.05.  DEFAULT: 701,278,358 samples, <25=75.561% <50=23.916% <100=0.384% <150=0.137%, and 12,664 samples past 200 us (0.00181%), 11 votes.  CAPPED: 725,927,388 samples, <25=69.718% <50=29.632% <100=0.470% <150=0.179%, and 7,917 past 200 us (0.00109%), 4 votes.  Excursions past the 211 us sync hold fall about 40% per sample and votes fall from 11 to 4.  The excursion figure rests on 700 million samples and is robust; the vote counts are one run each and are suggestive rather than settled.  THE ORIGINAL REASONING WAS RIGHT: rtpacer_advance_idle is the one place a single step can carry hundreds of microseconds of simulated time, the barrier is checked between passes and never inside one, and bounding the step bounds the overshoot.  IT IS NOT A COMPLETE FIX -- 7,917 excursions and 4 votes remain -- so something else also carries a machine past the hold.
  - addrs `081f2-08200` &middot; csects `FCMSFAIL,FCMSSYNC,FCMCSYNC,FCMST3,FCMCTT3,FCMIT3` &middot; procs `CPU` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_IDLE_CATCHUP_MS,YAGPC_SYNC_HISTORY,YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS` &middot; files `src/rtpacer.c,src/vehicle.c,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `shared clock separation,missed pulse,barrier overshoot,p99 0.7 ms` &middot; doc `rtpacer.c IDLE_CATCHUP_MAX_NS; #190 T3 hold constants` &middot; run `synchist,catchup`
- **#228** *(sync)* THE VEHICLE'S SIMULATED CLOCKS SIT ABOUT 116-144 MICROSECONDS APART, not the 25 us the barrier's delta implies and not the 0.7-2.2 ms earlier measurements suggested. Read from barPubUs[] -- every machine's time in ONE frame under ONE thread's view, at one instant -- rather than inferred by comparing what each machine last happened to log. On a restored five-computer vehicle: median 116.4 us, p90 130.3, p99 139.5, max 143.9, reported about equally by all five machines (779 to 839 each), so it is a property of the vehicle and not one straggler. THE BARRIER IS THEREFORE HOLDING TO ROUGHLY SIX TIMES ITS OWN DELTA, which is worth explaining on its own. BUT IT DOES NOT REACH THE MISSED-PULSE THRESHOLD: sync codes are held 0.211 ms (SVC, median, measured on one machine's own clock from consecutive 'out' events), and 144 us is comfortably inside that, so a machine that far behind still arrives while the pulse is up.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26 with YAGPC_BARSPREAD_US=100 (new, src/vehicle.c, default off, threshold-gated and capped) on a restore of examples/5gpc-3crt-subtitled.snapshot with YAGPC_FC_MDM=1 YAGPC_FC_SENSORS=1; 4,029 reports.  TWO EARLIER MEASUREMENTS OF THIS QUANTITY WERE WRONG AND BOTH ARE WITHDRAWN.  (a) Comparing each machine's last logged sim time across machines gave median 0.027 ms, p90 0.233, p99 0.736, max 2.153 -- but the per-machine EVENT GAP distribution is median 0.013 ms, p90 0.242, p99 4.146, which tracks it almost exactly, so most of that was staleness in the measurement rather than divergence.  (b) Before that, per-machine offsets taken from panel reset-release stamps (rounded to 0.1 s) produced a flat 6.000 ms 'separation' that was pure artefact; the offsets must come from the sync history itself, where GPC1's 'out <code>' and GPC2's '<- GPC1 <code>' are one event.  AND A THIRD: a 1.446 ms 'hold' from YAGPC_SLOWPHASE_MS was conditioned on that instrument's own 0.4 ms threshold -- a selection effect.  The unfiltered holds are SVC 0.211 ms median (max 0.319), I/O complete 0.213, IPR 0.274.  WHAT IS NOT YET SETTLED: this run is a RESTORE with ZERO self-fails, and the 4,000-line cap was spent early, so it describes a healthy vehicle at the start of a run.  Whether the spread exceeds the hold duration at the moment a computer actually fails is the open question, and the way to ask it is a threshold ABOVE the hold -- 250 us -- so the instrument speaks only when a miss is possible.
  - addrs `081f2-08200` &middot; csects `FCMSSYNC,FCMST3DL,FCMSFAIL,FCMCSYNC` &middot; procs `CPU` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_BARSPREAD_US,YAGPC_SYNC_HISTORY,YAGPC_SLOWPHASE_MS,YAGPC_IDLE_CATCHUP_MS` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c,src/rtpacer.c,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `clock spread,barrier delta exceeded,missed pulse,sync hold 0.21 ms` &middot; doc `barPubUs[] in vehicle.c; FCMST3 minimum duration 182 us` &middot; run `barspread,synchist,catchup,slowphase`
- **#230** *(method)* A TAPE THAT IPLs AND RUNS SSW AND NOTHING ELSE: ~/workspace/pass-run/OI340700-SSWonly.mmv. Every memory configuration is removed, so an OPS request finds no phases to load and the vehicle stays in OPS 0. That is the one configuration in which the timing unit is NOT bypassed -- GPC5, which stays in OPS 0, has kept all six FIOPRMPG slots live in every five-computer run measured -- so this should be a fixture where the MTU accumulators count WITHOUT YAGPC_FC_SENSORS and without the set-breaking side effects that switch brings.
  - evidence: Built 2026-09-26 with the tool that already existed for the build's own -noOPS136 variant: tools/abridge_volume.py VOLUME --con80 CON80 --drop-mc 1,2,3,4,5,6,8,9 -o OUT.  The CON80 deck is OI340600's overlaid with OI340700's, the layering tapebuild/build.sh does at stage 1; the tool checks the deck against the volume and refuses a deck that has moved on.  415 of 2665 blocks kept, EVERY ONE BYTE-IDENTICAL to OI340700-v44boot.mmv -- only allocations are removed, nothing is relinked or regenerated, so it carries no build risk of its own.  VERIFIED, not merely built: headless-gpcmem.sh with TAPE set to it reaches 'MODE: IPL; memory filled, bootstrap read from MM1 (72 blocks, 36864 halfwords)', then MODE: RUN, then the GPC MEMORY page, with mmu1 at 56 commands / 432 blocks and formatFills 8 -- the same signature the full volume gives, so removing 2,250 blocks costs the IPL and OPS 0 nothing.  NOT ESTABLISHED: what an OPS request actually DOES against it.  abridge_volume.py's own header says requesting a removed configuration is not supported, so the failure mode is undefined -- it may be ignored cleanly or it may hang, and that is worth knowing before relying on it.  A RELATED TRAP, #101: a vehicle that IPLs and runs OPS 0 but refuses every OPS request is ALSO the signature of the IDP major-function switch sitting at its PL default, because DM6OPS keys the GRT on the requesting major function and no row matches.  YAGPC_DEUMF=1 is GNC.  Do not read this tape's behaviour as a tape property without checking that switch.

- **#231** *(emulator)* FOUR COMPUTERS IN OPS 0 KEEP THE TIMING UNIT ALIVE AND HOLD THE SET, with NO experimental switch -- so both faults belong to the POST-TRANSITION state and neither is inherent to running four computers. On the SSW-only volume (#230), which carries no memory configuration and therefore cannot transition, a four-computer vehicle runs to the end of its script with ZERO fail votes and ALL SIX FIOPRMPG slots live on ALL FOUR machines. YAGPC_FC_SENSORS is off; the MDM answering beyond the default is off. This is the first multi-computer vehicle in this investigation in which the timing unit is read by every member by default.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26 (discretePanel/ssw4): --gpcs 1,2,3,4 --crts 2, tape OI340700-SSWonly.mmv, examples/4gpc-ops0-hold.script (4gpc-startup with the OPS 2 0 1 PRO removed, because a request against that volume is unsupported and its failure mode undefined), duration 700, captured at 640 s.  Result: 0 votes, 2,046 timing-unit reads, and all six slots live on GPC1, GPC2, GPC3 and GPC4.  AND THE CLOCK SPREAD IS AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE BETTER: 1,158,216,596 samples with 86.002% under the barrier's own 25 us delta and only 2,202 past the 211 us sync hold (0.00019%), against 4,707 to 9,905 (0.00066% to 0.00136%) in the ascent runs.  THE DIFFERENCE IS I/O LOAD: 32,617 bus commands here against roughly 1,000,000 in an ascent.  That ties the sync excursions to flight-critical traffic rather than leaving them independent -- more traffic, more bulk advances between barrier checks, more chances to land past the hold -- and it is consistent with the MDM answering making votes more frequent (#216) without being their cause.  WHAT THIS SEPARATES: the two problems have been confounded from the start, because the unit is only bypassed after a transition and the votes only happen in the configuration that bypasses it.  Every run before this had both conditions at once.  With the transition removed, both faults disappear together.
