- **#232** *(method)* A PER-THREAD QUANTITY MEASURED THROUGH A FUNCTION-LOCAL static IS NOT MEASURED AT ALL, and the resulting number is quietly bounded by whatever the threads are already synchronised to. The five GPCs are five THREADS IN ONE PROCESS, so a "static double last" inside a function every machine calls is ONE variable, and "now - last" is a difference between two ARBITRARY machines, not a step taken by one. Because the barrier keeps every machine within barDeltaUs (25 us) of every other, such a difference can essentially never exceed 25 us -- so a probe looking for steps of 200 us or more reports approximately ZERO, and the zero looks like a clean result rather than a broken instrument.
  - evidence: YAGPC_BIGSTEP was added at BOTH barrier call sites to explain a histogram claiming 29,196-39,396 steps of >=200 us per run. The instruction-path copy (run.c:2956) keeps its state in r->bigStepLastUs, a field on the PER-MACHINE Runner, and reported 34. The idle-loop copy (run.c:3110) used a function-local "static double idleLast" shared by all five threads and reported ZERO. Reading the two together as "neither call site accounts for the steps" was wrong: only one of them was measuring anything. Fixed by making idleLast a [6] indexed by gpcId. The SAME defect had already been found and fixed once in this session, in vehicle.c barSpreadHist, where one shared array across five threads produced buckets summing to 104 percent. RULE: any counter or last-value in a path the machines share must be indexed by gpcId, and a probe that reports zero must be shown to be capable of reporting non-zero before the zero is believed.
  - env `YAGPC_BIGSTEP_US,YAGPC_BARSPREAD_US` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/vehicle.c` &middot; symptom `probe reports zero because it is broken`
- **#233** *(method)* A HISTOGRAM WHOSE last-VALUE IS CARRIED INSIDE ITS OWN REPORTING GATE MANUFACTURES THE VERY OUTLIERS IT IS LOOKING FOR. vehicle.c step histogram updated barLastPubUs[gpcId] inside the "pub >= barSpreadAfterUs && n >= barSpreadMinN" block, so every stretch during which the gate was SHUT -- before YAGPC_BARSPREAD_AFTER, or whenever fewer than MINN machines were active -- was folded whole into the next difference and counted as ONE enormous step.
  - evidence: It reported 29,196 to 39,396 steps of >=200 us per run with max 286 us. Fixed by carrying the previous published time on EVERY call (barStepPrevUs[6], set outside the gate) and differencing against that. The gate exists for a good reason -- a flat budget is otherwise spent entirely during the staggered IPLs -- so the fix is to carry the value outside the gate, not to remove the gate. GENERAL RULE, and this is the second instance in one session: a gate may decide whether to REPORT a sample, never whether to ADVANCE the state a sample is computed from.
  - env `YAGPC_BARSPREAD_US,YAGPC_BARSPREAD_AFTER,YAGPC_BARSPREAD_MINN` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c,src/vehicle.h` &middot; symptom `histogram outliers that no call site can account for`
- **#234** *(emulator)* THE SYNC-PATH SELF-FAILS ARE A CONSEQUENCE, NOT A CAUSE, AND COUNTING THEM AS CAUSES POINTED THIS INVESTIGATION AT THE WRONG MECHANISM FOR DAYS. A computer takes an I/O error nobody else has and enters FCMSFAIL from FIOERRLC+0313; having self-failed it stops driving its sync code; its peers then time out on it in FCMSSYNC one FCMSNTO2 later and vote it out, first from the redundant set and then from the common set. The FCMSSYNC/FCMCSYNC entries are the VOTE, cast by a healthy survivor against the machine that already died. An earlier tally of "8 of 10 self-fails come from the sync paths" was counting votes as deaths.
  - evidence: Run idlestep (5 GPCs, OI340700-v44boot, YAGPC_FC_SENSORS, LANDMARKS 1948c with REGS). R7 is the link register and resolves through mafgen/augmented-*.json IDENTICALLY IN EVERY MEMORY CONFIGURATION (G2,G3,G8,G9,G16,S2): r7=9dc70031 -> 19dc7 = FIOERRLC+0313; r7=979c6031 -> 1979c = FCMSSYNC+0086; r7=8c4bf031 -> 18c4b = FCMCSYNC+00bd. TIMELINE ON THE SHARED CLOCK, twice: GPC1 FIOERRLC at 725.420626 -> GPC2 FCMSSYNC at 725.425127 naming GPC1 (r0=00100001, bit31=1 and bits 11-15 = 0x0010 = GPC1, and FCMSFAIL.asm:004400-005400 says SELF S BIT MUST NOT BE ON for this entry) -> GPC2 FCMCSYNC at 725.552608 naming GPC1 common-set (r0=80100001). Then GPC3 FIOERRLC at 880.940474 -> GPC2 FCMSSYNC at 880.944903 naming GPC3 (00040001) -> GPC2 FCMCSYNC at 881.072357 (80040001). THE LAG IS THE TIMEOUT CONSTANT: 4.50 and 4.43 ms against FCMSNTO2 = F 3850 = 3.85 ms (FCMCBLKS.asm:686), plus loop overhead. CORROBORATION FROM THE OTHER SIDE: GPC1 s own 8192-event sync ring, dumped at its failure, is CLEAN to the last entry -- all of GPC2/3/4 driving 001 I/O complete then 111 null within microseconds, no gap remotely near 3.85 ms. A machine that fails on a late peer does not have a clean ring. NOT EXPLAINED: GPC2 also voted GPC4 out at 755.185580/755.313008 with no FIOERRLC landmark from GPC4. R0 FOR THE FIOERRLC ENTRY IS NOT THE SYNC-PATH MASK -- it has bit 31 = 0 and 8bc40000 looks like an address halfword, so it must not be read as a GPC mask.  CONFIRMED INDEPENDENTLY, AND R0 IS AN ADDRESS: FIOERRLC.asm:073500-073900 does LHI R0,X8000 / ST R0,FIOICCFL / LA R0,FIOFTSPL / CALL FCMSFAIL, and FIOFTSPL resolves to 0x08bc4 in FCMZCONS (mafgen augmented-G2.json) -- exactly the 8bc4 in r0=8bc40000. THE REGISTERS AT THE CALL SETTLE WHO: the two instructions before the test are LH R5,TCVTCID (self id) and LH R6,FIOGPCID (id of the GPC with the error), and the landmark shows GPC1 r5=00010000 r6=00010000 and GPC3 r5=00030000 r6=00030000 -- self and the erring GPC are the same computer in both, which is the force-fail-self condition exactly. BOTH FAILURES ARE ON THE SAME TRANSACTION: r4=001a0000 in both, and R4 is the index added to FIOTRECT to address the transaction error counter (LA R2,FIOTRECT / AR R2,R4), so transaction index 0x1a = 26. THE FULL RULE, from FIOERRLC.asm:072700-073900: the transaction error counter must reach 2 (not 1), FIOGPCWE must be exactly 1 (only one GPC had the error), FIOGPCID must equal TCVTCID (that one is self), and the device must be neither SRB (FIOSRBID) nor PMU (FIOPMUDV) -- the source says why: SRB data is not used by FSW and PMU buses have no listeners, so a powered-down PMU can give a non-universal error legitimately.  SCOPE LIMIT, see #237: the landmark budget in both runs was gated on each machines OWN clock, which lags the shared frame by 112 to 223 seconds on different computers, so some FCMSFAIL entries were never armed. The FIOERRLC-then-vote ordering observed here is sound for the events that WERE recorded -- the times and the register decode stand -- but this evidence cannot show that every death follows that pattern, and it must not be cited as though it did. The gate is fixed; a run on the corrected build is what would settle completeness.  SECOND RUN (errwho, 2026-09-26): THE ORDER IS NOT ALWAYS FIOERRLC FIRST, AND A FOURTH CALLER APPEARS. r7=979c6031 also resolves to 1987f = FCMTSYNC+0029, the TIMER sync path. The collapse ran: 759.655704/708 GPC1 AND GPC2 at the same microsecond both vote GPC4 out of the redundant set from FCMTSYNC (r0=00020001), common set at 759.681801/805 (80020001); 759.808647 GPC2 force-fails ITSELF from FIOERRLC+0313 (r0=8bc40000 = &FIOFTSPL again); 759.815742 GPC1 votes GPC2 out (00080001) and 759.841887 out of the common set; 759.975673 GPC1 votes GPC3 out (00040001), 760.001902 common set. GPC1 is left alone. So the FIOERRLC-then-vote pattern DOES recur -- GPC2 self-fails at 759.8086 and GPC1 votes it out 7.1 ms later -- but it is not the opening move: GPC4 was voted out first, from the timer sync, with no FIOERRLC of its own on record. THE ERROR TERMINATIONS ARE UNIVERSAL, WHICH SHARPENS THE PUZZLE: over the run GPC1 took 47, GPC2 46, GPC3 42 and GPC4 49, all on the same BCEs 14-17 and 20-23 -- the devices are answering symmetrically. Yet GPC2 still reached FIOERRLC s "exactly one GPC had this error" test. Either the peers error masks are not reaching it over the ICC (#235), or the errors are universal ACROSS THE RUN but not on the SAME TRANSACTION, which is a different and testable thing: FIOERRLC counts per transaction error counter, so simultaneity is what matters, not totals. CAVEAT: this run still used the own-clock landmark gate (#237), and no offset is known for GPC3, GPC4 or GPC5, so their FCMSFAIL entries may simply never have been armed.
  - addrs `1948c-19673,19dc7,1979c,18c4b` &middot; csects `FCMSFAIL,FIOERRLC,FCMSSYNC,FCMCSYNC,FCMCBLKS` &middot; env `YAGPC_LANDMARKS,YAGPC_LANDMARKS_REGS,YAGPC_SYNC_HISTORY,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS` &middot; symptom `loss of sync,fail to sync,vote out` &middot; tsec `725.42-881.07` &middot; doc `FCMSFAIL.asm 004400-005400, FCMCBLKS.asm:686, FCMSSYNC.asm:180` &middot; run `idlestep`
- **#237** *(method)* YAGPC_LANDMARKS_AFTER GATED ON EACH MACHINES OWN CLOCK, WHICH IN A MULTI-COMPUTER RUN ARMS THE FIVE COMPUTERS AT FIVE DIFFERENT SHARED INSTANTS -- so a computer that entered FCMSFAIL inside its own suppressed window left NO landmark, and that is indistinguishable from never having entered. Any argument of the form "GPCn never self-failed, therefore its vote against it was a genuine sync miss" is unsound while this gate is on the own clock.
  - evidence: The offsets are not small. Run stepfix: GPC2 own t=602.844438 at shared=715.013987, an offset of 112.17 s; GPC1 own t = shared exactly (it is the frame origin). Run idlestep: GPC2 own t=613.454038 at shared=725.425127, again 111.97 s; GPC3 own t=657.860651 at shared=880.940474, an offset of 223.08 s. With YAGPC_LANDMARKS_AFTER=600 that arms GPC1 at shared 600, GPC2 at about shared 712, and GPC3 at about shared 823. In stepfix, GPC1 and GPC2 both entered FCMSSYNC naming GPC3 and GPC3 itself showed no landmark -- which was nearly read as "GPC3 was voted out without self-failing", a DIFFERENT mechanism from #234. It may be, but this run cannot say so, because GPC3 s landmarks were not armed. FIXED: the gate now compares against vehicle_shared_us(vehicle, gpcId) where a shared clock exists and falls back to the own clock only when it does not, which is the same quantity the landmark line has always PRINTED as shared=. The old own-clock numbers remain valid as observations; what they cannot support is a claim about ABSENCE.  THE FIX IS CONFIRMED TO MATTER, run iccage, 2026-09-26: with the gate on the shared clock GPC3 produced FCMSFAIL landmarks for the first time in this investigation -- "GPC3 LANDMARK fcmsfail #2 at 1948c t=493.976692 s shared=716.980739 r0=80080001", a vote against GPC2 out of the common set. GPC3 s own clock read 493.98 at shared 716.98, an offset of 223.00 s, so under the old own-clock gate with AFTER=600 GPC3 was not armed until shared 823 and this failure at shared 717 COULD NOT have been recorded by any earlier run. Every previous statement about which computers did or did not enter FCMSFAIL was drawn from a sample that excluded the laggards.
  - env `YAGPC_LANDMARKS_AFTER,YAGPC_LANDMARKS` &middot; files `src/run.c` &middot; symptom `landmark absent,never self-failed` &middot; run `stepfix,idlestep`
- **#240** *(emulator)* THE WHOLE LOSS OF SYNC IS ONE EVENT WITH ONE CAUSE: a listening computer is handed a new command sync while it still owes words of the previous reply, which is a Table 1.2 Sync Error, and two of those on one transaction counter trip FIOERRLC s force-fail-self. Every other FCMSFAIL in the run is the healthy majority correctly voting out a computer that had ALREADY killed itself. ALL 151 error terminations in the run are in FIOHFE02 and FIOMFE02 -- the forward and middle flight-critical MDM bus programs -- and NOT ONE is a receive time-out.
  - evidence: Run iccage, 2026-09-26, the FIRST run with the landmark gate on the shared clock (#237) so all five computers were armed. The ENTIRE cascade is eight entries and one death: 716.848796 GPC2 self-fails from FIOERRLC+0313 with r0=&FIOFTSPL; 716.853298/307/331 GPC4, GPC1 and GPC3 each vote GPC2 out of the redundant set from FCMSSYNC+0086 (r0=00080001), 4.50/4.51/4.54 ms later, which is FCMSNTO2 = 3.85 ms plus loop overhead; 716.980694-739 GPC4, GPC5, GPC1 and GPC3 vote it out of the common set from FCMCSYNC+00bd (80080001). Nothing else fails for the rest of the run. The earlier appearance of a multi-victim cascade starting with FCMTSYNC was an artefact of the own-clock landmark gate. THE ERRORS THEMSELVES: 151 ERRTERM lines, BCE program addresses all resolving into FIOMFE02+0206..0248 and FIOHFE02+00c4..02ca (mafgen augmented-G2.json); by BCE 14:42, 15:16, 16:44, 17:26, 20:4, 21:4, 22:13, 23:2. left= is NEVER zero -- 1 (70 times), 6 (28), 32 (44), 2, 5, 30, 34 -- so words were always still outstanding, and there are ZERO receive time-outs in the whole log, which per iop.c s own note means every one of these is a command sync arriving MID-RECEIVE. GPC2, the computer that died, had the FEWEST errors of all (21 against 44, 39 and 47), so it did not err more -- it erred ALONE on one transaction, which is the only thing FIOERRLC tests. WHY THE EXISTING MITIGATION DOES NOT CATCH THEM: mtu_carry_take exists for exactly this and fired only 24 times against 151 errors, because it declines any reader with echoPending set -- and the unconditional echo rule sets echoPending for EVERY listener on EVERY command, so the readers furthest behind, which are the ones that lose data to the following memset, are precisely the ones it refuses to carry.  WHEN, RELATIVE TO THE CREW: in run whocut the votes are stamped in cam.log at panel t=867.6 and the crew entered OPS 9 0 1 PRO at panel t=796.5, so the self-fail comes about 71 s AFTER the OPS transition -- not during the NBAT item entry that fills panel t 703-790, which the raw shared time (760.2) would suggest if read as panel time. That fits #231 (both faults post-transition) and fits the code: the fatal transaction lives in FIOMFE02, whose flight-critical MDM sequence is headed COMMON CODE TO ALL FF MDM S (G9). CONSEQUENCE FOR FIXTURES: a restore fixture must be captured AFTER the OPS transition has completed and BEFORE the first death -- a restore does not replay crew keystrokes, so a capture taken before the OPS request would never reach the failure.
  - addrs `1d766-1d7a8,1d8ac-1dab2,1948c-19673,19dc7` &middot; csects `FIOHFE02,FIOMFE02,FIOERRLC,FCMSSYNC,FCMCSYNC,FCMSFAIL` &middot; procs `BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17,BCE20,BCE21,BCE22,BCE23` &middot; env `YAGPC_FC_SENSORS,YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_ERRTERM_TRACE` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `loss of sync,sync error,fail to sync` &middot; tsec `716.84-716.98` &middot; doc `BCE PoO IBM-6246556A Table 1.2` &middot; run `iccage`
- **#242** *(emulator)* IS THE MODEL S REPLY PACING WHAT MANUFACTURES THE SYNC ERRORS? #217 named two remedies for its confirmed measurement -- hold the BCE, or correct the rate -- and recorded the first as tried and WORSE. This is the second, and it is now a measurement rather than a rebuild: YAGPC_MTU_WORD_US sets the reply word period, default 33 us as before.
  - evidence: THE RATE IS NOT ARBITRARY AND 33 IS DEFENSIBLE ONLY FOR ONE DIRECTION. BCE Principles of Operation IBM-6246556A part 3, section 3.3.3, read directly from the local ibiblio mirror: a bus word is 28 bits at 1 MHz, and "for a Message Out instruction this gap is fixed at 5 microseconds" -- so 28 + 5 = 33 us is the period for words a BCE TRANSMITS. For words it RECEIVES the same book allows "a gap of at most 20 microseconds to occur between data words" before raising its own error 21 - GAP, so a reply s word period legitimately lies anywhere in 28..48 us according to how fast the answering box is. This model charges 33 in both directions. BOTH OUTCOMES ARE WRITTEN DOWN BEFORE THE RUN. If a fast wire (YAGPC_MTU_WORD_US=10) largely removes the FIOHFE02/FIOMFE02 Sync Errors, the pacing is the constraint and the remaining work is to find the defensible rate. If it does NOT, pacing is refuted as the mechanism. AND THERE IS A REAL CHANCE IT MAKES THINGS WORSE, for a reason worth stating in advance: FIOGPCWE is counted per BCE, and a self-fail needs EXACTLY ONE computer in error on that BCE. While every listener is still waiting on a slow wire they are all cut off together and the count is 3, which is harmless; with a fast wire every listener has its words available at once and only the one that polls least often is still mid-receive, which is a count of 1 -- the fatal case. If that is what happens, the direction of the correct fix is the opposite of the obvious one. BASELINE, run iccage: 151 error terminations, 8 FCMSFAIL entries, one death (GPC2 from FIOERRLC+0313 at shared 716.848796).  CONFIRMED, run wire10, 2026-09-26, YAGPC_MTU_WORD_US=10 against the iccage baseline. ERROR TERMINATIONS 151 -> 17, an 89 percent fall, and the profile changes character completely: left= was 1 (70 times), 6 (28) and 32 (44), and is now 31, 38, 40, 68, 74, 121, 510, 1345 and 9189 -- a handful of large transfers instead of a flood of small cut-off reads. THE PACING IS THEREFORE THE DOMINANT SOURCE OF THE SYNC ERRORS, which is what #217 named and never tried. THE TIMING UNIT IS HEALTHIER TOO: timeReads 2332 -> 2609 and bus commands 817,178 -> 41,645, a twentyfold fall in traffic, which is twentyfold less retry. THE PREDICTED BAD OUTCOME DID NOT HAPPEN: the fast wire did not multiply the deaths. One computer was still lost -- GPC4 self-failed from FIOERRLC+0313 at shared 705.097986 and was voted out by GPC1, GPC2 and GPC3 from FCMISYNC+00bb (18f4f, a fifth caller) and by all four from FCMCSYNC -- exactly one death, as in the baseline. The later entries at 1b15c = FPMSVC+0022 are the SVC handler and all three survivors enter it at the SAME microsecond, which is synchronised behaviour and not a failure. BUT 10 US IS NOT A RATE THE HARDWARE CAN HAVE: a bus word is 28 bits at 1 MHz, so 28 us is the floor and 10 is below the word itself. This measures the mechanism; it is not shippable. NEXT, and the reason this result matters: what the fast wire really removes is the PARTIALLY DRAINED READER, because every word is available at once. A message on this bus is atomic -- a BCE does not release a half-received message to its program -- so releasing the whole reply at the instant the message would have finished, at the DOCUMENTED 33 us rate, should get the same effect legally.
  - env `YAGPC_MTU_WORD_US,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS,YAGPC_FC_MDM` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `sync error,command sync mid-receive,left=21` &middot; doc `BCE PoO IBM-6246556A part 3 sec 3.3.3` &middot; run `wire10`
- **#244** *(emulator)* THE TIMING-UNIT/MDM MODEL IS ONE OBJECT SHARED BY FIVE THREADS WITH NO LOCK OF ITS OWN, AND TWO OF ITS INPUTS ARE SCALARS EVERY THREAD OVERWRITES. run.c sets m->sharedUs (mtumodel_set_shared_us) and m->armedWords (mtumodel_set_armed_words) and then calls mtumodel_service_as, holding only vehicle busLock[busID] -- a PER-BUS lock. A computer on a different bus holds a different lock and can overwrite either scalar in between. Consequences: a command can be answered with ANOTHER BUS S armed length (so the reply is longer or shorter than the commander asked for), and a reader s readiness test, sharedUs >= wireUs + k*33, can be judged against ANOTHER MACHINE S clock. The second is a per-reader, per-poll error that falls on one computer at random -- which is the precise shape of the fault FIOERRLC punishes (FIOGPCWE == 1, #240), and it would explain why 151, 85 and 17 error terminations each cost exactly one computer (#243): the fatal errors are not the common ones, they are the racy ones.
  - evidence: FOUND BY INSPECTION 2026-09-26, not yet measured. mtumodel.c contains no mutex, no lock and no atomic; its only serialisation is the caller s per-bus lock, and sharedUs/armedWords are plain struct fields, not per-bus or per-reader. This is the same defect class as #232 (a static shared by five threads) found in today s instruments. MEASUREMENT ADDED, NO BEHAVIOUR CHANGE: each thread records what it set (thread-local) and mtumodel_service_as counts, at entry, how often another computer had already changed the shared clock or (on a command) the armed count underneath it -- a FLOOR, since a collision mid-call is not seen. FIX ADDED BEHIND A SWITCH: YAGPC_MTU_LOCKED=1 sets both values and serves the call as one step under a model mutex (mtumodel_service_locked), and the report says the counters should then be zero. The next run (fix4, four computers, three CRTs, the flown configuration) carries the counters with the lock OFF, so it measures the race rate as the failure actually occurs; a locked run on the same fixture is then the test.  THE RACE IS REAL AND MEASURED, run fix4, 2026-09-26 (four computers, three CRTs, lock OFF): of 93,672,542 calls into the model, another computer had ALREADY changed the shared clock underneath 3,736,992 of them -- 4.0 percent -- and the armed count underneath 26 commands, each of which was therefore answered with ANOTHER bus s reply length. Both are FLOORS: a collision that lands after entry, mid-call, is not seen. What is NOT yet shown is that the race changes an outcome: the shared-clock collisions shift a reader s readiness test by at most the barrier spread, about 25 us, which is under one 33 us word; the armed-count collisions are rare but each one is a wrong-length reply. YAGPC_MTU_LOCKED=1 removes both, and the counters then read zero by construction; a locked trial is the test of whether it matters.
  - env `YAGPC_MTU_LOCKED,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS,YAGPC_FC_MDM` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/mtumodel.h,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `asymmetric error,fail to sync,one death regardless of error count`
- **#246** *(emulator)* ON THE FLOWN CONFIGURATION -- FOUR COMPUTERS, THREE CRTS, 4gpc-g3-startup.script TO OPS 301, WITH THE MDM/SENSOR ANSWERING ON -- THE SET HOLDS BUT THE TIMING UNIT IS STILL LOST, and it is lost on EVERY computer within seconds of the OPS transition. So YAGPC_FC_SENSORS does NOT keep the unit alive where it matters, and an earlier statement that it did was wrong: it rested on the model s timeReads counter staying high in five-computer runs, and in those runs GPC5 never leaves OPS 0 and keeps its FIOPRMPG slots live, so its reads alone kept that counter up while the redundant set had already bypassed the unit.
  - evidence: Run fix4, 2026-09-26. OPS 3 0 1 PRO entered at wall +360 s; stopped at +781 s. cam.log: ZERO votes. Captures at shared 409.3, 529.4 and 649 s all show, via mtuscore.py, FIOBY51/52/53 and FIOBY51L/52L/53L BYPASSED on GPC1, GPC2, GPC3 and GPC4 alike -- already at the first capture, 60 s after the request. The slot addresses 1cc74..1cca8 are FIOPRMPG in G2, G3 and G9 alike (mafgen augmented-*.json), so the reading is valid in G3. THE TRIGGER, from GPC1 whose own clock is the shared frame: at 369.977-370.017 a burst of left=1 errors on buses 21-23, and at 370.001 errors at pc=1ccae with left=7 on buses 21 and 22. 1ccae lies just past FIOBY53L (1cca8) in FIOPRMPG, and seven words is the unit s own reply, so a LISTENER received NONE of the timing unit s words. The OI301700 source for that listener is FIOEL51L-53L: "#CMDI FIOFFIUA,FIOMDMRT  SET IUA. / #RDLI 6  RECEIVE DATA." -- the same IUAR-setting idiom as the fatal FA-MDM listener in #245. Errors across the four computers were near-equal (15-18 each), i.e. SYMMETRIC -- which is exactly why this configuration commfaults the string on everyone rather than failing one computer to sync. BOTH FAULTS THEREFORE RUN THROUGH ONE MODELLED INSTRUCTION: a listener s IUAR-setting #CMDI treated as a bus transaction (#245); when the resulting loss is shared it becomes a commfault and the MTU is bypassed, and when one computer holds it alone it becomes a FIOERRLC self-fail.

- **#247** *(emulator)* THE FATAL ASYMMETRIC ERROR IS A STALE COMMAND ECHO. mtumodel.c s echoPending never expires, so a listener that was NOT listening when a command went by keeps it pending and receives it whenever it next polls -- milliseconds later, as if fresh. If that stale command s IUA matches the listener s IUAR, its BCE takes it as the start of the message it has just armed for, receives no data, and is cut off by the next real command: a Sync Error on that one computer alone, which with FIOGPCWE == 1 is FIOERRLC s force-fail-self. Whether a listener is ambushed depends only on whether it happened to poll in a window, so it lands on one computer at random -- explaining why error totals never predicted deaths (#243).
  - evidence: SEEN DIRECTLY in the YAGPC_MTU_RING dump of trial fix-a, 2026-09-26 (four computers, restored OPS 0 fixture pre4-snap, OPS 3 0 1 PRO replayed), bus 15, GPC2 commanding: 55.916662 GPC2 issues 620040 (IUA 12) while GPC1 is not listening on bus 15; 55.928053 -- 11 ms later -- GPC1, now armed with #RDLI 33 for FIOFAIC1 at pc 1d700 (FIOMFE38+01a0 in G3), polls and is handed the STALE 620040 echo; 55.928066 ERRTERM GPC1 bce15 pc=1d700 left=34; 55.928070 GPC1 takes the fresh 400c0a listen-command echo; GPC1 NEVER takes the 6082a5 echo or any of its 34 words; 55.937896 GPC1 self-fails from FIOERRLC+0313. GPC3 and GPC4 had consumed the old echo in time and took the 6082a5 echo and all 34 words. ALSO REFUTED ALONG THE WAY: the listener s own #CMDI never reaches the model at all (#245). FIX, behind YAGPC_MTU_ECHO_EXPIRE=1: a reader that never began a transfer loses its echo -- and that transfer s data -- once the transfer would have left the wire: wireUs + (1 + transfer words) * 33 us + 200 us for poll granularity and the barrier s spread; a reader that has begun is untouched. Judged against the reader s OWN clock (tlShared), not the shared scalar #244 shows is racy. It is the rule iccmodel.c already applies to the ICC ("A BUS HOLDS NOTHING"). PREDICTION: the lone left=34/left=7 listener errors disappear, and with them the FIOERRLC self-fails; if the MTU s left=7 listener loss is the same mechanism, the timing unit stays unbypassed too.  PARTIAL, NOT A FIX, as of trial exl-c (2026-09-26). Tally on the restored 4-GPC fixture, deaths by trial: baseline-equivalent (base-a, iuar-a, iuar-b, fix-a, base-c) 0,0,0,1,2; echo expiry alone (ex-a, ex-b) 1,0; echo expiry plus YAGPC_MTU_LOCKED (exl-a, exl-b, exl-c) 0,0,1. The FA-MDM stale-echo death (FIOFAIC1, left=34) has not recurred with echo expiry on -- but BOTH deaths with it on (ex-a GPC3, exl-c GPC4) are a DIFFERENT transaction: FIONSPPG on bus 20, left=31, at about 40.5-40.9 s. There the listener program is #CMDI FIOMDMRT / #RDS 0,0 (NSP1 power discrete) / #RDS 0,1 (2-stage A discrete) / #RDLI 31 (NSP data), and the dying computer was already INSIDE #RDLI 31 holding one word when 525000 s command sync arrived -- its two #RDS had been satisfied one message early. Candidate under test: the adapter latch, which mia_data_available/mia_get_data fall back to -- including a latched COMMAND SYNC -- when the model has nothing newer. A BCE-side ring (YAGPC_BCE_RING: arms, every word taken with sync/latch/await flags, errors) now dumps beside the model s ring to show it.  ECHO EXPIRY TRADES ONE DEATH FOR ANOTHER -- confirmed by transaction across every death on the restored 4-GPC fixture. WITHOUT expiry (fix-a, base-c): all three deaths are the FA-MDM read, left=34 -- the stale-echo mechanism this entry describes. WITH expiry (ex-a, exl-c, exl-g): all three deaths are the NSP read on bus 20, FIONSPPG, left=31 -- and never the FA-MDM one. Lock trials exl-a..g: 2 deaths in 7, no better than baseline. THE NEW MECHANISM, from the BCE ring (YAGPC_BCE_RING) in exl-g: the healthy listener GPC3 arms #RDS 0,0 at 1cc2e, takes 526420 s sync and word, arms #RDS 0,1 at 1cc2f, takes 525000 s sync and word, then arms #RDLI 31 at 1cc32 -- exactly the program. The dying GPC2 is ALREADY PARKED at 1cc32 (#RDLI 31, 32 words, awaiting a sync) when the cycle begins, never executes either #RDS, so 526420 s IUA-10 sync releases it, 526420 s data becomes word 1 of 32, and 525000 s sync arrives mid-receive: left=31. So GPC2 s PREVIOUS #RDLI 31 never got its sync -- and a Listen-Mode receive has no time-out, so it waits a whole cycle to be ambushed. Withdrawing an echo from a listener that polls late is precisely how a listener is left waiting for a sync that has gone. THE REAL QUESTION IS THEREFORE WHY A LISTENER ARRIVES AT ITS RECEIVE LATE AT ALL: the commander s #DLYI FIODELAY / FIODLAY5 ("ALLOW LISTENER(S) TO SET UP") exist so that on the vehicle every listener is armed before the command goes out. Each withdrawal is now logged live (ECHOX, with how late the reader was) to measure it.  RESIDUAL, full4 (2026-09-26, 40-minute from-IPL run with all three switches): the parked-listener mechanism occurred ONCE (GPC4, bus 20, FIONSPPG+0036, left=31, 1606.6 s) and did not kill -- FIOERRLC needs two on one counter. It remains the one known path to a self-fail with the switches on. Its cause -- why a listener reaches its #RDLI 31 late enough to lose NSPRD s echo -- is still open; each withdrawal is logged (ECHOX) for when it next coincides with a death.  ECHO-EXPIRY WINDOW TOO TIGHT -- corrected 2026-09-26 20:38. Run s201-all1 (four computers, two CRTs, 4gpc-startup.script to OPS 201, all five switches) lost GPC1 at shared 759.70: GPC1 entered FCMSFAIL from FCMISYNC+00bb naming ALL THREE peers (r0=000e0001), i.e. none of them signalled I/O complete within 3.85 ms, and GPCs 2-4 then voted GPC1 out of the common set (cam 11/21/31/41). Its ECHOX log shows the rule withdrawing ROUTINE echoes: the listen command 401100 on bus 17 withdrawn from GPCs 1, 2 and 3 every 40 ms, each 250-340 us late -- the wire-time window for a no-reply command is one word plus 200 us, about 233 us, so ordinary listener latency fell outside it. The fatal stale echoes the rule exists for were 10.8 ms (FA read, fix-a) and 960 ms (the MTU read) old. FIX: an echo is now stale only past a floor of 5 ms after its command (YAGPC_MTU_ECHO_STALE_US overrides) -- fifteen times the routine lateness, half the youngest fatal stale echo. The too-tight window is the likely source of the parked-listener residual and of s201-all1 s loss; that is a hypothesis for the following runs to test, not yet shown. Runs acid-all2 and g3-all3 were already executing the OLD binary when this was built (their /proc exe was the deleted file) and test the old window.
  - addrs `1d700,1ccae` &middot; csects `FIOMFE38,FIOPRMPG,FIOERRLC` &middot; procs `BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17` &middot; env `YAGPC_MTU_ECHO_EXPIRE,YAGPC_MTU_RING,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `left=34,stale echo,one computer alone,fail to sync,MTU bypassed` &middot; run `fix-a`
- **#250** *(method)* THEORY REVIEW OF THE FIVE SWITCHES BEFORE MAKING THEM DEFAULT (YAGPC_FC_MDM, YAGPC_FC_SENSORS, YAGPC_FC_ANSWER_UNNAMED, YAGPC_MTU_ECHO_EXPIRE, YAGPC_MTU_LOCKED): one deadlock-free lock, one accepted fidelity trade, and one known residual.
  - evidence: LOCK (YAGPC_MTU_LOCKED): a LEAF lock. mtumodel_service_as calls nothing outside mtumodel.c -- no vehicle, iop, router, barrier, discretes or pause function -- so nothing is ever acquired while it is held and no lock-order inversion is possible; the caller holds vehicle busLock[bus] first, always in that order. Capture and restore (mtumodel_dump/load from vehicle.c) run only at the stop-the-world rendezvous, when every machine is parked at an instruction boundary and so outside any service call; mtumodel_report runs at shutdown. Measured cost: 1.0 billion locked calls in the 40-minute full4 run with the vehicle holding real time (rate to be recorded from the validation batch). ECHO EXPIRY: withdraws a command echo, and that transfer s data, only from a reader that NEVER BEGAN the transfer, once it has left the wire (command + reply words at 33 us, + 200 us). This is the real wire s behaviour -- a command a computer was not listening for is gone -- and iccmodel.c already applies it to the ICC. KNOWN RESIDUAL (#247): a listener that arms a receive after its transfer has passed now misses it outright and, Listen Mode having no time-out, waits into the next cycle; seen once in 40 minutes (full4) and never twice on one counter, which is what FIOERRLC needs to fail a computer. Real hardware would behave the same way given a late listener; why ours are occasionally late is open. ANSWER-UNNAMED: answers only reads addressed to the FF/FA MDMs (IUA 10/12) for which a BCE has ARMED a receive, with the armed length -- the MDM is present on every vehicle, so a read it is sent is a read it answers; commands with no receive armed are still declined, so the listen command and FIOHIBAD are unaffected. Observed to add exactly two codes (532aaa, 632aaa). MDM/SENSOR ANSWERING ITSELF: the reply is zeros -- channel data the model does not simulate. The alternative, no answer at all, is what commfaulted every string; zeros are a fidelity limitation (the flight software sees a quiet vehicle), recorded here so it is not mistaken for a claim that the data is real.
  - env `YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS,YAGPC_FC_ANSWER_UNNAMED,YAGPC_MTU_ECHO_EXPIRE,YAGPC_MTU_LOCKED` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/run.c`

The two runs also agree on medsXfers 1328, polls 1575 and wordsIn 285292 -- identical, not merely within tolerance. The switched build is judged on ABSOLUTE criteria (zero votes, zero FCMSFAIL, timing unit live on every computer, crew script completes, rate holds); the only sound comparison is the headless gate, whose reference numbers are a recorded standard rather than a run of the current default. The queued default-build replications were dropped for switched-only replicates. acid-def1 stands as an UNEXPLAINED possible defect on the default multi-computer path (GPC1 failing its OPS 9 load on the mass-memory bus, BCE 18, left=148), outside this change and not investigated here. - **#260** *(emulator)* INTERCOMPUTER WORDS WERE READ BEFORE THEY WERE SENT: about ONE TRANSFER IN FIVE on the ICC buses (1-5), at the default 25 us barrier, reached its reader before the reader's clock reached the transfer's send time -- a causality violation between computers. iccmodel.c handed a word over the moment the sender's thread wrote it; a sender ahead of the reader in simulated time is 'in the future'. The ICC time gate (YAGPC_ICC_TIMEGATE, icc_head_due) delivers a word only once the reader's shared clock reaches its send time, the rule the wire model already applies to buses 14-23 (#254).
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-28 with the end-of-run report's new count '(N read before they were sent)'. GATE OFF, one vehicle alone, four GPCs to OPS 201: gr1 8219 of 38816 transfers early (21.2%), gr3 8681 of 38816, every bus and every reader, by up to 29 us; at a 200 us barrier (gs10000-1-off) all 12 reader pairs early, by up to 201 us. GATE ON: gr2 0 of 38816, gr4 0 of 38826, gs9000-1-on 0 (min lag +2 us). NO REGRESSION FOUND: rate one vehicle alone off/on/off/on 0.985/0.985/0.985/0.985 (730 s simulated in 742 s wall, all four identical); 80 STBY->RUN rejoins gate-on (gtA1-2, gtB1-2) all clean, MTU live 4/4, 0 error terminations; 200 us stress 3 of 3 clean gate-on, 3 of 3 gate-off (gstress); earlier gfull1 (full run, gate on) clean rate 0.993 and 128 of 129 gated joins (the one, gk19, was #259's MTU cause). The lower rates in gtA1/gtB1 (0.971/0.963) were host load: they shared the machine with two 200 us stress vehicles; gtA2/gtB2 without them 0.985/0.984. NOT COVERED: five computers, restored vehicles, the 3-CRT configuration, runs longer than 40 minutes, OPS transitions beyond 201.  THE 3-CRT CONFIGURATION, 2026-09-28 (build 7d613d76a, examples/4gpc-g3-startup.script: four GPCs, CONFIG 3 / OPS 301, three CRTs, 900 s held in OPS 301, ONE VEHICLE AT A TIME): gate ON g3-gate1 and gate OFF g3-gate0 IDENTICAL -- 0 votes, 0 FCMSFAIL, no computer failed, MTU live 4/4, 0 error terminations, rate 0.992 (1339 s simulated in 1351 s wall) in both. Transfers read before they were sent: 0 of 84,392 gate on, 20,730 of 84,392 (24.6%) gate off. Remaining untested: five computers, restored vehicles, runs over 40 min.

## Fixed

- **#14** *(emulator)* MSC repeat state (mscRepeatActive/PC/UntilUs) survived a CPU-forced PC load, so a returning @RAW would not re-arm and used a stale deadline.
  - evidence: Correct as modelling; cleared on the local-store PC write. Did NOT unblock phase 18.
  - addrs `a201-a201` &middot; csects `FIOMDLY` &middot; instrs `@RAW` &middot; procs `MSC` &middot; files `src/iop.c` &middot; run `v55a`
- **#15** *(emulator)* exec_RDL read its count with a HALFWORD access masked to 16 bits and did not ignore the address LSB.
  - evidence: BCE POO: count is bits 14-31 of the main storage FULLWORD, LSB of the address ignored, range 0..262143. Same defect already fixed in its twin #TDL. LATENT: #RDL never executes here (BCE18 uses #RDLI).
  - instrs `#RDL,#RDLI,#TDL` &middot; procs `BCE18` &middot; files `src/iop_bce_instr.c` &middot; doc `BCE PoO IBM-6246556A pt3 (count = bits 14-31 of the fullword)` &middot; run `v84a`
- **#32** *(method)* gpc-buslog.py's KIND table had both enum pairs transposed. Correct order is XMIT_WORD=0, XMIT_CMD=1, RECV_WORD=2, RECV_POLL=3 (yaGpcIntegration.h:177) -- word before command, the opposite of the way one says it.
  - evidence: The wrong table made an ordinary DEU fill (1 command + 100 data words, emitted at a silence boundary) decode as '100 commands issued in zero simulated time', which reads like a bus program spinning. Cross-check that settles it: bus 6 XMIT_WORD must equal the DEU model's own wordsIn -- 111164/111164 for bus 6, 60957 for bus 7, 61477 for bus 8, all exact. Analyses that selected kind==1 for MM commands were unaffected (that IS XMIT_CMD) and are independently corroborated by the MMU model's log.
  - env `YAGPC_BUSLOG` &middot; files `gpc-buslog.py` &middot; doc `yaGpcIntegration.h:177 GpcServiceNumber` &middot; run `v91a`
- **#58** *(tape)* SECOND build defect in the same root load block: 0x1e8, 0x202, 0x218 and 0x22c hold REAL but WRONG Z-CON values, not fill. Tape/runtime have 117c, 13ec, 0be8, 0cc0; all 8 DASS dumps have 88be, 89f6, 85f4, 8660. Every DASS value has bit 0 SET (so a sector is applied) and every one of ours has it CLEAR (so the target is taken as sector 0). Deliberately NOT patched by tools/patch_root_zcons.py -- these want their own diagnosis rather than a hand-applied constant.
  - evidence: DIAGNOSED 2026-09-11 (#75): the four values are 2x the DASS value mod 2^16 (117c = 2*88be, 13ec = 2*89f6, 0be8 = 2*85f4, 0cc0 = 2*8660) -- every relocation to #CDSPSPC/#CDPDSPC/#CDXCCCS/#CDXRDMM was applied twice because each module was linked twice. The 'bit 0 clear' was the carry out of the doubling, not a missing sector. v42: all four equal SSW and G9; root block 0x1a8-0x247 160/160. ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Found by comparing the whole 160-halfword load block at dest=0x1a8 against DASS G9.fcm: 40 of 160 halfwords differed, 36 of them the 18 fill Z-CONs, leaving exactly these 4. The #ZDCDDS4/ACOS collision was in the whole-memory G9 link only.
  - addrs `1e8,202,218,22c,1e2` &middot; csects `#ZDCDDS4,ACOS` &middot; tape `v36,v41,v42` &middot; symptom `zcon high bit clear,sector 0 target`
- **#64** *(method)* YAGPC_BUSLOG LOST ITS TAIL on every SIGINT-terminated run -- which is how every HEALTHY run ends -- because its 1 MB stdio buffer (~87,000 events) was never written. Worse, when bus traffic collapses (the thing the log exists to catch) the whole collapse fits in that buffer and vanishes, so the file simply stops before the answer. Fixed: fflush once per simulated second.
  - evidence: b901.bus ended at 292.0 s and b201.bus at 273.4 s of 404 s runs, both at ~32 MB. With normal DEU polling at ~1,500 events/s the missing 110-130 s would have forced several flushes, so the truncation itself implied a collapse -- but hid when.
  - env `YAGPC_BUSLOG` &middot; files `src/run.c` &middot; run `b901,b201`
- **#67** *(method)* YAGPC_RINGTRIG could not see FULLWORD stores: cpu_store_fw passed the 32-bit value to a 16-bit comparison, so STM, ST and every PSW save were invisible and the trigger's silence read as 'nothing ever wrote this'. Fixed: a fullword store is tested as two halfword stores at addr and addr+1.
  - evidence: W901 watched FP's R7 slot (932e) for acf8, the value STM would put there, and never fired -- through a run whose ring shows FPMGMTIM's BR 7 landing on 0x1acf8 eight times.
  - env `YAGPC_RINGTRIG` &middot; files `src/cpu.c` &middot; run `W901`

The cause throughout is the build's UNRESOLVED CROSS-PHASE RELOCATIONS; the real repair is in the per-phase links (see #59, #60), and the tool is a labelled workaround until then.

INTERACTIVE, by the user with MEDS on v41: OPS 201 (UNIV PTG), 301 (DEORB MNVR COAST, title now shown), 302 (DEORB MNVR EXEC), 801 (FCS/DED DIS C/O), 901 and many GNC 9 displays, 101 (LAUNCH TRAJ 1). Tool check: v36 -> tool -> byte-identical to v41; a second run fills nothing, 0 refused, exit 0. An intermediate version with wildcarded neighbours but no distinctiveness guard wrote a Z-CON value into fill inside an UNRELATED load block (flat 1186050) -- the MIN_ANCHOR guard exists because of that.

Found and fixed on the way, each verified by comparison: (1) compilePASS needs --sdl (160 PROGRAM objects otherwise gain START/stack ERs) and --release=OI340700 (CPUSLS/CPTOSV CARDTYPE ACBC; else 8 phase-15 objects cascade away) -- then 1,981/1,981 objects match in ESD/TXT/RLD; (2) dfg was Don's UNCOMMITTED working tree: pinned as upstream 7d90b05 + toolchain-patches/dfg-7d90b05-to-OI340700.patch, verified file-for-file; (3) compilePASS's dfg --release probe never matched because rich styles the two dashes separately -- fixed; without it CS2120 VPD is 00D3 where the S2 DASS dump has 00D5; (4) lib/runtime/RUN is RUNASM with --fill=C6C6 (205/205 byte-identical), ZCON from compilePASS; (5) SYSLIBL1 = one hard link per SD, name order, first definer wins, START excluded (4,275/4,275); (6) sdfpad = 3360-byte SDFs + one zero byte; pchsrc = SSSRC/PCH*.asm without extension; (7) per-phase csect tables regenerate byte-identically from PFS/mafgen; extsyms-02-plus.json (generator lost) committed; (8) DEU load modules = 24 blocks extracted from pass-910.mmv into inputs/deu-loadmodules.mmv; (9) CSPCLB source fixes committed as a patch; (10) toolchain = fork db9d34b + 3 branches, tree a68da6e6 verified; (11) con80build links NOTHING unless build/lib/runtime/{RUN,ZCON} exist in the toolchain checkout -- found only by following the document from an empty directory; now pointed at our own runtime. The build is deterministic (two identical builds byte-identical).

- **#85** *(emulator)* With MEDS2.py (or any display unit in another process) the GPCIPL page is hit-and-miss: the clock counts over a blank page. Cause: the DK peer answers LATE IN WALL TIME. MEDS2.py's IDP shares one Python event loop with the MDU's drawing and sometimes cannot read the socket for tens of ms; GPCIPL gives the DK BCE a 5.0 ms message time out (MTO 303) and the MSC gives up sooner, so the BCE error-terminates (ERROR 44 BCE TIME-OUT N RETRY FAIL-DEUIPL, 96 REAL TIME MSC TIME OUT, 97, 99). GPCIPL re-IPLs the unit (a second zero-fill load) and after a second failure gives up on it, never sending the one-shot 509+254-halfword menu fill; time fills still flow, hence the clock. Fixed in yaGPC2: a peer in another process may HOLD the machine (no simulated time passes) while a reply it owes is on its way -- iop.c peerWait, bcenet_framer_peer_wait -- so the reply lands inside the software's own window.
  - evidence: Reproduced in private network namespaces (unshare -rn, Xephyr, Mesa EGL): v44 tape, two MEDS2.py instances, scripted discretePanel. Without the hold 4 of 4 runs failed: DEU zero-filled 2-3 times, menu never sent, 46-60 BCE6 RECV TIMEOUTs (waited=5.00 ms), SVCs 44/96/97/99. With it 7 of 7 showed the menu (screenshot: GPCIPL MENU (1) ... GPCIPL 09.05.00.00.01 LOADED): one load, 0-1 BCE6 time outs, ~150 holds per run, longest 57-65 ms wall. Full run to PASS (ITEM 1 EXEC on KYBD1, RUN): GPC MEMORY drawn by MEDS2.py, 378 holds, 0 time outs. User's logs: 0911-0821 one load then menu; 0911-0904 two loads. Lengthening the time out instead was already refuted (iop.c RECV_TIMEOUT_FLOOR_US: 20 ms floor = load never completes). Headless --deu-model unaffected (no hook installed): DEU image byte-identical; unit tests identical counts to HEAD.
