- **#141** *(emulator)* THE CAM NEVER SHOWED A VOTE THE FLIGHT SOFTWARE SET AND CLEARED BETWEEN TWO SAMPLES, HAD NO DIAGONAL AT ALL, AND MISNAMED THE FAIL-DISCRETE REGISTER'S TOP BIT AS A SELF VOTE. The fail-vote register was published by sampling every 1024 steps, so a vote set and reset within ~2 ms never reached a panel; nothing published a computer's Computer Fail (the CAM diagonal); and discretes.c named register bit 0x10 'fail vote N+0 (self)'. SOURCES: IOP POO (IBM 6246556A) @SFD 'allows the MSC ACC bits 0,1,2,3, and 4 to be logically OR'ed with the Fail Discrete register ... Setting bit 0 will inhibit the output of the four fail discretes from bits 1-4', so 0x10 is the OUTPUT INHIBIT and 0x08..0x01 are fail discretes 1-4 = votes against N+1..N+4 (rotation confirmed by le-g5-0). RM status bit 15 VOTER FAIL LATCH 'in normal operation would indicate Computer Failure ... set ... when the hardware voter receives at least two of four inputs'; FCMSFAIL.asm lights it on purpose -- 'IF SELF IS ALONE AND NOT EXACTLY 2 GPC SET ... ISSUE GPC TEST VOTER COMMAND TO LIGHT SELF'S DIAGONAL' (LOAD TEST REGISTER 8810 with FCMFAIL F'3', 2 votes) -- and FCMSVC SVC 25 request 2 op 2 'SET GPC FAIL LIGHT' does the same. FIX: MSC.failDiscSeen records every @SFD bit and run.c publishes a sampled-away vote as both edges; new discrete register DISCRETES_REG_CFAIL (5, bit 31) carries the diagonal, composed FOR THE LAMP ONLY -- the rmVoterFail self test, or two non-inhibited votes against the computer (vehicle_votes_against), latched until the mode switch goes to HALT -- so nothing the flight software reads changes (fail votes between IOPs are still not fed to the emulated voter, whose latch would halt the processors); bit 0x10 renamed 'inhibit fail discrete outputs'. discretePanel: discretes.py knows REG_CFAIL; cam.py listens on all five GPC channels, un-rotates each row (0x10 blanks it), draws the diagonal from REG_CFAIL, asks each computer for both registers at start-up, takes messages one at a time and holds every lit lamp HOLD_MIN_S = 40 ms (the user's floor). VERIFIED: gate cm-regress ALL MATCH; offline cam.py test (cells 14/34/54/24/44, inhibit, VALUE, a 0.5 ms pulse held to 40 ms, a 60 ms pulse cleared at once); cf-short-0 (2 GPCs, YAGPC_MMU_READ_LATENCY_US=0 to bring back the #139 overlay drop): GPC1 0x08 lit cell 12 (held 40 ms), GPC2 0x01 lit cell 21, no diagonal; cf-g3-0 (3 GPCs, same fault): the set broke up with every computer alone at shared 698.61 s and cam.py lit 11, 22 and 33, out after 0.20-0.84 s. The two-votes-against path of the diagonal was not exercised.
  - addrs `1948c` &middot; csects `FCMSFAIL,FCMSFCAM,FCMSVOTE,FCMSVC,FCMCVTM` &middot; instrs `@SFD,@RFD,@LAR` &middot; procs `MSC,RM` &middot; env `YAGPC_SYNCTRACE,YAGPC_MMU_READ_LATENCY_US` &middot; files `src/iop.h,src/iop.c,src/iop_msc_instr.c,src/discretes.h,src/discretes.c,src/vehicle.h,src/vehicle.c,src/run.h,src/run.c,discretePanel/cam.py,discretePanel/discretes.py` &middot; symptom `CAM lamps,fail vote pulse lost,CAM diagonal,computer fail lamp,fail discrete inhibit bit` &middot; doc `IBM 6246556A @SFD/@RFD and RM status; FCMSFAIL.asm; FCMSVC.asm` &middot; run `cm-regress,cf-short-0,cf-g3-0`
- **#142** *(emulator)* PASS'S GMT RAN BEHIND THE REAL TIME OF DAY BY HOWEVER LONG THE GPC WAS HELD IN HALT. The MTU reported cpu.dateTimeAnchorEpochSec (host clock at start-up) plus the calling GPC's own simulated elapsed time; a GPC held in HALT (setting up the IPL) accrues no simulated time, and rtpacer_resync writes that wall time off on release, so FPMMTURM initialised GMT that much late. FIX: the timing unit is a box that kept running -- mtumodel_set_clock_offset adds the calling computer's written-off time (BatchRunner.writtenOffUs = rtPacer.statRebaseLostMs x factor, refreshed by batchrunner_resync at both resync sites) to its time of day only; the computer's own clock, and so GPCIPL's mission time from 0, is unchanged. YAGPC_TIMEFILL now also prints the host's time of day. VERIFIED: run gmt-4 (headless-gpcmem.sh, YAGPC_TIMEFILL=1): 16 PASS time fills, GMT minus host 0 to +1 s (rounding); same run's gate ALL MATCH; gate mt-regress ALL MATCH. ALSO SETTLED WHILE CHASING A 'GPCIPL CLOCK COUNTS FAST AFTER IPL' REPORT (do not re-investigate on the emulator side): the CPU is genuinely halted in HALT (sim 0.00 through 18 s held, one resync writes it off); after release sim time runs 1.00 and interval timer 1 fires 50 per 2 s; GPCIPL's time fills advance 10 s per 10 s wall (clk-0/1 headless discretePanel, clkO6-0 panelO6.py -- its break-before-make gap is already ignored by mode_switch_held_uncached); with --bce-network and MEDS2.py, IDP on throughout (clknet-1), IDP off throughout (clknet-0) and IDP switched on at 60 s by an IDP_POWER message (clknet-2/3/5/6), peer holds are ms and never repaid as a sprint; a wire sniffer on _IDP1 saw the IDP's CLOCK messages step exactly 1 s per 1 s; MEDS2's MDU drew each second TWICE (GPCIPL time-fills every poll, 2 Hz) and screenshots of the window read 00:46 to 00:56 over 9.95 s. FAZ2DEU bumps TIME only on polls that start (no backlog), REALEXEC reloads clock 1 as current+PC1STD (drift-free catch-up, not exercised here). Harness trap found on the way: --rt-idle-timeout is MILLISECONDS; scripts passing seconds abort on any wait longer than that.
  - csects `FPMMTURM,FAZ2DEU,REALEXEC,GPCRTOPT` &middot; env `YAGPC_TIMEFILL,YAGPC_PACETRACE,YAGPC_CLKTRACE,YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE,NSTS_IDP_POWER,NSTS_CLOCK_LOG` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/mtumodel.h,src/run.c,src/run.h,src/deumodel.c` &middot; symptom `GMT behind real time,time of day lags HALT duration,GPCIPL clock counts fast,header clock racing,MEDS clock` &middot; run `gmt-4,mt-regress,clk-0,clk-1,clkO6-0,clknet-0,clknet-1,clknet-2,clknet-3,clknet-5,clknet-6,clknet-7`
- **#143** *(emulator)* SEVERAL GPCs COULD NOT RUN AGAINST REAL NETWORK DISPLAYS (--bce-network with MEDS2.py): the planned per-computer fan-out of bus traffic was never built. One BceNetTransport serves the vehicle with one socket per bus, and every machine's BceNetFramer read that socket directly, so a display unit's reply went to whichever computer's thread read first; and the transport drops everything from its own transmit socket as an echo, so no computer ever saw another's command word, which a Listen Mode receive waits for (busword.h). Every earlier multi-GPC success used the in-process display model. FIX (bcenet_framer.c): framers register in a process-wide table; with more than one, every datagram read from buses 1-23 is appended to the queue of every framer that uses that bus, whoever read it, and a computer's command word is shown to the others as a command-sync word after clearing their queues (a new command ends the last transaction), as the in-process models show listeners the commander's command (#136, #137); data words are not copied; bus 24 is per computer and excluded; one mutex, taken only when fanning out; queue elements widened to 32 bits for the mark. With one framer nothing changes. ALSO (run.c): vehicle_dk_claim is skipped when no in-process display unit exists -- panelO6.py's one BFC CRT select reaches every computer, and the claim, which only arbitrates in-process units, flipped between them on every pass (53,000 'dual commanders' lines in netrs2-0). VERIFIED: gate fo-regress ALL MATCH; fo-net1 (1 GPC, MEDS2, IDP on) menu loaded, 175 clock sends, no queue overflow; netrs2-1 (scratchpad netrs2b.sh: yaGPC2 --gpcs 1,2 --bce-network, MEDS2.py crt1/idp1, panelO6.py --script one column per GPC, keys injected on KYBD1, DEU LOAD before GPC2's IPL): set formed 452.1 s and held to the end at 715.1 s, SVC/timer/SSIP counts equal per 20 s bin, no votes, no FCMSFAIL, no FIOMGSNC error, no queue overflow, 0 dual claims, both computers stopped on SIGINT, and the final MEDS2 screenshot shows UNIV PTG 2011 with the real time of day 256/20:48:27. PROCEDURE NOTE: a second computer's GPCIPL needs DEU LOAD pushed on the display's pane first, since the first computer already loaded the unit (netrs2-0 without it: GPC2's clock ran on a blank page).
  - csects `FIODEUPG,GPCIPL` &middot; procs `DK6,MIA` &middot; env `YAGPC_BARRIER_US,NSTS_IDP_POWER` &middot; files `src/bcenet_framer.c,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `multi-GPC with MEDS2,network display replies split between computers,listener never sees command word,dual commanders log flood,GPCIPL menu missing on second IPL` &middot; run `fo-regress,fo-net1,netrs2-0,netrs2-1`
- **#144** *(emulator)* OVERNIGHT 2-GPC NETWORKED RUN LOST 1h14m OF SIMULATED TIME AND THE REDUNDANT SET BROKE while the screen was locked. MECHANISM, reproduced and fixed: MEDS2.py served each IDP's buses on the GUI thread (QSocketNotifier), the thread that also draws every MDU, so anything holding that thread held every poll reply; the GPC then waits on its display (peer wait, up to 200 ms each), the pacer carries the deficit as debt (and repays it at ~1.37x once replies are prompt again), and late replies become I/O ERROR CRTn and fail-to-sync. FIX (MEDS2.py): class BusPump -- one daemon thread with a selector over every IDP bus socket plus the IDP heartbeat timer; IDP.start() moves its buses there, so DEUUnit, replies, fills to the MDUs, IDP POWER and DEU LOAD all run off the GUI thread; the MDUs keep their buses on Qt; sys.setswitchinterval(0.001). NSTS_IDP_THREAD=0 restores the old path; NSTS_GUI_STALL_MS (and NSTS_GUI_STALL_BUSY) is the test hook. INFERRED, not reproduced: that the screen lock was what held the GUI thread (NVIDIA driver throttling swaps for unseen windows).
  - evidence: Overnight run (simulatePASS --gpcs 1,2, 22:14:04 start): cam.log 21 ON / 12 ON, never OFF, last write 22:59:19; mate-screensaver lock-enabled, idle 30 min, unlock 04:44:50; no suspend; NVS 510 driver 470.256.02; clock lag 1h06m36s at 05:05:03 repaid 22 s/min (03:57:05 -> 03:58:27 over 60.0 s wall), ~1h14m at unlock; GPC2 I/O ERROR CRT2 every ~5 s, last at simulated 05:14:28 = ~04:43 wall. A/B test 2026-09-14, one GPC IPL -> ITEM 1 EXEC -> PASS on CRT1, NSTS_GUI_STALL_MS=500 per 16 ms redraw tick, YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE=1, 240 s: B (NSTS_IDP_THREAD=0) never reached the PASS display, 276 peer holds of which 176 got no reply, median 200.0 ms, 46.4 s held; A (threaded, busy stall holding the GIL) reached PASS, 282 holds, 0 unanswered, median 6.5 ms max 39 ms total 2.5 s; C (threaded, sleeping stall) reached PASS, 132 holds, 0 unanswered, median 0.31 ms max 74 ms total 0.25 s. Regression without stall: simulatePASS --gpcs 1,2 --crts 2 with the crt2 panel/keys scripts through OPS 2: meds1 load started 2 / complete 2 / DEU LOAD 1 / PASS geometry 2 / 14 EXEC; meds2 load started 1 / complete 0 / PASS geometry 1; CAM 12,21 ON then OFF (set formed and held) -- every count identical to the same script with the old MEDS2 (run crt2). CONFIRMED IN USE (owner, 2026-09-14 afternoon): a simulatePASS --gpcs 1-4 --crts 2 run started after the fix, left with the host display allowed to switch off for about 3 hours, still tracked real time on the MEDS header clock afterwards and the CAM showed no synchronization loss.
  - csects `FCMSFAIL,FCMISYNC` &middot; procs `DK7,BCE7,IDP2` &middot; env `NSTS_IDP_THREAD,NSTS_GUI_STALL_MS,NSTS_GUI_STALL_BUSY,YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE` &middot; files `discretePanel/MEDS2.py,src/rtpacer.c,src/bcenet_framer.c,discretePanel/simulatePASS.py` &middot; symptom `clock lags real time then runs fast,CAM 12 21 lit,I/O ERROR CRT2,BCE STRG 1 NSP,BCE STRG 3 NSP,TIME,fail to sync overnight,screen lock,screensaver` &middot; tsec `22:59-04:44 wall` &middot; run `stall-A,stall-B,stall-C,thr2,crt2`

In each loss the other three vote the computer out and its own CAM diagonal lights. Looks like a small per-event probability, not a start-up transient, so one clean run proves nothing. Cause not investigated; no sync traces were on.
  - evidence: Runs perf-A-default .. perf-F-notrace (CPU measurement matrix, 2026-09-14): simulatePASS.py --gpcs 1-4 --crts 2, MEDS2.py on --bce-network, cam.py, panelO6.py script (every GPC IPLs from MM1 via BFC CRT 2+3 and IDP 2 LOAD, one at a time, RUN at 162/342/522/702 s), NBAT keys 715-819 s (target set 1-4, strings 1-4 to GPC 1-4, CRT1 GPC1, CRT2 GPC2, MM1 GPC1, MM2 GPC2), OPS 2 0 1 PRO at 840 s, run 1230 s, tape OI340700-v44boot.mmv. CAM (cam.log, file mtime vs OPS request): A defaults GPC3 out at +26 s (cells 13 23 43 and diagonal 33); B --barrier-spin-us 20 GPC2 at +170 s (12 32 42, 22); C --barrier-spin-us 0 none; D --rt-idle-poll-ms 4 none; E --rt-min-sleep-ms 10 GPC4 at +158 s (14 24 34, 44); F defaults without YAGPC_PACETRACE GPC4 at +26 s. Every run reached UNIV PTG 2011 on both CRTs, no fault message on CRT1, barrier 0 abandoned, all four GPCs holding rate 1.000 (2.00 s sim per 2 s wall around the loss in A). No knob pattern: the defaults both lost and C/D kept the set, so the pacing options are not the cause. CONTROLS THAT HELD: le-g4-0 and cr-g4-0 (#139/#140: rs40.sh/rs41.sh) synced about 200 s with no votes -- but they used in-process --deu-model display units, no --bce-network, YAGPC_DEUKEYS, GPC2 and GPC4 loading from MM2, and YAGPC_SYNCTRACE=1, and their ~200 s windows are shorter than two of these losses. The user's hand-run 4-GPC 2-CRT networked simulatePASS set (logs discretePanel/simulatePASS-logs, 2026-09-14 10:45, defaults) held from 12:59 to 16:23 (3 h 24 min) with the CAM dark -- all four diagonals lit and cleared together at 12:59:01, during the OPS 2 transition, not at shutdown (corrected 2026-09-14 20:10; its logs are simulatePASS-logs/prev-0914-185508, run 12:47-16:23). Differences from that run to test first: the scripted pace (GPC4 RUN only 138 s before OPS 2; NBAT at 8 s steps), all IPLs from MM1. NEXT: rerun the scripted networked config with YAGPC_SYNCTRACE and the FIOERRLC/FCMSFAIL entry traces (as in #136/#137) to see which sync program or I/O error takes the computer out, and whether it is FIOERRLC+0313's one-GPC-with-errors rule on a DK bus (6-9) now that real MEDS2 units answer over UDP. HAND-WORKED RUN THAT FAILED (2026-09-14 evening, discretePanel/simulatePASS-logs, defaults, simulatePASS --gpcs 1-4 --crts 2 --size 384): started 18:55:08; GPC4 MODE RUN at 19:04:09 (last yaGPC2.log write); the OPS 2 overlay load (panel.log MM2 ACTIVITY BUSY/READY x9) ended before the panel's last write at 19:06:52 (IPL SOURCE MMU 1 -> OFF); keyboard1 OPS 2 0 1 PRO then SPEC 6 PRO (log mtime 19:07:00); cam.log changed last at 19:19:26: 44, 34, 24, 14 ON -- GPC1-3 voted GPC4 out, GPC4 lit its diagonal. So the loss came ~13 min after OPS 2. yaGPC2.log has nothing after the MODE lines; the voted-out GPC4 thread still used 88% of a core afterwards like the other three. Note the owner switched IPL SOURCE back to MMU 1 after GPC4 RUN and then OFF (panel.log) -- IPL source is only consulted in HALT, unlikely to matter, unverified. The earlier 3+ hour clean hand run (10:45 logs) is therefore not a control that the configuration works, only a long sample without a loss. HANDOFF-SYNC-MARGIN.md plans the graded measurement this needs. HOST EVENTS CHECKED 2026-09-14 evening (owner's hypothesis: swap, large memory moves, file I/O freezing the host): (1) journalctl +-2 min around all five losses (16:40:57, 17:03:56, 18:05:31, 18:23:56, 19:19:26): no kernel messages at all (no OOM, hung task, disk error); nearest entries routine cron jobs 30 s to 2 min before, an update-cache refresh 30 s AFTER F's loss and timedated 19 s AFTER the hand run's -- nothing before a loss. sysstat collection is inactive (cron runs debian-sa1, service disabled), so there is no minute history. (2) SWAP RULED OUT for the hand run: swap 1.6 of 2 GB used (swappiness 60, 49 GB RAM free, pswpin 669k pages since boot), but yaGPC2, both MEDS2, cam, panelO6 and the keyboards had VmSwap 0 and ZERO major page faults since their 18:55 start, through the 19:19 loss. PSI totals since boot (7 days): memory full 365 s, io full 6081 s. (3) LOG-WRITE STALLS NOT NEEDED: the hand run logs to /mnt/STORAGE (/dev/sda1, 8 TB spinning HGST), but the scripted runs A-F logged under /tmp on the NVMe root and 4 of 6 still lost a GPC. (4) NO LONG THREAD STALL: every traced loss run reported barrier 0 abandoned (BARRIER_MAX_HOLD_SEC 0.25). (5) DISPLAY PEER HOLDS DO NOT LINE UP: pace-trace 'peer holds: N (M answered)' -- unanswered includes a reply that started and then ran out of its partial budget (bcenet_framer_peer_wait: PEER_HOLD_PARTIAL_SECONDS 5 ms MINUS the time already held for that command, cumulative). Unanswered increments after OPS 2, seconds from driver start: A loss GPC3 ~866 -- GPC4 1013, GPC1 1040; B loss GPC2 ~1011 -- GPC4 987, GPC3 997; E loss GPC4 ~999 -- GPC3 1009, GPC1 1072; clean C -- GPC3 1005, GPC4 1031; clean D -- GPC3 1009, GPC4 1019, GPC1 1038. The voted-out computer never had an unanswered hold near its loss (GPC3's only one in A was at 371 s, its IPL), clean runs have as many, and all runs show 2-3 of them ~150-200 s after OPS 2, which looks like a PASS event rather than host stalls. STILL OPEN: GPCs 3 and 4 are only ever LISTENERS on the DK buses (CRT1 GPC1, CRT2 GPC2), and a listener does not peer-hold, so a late display reply reaching a listener is untested; and sub-250 ms stalls of one thread are not recorded anywhere. Traces for that exist: YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE (+_PE, _FROM; run.c prints the peer hold held ms), YAGPC_ERRTERM_TRACE, YAGPC_SYNCORDER. CORRECTION AND LEAD HYPOTHESIS (owner, 2026-09-14 20:03): the scripted comparisons above are weaker than stated -- runs B-E changed pacing/barrier settings, so only A and F (defaults, both lost a GPC, logs on NVMe) are like-for-like; 'failed as often' means 2 of 2 defaults. The owner's observation: the one long clean run (12:59-16:23) was while the machine was UNATTENDED, and the losses -- the hand run's and the scripted ones -- came while the owner was actively using the desktop (opening and closing large programs such as an email reader). Not testable from logs after the fact: the user and system journals have no entries in the 2 min before any loss (desktop program launches do not log there), and /proc shows only programs still running (java 3.2 GB, WebKit, chrome, all started 19:48 or later, after the 19:19 loss). So it needs a controlled test: default-setting runs, quiet vs deliberately disturbed at timestamped moments, with a scheduling-latency and stall recorder (HANDOFF-SYNC-MARGIN.md). SECOND LOSS IN THE SAME HAND RUN, WITH THE DESKTOP BUSY (2026-09-14): cam.log changed at 19:51:46.07 -- 24 OFF, 22 ON, 32 ON, 12 ON: GPC2 left the set (its vote against the already-failed GPC4 cleared), lit its own diagonal, and GPC1 and GPC3 voted it out, leaving GPC1+GPC3. The owner noticed at ~20:09. Host activity around it, from /proc start times and git: Eclipse C/C++ 2024-06 (/mnt/STORAGE/home/rburkey/eclipse/cpp-2024-06, java -Xms1g -Xmx4g, RSS 3.2 GB) started 19:48:22 and had used 99.5 s of CPU by 20:10 -- a heavy start-up, very likely CDT indexing off the spinning disk, 3 min 24 s before the loss; its WebKitWebProcess started 19:48:56; git commits 19:48:42 (Claude, ledger) and 19:49:32 (owner, discretePanel README); two chrome renderers (new tabs) at 19:51:54-55, 8 s AFTER the loss; nothing Claude ran between 19:48:42 and 19:54. Journals: nothing. Consistent with the owner's desktop-activity hypothesis (the first loss at 19:19:26 came while the owner was writing an email), not proof: launches of programs that have since exited cannot be seen, and nothing records sub-second host stalls. TRACED EVENING RUN eve1 (defaults, simulatePASS port base 25000, YAGPC_SYNCORDER/ERRTERM_TRACE/TIMEOUT_TRACE on DK 6-9, atop 1 s, 1 ms latency probe; HANDOFF-SYNC-MARGIN.md): LOSS at 21:02:17.248 with the owner away -- GPC4 out, its diagonal lit. Sync margins normal to the end (13 s before: worst 0.18, p99 ~0.11). GPC4 was last in its final nine SVC handshakes, lag growing 12 -> 475 us (beyond the 25 us barrier, so extra execution on GPC4, not holding), then issued an SVC sync code (tshared 1118.793778 s) that no other GPC matched, held it 5.35 ms (past FCMSNTO2 3850 us), returned to null and never synced again, while GPC1-3 continued. So GPC4 DIVERGED and failed itself -- not a slow handshake, not a self-only I/O error (ERRTERM identical on all four: BCE 8 absent IDP3, 20, 22). Host: one blip 3-4 s earlier (21:02:13, a new chrome renderer, 140% CPU, RDELAY 0.12 s, probe max 2.96 ms), none at the moment (+-1 s mapping). New hypothesis: host delays act through INPUT DIVERGENCE -- wall-time data from networked peers read by each GPC at its own simulated instant -- not through sync spread, which the barrier hides. eve1 LOSS 2 at 21:04:12.354, owner away: GPC2 out (diagonal), leaving GPC1+GPC3. Host: nothing (probe no wake > 2 ms; RDELAY/BDELAY 0.00 for yaGPC2 and MEDS2). Margins normal; GPC2's last handshakes -- two IPR (routine, ~9/s) and an IOC at tshared 1233.870930 s -- COMPLETED with all three, then GPC2 returned to null and never synced again; no unmatched GPC2 event; in ~20 s of log no ERRTERM off BCE 8/20/22, no BCE6/7 receive time-out, no unanswered peer hold (one BCE7 hold 40.59 ms -> reply ~1 s before). So neither eve1 loss shows its cause in these traces: no self-only I/O error, no near-timeout handshake, no host stall at the moment. Next: landmarks on FCMSFAIL entry and its call sites so a loss names its FCOS path. eve3 (landmark YAGPC_LANDMARKS=1948c:FCMSFAIL,19674:FCMSFINT; FCMSFINT spent its 20 hits at the IPLs and OPS 2 transition). OFFSET RULE: a GPC rejoining the barrier at HALT->RUN logs a second SYNCORDER-JOIN; use the last join with own <= the landmark t. LOSS 1 21:44:04.321 (disturbance block 1, host quiet): GPC4 SLIPPED ONE I/O-COMPLETION HANDSHAKE -- on IOC handshake 1 (GPC1-3 issue 1986.272490-.272513, GPC4 .272522) GPC1-3 returned to null at .272741-.272769 but GPC4 held IOC until the others' NEXT IOC handshake (.273655-.273681, all null .27389-.27392), then ran one behind; its 4th IOC (.275310) was answered by nobody; FCMSFAIL landmark on GPC4 at shared 1986.279441 = 4.13 ms later (FCMISYNC timeout + handling), GPC1-3 at ~1986.2850 (voting). Issue-time spread was only 9-32 us -- the spread metric cannot see a slip; the quantity is the common overlap of all members' held codes. No GPC4-only error in the log around it. eve1's GPC4 loss (last SVC held 5.35 ms, unmatched) is very likely the same slip. LOSS 2 21:46:37.531 (block 1, host quiet): GPC3 out. A GPC3-ONLY I/O ERROR: 'ERRTERM gpc=3 bce=20 pc=1cc2e left=1' at shared 2139.468247 -- timing-unit bus 20, a DIFFERENT pc from the steady all-GPC BCE20 errors (1cc32, GPC1 1cc1c), ONE word short (the steady ones are left=25), no matching error on GPC1/GPC2; then IPR handshakes at .470284 and .471090 (GPC3 238 us late on the second), an IOC handshake completing at .473033, and GPC3 ALONE entering FCMSFAIL at .473263 with no handshake pending; GPC1/GPC2 at .484938/.484945. That is FIOERRLC's one-GPC-with-errors self-FTS -- the #136 family (a listener taking different words from a shared timing-unit model than the commander) -- an emulator defect, not a host event. 2026-09-14/15 overnight (fixed build bca2581d5): the unexplained ('class b') losses -- eve6 x2, eve8 x2, eve10 x2, eve11 -- are ONE mechanism. Within a run they fall at exact multiples of 12.000 s shared simulated time, phase 11.95 s. Each is a SPLIT I/O-completion handshake: some GPCs issue IPR (010), the others IOC (001); stall/splits.py finds such splits after OPS 2 only at that phase (eve11 1151.947, 1163.947, 1187.947, 1199.947). eve11 (keys-late, OPS 2 at 1140 s -- late keys did NOT help): GPC4 IPR at 1151, 1163, 1187 -> FIOERRLC's error count reached 2 -> self fail-to-sync, R7=19dc7 (FIOERRLC+0313) at FCMSFAIL entry, R4=0018=FIONSPID (NSP device), R5=4; GPC1-3 entered FCMSFAIL from FCMISYNC+00BB (R7=18f4f). YAGPC_ERRTERM_TRACE (all BCEs) prints NOTHING at any post-OPS 2 split, so the NSP error is decided by flight software (FIOBCERR/FIOMSCTO/FIOMMERR/FIOPSDTO), not by an emulator time-out. Split sits ~0.5 ms before a FIODEUPG poll arm (BCE6/7/8 every 0.48 s; 12 s = 25 polls). eve13 traces those four FIOERRLC entries. eve13 (RANGETRACE on FIOERRLC's error sources): THE NSP ERROR IS AN MSC TIME-OUT (FIOMSCTO, 19b9d: a listener BCE still busy when the MSC's wait ends). At the fatal split 1163.9476 only G1 (BCE22) and G4 (BCE20+22) took FIOMSCTO for IOQE 905a, the NSP cyclic read; at 1151.948 all four did, so no split. FIOMSCTO is ROUTINE on every GPC (IOQE 6764 x4938, 6772 x4936, 905a x1611, 904c x1239, 6780 x1239 in eve13), each GPC's mask naming the FF buses it only listens on. Every BCE20-23 receive in eve13 is loaded mto=2 (33 us) and runs timeout=2.00 ms: the 2 ms floor of #50 (CONFIRMED; safe per #52 run u2), which --bce-network keeps. TEST from eve15: YAGPC_RECV_FLOOR_US=0. eve15 (YAGPC_RECV_FLOOR_US=0) REFUTED the floor: GPC2 lost to the same 12 s split, FIOMSCTO no fewer. ROOT CAUSE: src/mtumodel.c echoed a bus command to listening computers ONLY for its own read (0x24C26), but a Listen-Mode receive on a sync-marked bus (18-22) waits indefinitely for a command at its IUAR (iop.c iop_bce_receive) -- so NSP listeners (FIONSPPG FIONSL11/31) on buses 20/22 never finished, eve13's BCE20-22 RECV ARMs stopped at OPS 2, and the MSC's single look (FIOMCNTL: 70 x 33 us = 2.31 ms) found them busy on every GPC every cycle; at the 12 s phase (MEDS DK collection 0.48 s vs downlist data cycle 1 s) the look came out differently per GPC. Introduced with #146 (deaa3bad0), which narrowed the echo from any IUA-10 command to the MTU read. FIX 273563b7f: echo every command to listeners, data only for the read. Gate echo-regress ALL MATCH. eve16 at shared 1219.5 s: FIOMSCTO 4 (one NSP read at 1141.87 on all four GPCs together) vs eve13 ~14,400; splits after OPS 2 none vs 2; FCMSFAIL 0 vs 9; listeners now arm (1ccae 48/bus, 1cc2e and 1cc68 279). LONG RUN: eve16 ran its full duration (00:49-05:54, shared 15,728 s, OPS 2 at 1140 s): FIOMSCTO 4 in total, IPR/IOC split handshakes after OPS 2 ZERO in ~14,600 s (eve13 split every 12 s), and the only fail-to-sync was the unrelated IPR slip of #148 at 6021 s; the other three GPCs held the set to the end.

- **#146** *(emulator)* THE TIMING-UNIT MODEL ANSWERED EVERY COMMAND TO IUA 10 AS A TIMING-UNIT READ, BUT IUA 10 IS THE FORWARD MDM, THROUGH WHICH THE NSPs ARE READ TOO. Each of the NSP's commands refilled the MTU's 7-word reply and reset every reader: listeners' one-word NSP power-discrete reads picked up stray MTU words (so PASS never bypassed the absent NSP and its 32-word data read failed every cycle, 25 words short), and a listener whose read straddled a refill came up empty on one GPC only -- FIOERRLC counted it as that computer's own error and at the second it failed itself out of the redundant set.
  - evidence: Flight source OI301700 SSSRC: FIOPRMPG 'FIOMTURD EQU X'00024C26' MTU READ - MDM FF01 CARD=03 CHANNEL=01 WORD COUNT=7' sent '#MINC FIOFFIUA,FIOMTURD'; FIONSPPG '#MINC FIOFFIUA,FIONSP1P' (X'26420'), FIONSPDR (X'25000'), FIONSP2P (X'27020'), FIONSPRD (X'26C7F'); FIOFFIUA EQU 10 in both. G2 csects: 1cc2e = FIONSPPG FIOBYNC3+4 (listener #RDS 0,0, NSP1 power discrete), 1cc32 = FIOBYNC3+8, 1cc1c = FIOBYNC2+6, 1cc0a/1cc44 the commander's first reads. Losses caused: eve1 GPC2 (ERRTERM gpc=2 bce=20 pc=1cc2e left=1 at shared 1233.866, final sync 5 ms later) and eve3 GPC3 (second such error at 2139.468 after a first at 1924.908; IPR handshakes, IOC, then GPC3 alone into FCMSFAIL at 2139.473263 with nothing pending) -- ledger #145. mtumodel.c's own header had noted 'the only six-word command seen on buses 20-22 is 524c26' but tested only the IUA. FIX: MTU_READ_CMD 0x024C26, CMD_FIELD(c)=c&0x7ffff; XMIT_CMD refills only when CMD_IUA==10 and CMD_FIELD==MTU_READ_CMD, every other command on buses 20-22 silences all readers. (An IUAR filter was considered and rejected: listeners' IUAR is FIOFFIUA=10 for MTU and NSP alike.) VERIFIED: regression gate nsp-regress (headless-gpcmem 420 s, single GPC) EVERY EVENT COUNTER EXACT (mmu 56/431/220731/220011/720/3-3-0, deu formatFills 8, resets 1, modeStatus 6, ipled, 0 abandoned/bite/dumps/headerless/unknown); periodic deu 1805/342/566/334/572, mtu commands 2951 (earlier gates ~7897 -- the NSP reads now bypassed) -- note an unrelated four-GPC run (eve4, old binary) was still running during it by mistake. eve5 (4 GPCs, patched) first minutes, GPC1 in OPS 0: NSP errors bus 20 (1cc0a,left 1) x2 + (1cc1c,left 32) x1, bus 22 (1cc44,left 1) x2, then none -- against x428/x427 every cycle on the old binary -- so PASS now bypasses the absent NSP. Expected consequences: no one-GPC 1cc2e errors; the owner's repeating SPEC 99 'BCE STRG 1 NSP'/'BCE STRG 3 NSP' faults appear once and stop. PENDING: eve5 in OPS 2 to confirm no one-GPC NSP error and fewer losses; the handshake-slip losses (GPC4 in eve1 and eve3) are a separate, unfixed cause.
  - addrs `1cc0a-1cc0a,1cc1c-1cc1c,1cc2e-1cc2e,1cc32-1cc32,1cc44-1cc44,1cc56-1cc56,1cc6c-1cc6c` &middot; csects `FIOPRMPG,FIONSPPG,FIOERRLC,FCMSFAIL` &middot; instrs `#MINC,#RDS,#RDLI,#CMDI` &middot; env `YAGPC_ERRTERM_TRACE,YAGPC_LANDMARKS,YAGPC_SYNCORDER` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `GPC voted out,self-only I/O error,ERRTERM bce20 left=1,BCE STRG NSP fault repeating,NSP never bypassed,timing unit reply refilled by NSP command,thread race` &middot; run `eve1,eve3,nsp-regress,eve5`
- **#147** *(emulator)* A LATER GPC'S IPL BROKE THE COMMON SET OF THE COMPUTERS ALREADY RUNNING, BECAUSE A SATURATED X SERVER STALLED panelO6.py AND yaGPC2 TREATED THE PANEL'S SILENCE AS HALT. panelO6 published its discretes from its Tk thread, which waits on the X server; with Xorg pegged at a full core (our own MEDS2/panel/CAM windows during IPL display traffic) it went quiet for more than DISCRETES_STALE_SEC (1.5 s), every running GPC's mode bits went stale at once, run.c's 'silence is HALT' rule held each CPU (2 ms sleeps, leaving the simulated-time barrier, rejoining with new offsets), and their sync handshakes timed out together.
  - evidence: eve4 (21:53:41) and eve5 (22:09:25): during GPC3's IPL, GPC1 and GPC2 (PASS OPS 0, common set) entered FCMSFAIL within ~15 ms with no I/O error; SYNCORDER-JOIN lines show GPC1/GPC2 repeatedly leaving and rejoining the barrier during each later GPC's IPL (only the held path clears barActive), and at eve5's break the shared frame jumped from GPC2's clock (~234.5 s) to GPC1's (400.0 s). New YAGPC_HELDTRACE in run heldrep (2 GPCs): 'GPC1: HELD nothing published t=84.793043 value=49000000 driven=00000000 age halt=1.510 stby=1.510 run=1.510 ipl=1.510', again at 85.77 (1.598) and 87.51 (1.742), each released by the next datagram. atop 1 s: panelO6 0-5% CPU, RDELAY 0 throughout (not busy; its Tk thread has no blocking socket call), Xorg 101% of a core 22:21:12-30 (heldrep), 79-102% 21:53:28-46 (eve4), 94-102% 22:09:09-28 (eve5); ~80% with one simulation's windows open. FIX 1 (discretePanel/panelO6.py): a publisher thread _pub_loop does all discrete sending (RESET then SET per register, on change and every REPUBLISH_MS), the Tk side only hands it the columns; 'Tk tick N ms late' logged past 200 ms; NSTS_PANEL_STALL=start,seconds test hook. FIX 2 (yaGPC2 run.c/run.h): a machine last heard in RUN or STBY rides through a silent panel until YAGPC_DISCRETES_HOLD_SEC (default 60 s) of silence, then is held; logs 'crew panel silent N s; keeping RUN', 'holding the CPU until it is heard', 'crew panel heard again'; the held decision is also re-evaluated every 4096 steps (silence sends no datagram); a last-heard RUN/STBY bit that is still SET but only stale is not a switch gap (the panel's first datagram after a silence is the RESET). A machine that never heard a position is still held. VERIFIED: stale-test (1 GPC): 5 s Tk stall -> 'Tk tick 4960 ms late', no silence in yaGPC2 (time.sleep releases the GIL, so a real X wait is not proven identical); 5 s SIGSTOP -> keeping RUN / heard again; 70 s SIGSTOP -> held at 60.0 s (bits 60.044 s old), released on resume; stale-test2 (refined): 5 s SIGSTOP -> keeping RUN / heard again with NO HELD on resume; regression gates stale-regress and stale-regress2 EVERY EVENT COUNTER EXACT. PENDING: overnight four-GPC runs on this build (eve8+) with YAGPC_HELDTRACE to confirm no IPL-time breaks; the OPS 2 losses (slips; IPR->IOC->silence with no logged error, eve6) are separate and unexplained.

- **#150** *(emulator)* The CAM diagonal latched and nothing could clear it. yaGPC2 lit a computer's Computer Fail lamp when two or more peers voted against it and kept it lit until that computer's mode switch moved into HALT -- so with the set re-formed and every vote dropped, two diagonals stayed lit while the flight software's own SPEC 6 GPC/BUS STATUS said no GPC was down. I/O RESET EXEC cleared nothing, because there were no votes left to clear. FIXED: the lamp now follows the present state -- two or more votes against this computer NOW, or its own RM voter failing its self test -- with no latch; cam.py's HOLD_MIN_S (40 ms) is what keeps a brief vote visible.
  - evidence: Seen 2026-09-16 in the owner's 4-GPC OPS 2 demo: cam.log showed 11 and 22 ON with 21, 31, 32, 41, 42, 43 all OFF (the diagonals of GPC1 and GPC2, no votes at all); 6 s of discrete bus gave GPC1 3724, GPC2 3725, GPC3 3726, GPC4 3724 sync-line changes, so all four were in a set; both CRTs were on UNIV PTG (page 2011); SPEC 6 PRO reported no GPC down; I/O RESET then EXEC changed nothing. The lamp came from run.c: 'if (vehicle_votes_against(...) >= 2) r->cfailLatched = true;' published as 'cfailLatched || rmVoterFail', with the only clear at run.c:1454 'if (mode & MODE_HALT) r->cfailLatched = false'. rmVoterFail is NOT the peers' votes: iop.c:809 sets it from the RM voter's LOAD TEST REGISTER self test. The latch was the invention (#141 added the diagonal, which had been missing); the votes themselves are the flight software's own fail discretes and had already gone. Now: 'bool votedOut = vehicle_votes_against(...) >= 2; discretes_publish_cfail(..., votedOut || rmVoterFail);' and run.h's cfailLatched removed.
  - csects `FCMSFCAM,FCMSFAIL` &middot; procs `MSC` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/run.h` &middot; symptom `CAM diagonal stuck,Computer Fail lamp,voted out,SPEC 6 says no GPC down` &middot; run `demo-0916-1120`
- **#152** *(emulator)* A GPC THAT IS NOT EXECUTING KEEPS ITS LAST OUTPUTS ON THE WIRE INSTEAD OF GOING DEAD -- the sync code, the fail-vote register and the computer-fail lamp alike. batchrunner_step returns early while mode_switch_held is true, so it reaches neither vehicle_refresh_lines nor the fail-vote and cfail publishing below, and every one of them stands on the wire for as long as the machine sits in reset. The sync code stays at 111 null, present and idle, so the set reads a halted computer as present-and-silent -- a fail-to-sync -- and votes it out rather than accepting it as departed; 000 is the code for halt/standby/dead per BILDNEW5.asm, a computer leaving a set forces it deliberately (FPMZSYNC, FCMSWMON) so the survivors do not take a fail-to-sync, and it is what FCMCSYNC admits a member on -- an observed 000 to 111 -- which a machine that never shows 000 cannot offer, so it blocks rejoining too. The fail-vote register keeps ACCUSING computers from a processor in reset.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-16 on live four-GPC runs (--gpcs 1,2,3,4 --bce-network, port base 6900). SYNC CODE: after GPC1 was voted out at t=794931.477 its last out was 111 null and it issued nothing for 3.5 hours, zero out events in the last 40 MB of a 9.7 GB YAGPC_SYNCTRACE log against ~46,900 each for GPC2/3/4, while per-thread sampling showed all four threads in state R taking equal CPU (338/338/337/333 ticks per 4 s) -- executing, not blocked. A re-IPL cleared the votes (fail-vote 2->0, 1->0, 4->0, computer fail lamp OFF, all four cam.py lamps off) and the peers re-voted the instant it was moded to HALT. FAIL-VOTE: in a later run the crew moded the vehicle down and GPC4, still running, voted against GPC1 and then GPC2 as each went silent -- correct at the time -- then went to HALT itself holding 00000006 and went on accusing both from reset until it was IPLed, when it published 0 and they cleared. FIX: the held path drives the OUT register's DISCRETES_OUT_SYNC_MASK (bits 20/24/28, X'0888') clear to the neighbours, publishes failvote 0 and cfail false, and prints one '000 dead/halt/standby (held in reset)' trace line per hold -- because what the neighbours see is changed WITHOUT touching this machine's own OUT register and discretes_synctrace prints from that register, so none of it would otherwise appear in a trace and a reader would conclude the fix was not working. The register itself is left alone: the lines are driven by reset state, not software, and cpu_reset hands FCMBOOT a fresh one on release. vehicle_refresh_lines is a no-op unless vehicle_multi, so single-GPC runs are untouched. VERIFIED IN USE: with the sync-code half in, GPC3 moded to STBY drove 000 dead (FCMSWMON zeroing X'0888'), and STBY -> RUN -> OPS 201 PRO then re-admitted it -- all four handshaking together, the three votes cleared, the CAM dark, with no IPL.

- **#159** *(emulator)* ONE GPC DRIVING THREE DISPLAYS NEEDS MORE THAN ONE CORE, AND A GPC ONLY GETS ONE -- that is why three-IDP setups lost a computer, and it is NOT the cost of the third display itself. A display powered before it has been ASSIGNED goes to whoever is already running: USA005350 3.2.15.1 (via #99) says a PASS GPC moded to RUN takes IDPs 1 TO 3 when the common set commands no DK buses, so with all three powered during the IPLs GPC1 alone drives every display while the others idle. Its thread saturates one core and still delivers only 0.42 of real time; a GPC's emulation is serial, so the host's other sixteen cores cannot help it. The barrier then correctly paces every computer to the slowest, MEDS2's WALL-CLOCK watchdogs (POLL_FAIL_MS 4000, IDP_LOST_MS 2000) start firing against a vehicle running at 0.42, the display stops answering, and a set member alone with an I/O error fails itself out (#137). Whichever computer is unlucky goes: three runs lost GPC1+GPC2, then GPC2, then GPC3. THE FIX IS TO NEVER LET THAT STATE EXIST: power each display only after the NBAT has named its commander, so no computer ever drives more than one. Three displays then cost NOTHING -- all four computers hold rate 1.00. TWO EXPLANATIONS REFUTED ALONG THE WAY, do not retry them: barrier tuning (Delta 200 cut holds by a third and moved the rate not at all) and host CPU starvation (sixteen cores idle).
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-17 on examples/4gpc-g3-startup.script (OPS 301, memory configuration 3) with --crts 2 and --crts 3. RATE, computed as the SIMULATED TIME each computer reports at exit over the run's wall duration: two CRTs 0.735/0.811/0.976/0.895, three CRTs 0.417/0.315/0.362/0.482. CORROBORATED INDEPENDENTLY BY THE SSIP CADENCE, which is 160.0 ms on every computer in the working run (medians 159.99/159.99/160.02/160.02, 10-90% inside 157.8 to 161.8) and 377-386 ms in the failing one -- 160 / 0.42 = 381, so the slowdown is exactly the rate. The dying computer is distinguishable: in the failing run it issues SSIP with a median gap of 1.08 ms, 350 times faster than its peers, for 157 iterations (~170 ms, the width of FCMASYNC's DIA sampling window) and then stops dead with no fail vote and no error line. BARRIER, from the runs' own exit lines: two CRTs 'held 54,567,630 times, 549.470 s total, 0 abandoned, 53,476,312 released while spinning, 368,722 sleeps' over 720 s wall; three CRTs 'held 217,127,677 times, 1595.210 s total, 0 abandoned, 213,693,534 released while spinning, 230,358 sleeps' over 900 s. Per-thread CPU sampled live during a three-CRT run: 100.0/100.0/100.0/100.0 percent of a core on nproc=20. RULED OUT AS THE CAUSE, though real: GPC1 takes 120 BCE8 (DK3) receive timeouts, every one gotAny=1 with left=15, median 6.19 ms and max 102.09 ms -- but they total only 810 ms in a 420 s run, 0.2%, far too little to explain the rate. Their source is visible in the MEDS logs: IDP3 logs 'load started' and NEVER 'load complete', taking 5 fills where IDP1 takes 174 and IDP2 144, so it keeps asserting IPL_REQUIRED and keeps being reloaded. An IDP that starts unpowered comes up with ipled=False by design (MEDS2.py, 'a unit then comes up cold and asks to be loaded, as it would on the vehicle'), and the script never does idpload 3 where it does idpload 1 and idpload 2. BARRIER TUNING REFUTED AS THE FIX, 2026-09-17: the same three-CRT script run with YAGPC_BARRIER_US=200 instead of 25 cut the barrier cost by a third -- held 89,838,322 times, 634.656 s total, 0 abandoned, against 217,127,677 times and 1595.210 s -- taking held thread time from 44% down to 29%, AND THE RATE DID NOT MOVE: 0.444/0.270/0.349/0.552 against 0.417/0.315/0.362/0.482. So the holding is not lost time. The fast machines are correctly waiting for a genuinely slower one, total throughput is set by the SLOWEST computer's emulation speed, and the barrier is exonerated -- do not spend another run on Delta. The prediction that removing the barrier's share would give about 0.71 was wrong. What remains is the raw cost of a single computer driving three displays, which points the fix at DISTRIBUTING the displays rather than at the barrier: before the NBAT is typed, GPC1 holds all three (USA005350 via #99: a GPC moded to RUN takes IDPs 1 to 3 when the common set commands no DK buses), and after it CRT3 belongs to GPC3. THE FIX, VERIFIED 2026-09-17: examples/4gpc-g3-startup.script now runs --crts 3 with idppower 3 / majfunc 3 / idpload 3 moved AFTER 'OPS 3 0 1 PRO', and ITEM 14 +3 giving CRT3 to GPC3 before it is powered. Result: the four-computer set forms and holds with all three displays live, SSIP cadence 160.04 / 160.05 / 160.08 / 160.04 ms against a nominal 160 -- rate 1.00 on every computer -- and ZERO BCE8 receive timeouts where powering it early cost 120. IDP3 takes its fills normally (209 in the run). The intermediate phase is its own measurement: with three MEDS2 processes running but IDP3 still powered OFF, GPC1's cadence is 159.96 ms, rate 1.00, so the extra PROCESS costs nothing and only an answering display does. RESIDUAL LIMIT, unfixed and worth knowing: a single computer driving three displays still runs at about 0.42, so any configuration that concentrates displays on one GPC -- which the real vehicle does freely -- remains beyond this emulator at real time. What was fixed is the configuration, not the emulation speed. FINAL NUMBERS FOR THE FIX, 18-minute run: whole-run rate 0.979 / 0.823 / 0.925 / 0.874 against the two-display baseline's 0.735 / 0.811 / 0.976 / 0.895 -- so three displays now cost NOTHING measurable. Zero fail-votes and zero BCE8 receive timeouts in the whole run, where powering the third display early cost 120. The DK census shows exactly one dominant commander per display with the others listening, which is what the NBAT asks for: bus 6 GPC1=877,676 (others ~192,000), bus 7 GPC2=661,661 (others ~222,000), bus 8 GPC3=1,028,140 (others ~588,000).

- **#160** *(emulator)* THE EMULATOR SPENT MORE TIME LOOKING UP HOW LONG AN INSTRUCTION TAKES THAN EMULATING IT, and more again moving UDP datagrams than doing either. Found with perf once kernel.perf_event_paranoid was lowered from Ubuntu's 4 to 1 -- before that no profiler could run at all and every earlier conclusion about where the time went was inference from rate differences, which had been wrong twice. ONE: both instruction-timing tables are keyed by the MNEMONIC AS TEXT and were searched LINEARLY ON EVERY EMULATED INSTRUCTION -- find_poo_entry strcmp'd through 134 rows of Principles-of-Operation timings and poo_override tested twenty more names for special cases, about 87 string comparisons per instruction to look up a constant for an instruction that had ALREADY been decoded. __strcmp_avx2 was 21.79% of all CPU against 5.76% for instr_decode. Both are now memoised on the desc->nm POINTER (a literal from the static instruction table, so stable and unique per opcode) and poo_override gets an early-out, since every return inside it is guarded by a strcmp on nm. TWO: with that gone the profile is dominated by the network -- grouped over a saturated four-GPC run, network and syscalls 27.5% against emulation proper 2.7%, ten to one. The transport sent ONE DATAGRAM PER sendto because the subsystems use datagram LENGTH to tell a command from data; sendmmsg keeps the distinct datagrams and the message boundaries while collapsing up to 64 syscalls into one.
  - evidence: perf 6.8.12 on kernel 6.8.0-139, yaGPC2 built -O2 -g and not stripped, kernel.perf_event_paranoid lowered 4 -> 1 and kptr_restrict 1 -> 0 by the user 2026-09-17. ONE-GPC gate workload, flat profile: __strcmp_avx2 21.79%, entry_SYSRETQ 9.60%, instr_time_poo 6.30%, instr_decode 5.76%, ap101_exec1 and iop_exec_processors ~1.5% each. Call graph: batchrunner_step -> ap101_exec1 -> cpu_exec1 -> instr_time_poo 19.75%, of which find_poo_entry (inlined) -> __strcmp_avx2 15.92%. After the memo: __strcmp_avx2 5.15%, instr_time_poo 3.31%. After poo_override's early-out: __strcmp_avx2 3.54%. SATURATED four-GPC run (three displays on one computer), whole-run rate per computer 0.315/0.362/0.417/0.482 before and 0.707/0.803/0.897/0.987 after -- roughly double. Regression gate byte-identical at every step: mmu1 56/431/220731/220011/720, deu 1816 commands / 343 fills / formatFills 8 / 575 polls, one MODE: IPL, keystrokes at poll=247 (runs reg-memo, reg-ov). MEASURED AND REJECTED, do not retry: YAGPC_BUS_SERVICE_US 2 -> 10 us, which changed CPU not at all (6.2% of a core against a 5.7% baseline) because drain_bus issues one recvfrom PER DATAGRAM until the socket is empty -- the syscall count follows the datagram count, not the service rate, so looking less often saves only the cheap poll(). A BENCHMARKING NOTE THAT COST TIME: a one-GPC gate is paced to real time and uses about 6% of a core, so it cannot show a few per cent of change; optimisation has to be measured on a SATURATED configuration where the metric is throughput at 100% CPU, not percent-of-core.
  - csects `find_poo_entry,poo_override,instr_time_poo,pump_once,transport_send_now,drain_bus` &middot; env `YAGPC_PACETRACE,YAGPC_BUS_SERVICE_US` &middot; files `src/timing.c,src/bcenet_transport.c,src/rtpacer.c,src/rtpacer.h,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `slow emulation,rate below 1.0,strcmp,getenv,syscall overhead,sendto per datagram,instruction timing lookup,perf_event_paranoid,cannot profile` &middot; doc `AP-101S Principles of Operation timing tables (the POO_TIMING_TABLE rows); BCE PoO 3.4.1 for the 16.5 us MIA sampling interval` &middot; run `reg-memo,reg-ov,reg-bus10,sat-after`
- **#166** *(emulator)* A STATISTICS COUNTER WAS COSTING HALF THE EMULATOR'S THROUGHPUT. discretes_poll_one() called attend() unconditionally, and attend() reads the wall clock; discretes_poll_one() runs from mode_switch_held() at the top of batchrunner_step, so there was a clock reading on EVERY EMULATED INSTRUCTION. Sampling it every 32nd call instead roughly DOUBLES throughput and does not make the counter wrong.
  - evidence: Uninstrumented one-GPC benchmark on a 2011 i7-3930K, 600 s, steps per wall second: 340906 baseline -> 698496, +105%. Independently corroborated by the crew panel's own waits on the SAME IPL procedure: 'wait crt 1 new-screen' 41.2 s -> 13.6 s and 'wait gpc 1 mode-tb RUN' 163.8 s -> 40.6 s, so the GPC IPLs in 40 seconds instead of 164 doing demonstrably the same work (same MODE sequence, mmu1 56/432/221243 words, CRT1 taking 408 fills). Gate PASSES with every scored counter exact and keystrokes back at the REFERENCE poll=247, where the pre-fix run on this host gave 259. WHY IT WAS INVISIBLE BEFORE: where clock_gettime is a vDSO read of the TSC it costs nanoseconds, but this host's kernel measured 471M cycles of TSC warp at boot, marked the TSC unstable and fell back to the hpet clocksource, which makes every reading a full syscall into a memory-mapped timer. A call-graph profile put read_hpet at 31.19% of all cycles with 30.36% of it arriving through this ONE line, and clock_gettime at 44.12% inclusive against 12.43% for emulating the AP-101S. SAMPLING IS SAFE because attentive accumulates the GAP between readings, so a gap measured across 32 calls is the elapsed time those 32 calls took; only the resolution changes, and it is compared against staleSec, which is SECONDS. discretes_poll() already gated itself this way; discretes_poll_one() never did.
  - env `YAGPC_PACETRACE` &middot; files `src/discretes.c` &middot; symptom `core utilization,read_hpet,clock_gettime,slow emulation`
- **#167** *(emulator)* THE PER-INSTRUCTION recv() IS GATED, worth 7.6%, and the display overrun that blocked it was a KERNEL BUFFER LIMIT, not a fault in the change. mode_switch_held ran at the top of batchrunner_step and so did a recv() syscall on EVERY emulated instruction, against a socket a human at a crew panel writes to a few times a minute. Gating it to every 64th call is correct because the datagrams QUEUE; they are still taken one at a time when the look happens, which is what stops a pushbutton press and its release cancelling out.
  - evidence: Uninstrumented one-GPC benchmark, 600 s each leg, after net.core.rmem_max was raised to 8 MB: 678925 -> 730568 steps/s, +7.6%, with ZERO transfers abandoned in BOTH legs. Gate passes byte-identical: mmu1 56/431/220731/220011/720, formatFills 8, one MODE: IPL, GPC MEMORY, poll=247, simt=133.706 s. THE COUNTER MUST NOT BE r->step: while a machine is held the step count does not move, so a step-based gate would never fire in HALT and the switch leaving HALT would never be seen; discretes_poll() gates itself the same way on its own call counter. WHY IT WAS BLOCKED FOR AN AFTERNOON: the faster emulator outran the Python display clients, whose UDP receive buffers then truncated DK bus transfers -- and a lost fill carrying the GPCIPL title deadlocked an IPL outright. MEDS2.py had always asked for a 4 MB SO_RCVBUF and logged 'recv buffer 425984 bytes (asked 4194304; raise net.core.rmem_max)' on every bus of every run; net.core.rmem_max was 212992 and silently capped it. The message named the remedy and had been printing for as long as the code existed. CAUTION: sysctl -w does NOT survive a reboot -- it reverted at the 19:21 reboot and had to be written to /etc/sysctl.d/99-nsts-bus.conf.
  - files `src/run.c,src/run.h,discretePanel/MEDS2.py` &middot; symptom `transfer abandoned,halfwords short,receive buffer errors,IPL deadlock,display data loss`
- **#172** *(emulator)* --dump-state and its triggers were vehicle-wide, not per-computer: in a multi-GPC run captures were lost, overwritten, and torn between state and memory, silently. YAGPC_DUMPSTATE_AT, YAGPC_DUMPSTATE_BUSY and YAGPC_LANDMARKS kept cursors in function-scope statics inside batchrunner_step, shared by every GPC thread, and both on-stop dump sites wrote the bare --dump-state path.
  - evidence: 2 GPCs armed at 2 times should give 4 captures; gave 2, and not two good ones. Before: dump-1e-06.{json,json.protect.bin,mem.bin}, dump-5e-06.{json,json.protect.bin} with NO mem.bin, plus an orphan dump-0.mem.bin -- two threads interleaved inside the snprintf/dump_main_storage pair ageharness.c says must be atomic, so state and memory came from different instants. After: dump-gpc{1,2}-{1e-06,5e-06}.{json,json.protect.bin,mem.bin}, four complete triples. lmHits counted arrivals across the WHOLE VEHICLE, so a 'stop on Nth hit' landmark summed two machines' hits and then stopped only one computer while the rest ran on un-snapshotted. On-stop path: 1 GPC keeps the exact name given (the --break/--state round trip), several give f-gpc1.json. Fix moves the parsed config as well as the cursors into BatchRunner, which also removes an init race (main() inits every machine before creating any thread) and takes the env lookups out of the per-instruction path. Prior single-GPC findings that used these hooks (#29, #37) are UNAFFECTED: headless-gpcmem.sh is one computer, where the shared statics behave correctly.
  - procs `CPU` &middot; config `G2` &middot; env `YAGPC_DUMPSTATE_AT,YAGPC_DUMPSTATE_BUSY,YAGPC_LANDMARKS,YAGPC_LANDMARKS_AFTER,--dump-state,--state,--gpcs` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/run.h` &middot; symptom `only one GPC dumps,missing mem.bin,orphan dump-0.mem.bin,landmark stops one computer,captures overwrite each other,state and memory from different instants`
- **#178** *(emulator)* A RESTORED VEHICLE HAD NO MASS MEMORY. simulatePASS passed --resume INSTEAD of --mmu-model, on the premise that a restored machine is past its IPL; so re-IPL inside a restored vehicle read zero words (message wrongly blamed the tape: 'carries no bootstrap at the FMAIPL2 allocation', printed whenever got==0), and any OPS transition needing mass memory would fail too. Fixed: tape attached on every restore; vehicle.json records tape path + SHA-256; restore onto another volume refused. STILL OPEN (same premise, not yet fixed): the mass memory's STATE is not captured -- blocks PASS wrote (writes live only in MmuModel.blocks, never the .mmv), tape position, write-enable -- nor the ICC or MTU models.
  - evidence: ipl-resume-1/-2 (before): no mmu report at shutdown, no YAGPC_MMUTRACE output, 'no bootstrap'. ipl-resume-4 (after): 'MODE: IPL; memory filled, bootstrap read from MM1 (BCE 18) ... 72 blocks', released at 0x0014b, 0 votes. Commit 70ca3708f.
  - csects `FMAIPL2,FCMBOOT` &middot; procs `MMU,BCE18,BCE19` &middot; env `YAGPC_MMUTRACE` &middot; files `discretePanel/simulatePASS.py,src/run.c,src/mmumodel.c` &middot; symptom `re-IPL after restore fails,carries no bootstrap,no mass memory after restore,snapshot resume missing MMU,IPL got 0 words` &middot; run `ipl-resume-1,ipl-resume-2,ipl-resume-4`
- **#179** *(emulator)* CRT2 ENDED UP COMMANDED BY GPC1 IN A 4-GPC 2-CRT IPL, and the set failed to form at the OPS 2 transition (fail votes in every bad run, none in any good run). Bisected to 47efdb3f3, which gated mode_switch_held's per-instruction socket drain to every 64th call. A neighbour's sync code is written into this machine's input register DIRECTLY by vehicle_route_out (both halves in one step) and ALSO sent as a datagram for monitors; this machine's own socket received that datagram and applied it a SECOND time, late and in two halves, so the register passed back through an old code and a half code. Per-instruction draining had kept that window a few instructions wide. FIX: a machine ignores received datagrams for input bits a neighbour in this same process drives (discretes_set_local_wired, set from vehicle_add_machine); the gate and its 7.8%-of-cycles saving are kept. NOTE the measure: with a redundant set, every member runs the same bus programs, so a bus TRANSACTION CENSUS cannot identify a commander -- use the MIA transmit enables (YAGPC_PROCDUMP 'iop mia: xmit='), BCE6=CRT1, BCE7=CRT2, and the GPC is named by which ICC bit 1-5 it drives.
  - evidence: Six bisect runs of the owner's examples/4gpc-startup-subtitled.script in a nested X display, port base 7300: 426ad06b8 (09-15) GOOD twice (GPC1 xmit=42b23880 CRT1, GPC2 21013480 CRT2, 0 votes); f1a80bf07 GOOD; 19c6b878a GOOD; 47efdb3f3 BAD (GPC1 43b23880 commands BOTH, 4 votes); 6054cbee0 BAD (9 votes); 2362da1d7 BAD. HEAD with MODE_POLL_EVERY=1 alone: GOOD, 0 votes. HEAD with the fix: 4 runs, CRT2 on GPC2 in all four, baseline-identical masks and 0 votes in three (fix-1 had 6 votes and a GPC3 mask of 10003080). Commit 4f0c47516.

- **#180** *(emulator)* RESTORE-TIME MUTUAL VOTES ARE A LOST I/O AT THE CAPTURE INSTANT, AND WHETHER THEY APPEAR DEPENDS ON WHETHER BOTH COMPUTERS LOSE IT. Right after a resume both machines put up 010 IPR (input problem report) together and carry on -- that run is CLEAN; when only one does, each declares the other failed to sync within 10 ms (FCMISYNC on one, FCMTSYNC on the other, redundant-set failures, landmark 1948c r0=00080001/00100001). The lost input is a transfer that was in flight when the capture was taken: the display and peripheral PROCESSES restart fresh on a restore, so anything mid-transfer on their side is gone even though the GPC's own BCE state is restored faithfully. Measured rates with one binary: the owner's snapshot201 CLEAN 5/5 -- and it is the ONLY capture where GPC2 commands no display bus (taken while GPC1 drove both CRTs, the #179 defect); every capture with two display-bus commanders is flaky, 1 of 3 to 0 of 6, and the SAME capture can go either way run to run. RULED OUT: #179's stale sync copy; the ICC, mass memory and timing unit being uncaptured (captured now, and both live ICC queues were EMPTY in every capture); restoring the barrier spread, which made it worse (9 of 9 voting with it on) and is off behind YAGPC_BARRIER_RESTORE=1; settle time before the capture (10 minutes at OPS 201 is still 1 of 3); the BCE latch state, which does not separate clean from voting captures. NEXT: quiesce the display/peripheral transfers before the rendezvous, or refuse a capture taken mid-transfer -- 'refuse, don't degrade', as the plan says of the peer hold.  CAVEAT ADDED AFTER #187: every rate above was scored by syncscore.py from a cam.log WITH NO TIMESTAMPS, and a run's SIGINT teardown casts votes of its own.  A run counted here as voting may have been voting only at the shutdown, so THESE RATES ARE AN UPPER BOUND on restore-time sync loss and the clean/voting split may be softer than it looks.  cam.py stamps its log now and syncscore.py separates the teardown votes; re-score before building on the numbers.  FIXED 2026-09-21: THE CAUSE WAS RESUME STAGGER.  After a restore each machine started executing as soon as IT had loaded, and they load at different speeds -- measured on the shared clock, four machines began 0, 6.2, 13.4 and 17.9 ms apart.  The first one out walks into a sync point with peers that are not running and fails them; a wider sync window cannot help when the stagger exceeds any window (tested: 12 ms, still failed).  Two further layers hid under that: a restored machine is held until the FRESH panel process is first heard ('silence is HALT'), and while held it leaves the time barrier and withdraws its outputs to 000 -- so each was released at a slightly different moment and rejoined the barrier levelled to whoever got out first.  THE FIX: vehicle_resume_gate at each machine's FIRST INSTRUCTION AFTER THE PANEL HOLD, holding all until every machine has arrived, with vehicle_barrier_prejoin putting all four in the barrier at the same simulated instant before release.  RESULT, snapshot g3-3crt-ops301-wide (4 GPCs, 3 CRTs, OPS 301): no gate 0 of 3 clean; gate after load 0 of 1 (stagger 6.9 ms); gate after load plus pre-join 1 of 6 (stagger 1.0-6.9 ms); GATE AFTER THE HOLD 6 of 6 CLEAN AT RESUME, every release '4 of 4 loaded'.  One of those six later lost a machine at t=16.9 s -- one in six two-minute holds is about the steady-state rate of #190, so that is consistent with the 3-CRT vote landing during the hold, not a restore failure.  ALSO CORRECTED: capturing regDiscreteInA (commit 36d95a0b0) was right to do but for the wrong stated reason -- the peers' sync codes do not live in that register, they live in the Discretes object and reach the CPU through the overlay on read; a fresh capture shows discreteInA=0x0b000000 on every machine, local bits only.
  - evidence: Restore rates, one 2-min hold each, port base 7300, nested X display, synctest.sh + syncscore.py, all on builds with #179's fix. The owner's snapshot201 (taken 09-18, BEFORE that fix): CLEAN 5 of 5. Every capture taken after it: snap3 4 of 6 / 1 of 1 / 1 of 3; snap4 0 of 12; snap5 (ten minutes' settling) 1 of 3; snap6 (set-agreement check) 4 of 9; snap7 (stricter mid-receive check, never fired) 1 of 9; snap8 (ONE CRT, so a single display-bus commander, the one structural feature snapshot201 has and the others lack) 0 of 3. REFUTED: the one-commander configuration; settle time; the barrier spread (9 of 9 voting with it on, off by default behind YAGPC_BARRIER_RESTORE=1); the BCE latch state, which does not separate clean from voting captures; and 'the clean ones are not a redundant set' -- the reverse holds, a CLEAN restore shows 28883 I/O-complete syncs against 4 in a voting one, so the protected I/O stops because the set broke. CONFIRMED MECHANISM: both machines raise 010 IPR together right after a resume and carry on (clean); when only one does, FCMISYNC on one and FCMTSYNC on the other each fail the other within 10 ms (landmark 1948c, r0=00080001/00100001, redundant-set failures). WHAT SNAPSHOT201 HAS THAT THE OTHERS DO NOT IS STILL UNKNOWN.

- **#184** *(flight-sw)* 'GPC MEMORY 1' ON CRT2 AFTER AN IPL: the digit names the GPC commanding the CRT, and CRT2 stayed with GPC1 because our IPL procedure omitted two steps the vehicle's own procedure has -- deassigning the IDP from PASS before lending it to the GPC being IPLed, and assigning it to that GPC once it is in RUN. With both in place CRT2 is GPC2's, reads 'GPC MEMORY 2' in OPS 0 and 'UNIV PTG 2' in OPS 201, and GPC1 no longer keeps it.  IT WAS NEVER COMMANDED BY TWO COMPUTERS AT ONCE: possession of a display bus is exclusive with the fix and without it, and what the two entries change is how long the wrong computer holds CRT2 (#185, #186). See #185 for the measurements and the Workbook citations.
  - evidence: Run assign201 (2gpc-ops201.script WITH the assignment, YAGPC_CMDTRACE=6,7): bus 6 GPC1 only throughout; bus 7 GPC2 from t=60 s, GPC1 only in transients at t=40-60, 80-120, zero after. CRT2 frames: 'GPC MEMORY 1' at 09:28:52, 'GPC MEMORY 2' at 09:28:59 (after the entry), 'UNIV PTG 2' at 09:31:15. Run dk7 (without it): bus 7 carries ~170-178 commands per 20 s from EACH of GPC1 and GPC2 between t=86 and t=138. Run both6: SPEC 6 captured from both CRTs at once (NSTS_ANNOUNCE_ROWS=all) -- identical CRT rows on both, each drawn by its own GPC.

- **#185** *(method)* WHY GPC1 KEPT CRT2, AND THE TWO KEYSTROKES THAT HAND IT OVER PROMPTLY.  GPC1 IPLs alone, and out of GPCIPL's blanket enable it commands nearly every bus (1,6,7,8,10-24); in OPS 0 there is no NBAT and no IDP assignment from which FCOS could narrow that, so it keeps CRT2's bus.  The crew then LENDS CRT2 to the computer being IPLed, and without the vehicle's own deassign step GPC1 goes on holding that bus for minutes afterwards -- which is the 'CRT2 is controlled by GPC1' the owner reported, and the 'GPC MEMORY 1' rather than 'GPC MEMORY 2'.  FIX (procedure, not code): 'GPC/CRT 0 2 EXEC' on KB1 before CRT2 is lent, and 'GPC/CRT 2 2 EXEC' on KB1 once the new GPC is in RUN, per the DPS Workbook ('The IDP is deassigned from PASS and BFS', '(to avoid any conflict in CRT commanders)').  CORRECTION TO THIS ENTRY'S EARLIER TEXT: it said the two computers commanded the bus AT THE SAME TIME and that the fix removed a simultaneous-commander state.  THEY NEVER DID.  Every overlap cited was two per-machine clocks compared as one timeline (see #186).  Possession of a display bus is strictly exclusive in every run measured on the shared clock, before AND after the fix; what the fix changes is HOW LONG the wrong computer keeps it.
  - evidence: Paired 2-GPC runs, same tape, same script, run concurrently so both saw the same machine load, scored on the vehicle's shared clock (12712 and 12586 command words, 0 parser rejects).  WITHOUT the two GPC/CRT entries (run nofix): GPC1 holds bus 7 at 44.6-51.9 and again 96.5-234.8 -- 1303 commands -- and GPC2 gets its own CRT only at t=235.0.  WITH them (run fixed): GPC1 has bus 7 for 44.6-47.1, 25 commands, and GPC2 holds it from t=68.9 to the end.  Shared-clock seconds with two commanders: 0 in BOTH runs, on bus 6 and bus 7 alike.  4-GPC corrected script (run 4gpc2): possession passes gpc1 59.6-73.6, gpc2 98.0-160.0, gpc3 183.8-246.4, gpc4 269.7-328.9, gpc2 329.3-668.8 -- five tenancies, no overlap, closest approach between two different commanders 0.362 s.
  - csects `FCMBUSCM,FCMBMAN` &middot; procs `BCE6,BCE7` &middot; env `YAGPC_CMDTRACE,YAGPC_XMITENA_TRACE` &middot; files `discretePanel/examples/2gpc-ops201.script,discretePanel/examples/4gpc-startup-subtitled.script` &middot; symptom `CRT2 commanded by GPC1,GPC MEMORY 1 on CRT2,wrong GPC drives a display,dual commanders (NOT REAL -- see #186)` &middot; doc `DPS Workbook USA005350 Rev B: IDP load procedure, DK Assignment` &middot; run `nofix,fixed,4gpc2,deassign,4gpcassign2`
- **#193** *(emulator)* THE ICC QUEUE DROPS THE ARRIVING WORD, NOT THE DEAD ONE -- backwards for a wire that holds nothing.  Each (bus, receiver) pair has its own 2048-WORD FIFO (iccmodel.c ICC_QUEUE; a transfer is up to 512 words from its command word's (w&0x1ff)+1, and runs about 119 words in practice, so the queue is about four maximum transfers or seventeen typical ones).  Reads are PULL-DRIVEN -- only when the receiving BCE executes a receive -- and staleness is judged only THEN, discarding head words older than expireUs (10 ms, YAGPC_ICC_EXPIRE_US).  Nothing detects wraparound.  So a receiver that stops reading freezes its queue full of dead words and every later broadcast to it is DISCARDED AT THE DOOR: icc_broadcast does 'if (count >= ICC_QUEUE) { dropped++; continue; }'.  The words in the queue at that moment are by definition the stale ones and the arrival is the live one, so the policy should be drop-oldest (or expire on write), not drop-newest.  LATENT, not biting: while a receiver drains normally the queue never reaches the bound.  Noticed by the owner 2026-09-21 while asking how the FIFO works.  ALSO RECORDED SO NOBODY 'FIXES' IT: the flight software does NOT need cross-sender time ordering.  AIESIP walks the per-sender buffers in INDEX order (DO FOR AIEV_ICC_BUF_NBR = 1 TO 5) and treats each sender's current-cycle message independently, with rules keyed to WHICH sender rather than when -- HEX'11EE' honoured only from buffer 1, its own buffer compared against TFCMID, and the PMU sender chosen by an order-independent minimum.  A cycle's messages are a SET of per-sender contributions, not a causally ordered stream.  What IS required is that a buffer hold THIS cycle's message, which is what the expiry enforces and what #129/#131 violated.
  - evidence: FIXED 2026-09-22: icc_broadcast now drops the queue's OLDEST word and takes the arrival, instead of discarding the arrival.  Covered by a new unit test, test/test_icc_queue.c -- nothing linked iccmodel.c on its own before, so the policy had no test at all.  The test offers a command word plus 2548 data words to a receiver that never reads, then drains: it must hold exactly 2048 words, the last read must be the last sent (data word 2547), the first must be data word 500, and a receiver that has caught up must take new traffic.  Verified to CATCH the old policy, by linking the same test against the pre-fix iccmodel.c: 'newest word survived: 2046, expected 2547'.  Still latent in the vehicle.  CORRECTION to this entry's original evidence: the six-figure drop totals it cites were NOT receivers before their first read or a voted-out machine -- they were a receiver that does not exist, see #201.  With that fixed the same run reports 0 dropped and 0 pending, so the bound is genuinely never approached by a real receiver.
  - csects `AIESIP` &middot; procs `ICC1,ICC2,ICC3,ICC4,ICC5` &middot; env `YAGPC_ICC_EXPIRE_US,YAGPC_ICCSEQ` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c,test/test_icc_queue.c` &middot; symptom `ICC queue full,dropped words,stale ICC,cycle mixing,older words still queued` &middot; doc `AIESIP.hal:1102,1600,1659 (per-sender buffers, index order, min reduction)` &middot; run `ring-1,hist-1`
- **#196** *(emulator)* The 3-CRT redundant-set vote (#190) is a LOST NEIGHBOUR SYNC CODE: discretes.c apply() updated discrete input A/B with an unlocked read-modify-write of the whole register, racing the neighbours' in-process writes (discretes_apply_external_pair, locked) from their own threads.  A datagram for an unrelated bit (e.g. MM READY) could write back the old word over a neighbour's freshly raised sync code; losing the raising edge loses the whole pulse, since the lowering edge then changes nothing.
  - evidence: svc7500-1 (restore of g3-3crt-ops301-wide, 3.85 ms window, SVC recording added to the sync ring): GPC3 raised 100 SVC at sim 528.309344 and dropped it at .309555 (its own ring); GPC1 and GPC2 recorded both edges; GPC4 recorded NEITHER -- its record goes GPC3 111 (.309205) straight to GPC3's NEXT 100 (.309701).  GPC4 held its own SVC sync (8746c081, pl 0015 0116) until it saw that next 100 and dropped at .309910, 0.34 ms after the others; from there it ran exactly one SVC behind (its I/O-complete sync came before SVC 9db92081 where the others' came after), the others' later SVC syncs matched GPC4's stale held 100, and finally GPC4 issued SVC 1c085 (psw1 a3fec085) that nobody answered: FCMSFAIL from FCMSSYNC+0x86 (0x1979c), r0=001c0001, the others failing it 5.1 ms later from 0x1987f.  Same shape in the four T3-soak failures (#195): the victim issues one SVC its peers do not.  Explains why neither widening FCMSNTO2 nor raising T3 helped: nothing is late, a code is gone.  Fix: take d->lock around apply()'s update.  FIXED e46bb0eac, VERIFIED: 16 restores of g3-3crt-ops301-wide on port bases 7500/7700/7900/8100 in parallel, 3.69 simulated hours at the stock 3.85 ms window, all four computers in RUN every run, zero FCMSFAIL landmarks and zero votes, against ~5.4 expected at ~1.5/h (P(0) ~ 0.5%).
  - addrs `1979c,1987f,1948c` &middot; csects `FCMSSYNC,FCMSFAIL` &middot; procs `CPU` &middot; config `G3` &middot; env `YAGPC_SYNC_HISTORY,YAGPC_LANDMARKS` &middot; files `src/discretes.c,src/vehicle.c,src/cpu_instr.c` &middot; symptom `GPC4 voted out,three CRTs,4-GPC 3-CRT,intermittent sync loss,lost sync code,one SVC behind,SVC sync one machine only,race,thread` &middot; run `svc7500-1,fix7500-1..4,fix7700-1..4,fix7900-1..4,fix8100-1..4`

MEDS2.py _fill() marks 'load started' on ANY fill, so its iplRunning=true is misleading.  Snapshot g3-3crt-ops301-wide captured idp3 ipled=false iplRunning=true, 260 non-zero halfwords vs 4101 for IDP1/2.  User observation 2026-09-21: CRT3 foreground only, for hours.  Fix: move idppower 3 / majfunc 3 / idpload 3 to after ITEM 14 +3 and BEFORE OPS 3 0 1 PRO in 4gpc-g3-startup.script; then recapture the 3-CRT snapshot.  Open question: which GPC commands DK3 in OPS 0 between the NBAT and the OPS transition.  FIXED by the owner's own procedure, not a code change: IDP LOAD pressed with the IPL, so GPCIPL loads the unit.  4gpc-g3-startup.script now powers IDP3 with the others and IPLs GPC3 onto CRT3 (kybdsel right 3; ipl-one-gpc.script gpc=3 crt=3 idp=3 kb=KB2; kybdsel right 2).  Run idp3ipl-1 (fixed emulator e46bb0eac): GPCIPL MENU on crt3, 8 func=916 blocks, 'load complete ... as unit 3', 2946 page fills after; OPS 301 reached, 1695 s simulated, cam.log no votes at all, barrier 0 abandoned.  One run: early IDP3 power did not reproduce #159's losses, which were measured before the #196 race fix.  SOAK FROM THE NEW CAPTURE (/mnt/STORAGE/home/rburkey/workspace/pass-run/g3-3crt-ops301-idp3, taken by the owner from this script; all three IDPs ipled, unit ids 1/2/3, 4099 halfwords each): 16 restores on four port bases in parallel, 3.75 simulated hours, all four computers in RUN every run, zero FCMSFAIL, zero votes, no display flagged unloaded, IDP3 taking 1735-1831 page fills per run.  Early IDP3 power costs nothing now.
