- **#199** *(emulator)* OPS 3 cannot be re-entered after any earlier OPS transition because the emulator models no GPC as a TERMINAL on the mass-memory buses: PASS's default overlay source (GPC/MMU, DPS UTILITY ITEM 9) fetches the major-function base from a GPC that already has it, the commands go unanswered, every target is marked failed, and ARC's source loop exits before it ever tries mass memory.  Forcing the MMU (DPS UTILITY ITEM 10) works and is the workaround.
  - evidence: Owner's live run 2026-09-22 (simulatePASS-logs, keyboard logs stamped): OPS 3 0 1 PRO at t=917, 963, 1056 -- no MM activity; OPS 2 0 1 PRO at t=25 (MM1 busy 28-38) and t=1011 (MM2 busy 1014-1024) both load.  Headless repro ops3-A: restore, KB2 OPS 0 0 0 PRO, KB1 OPS 3 0 1 PRO -> no MM activity (mmu1/mmu2 commands 0).  ops3-B (with OPS 2 between) -> same refusal.  The IPL script's own OPS 3 0 1 PRO from post-IPL OPS 0 works (idp3ipl-1).  NARROWED 2026-09-22 (owner away; all headless or IPL runs):
(1) 301->000 directly is simply not taken (owner: the legal path is 301->201->000); ops3-A/A2 never left OPS 301, so they tested nothing.
(2) The LEGAL path 301->201->000->301 reproduces the owner's symptom (ops3-C): no MM activity, all CRTs back on GPC MEMORY, no message, no votes.
(3) NOT a refusal: landmarks (ops3-dm6) show DM6OPS entry 45360 -> A4 validity 45552 -> A3 454de -> A1 TARGET_RUN_GPC 4545c -> A2 PRE_POSIT 454be -> ARC_GPC_RECONFIG 40f34 scheduled.  Addresses from PFS mafgen csects-G2/G3.json; our image's DM6OPS entry matches (e9f3 2c00 = LA to its data #DDM6OPS 2c00).
(4) The G3 ARCHIVE in upper memory is intact in all four GPCs: top-of-memory table 0066 / 0002a96c / ptrs ff5f000f fec700f0 fe3800f0 fe37000f; FCMMGPOV's G3 case table puts STORE at 0x7fec7 and RETRIEVE at 0x7fe38 (both begin c8fb 9484 = STM FC$MGPOV, as FCMG3RTV's source does).
(5) ARC never asks for it: FCMMGPOV 192ce entered 3x after the request, never reaching 7fe38 or 7fec7, i.e. case 1 (ordinary overlay), and that overlay issues no MM command.  ARCGPC.hal ~1290: the RAPID/MOVE-AND-TRANSFER retrieve needs ARCB_G3ARCH_STAT(16) = 'ITEM 49 ON DPS UTIL' enabled; without it an ordinary overlay should still load G3 from mass memory, and does not.
(6) Same failure with SPEC 1 / ITEM 4 9 EXEC first (ops3-E), and going OPS 2 -> OPS 3 directly (ops3-F: ends in OPS 0 on GPC MEMORY).
(7) NOT snapshot-related: a vehicle IPL'd from scratch (runs/ipl-legal) loads G3 from MM1 at its first OPS 301 (three MM1 bursts 358-371 s, consistent with ARC's 'upper memory load' case: MM read, then archive store), OPS 201 from MM1, then 000 -> 301 fails identically.
NEXT: why ARC's ordinary G3 overlay (MEM_SRC 3/4, ARC_MEM_OVL) issues no MM command once CZ2V_G3ARCH_MM_AREA is set -- land ARC_MEM_OVL (A?ARCGPC) and FCMMGOV; and whether a real crew must use ITEM 49 (owner's knowledge).
Side finding: DPS UTILITY (SPEC 1, format 0010) draws data with NO title or background -- CR93220A moved its background into MEDS itself, which MEDS2 does not have.  2026-09-22, further (ICC trace YAGPC_ICCMSG=1, which reports ARC's own reconfiguration messages -- header 09F6 is ARC_ICC_MSG type 1, REDUNDANT SET FORMATION):
(8) THE G3 ARCHIVE IS A RED HERRING.  Word 11 of the type-1 message is ARCB_G3ARCH_STAT and reads 0002 throughout every run, before AND after the first OPS 301 -- bit 15 (archive LOAD armed) on, bit 16 (RETRIEVE) off, which is exactly what AIBGPCLO writes at GPC IPL.  ARC arms the archive STORE (bit 12) only when transitioning to G1 from OPS 9 with ITEM 48 set (ARCGPC.hal ~745), i.e. at launch, so our runs never store one, ITEM 49 on DPS UTILITY is correctly refused (word 11 unchanged after ITEM 4 9 EXEC, run ops3-item49), and the upper-memory content is whatever the IPL image held.  Authentic behaviour, not a defect.
(9) THE FAILING OVERLAY NEVER ASKS MASS MEMORY.  Run ipl-icc (fresh IPL): mmu1 249 commands / 2400 blocks (all from the IPL, the FIRST OPS 301 and the OPS 201), mmu2 6 commands / 0 blocks; the second OPS 3 adds nothing.  ARC_MEM_OVL sets OVSRCE=19 for a mass-memory overlay and 275 for GPC-to-GPC, so the three ARC_MEM_OVL calls were GPC-to-GPC.
(10) ITS SOURCE MASKS DIFFER FROM THE WORKING ONE.  Type-1 words 4 and 5 are ARC_SRC_RS_MASK(1) and (2): first OPS 301 (loads from MM1) 003d / 003d; the failing one 8380 / 26b0.  No type-3 message (42BD, which tells a source GPC to transmit) is ever sent.
WORKING HYPOTHESIS: ARC believes other GPCs still hold the G3 program overlay -- CZ2V_PROG_OVLY / CZ2V_MF_OVLY as seen across the common set, which travel by ICC -- so it chooses a GPC-to-GPC source that has nothing to give, never falls back to mass memory, and every target fails.
NEXT TEST: the same sequence on a SINGLE-GPC vehicle.  With no other GPC to source from, ARC must use mass memory; if OPS 3 then succeeds, the GPC-to-GPC source choice is the fault.  SETTLED 2026-09-22.
(11) DPS Overview Workbook (ibiblio mirror, ~/Desktop/sandroid.org/public_html/apollo/Shuttle/DPS Overview Workbook.pdf, p.42 and the MMU-related sections): 'MMU source/bus is used for OPS transitions... The default option (ITEM 9) is GPC/MMU.  If a GPC currently has the requested software, it is used as the source of that software.  Otherwise, the MMUs are used... If we wish to force the software to come from an MMU regardless of whether or not a GPC is available as a source, ITEM 10 is selected.'  Same page: GPC MEMORY's ITEM 48 is ERR LOG RESET, which also CLEARS GPC FAIL VOTES -- that, not the archive, is the ITEM 48 Don recommended to the owner.
(12) ITEM 10 FIXES IT.  Run ops3-item10 (restore, OPS 201, OPS 000, SPEC 1, ITEM 1 0 EXEC, RESUME, OPS 3 0 1 PRO): MM1 busy 184.7-198.3 s and all three CRTs come up on DEORB MNVR COAST; syncscore CLEAN, 0 votes.
(13) THE FAILING ATTEMPT ADDRESSES A GPC, NOT THE MMU.  YAGPC_CMDTRACE=18,19 (run ops3-bus): the working loads command iua=11 (the mass memory); during the failing OPS 3, GPC1 commands iua=5 on BOTH buses and GPC3 sends 162 more, all unanswered -- mmumodel.c:537 replies only to its own IUA 11, and no GPC is modelled as a terminal on bus 18/19 at all.
(14) WHY THE FIRST OPS 301 ALWAYS WORKS: after IPL no GPC holds the GNC major-function base, so ARC has no GPC source and uses mass memory; after any transition every GPC holds it, ARC takes the fast path, and ARC_MEM_OVL's loop exits at 'IF TARGET_GPCS = ARC_ERR_FLAGS(6 TO 10) THEN EXIT' before MEM_SRC 3/4 (mass memory) is ever tried.
FIX: model a GPC as a responder on the mass-memory buses (the GPC-to-GPC overlay path), or, until then, ITEM 10 in any procedure that re-enters an OPS.  FIXED 2026-09-22, de045afc0 + 4fa2666b8 (src/mmumodel.c).  The mass memory is the only object every computer on its bus shares, so it now plays the WIRE for a GPC-to-GPC transfer: a command addressed to the GPC overlay address (IUA 5, YAGPC_GTG_IUA) is echoed to the other computers' listener queues as a command-sync word, and the data words that follow are queued for them.  THE PACING IS THE POINT: the words are delivered ready-at-once and never aged (a negative due time in tap_push), because the wire pacing of such a transfer is the TRANSMITTING computer's own bus program, already paced by its BCE.  Pacing them a second time in the model left the receivers missing most of them -- 554,421 words past unread against 359,378 carried -- and the whole set then failed, every computer lighting its own fail lamp.  Verified: 301->201->000->301 from a restore reaches DEORB MNVR COAST on all three CRTs, twice, CLEAN, 184,934 words carried on bus 18 each time; the same sequence from a fresh IPL, same result and same count; a 10-minute restore making no such transfer carries none and is CLEAN.  A first attempt that echoed EVERY command, not just those to IUA 5, flooded the listener queues and cost the set its sync -- the narrowing matters.  YAGPC_GTG=0 restores the old behaviour, where DPS UTILITY ITEM 10 (force the MMU) is the way round it.
  - addrs `45360-4563e,40f34-4163e,192ce-193df,7fe38,7fec7` &middot; csects `DM6OPS,ARCGPC,FCMMGPOV,FCMG3RTV,FCMG3STR,ASMAUX` &middot; procs `MMU1,MMU2` &middot; config `G3` &middot; files `src/mmumodel.c,src/bcenet_framer.c,src/vehicle.c` &middot; symptom `OPS 3 refused,no MM activity,GPC-to-GPC overlay,iua=5 unanswered,ITEM 10 works,ERR LOG RESET clears fail votes` &middot; run `ops3-gtg3,gtg-repeat,gtg-steady,ipl-gtg`
- **#201** *(emulator)* THE ICC QUEUED EVERY WORD FOR COMPUTERS THAT ARE NOT IN THE VEHICLE, and the drop counter was measuring that rather than anything real.  icc_broadcast looped over GPC ids 1-5 unconditionally, so in a 4-GPC vehicle GPC5's queue filled once, stayed full, and every later word on every bus was counted dropped.  FIXED 2026-09-22: a computer announces itself by using the bus at all (IccModel.present[], set on any iccmodel_service call), and nothing is queued for one that never has.  A present computer that has not yet touched the bus also misses those words, which is correct and not merely tolerable -- a receiver's first read flushes the whole queue by age regardless.
  - evidence: Before, 4-GPC 3-CRT restored run ops200-7500 (6 min, OPS 000/201/000/301): every bus 'icc: bus N (GPCN's): 2909 commands out, ~1032475 words read, 2048 pending, 333842 dropped' -- 2048 is exactly one full queue and the four buses summed to 1335368 dropped words.  No per-receiver line was printed for that queue because it never read, which is why it was never identified.  After, run iccgate-7500, same script and snapshot: '0 pending, 0 dropped' on every bus, totals {pending 0, dropped 0}, 1365786 words transmitted and 4128080 received (three present receivers x every word).  Run CLEAN both times, pacing 96.1% then 98.9%.  This also corrects #193's evidence, which attributed the six-figure drop totals to receivers before their first read and to a voted-out machine.
  - procs `ICC1,ICC2,ICC3,ICC4,ICC5` &middot; config `G3` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c,test/test_icc_queue.c` &middot; symptom `huge ICC dropped count,2048 pending per bus,drop counter useless,phantom GPC5` &middot; run `ops200-7500,iccgate-7500`
- **#202** *(emulator)* A FIVE-GPC VEHICLE COULD NOT BE SNAPSHOTTED, for two independent reasons, both now fixed.  ROOT CAUSE: an error-terminated BCE was left holding recvLeft, the count of words that never arrived, while recvActive said it was not receiving -- a state no real BCE can be in.  recvLeft is assigned only when a receive is ARMED and counted down only by words that actually come, so nothing ever cleared it; iop_bce_error_terminate tore down everything else about the abandoned transfer (MIA drained, queued DMA dropped) and left the count.  vehicle_io_agrees compares recvLeft across the machines, so once one computer had error-terminated a receive on a bus and another had not, they disagreed PERMANENTLY and no capture could ever find the vehicle quiet.  SECOND DEFECT, which turned that into a corrupt result rather than a refusal: the forced-capture retry counter was incremented only for the vehicle's capture writer, so only that machine could reach the 'taken anyway' branch and a forced capture wrote ONE computer.
  - evidence: END-TO-END CONFIRMED 2026-09-22, all three scenarios on the fixed build.  (1) Five GPCs IPLed and left in OPS 0: capture 0 deferrals, 0 forced, 5 'SNAPSHOT at t=' lines, 15 gpc files.  (2) Five GPCs in one OPS 301 redundant set, the configuration this was found in: run clean to 1500 s -- 0 FCMSFAIL, 0 fail-to-sync, 0 905a, 0 error terminations, 0 receive timeouts, 0 votes, five machines in lockstep at NIA 404b4-404c2 BSR=8 DSR=1 -- and the mid-run capture also 0 deferrals, 0 forced, 15 files.  (3) THAT SNAPSHOT RESTORED: all five machines resumed (GPC1-5 RESUME plus mmu1, mmu2, mtu and the intercomputer bus), ran to shutdown, 0 FCMSFAIL, 0 fail-to-sync, 0 error terminations, no X'0503' Instruction Monitor storm and 0 votes.  So a five-GPC vehicle can now be IPLed, captured and restored, which it could not before.  ROOT CAUSE was stale recvLeft after an error termination; see the claim and src/iop.c.
  - procs `CPU,MSC` &middot; config `G3` &middot; files `src/iop.c,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `snapshot incomplete,five GPCs,set never agreed about its I/O,recvLeft stale,error terminate,capture forced` &middot; run `g5set`
- **#205** *(emulator)* THE EMULATOR SEGFAULTS AT TEARDOWN, and has done seven times in two days without anything in our own logs saying so.  It is an EXIT RACE: the main thread is inside exit() flushing stdio while bcenet_transport.c's tx_thread_main is still running and reads memory that has gone.  It happens AFTER a run is scored, so no measurement is known to be wrong -- but the crash lands while libc is flushing, so buffered output (the shutdown SIMULATED TIME lines, the mmu and icc reports) can be lost, and a run whose tail is missing looks like a run that ended cleanly.
  - evidence: ROOT CAUSE AND FIX, 2026-09-23.  bcenet_transport_open_bus is called from EVERY machine's own emulation thread, as that machine opens its first bus, and it took NO LOCK AT ALL.  The transmit thread was started there by an unguarded test-then-create-then-set: 'if (!t->txThreadRunning) { if (pthread_create(&t->txThread, ...) == 0) t->txThreadRunning = true; }'.  Two computers opening their first bus at the same moment both saw the flag clear and both created a thread; t->txThread then held only the SECOND handle, so bcenet_transport_free joined that one and ORPHANED the first.  The orphan went on reading t->txStop out of the transport for the rest of the run, and when free(t) (bcenet_transport.c:465) released it the next read was of memory that had gone -- which is why every one of the seven crashes was at the same instruction, why they were 'error 4' reads of an absent page, and why the main thread was always already inside exit() flushing stdio.  FIXED by taking the transport's own lock around the start, and by making txStop atomic rather than merely volatile (volatile promises nothing between threads).  A THEORY TESTED AND REFUTED ON THE WAY: that run.c:919's '!veh->built' test created one transport per machine, the same trap vehicle.c:969 documents for the mass-memory load.  Instrumented bcenet_transport_create and measured -- a five-GPC run creates exactly ONE.  VERIFIED WITH THREADSANITIZER, which is the evidence rather than the argument: against the unfixed file TSan names the race in bcenet_transport_open_bus at the txThreadRunning line; against the fixed file that race is gone.  New regression test test/test_bcenet_txthread.c opens eight buses from eight threads released together by a barrier and then frees the transport, which is where the orphan died; 'make tsan-bcenet' is the build that would have caught this originally.  make test: every stage passed.  STILL OPEN, FOUND BY THE SAME TSan RUN AND NOT PART OF THIS CRASH: pump_once reads b->fd across every bus slot while open_bus writes it (bcenet_transport.c:883) -- a real race on a socket descriptor, recorded as its own entry; and yagpc_port_base's lazy init (discretes.c:43), which the emulator itself never reaches because main.c sets the port base before any thread starts.
  - files `src/bcenet_transport.c,test/test_bcenet_txthread.c` &middot; symptom `segfault,crash at exit,tx_thread_main,exit race,lost shutdown output,truncated log` &middot; run `rgt7700-2,ascent5-7500`
- **#206** *(emulator)* THE TRANSMIT THREAD READS BUS SOCKET DESCRIPTORS WHILE ANOTHER COMPUTER IS OPENING THEM.  pump_once walks every bus slot testing b->fd, and bcenet_transport_open_bus writes b->fd, b->txFd and b->port from each machine's own emulation thread WITHOUT TAKING THE TRANSPORT LOCK -- so the transmit thread can see a descriptor that is half set up.  Unlike #205 this is not a crash at teardown; it is a live race on a socket, in the bus transport every multi-GPC run uses.
  - evidence: FIXED 2026-09-23, and it was smaller than deferring it implied -- pump_once's locking did not have to change at all.  open_bus now builds both sockets into LOCALS, then takes the transport lock, tests b->fd again, and publishes port, txFd and txPort before storing fd LAST with a release.  pump_once's read of b->fd is an acquire, so seeing a live descriptor means everything it implies is already there.  THAT CLOSES BOTH HALVES.  Order: b->fd used to be written FIRST, then b->port, with b->txFd left at -1 until a second socket had been made -- and pump_once treats any slot with fd >= 0 as live, so the transmit thread could see a bus whose port was unset or whose txFd was still -1, silently taking the no-transmit-socket fallback for that pass.  Exclusion: the early-out 'if (b->fd >= 0) return true' was not exclusion at all, because a shared bus (DK, MM, FC) is ONE slot for every machine and only the intercomputer bus has a slot per computer; two computers first touching the same bus together both got past it and both bound the port, and since these are multicast sockets with SO_REUSEADDR THE SECOND BIND SUCCEEDED -- no error, no log line, one socket orphaned in the group with nobody reading it.  So the earlier observation that no log anywhere contains a bind failure was worthless as evidence, and is withdrawn.  The loser of the race now closes what it built and uses what the winner published.  WHY IT WAS NOT ONLY A TEARDOWN PROBLEM: a bus is opened the first time EACH MACHINE touches it (bcenet_framer.c's ensure_bus), so the window reopens mid-run -- a GPC taking over a display bus at an OPS transition, the mass-memory buses at the first overlay, the ICC buses as the set forms.  VERIFIED: ThreadSanitizer reported this race at bcenet_transport.c:883 in pump_once before the change and reports NOTHING AT ALL after it (0 warnings, where the same build reported 3 when #205 was fixed).  yagpc_port_base's lazy init went atomic in the same pass, which was the last of the three.  A five-GPC OI340700 vehicle restores and runs clean on the rebuilt transport: all five RESUME, 0 votes, 0 FCMSFAIL, 0 fail-to-sync, no bind failures, no transmit-socket fallbacks.  make test: every stage passed.  STILL UNKNOWN whether it ever actually bit: the ways it would show -- a datagram to an unset port, a send down the fallback path -- look like a lost bus word, not like a race.
  - procs `BCE6,BCE7,BCE8` &middot; files `src/bcenet_transport.c,src/discretes.c` &middot; symptom `data race,socket descriptor,pump_once,open_bus,lost bus word`
- **#207** *(emulator)* DPS UTILITY (SPEC 1 PRO, display 0010) draws foreground only -- no title, no MMU ASSIGN/PORT ASSIGN/G3 ARCHIVE labels, no boxes -- even on a FULLY LOADED IDP.  Not the unloaded-unit problem of #197: the background is not supposed to come from the GPC at all.  CR93220A (01/23/08, OI3404) MOVED THE DPS UTILITY BACKGROUND TO MEDS: in SSSRC/CD0010.dfg it DELETEs lines 002000-005446 -- the whole static drawing -- and ADDs two lines, 'DRAW DPS UTILITY BACKGROUND' and VDISP=158.  So the GPC now only NAMES the background and the display unit draws it.  MEDS2.py decodes the VDISP FCW (001111vvvvvvvvvv) and then does nothing with it -- drawFCWS's comment says so outright: 'VDISP latches state this renderer does not draw.'  CD0010.dfg is the ONLY deck in the flight source that uses VDISP, which is exactly why this is the one screen in the vehicle with no background.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-23, one GPC, port base 7400, tape OI340700-v44boot.mmv, IDP1 IPLed normally (8 func=916 format fills, 'load complete ... as unit 1').  GPC MEMORY draws with its background ('0001/   /       GPC MEMORY         1'); SPEC 1 PRO on the same loaded unit announced '0001/001/                          1' -- title text gone -- and the screenshot showed only the item asterisks, RDY/BSY and the 325E VAR PARAM column, in the positions PASS User's Guide figure 3.001 (PDF p.123, USA002869 OI32) gives them.  So the foreground was right and the background absent.  Primary sources: PFS OI340600/SSSRC/CD0010.dfg lines 25-27 (the CR and its ADD/DELETE) and 53-54 (VDISP=158); grep -rln VDISP over OI340600/SSSRC returns CD0010.dfg alone; MEDS2.py:971 (the FCW) and the old comment at :5760.  FIXED in MEDS2.py: a third draw pass, on its own group under the resident one, drawing the halfword stream named by the VDISP number (158 -> data/0010-D-DPS_UTILITY.dfb).  WHERE THE COMMAND ARRIVES was measured, not assumed, and is not where it was expected: a trace put it in the BACKGROUND_TOP stream, not in the per-cycle display list, so PASS points BACKGROUND_TOP at a stub holding the VDISP and nothing else -- which is exactly what the deck compiles to now that its drawing is gone.  The name is therefore recorded from whichever walk finds it (never from the MEDS background's own) and takes effect on the next refresh, a half-second one-frame lag of the same kind _fmtAdj has.  THE CONTENT IS NOT HAND-DRAWN: tools/build_meds_background.py lifts cards 006900-012500 of 'OI301700 as received/SSSRC/CD0010' -- the release before the CR, which still draws it -- into a CRTFMT/STAT/END deck and runs Don's dfg over it, 57 cards -> 388 halfwords ending in CF_PAD 111E.  Verified on a fresh run: '0001/001/       DPS UTILITY        1' with 'MMU ASSIGN   PORT ASSIGN' beneath, and a screenshot matching figure 3.001 label for label -- MMU ASSIGN/STATUS/SOURCE-BUS, CKPT RETRV, RTC 13 and CMD 14 with their underscores, PORT ASSIGN with the PRI/SEC rule, UL CNTL, IPL SOURCE SW MASK 38, VAR PARAM ID LIST, the G3 ARCHIVE and GROUND OPS boxes.
  - csects `CD0010,XD0010,DMIMCD,AIGDEU` &middot; procs `IDP1,IDP3,DK6,DK7,DK8` &middot; files `discretePanel/MEDS2.py,discretePanel/data/0010-D-DPS_UTILITY.dfb,tools/build_meds_background.py` &middot; symptom `no background,DPS UTILITY,SPEC 1 PRO,foreground only,no title,VDISP,display 0010` &middot; doc `PASS User's Guide USA002869 OI32 3.5.001 fig 3.001; CR93220A in CD0010.dfg`
- **#208** *(flight-sw)* A powered, never-loaded IDP is NOT picked up by PASS's own DEU loader even when the gate in DMIMCD says it should be.  DMIMCD.hal ~016700 schedules AIG_DEU_LOADER when the poll reply asks for an IPL AND 'CZ2V_POST_IPL = 0 OR MC = PL_9_MC OR SM_2_MC OR SM_4_MC' -- post-IPL OPS 0 is the FIRST of the allowed configurations.  A GPC sitting in OPS 0 GPC MEMORY is in exactly that state, so an IDP powered during the IPLs should be loaded by it and should not have to wait for a GPCIPL of its own.  It is not.  This is the part of #197 that a script re-ordering hides rather than answers: with it working, CRT3 would get its background the moment it is powered, whatever order the computers are IPLed in.
  - evidence: MEASURED, NOT INFERRED, and not specific to IDP3: with ONE GPC left in post-IPL OPS 0 and IDP1 -- a unit GPCIPL had loaded perfectly, 8 func-916 format fills, 'load complete ... as unit 1' -- the panel's DEU LOAD was pressed.  The unit cleared its memory and asked for an IPL; PASS sent one display fill and never loaded it.  So PASS's own DEU loader does not run for ANY display, and CRT3's blank background in a five-computer run is that, not anything about IDP3.  THE CHAIN, each link measured: (1) DMIMCD reaches DIO(DMIVL_POLL1SN) for the unit -- the clock on an unloaded CRT3 keeps running, so the time fill is going out; (2) it then requires CZ1V_D_TIME_STAT halfword 1 to be non-zero before it will even look at the poll reply; (3) that status is the BCE Status Register, stored by #SST; (4) in this emulator that register was set by NOTHING but a self-test failure -- YAGPC_SSTTRACE over a whole run: 2,984 stores, every one bst=00000000, so the test could never be true; (5) the BCE Principles of Operation gives an error termination three parts -- Program Exception to 0, BCE-MSC Indicator to 1, and 'it records the cause of the error in its own BCE status register' -- and iop_bce_error_terminate did the first two and not the third.  WHAT A REAL DISPLAY DOES, from the DPS Console Handbook's DEU IPL request entry: 'the DEU will respond to the first DEU poll request, returning a single poll header response word (with the DEU initialization required bit set).  The IOP, however, is expecting a 16-word response and will therefore log a T/O ...  When an I/O error is detected on a DEU poll, the UI software (DMI MCDS IN) checks for a DEU load request.'  DMI MCDS IN is DMIMCD: the REQUEST IS DELIVERED AS A TIMEOUT, and the header only says which kind.  MEDS2's DEU answered all sixteen words unless a load was already running, so there was no timeout and no request.  BOTH HALVES NOW FIXED, NEITHER ENOUGH.  iop_bce_error_terminate records the cause (BST_S for a command-sync word mid-receive, BST_ITO/BST_TO for the first or a later word never arriving), Table 1.2's masks are in iop.h, test_bce_status 15/15.  MEDS2's DEU answers a poll with one word whenever it is un-IPLed, not only during a load.  Measured with both: BCE6 takes 120 receive timeouts where it took none, the status register carries ITO, GPCIPL's own load still works unchanged (8 format fills, 'load complete'), and PASS STILL DOES NOT LOAD THE UNIT.  REGRESSION, and the reason the second half could be committed: the owner's five-computer acid test re-run with it -- examples/5gpc-3crt-subtitled.script, five GPCs, three CRTs, IDP3 powered at t=67 and not loaded until GPC5's IPL onto CRT3 at t=600, so nine minutes of an un-IPLed display being polled by a running redundant set -- reached script complete at t=884 with ZERO fail votes, CRT1/CRT2 in 1011 LAUNCH TRAJ 1 under GPC1/GPC2, CRT3 in 0001 GPC MEMORY under GPC5, and IDP3 loaded normally when its turn came.  The added error terminations do not end the set.  NEXT, and the whole of what is left: the link after the status register.  Find what FCOS puts in transaction status halfword 1 -- AIGDEU tests halfwords 3 and 4 for a clean transaction while DMIMCD tests 1, so they are not the same word -- and whether the recorded bit survives to be read at all, since only 1 of 120 terminations reached an #SSC that stored it non-zero: the BCE goes to Wait on an error, so the program's own store never runs and FCOS must be reading the register through PCI instead.  AND THE LOADER IS NOT MERELY FAILING, IT IS NOT RUNNING.  With both halves in, the DEU LOAD test was repeated under YAGPC_MMUTRACE: every mass-memory access in the run is at or before simulated t=38.4 s, which is the IPL and the memory configuration; the DEU LOAD is at wall t=62.4 s and the run goes on to t=182 s with NOT ONE further MMU command.  AIG_DEU_LOADER reads the DEU load module from mass memory (MMURRDCK, AIGDEU.hal ~003900), so it does no I/O.  THAT WAS READ AS 'never scheduled, the failure is still in DMIMCD's gate', AND THAT READING IS WRONG -- the memory diff below shows DMIMCD running its gate to the end and setting the load request bit.  Absence of mass-memory traffic bounds where the loader gets to, not whether it was asked.  Nor does PASS annunciate: a whole-frame capture (NSTS_ANNOUNCE_ROWS=all) over the same window shows no 'I/O ERROR CRT' line, which the DPS Console Handbook says is correct ONLY when the software has seen the error and found the initialization bit set -- so that one observation does not separate 'saw it and suppressed the message' from 'never saw it'.  The two later gates look satisfied on paper: CZ2V_POST_IPL is a COUNTER (CZ2COMMO.hal INITIAL(0), incremented by ARCGPC on an OPS transition, and DLALIGHT comments '= 0 ... CURRENT OPS IS POST IPL'), and the test GPC had IPLed and taken no OPS, so it is 0; AIG_DEU_LOADER cannot be busy if it never runs.  That leaves transaction status halfword 1.  FCOS never READS the BCE status register -- the only access to it in the whole flight source is FIOERRLC's FIORESET, which WRITES zeros to bank C words 6 and 7 of the offending BCE (FIOBCEA6 EQU X'A20B') to reset it -- so halfword 1 is composed by FCOS's own error path, not copied from the hardware, and the next thing to find is what makes FCOS compose it.  The cheapest route is probably an address rather than more reading: CZ1V_D_TIME_STAT is a COMPOOL variable, its offset is in the SDFs, and a snapshot's gpcN.mem.bin can simply be read at that address while a display is asking.  THE GATE IS NOW PASSED, MEASURED IN MEMORY, and the failure has moved inside the loader.  Two snapshots of the same one-GPC configuration -- one with IDP1 loaded and left alone (idp1.json ipled=true), one with it asking after a panel DEU LOAD (ipled=false) -- were diffed halfword by halfword, and the compool structures were located by their own signatures rather than by symbols, which we have none of.  CZ1V_D_TIME_FILL is at 022a4: three 14-halfword structures whose MISSION_TIME decodes as 23,107,984.5, which is day 267 10:53:04 GMT to the second, matching the snapshot's own epoch.  CZ1V_D_POLL is at 02188: four 18-halfword structures, the first with a plausible key count and codes, the other three all zero for the three unpowered units.  READ OUT: (1) CZ1V_D_TIME_STAT for buffer 2 -- which is the one DMIMCD indexes, DMIV_INDEX being bits 9-10 of the poll header plus 1, and our header has bit 10 set -- goes from 0000 0000 0000 0000 to 7000 0000 0000 22d3, so the status test passes; (2) CZ1B_D_POLL_MSG_HEADR for DEU 1, at 0218a, goes from 0040 to 0041, so the 'DEU initialization required' bit is in memory and the header test passes; (3) 02253 goes from 0000 to 8000, the only such transition anywhere in the compool region and exactly what SET_BIT_16(CZ1B_D_DLT,1) writes -- so CZ2V_POST_IPL was 0, DMIMCD reached the end of its gate, REGISTERED THE LOAD REQUEST and scheduled AIG_DEU_LOADER.  The bit is still set at capture, about three minutes after the request, so nothing ever cleared it.  Both committed fixes are therefore necessary and both work: the failure is no longer 'the request is never heard' but 'the request is registered and not serviced'.  NEXT: why AIG_DEU_LOADER does not run.  It produces no bus fills and no mass-memory reads at all, so it is dying at or before its start rather than failing partway -- suspect the SCHEDULE itself (PRIORITY(PRIO_AIG)) or the process not being present in the post-IPL OPS 0 memory configuration.  AIGDEU.hal's own first act is to find the requesting unit in CZ1B_D_DLT and it checks CZ2V_POST_IPL again at ~004500, so a third snapshot taken while the bit is set would show whether AIGV_DEUIPL_ERR_CODE or the AID_DEU_LOGGER table ever gets written.  A CONTROL RUN REFUTES THE TWO PARAGRAPHS ABOVE, and they are left standing only so nobody re-derives them.  The claim was that the display's request was never heard until the BCE status register recorded a cause and the unit answered a poll with one word.  It is wrong.  With BOTH of those backed out -- MEDS2 returning to the iplRunning test, iop_bce_error_terminate passing cause 0 -- and the DEU LOAD test repeated under YAGPC_CMDTRACE=6: 122 BITE STATUS commands on the display bus, first at t=56.5 s, which is the DEU LOAD.  With both in place: 124, first at t=55.2 s.  AIG_DEU_LOADER only issues a BITE once it has been scheduled, found the unit in CZ1B_D_DLT and reserved the SM common buffer, so THE LOADER WAS ALWAYS RUNNING and DMIMCD's gate was always passing.  Both changes remain in the tree because each is right on its own evidence -- the Principles of Operation for the status register, the DPS Console Handbook for the one-word poll -- but neither was the defect, and the commits that shipped them say otherwise.  WHAT THE DEFECT ACTUALLY WAS, in two parts, both in MEDS2's display-unit model and both found by reading the machine's memory.  FIRST, the BITE reply had no message header.  CZ1COM.hal's CZ1B_D_BITE structure is XMIS_STAT ARRAY(4), then CZ1B_D_DEU_MSG_HDR, then CZ1B_D_BITE_STAT ARRAY(4) -- nine halfwords per unit -- and the reply lands at the header.  MEDS2's biteResponse began with BITE word 1, so every word landed one slot early.  Overlaid on a capture taken while a unit was asking: DEU1 at 022db reads XMIS_STAT=0000 0000 0000 0000, MSG_HDR=c000, BITE_STAT=0000 2000 20c0 0000, with DEUs 2, 3 and 4 at +9, +18 and +27 all zero, which is what fixes the base.  c000 is BITE1_HEALTHY sitting in the header's slot; CZ1B_D_BITE_STAT(1), which AIGDEU tests, holds the 0000 that belongs one place further on.  SECOND, and underneath it, IPL PERFORMED was tied to the wrong thing.  biteState set BITE1.IPL_DONE only when the unit was already loaded, but AIGDEU reads that bit on a unit that has just ASKED to be loaded and refuses unless bits 2 to 4 read BIN'100' (AIGDEU.hal 008500).  Tying it to the load makes the test unsatisfiable exactly when it is asked and the loader dead code on a working orbiter; the bit is the unit's own initialization, which the DPS Console Handbook describes as what a DEU does the moment it detects an IPL request.  FIXED: the reply leads with the header, and IPL PERFORMED is set for any unit not reporting a fault.  MEASURED, on the wire, with NSTS_BITE_TRACE: before, 0041 8000 0000 2000 5fbf; after, 0041 c000 0000 2000 1fbf.  AND THE LOADER MOVES.  Mass-memory commands in the same test: 56, all at or before t=37.4 s -- the IPL and the memory configuration -- before the fix; 440 after, running to t=201.3 s, beginning at t=55 s with the DEU LOAD.  It positions to 4/4/0 and reads 17 blocks from 4/4/0/7, which is 8704 halfwords and exactly AIGV_READ.DWDCT, and it does this 86 times.  STILL OPEN, and this is now the whole of it: the load does not complete.  No format fill ever follows, so AIGDEU is failing after its mass-memory read -- either CDHV_RW_TSW is non-zero, which would set AIGV_DEUIPL_ERR_CODE to 9 or 11 and skip the fills, or the eight fills of the OUTER loop are issued and fail (code 3).  The retry count says it is looping, not stopping.  Next: read CDHV_RW_TSW, and check that what sits at 4/4/0/7 on our tape is the display control program AIGDEU expects, since CZ1V_MM_ADDR_TBL(5) is what points there.  AND THE LAST LINK IS NOT CODE AT ALL -- see #209.  The loader's mass-memory read is of block 9223, CZ1V_MM_ADDR_TBL entry 5, and no volume we have holds a block there: not our OI340700 build, not pass-910.  It reads 8704 zeros, its read-with-check fails, and it retries.  So #208's chain is now traced end to end -- the request is made, the gate passes, the loader runs, asks the unit for its BITE status, is now told the truth, goes to mass memory, and finds a hole.  The two defects in this entry were real and are fixed; what remains is tape content, which is #209's.  FIXED, END TO END.  Two more defects, both in the same display model. THIRD: function 380 carries two different things and the model told them apart by neither.  onData routed EVERY 380 transfer to _timeFill regardless of length, so the eight memory fills AIG_DEU_LOADER sends -- 510 halfwords five times, then 202, 3 and 252, which is AIGV_FILL_LENGTH plus two, element for element -- were each stored as a header clock and thrown away.  Measured with YAGPC_CMDTRACE=6: 64 complete passes of all eight fills in one run, 320 of them at 510 halfwords, and not one halfword reaching the unit's memory.  Seven halfwords is the clock; anything else is a fill.  FOURTH: the unit never asserted a critical BITE.  Having loaded the control program the loader polls and requires header bits 15 to 16 to read BIN'10' -- critical BITE present, no IPL required (AIGDEU.hal 015700) -- and gives up with code 6 otherwise.  A unit with a control program and no critical formats fails its own format checksum, which IS a critical BITE, and the loader's next act is to send the formats and re-check that checksum (017700).  The model now asserts it in exactly that window.  DMIMCD reads the same bit only to decide to checksum the poll response (DMIMCD.hal 021100), which this model computes.  RESULT, and it is the first time PASS has ever loaded a display unit by itself: DEU LOAD, poll 0041 IPL_REQUIRED, eight fills at 0f49 1145 1341 153d 1739 1935 1fe4 0002 of 508 508 508 508 508 200 1 250, 'load complete ... as unit 1', poll 0042 BITE_CRITICAL, then eight format fills at 0100 02fc 04f8 06f4 08f0 0aec 0ce8 0ee4 of 508 seven times and 101, poll 0040 healthy.  Every address and length is AIGB_DEU_FILL_LOC, AIGV_FILL_LENGTH, AIGB_DEU_DFT_LOC and AIGV_DEU_DFT_FILL exactly, which is what says the model is now right rather than merely permissive.  The display draws 0001/GPC MEMORY with its background afterwards.
  - csects `DMIMCD,AIGDEU,AIDDEU,CZ2V_POST_IPL,FIOSVC1` &middot; procs `IDP1,IDP3,DK6,DK8,BCE6` &middot; files `src/iop.c,src/iop.h,discretePanel/MEDS2.py,src/deumodel.c` &middot; symptom `no background,CRT3,IDP3 not loaded,IPL_REQUIRED,load started no load complete,foreground only` &middot; doc `OI301700/SSSRC/DMIMCD.hal 014400-017610; BCE Principles of Operation IBM-6246556A part 3 Table 1.2 (p.14-16) and 2.2; DPS Console Handbook, DEU IPL request`
- **#213** *(emulator)* THE THREE MTU ACCUMULATORS ARE THREE DIFFERENT COMPUTERS' CLOCKS in the default build, not three taps off one oscillator. mtumodel was given whichever COMPUTER happened to be calling (mtumodel_set_clock(br->mtu, br->clockUs)), so accumulator 1, read by GPC1 on bus 20, comes from GPC1's clock while accumulator 2, read by GPC2 on bus 21, comes from GPC2's -- and FPMMTURM, whose header says it is 'VALIDATING THE TIME SOURCE EVERY 960 MILLISECONDS', is then comparing three computers' clocks wearing a timing unit's name. mtumodel_set_shared_us fixes it by handing the model the vehicle's shared clock (run.c router_shared_us), which is what mmumodel_service_as has taken as an argument since it was written. THE FIX IS PRESENTLY GATED BEHIND YAGPC_FC_MDM and so is NOT in the default build, which is a deliberate stop, not a judgement that it is wrong: see the evidence.
  - evidence: The owner, looking at the three accumulators in OPS 0 on 2026-09-25: 'The almost-exact agreement is almost disturbing. I'm glad we saw one that was a millisecond off, or I might suspect those were just duplicates.' A millisecond apart is the signature of three separate computer clocks; one oscillator read three ways would agree exactly. WHY IT IS GATED RATHER THAN SHIPPED: the default-configuration gate (five computers, whole acid test, 2400 s, YAGPC_FC_MDM unset) took FOUR fail votes at t=1914.4, all four members against GPC2, with three behaviours active that the MDM work had made unconditional -- the shared clock, the wire pacing, and an unconditional listener echo. All three are now behind the switch and the default build is arithmetically what the owner validated clean over an hour. WHICH OF THE THREE COST THE VOTES IS NOT KNOWN: they were changed together and each test is a 40-minute run. The listener echo is the prime suspect, because with it the OFF arm delivered 3,063 words to listening computers where the baseline rule delivers essentially none (#210: 'no listener was ever echoed one'). THE DISCRIMINATING TEST is a gate run with the shared clock alone re-enabled, which is cheap to arrange behind its own flag and has not been done. A SEPARATE TRAP FOUND WHILE GATING, and worth stating because it is invisible: mtu_fill_time had folded the halt offset into 'us', which drives the mission ACCUMULATORS, where the baseline applies it only to the GMT epoch. That moved every accumulator in the DEFAULT build. 'us' and 'epochUs' are now kept apart.  CLOSED 2026-09-28. The shared clock has been the default since YAGPC_FC_MDM went default-on (2026-09-26): mtu_fill_time takes m->sharedUs, so all three accumulators come from one time base. With fc482e01a (0.125 ms resolution), run mtut1 (four GPCs, OPS 201, examples/4gpc-mtu-time.script, NSTS_ANNOUNCE_ROWS=all, screenwatch.py): 221 TIME-display frames over 3.5 min, MTU ACCUM 1, 2 and 3 IDENTICAL to each other and to GPC time in 220 (the other caught between two 960 ms updates: accumulators exactly 0.960 s behind), no down arrow in any frame, GMT 271/17:50:32 at 12:50:32 CDT -- the vehicle's clock on the real time of day.

- **#214** *(emulator)* WHY THE TIMING UNIT IS STILL FROZEN IN A FIVE-COMPUTER VEHICLE EVEN WITH THE MDM ANSWERING ON: it is the LISTENER half of the NSP power-discrete read, and it loses a race with the next command. The answering satisfies the COMMANDER's read of FIONSP1P/FIONSP2P with one zero word ('the box is not powered'), and that works. The listener's own element does not: mtumodel clears every reader's pending answer on EVERY command ('clear always, echo always'), and buses 20 and 22 carry about 940 commands a second, so a listener has roughly one millisecond to drain a word it is handed. It usually wins and sometimes does not, and FIOERRLC's threshold is TWO. Two counted errors call FCMBCEMD, which bypasses the whole STRING; FCMRTBLE is titled 'NSP AND MTU RESTORE TABLES' and groups the two, and FIONSPPG's elements sit immediately before FIOPRMPG's in store -- so the timing unit goes down with the NSP it has nothing to do with. THE FIX IS THE LISTENER DELIVERY, not more coverage: a listener must receive an answered read whether or not it polls before the next command.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-25 on acid-wire, a five-computer acid test with YAGPC_FC_MDM ON (switch confirmed in the run's own output: nspReads 21548, biteReads 24; the OFF arm gate-final prints no such line at all -- that, and not the 7.0 words-per-read ratio, is the marker, since wordsOut counts MDM answers too). 2,035 error terminations, and 1,701 of them (84%) are just TWO program addresses: pc=1cc2e left=1 x831 on bus 20 and pc=1cc68 left=1 x870 on bus 22. Resolved against mafgen/csects-G9.json and csects-SSW.json (not recalled): 1cc2e = FIONSPPG FIOBYNC3+4 and 1cc68 = FIONSPPG FIOBYNC7+4, with FIOPRMPG FIOBY51 at 1cc74 immediately after. left=1 means the element armed for one word and got none. THE SHAPE IS STRUCTURAL AND CONFIRMS THE READING: GPC1 holds 303 (1cc68 only -- it COMMANDS bus 20 and only listens on 22), GPC3 holds 304 (1cc2e only -- it commands 22), and GPC2 and GPC4 hold 586 and 598, both addresses, because they command neither and listen on both. AND THE ANSWERING DOES NOT PREVENT THE BYPASS: acid-wire's errors cease at t=758.5 s and gate-final's at t=759.5 s -- the same moment -- and acid-wire's timeReads is 2142, IDENTICAL to the answering-OFF arm's 2142. The answering cut errors 25% (2035 against 2729) and kept the set (0 votes against 3) but left the timing unit exactly as dead. The two-computer fixture does not show this because it has at most one listener per bus and far less traffic. WHAT NOT TO DO: a per-reader QUEUE was tried and produced 158,169 runaway BCE lines by burying a listener in backlog, and withholding the echo from a busy reader LATCHES it for ever, since a Listen-Mode BCE stores nothing until it sees a command sync bearing its own IUA and waits indefinitely. The delivery has to be prompt AND bounded.  FIXED 2026-09-26, AND THE CAUSE WAS NOT THE 'clear always' RACE THIS ENTRY SUPPOSED.  THE WIRE PACING CARRIED A STALE INDEX: mtumodel paced on 'head', the cursor into the timing unit's REPLY buffer, which only mtu_fill_time resets -- so after a seven-word MTU reply it sat at 7 and the next MDM answer on that bus was withheld for 8*33 us instead of 33.  FIONSPPG issues its next command about 100 us later, which cleared the answer first, and the listener was handed a command sync where its data belonged: Table 1.2 Sync Error, error termination with left=1.  It paces on 'sent' now -- words this reader has taken of THIS transaction, reset wherever a transaction begins.  THE PACING INTRODUCED THIS: before it, ready was always true and the listener got its word, which is why #210's two-computer result predates the pacing and why acid-wire shows timeReads 2142, identical to the control.  MEASURED with YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE=20,22, five computers, same script and tape either side: FIOBYNC3+4 went from 2,240 command syncs against 8 data words to 2,352 against 1,176; FIOBYNC7+4 from 2,268/6 to 2,352/1,173; total error terminations 1,560 to 51; fail votes 3 to ZERO.  2352:1176 is exactly what the wire carries -- the listen command, the command matching the listener's IUAR, then one data word of 0x0000.  THE 51 THAT REMAIN ARE UNIVERSAL: aligned on a shared wire event they fall at T+15.7, T+16.7, T+62.9 and T+63.1 on GPC2, GPC3 and GPC4 together, which is the commfault path, not the self-fail path.  THE UNIT IS STILL BYPASSED at t~760 in both arms; the remaining trigger is 14-word reads at 1d5b6, 1d636 and 1d67c -- FIOIMUC1, the IMU -- see #216.  test/test_mtumodel.c holds the case, verified adversarially.
  - addrs `1cc0a,1cc2e,1cc44,1cc68,1cc74,1cc7e,1cc88` &middot; csects `FIONSPPG,FIOBYNC1,FIOBYNC3,FIOBYNC5,FIOBYNC7,FIOPRMPG,FIOBY51,FCMRTBLE,FCMBCEMD,FIOERRLC` &middot; instrs `#RDS,#MIN,#MINC,#CMDI` &middot; procs `BCE20,BCE21,BCE22,MTU` &middot; config `G9,G2,SSW` &middot; env `YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_ERRTERM_TRACE` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `MTU ACCUM blank with M,listener times out left=1,timeReads identical to control,string bypassed,FIOBYNC` &middot; tsec `364-759` &middot; doc `mafgen/csects-G9.json; FCMRTBLE 'NSP AND MTU RESTORE TABLES'; BCE PoO 4.1 Listen Mode` &middot; run `acid-wire,gate-final`
- **#218** *(emulator)* THE LISTENER DEFECT OF #214 SURVIVES ON BUSES 14-17 -- the 'sent' pacing fix cured it only where the gap between commands is wide enough. On the forward and aft MDM buses a listener takes the command sync and NEVER the data word, while the COMMANDER on the same bus takes its data normally. The model hands both the same answer on the same command, so the difference is entirely when each next polls: the listener's pending words are cleared by the commander's next command before the listener returns for them, and on these buses the commands are closer together than on 20-22. THE REMEDY IS THE ONE #210 NAMED AND NOBODY HAS BUILT: a BOUNDED per-reader capture, so a listener that is one transfer behind still receives that transfer, without the unbounded backlog that produced 158,169 runaway BCE lines when a plain queue was tried.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26, five computers, YAGPC_FC_MDM=1 YAGPC_FC_SENSORS=1, YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE=14,15,16,17 (discretePanel/fa). On those buses 501 words taken and 186 error terminations. By element and transmitter state: pc=1dcbc took 48 words, ALL of them command sync, with xmit=0 -- a listener -- and error-terminated 48 times with left=6; pc=1da3a, 1dab2, 1da76 and 1da02 each took 9 words, all command sync, xmit=0, and error-terminated 9 times each with left=1. Against that, pc=1dc94 (64 words), 1dc82, 1dc88, 1dc8e (48 each) and 1d752, 1d758 (32 each) are all xmit=1 -- COMMANDERS -- and every word they took was DATA. Every element that error-terminates did take at least one word, so nothing is being starved of the command sync itself; what they never get is the data that should follow it. Resolved earlier against mafgen/csects-G9.json, these are FIOHFE89's FIOBYLR1..8 and FIOBYRR1, FIOMFEG9's FIOBY27L/28L/29L and FIOGNIPG's FIOELG8L/FIOELG4L -- every one an L element. RELATED MEASUREMENT, #217: on buses 20/22 the median interval between one command and the next is 198 us for a one-word receive and 561 us for a 21-word one, and half of all 21-word reads have the wire reclaimed before the reply could physically pass. DO NOT RETRY THE WIRE HOLD: YAGPC_WIRE_HOLD_BUSES over 14-17 and 20-23 makes both the timing unit and the set worse (#217).  FIXED 2026-09-26 by a BOUNDED per-reader backlog (src/mtumodel.c mtu_carry_take, gated with the rest of the answering): when a command arrives and a reader is MID-TRANSFER -- it has taken its sync and still wants words -- what it still wants is moved aside and delivered AHEAD of the new command's sync, the order the wire had them in.  LISTENERS ONLY: a commander that has just issued a command has finished the receive before it, and the existing test caught that mistake.  BOUNDED AT ONE, because a plain queue gave 158,169 runaway BCE lines (#210); a reader two behind loses the older one, and a test says so.  The echo is never withheld, since a Listen-Mode BCE that is not echoed can never drain.  MEASURED, five computers, same fixture and tracing either side (discretePanel/fa against discretePanel/carry): pc=1dcbc goes from 48 command syncs and ZERO data words to 36 and 36, and its left=6 error terminations fall 48 to 36.  THE TWELVE THAT GO DO NOT VANISH: pc=1dcbd, the NEXT instruction in that chain, gains ten with left=12, and 1dcbc gains two with left=1 -- the listener now COMPLETES its receive and advances, and the element after it fails for its own reason.  Total error terminations therefore unchanged at 186, the unit still bypassed, vote count unchanged: a correctness fix to the listener path, NOT a fix for #216.  22 transfers carried over the run, none dropped as a second, so the bound was never reached.  Not saved in a snapshot: a restore loses at most one pending transfer per reader.
  - addrs `1dcbc,1da3a,1dab2,1da76,1da02,1dc94,1dc82,1dc88,1dc8e,1d752,1d758,1d774,1d78e,1d7a8` &middot; csects `FIOHFE89,FIOMFEG9,FIOGNIPG,FIOBYLR1,FIOBYRR1,FIOBY27L,FIOELG8L` &middot; instrs `#RDLI,#MIN,#MINC` &middot; procs `BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS,YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `listener takes only command syncs,no data word,left=6,left=32,commander fine,L element` &middot; doc `BCE PoO 4.1 Listen Mode; csects-G9 element names` &middot; run `fa`
- **#238** *(method)* THE TENS OF THOUSANDS OF CLOCK EXCURSIONS AND BIG STEPS NEVER EXISTED. Both summary lines in vehicle.c summed their histograms FROM THE LAST BUCKET BELOW THE THRESHOLD, so each reported the band just under the threshold as though it were past it. The step line announced 29,225 to 39,396 steps of ">=200 us" in runs where the true count is 45, and the spread line counted the 150-200 us band as "past 200 us (the 211 us hold)".
  - evidence: The bucketing is "first k with x < EDGE[k]", so for the step edges {1,3,10,25,50,100,200,400,...} bucket 6 is NAMED "<200" and holds 100 <= d < 200; the first bucket at or above 200 is 7. The report summed from k=6. The spread edges {25,50,100,150,200,250,...} put "<200" at bucket 4 and the report summed from k=4. CAUGHT by adding per-call-site attribution for the big steps: it returned instr->instr 45, instr->idle 0, idle->instr 0, idle->idle 0 -- a total of 45 against the same line s own claim of 29,225 in the same run, which is impossible and pointed straight at the summation. The 45 matches the independent per-machine probe in run.c (34 and 45 in two runs) exactly. CONSEQUENCES FOR EVERYTHING MEASURED BEFORE 2026-09-26: every excursion count quoted in this ledger -- 19527, 12664, 9905, 7917, 4707, 2202 -- includes the band BELOW its threshold and is too large; comparisons BETWEEN runs are still valid because the bias is identical. #227 s claim that capping the wait-state pass cut excursions from 12664 to 7917 is a ratio of two equally inflated numbers and survives; the absolute figures do not. AND THE HUNT IT CAUSED: a day went into explaining tens of thousands of steps that two correctly written probes could not find, and the probes were right. Fixed in both lines, with the reason recorded at the code.
  - env `YAGPC_BARSPREAD_US,YAGPC_BIGSTEP_US` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c` &middot; symptom `excursions,big steps,clock spread` &middot; run `errwho,stepfix,idlestep`
- **#241** *(emulator)* FIX ATTEMPTED FOR #240: ff_nsp_words answered a surveyed read only when its IUA was 10 or 12, while the command was echoed to every listener whatever its IUA. A listener on any other unit therefore armed its receive for n words, was handed none, and was cut off by the next echoed command -- a Table 1.2 Sync Error with left = n. The IUA gate is now lifted when YAGPC_FC_SENSORS is on, leaving the by-name survey test to decide, which is what it is for.
  - evidence: THE LENGTHS PROVE THE ATTRIBUTION. The reads the survey names at the three IUAs this declined are FIODLCM1/FIODLCM5 at 1 word (IUA 17), FIOHI1EI at 6 (IUA 17, 23, 24) and FIOFAIC5 at 32 (IUA 17, 23, 24). The error terminations in run iccage carried left=1 seventy times, left=6 twenty-eight times and left=32 forty-four times -- 142 of 151 -- every one of them inside FIOHFE02 or FIOMFE02, and there was not one receive time-out in the whole log, because the next echoed command always arrived before the time-out could. WHY THE GATE IS REDUNDANT: fc_sym()/surveyed already declines a command the survey never saw or that takes no receive, which covers FIOHIBAD and the FIOLMIUA listen broadcast -- the two cases the gate was really protecting against -- and the length answered is the count the BCE actually armed for (iop_bce_armed_words), so nothing is left over for the next transfer. WHY IT IS GATED ON THE SWITCH: with the answering off fc_ours() is a short list that includes FIOMDMRT and the FIOFAIC prefix, so lifting the gate unconditionally would newly answer boxes in the DEFAULT build, which is the configuration the owner has validated clean over an hour. PREDICTION, recorded before the run that tests it: the error terminations in FIOHFE02/FIOMFE02 should fall towards zero, and with them the FIOGPCWE == 1 condition that force-fails a computer, so the set should hold. BASELINE TO BEAT, run iccage: 151 error terminations, GPC2 self-failing from FIOERRLC+0313 at shared 716.848796, and 8 FCMSFAIL entries in all. If the errors persist the attribution is wrong and this must be recorded as refuted.  REFUTED BY ITS OWN PREDICTION, run iuafix, 2026-09-26, AND REVERTED. The prediction was that the FIOHFE02/FIOMFE02 error terminations would fall towards zero and the set would hold. They did not and it did not. ERROR TERMINATIONS 151 -> 131, a 13 percent fall where near-elimination was predicted. FCMSFAIL ENTRIES 8 -> 14, and the run lost TWO computers where the baseline lost one: GPC2 self-failed from FIOERRLC+0313 at shared 713.897280 and was voted out by GPC1, GPC3 and GPC4; then GPC1 ITSELF self-failed from FIOERRLC+0313 at shared 760.715599 and was voted out by GPC3 and GPC4 from FCMTSYNC+0029 (r7=987f6031 -> 1987f) and by GPC5 from FCMCSYNC. THE LENGTH PROFILE SHOWS WHY THE ATTRIBUTION WAS WRONG: left=6 is UNCHANGED at 28 occurrences, which is the FIOHI1EI count the argument rested on most heavily; left=1 fell 70 -> 57 and left=32 fell 44 -> 24, so lifting the gate removed only some; and left=30 ROSE 2 -> 9, left=34 ROSE 2 -> 4, with new error sites appearing at BCE program addresses 1dcbc, 1dcfc and 1dd28 that the baseline never touched. So answering the previously-declined IUAs removed some Sync Errors and MANUFACTURED others, and left the vehicle worse. The matching of left-values to the lengths of reads at IUA 17/23/24 was a coincidence of a small set of common lengths (1, 6 and 32 are the commonest reply lengths in the whole survey), not evidence of the mechanism. THE IUA GATE IS NOT THE DEFECT. DO NOT RETEST. #240 s diagnosis of the FAILURE CHAIN still stands -- it was measured directly and independently -- but its account of WHY one computer holds an error its peers do not is now open again.  REOPENED 2026-09-27 -- the refutation above ran WITHOUT echo expiry and WITHOUT the lock, so its deaths were stale-echo deaths and it could not test answering at other IUAs (the same confound that invalidated aa-a, #249). The case is live again: run hk1a (five computers, every fix on, wide rings) lost GPC2 at shared 713.99 after two rounds (713.904 and 713.983) in which ALL FOUR set members waited for b8401f -- FIOFAIC5, a SURVEYED 32-word read at IUA 23 in FIOMFEG9 -- and nobody answered: the model ring shows GPC2 commanding b8401f on bus 15 and the three listeners taking its echo, with no reply queued, and every computer then erring left=32 (commander at FIOMFEG9 +0214/+0228..., listeners at the matching +xx). The acid test s other universal G9 errors are the same family: left=1 at FIOHFE89+01fe/+021a and left=6 at FIOHFE89 (FIOHI1EI, 6 words, IUA 17/23/24). NARROWER THAN BEFORE: YAGPC_FC_ANY_IUA answers only a read NAMED in the survey WITH a receive length, that a BCE has ARMED a receive for, at any IUA; unnamed reads are still answered only at 10/12. Being tested (acid-ai1..4, g3-ai1, s201-ai1), with the four-computer configurations as the regression check.  FIRST RESULTS, acid-ai1 and acid-ai2 (2026-09-27, five computers, every fix plus YAGPC_FC_ANY_IUA): both CLEAN -- zero votes, zero FCMSFAIL, the timing unit live on all five -- and error terminations fell from 97-120 per run to 32. The DECLINED log (new) listed what the model still refuses: FIOFAIC5 (32 words) and FIOHI1EI (6 words) at IUAs 17, 23 and 24, which ANY_IUA now answers (the "IUA not 10 or 12" lines for them were a logging mistake -- printed on the gate rather than on answering nothing -- since fixed), and 631c20 FIOMDMRT at IUA 12 with armed=1, refused because the survey lists that code only as a command. ALL 32 remaining errors were that event: a one-word receive on buses 14-17 awaited by the commander (FIOHFE89+01fe) and all three listeners (+021a) together. fcbustable.h s own header says only the run-time state can tell FIOMDMRT s read from its bare command, so for FIOMDMRT ALONE the armed count now overrides the survey (in ANY_IUA); the survey s no-receive verdict still stands for everything else, because FIOHIBAD is a read PASS sends deliberately to a missing channel to stop a BCE.  FIXED, and made a default 2026-09-27 01:39 pending no-environment validation. acid-ai3 and acid-ai4 (five computers, every fix, YAGPC_FC_ANY_IUA with the FIOMDMRT armed-count rule): ZERO error terminations in the entire acid test, both runs -- zero votes, zero FCMSFAIL, the timing unit live on all five, and NOTHING declined any more (no DECLINED lines). The same test carried 97-120 errors with the other fixes alone, and 8,245 on the old default. The four-computer configurations with ANY_IUA (g3-ai1, s201-ai1) are clean with 5 and 1 errors -- no regression. THEORY: answers only reads NAMED in the survey with a receive length, which a BCE has ARMED, at any flight-critical IUA -- the FA MDM reads FIOFAIC5 (32 words) and FIOHI1EI (6 words) at IUAs 17, 23 and 24 -- plus FIOMDMRT by armed count; unnamed reads are still answered only at 10/12, and the survey s no-receive verdict still protects FIOHIBAD. #241 s original refutation was a confounded run and is superseded.
  - csects `FIOHFE02,FIOMFE02,FIOERRLC` &middot; env `YAGPC_FC_SENSORS,YAGPC_FC_MDM` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `sync error,fail to sync,loss of sync` &middot; run `iccage,iuafix`
- **#249** *(emulator)* THE TIMING UNIT IS LOST BECAUSE THE MODEL ANSWERS NOBODY FOR A RUN-TIME-BUILT MDM READ. Commands 532aaa (FF MDMs, IUA 10) and 632aaa (FA MDMs, IUA 12) reach the model twice a run, once per string, with a one-word receive armed on the commander AND on every listener. No equate in the build carries their value, so the static bus survey cannot name them, and ff_nsp_words declines anything unnamed -- so no computer is answered, all four error on every string at the same instant, the flight software commfaults every string, and the timing unit is bypassed along with its string (FCMRTBLE). It is universal, which is why it never fails anyone to sync and why it was mistaken for a sync problem s shadow.
  - evidence: A whole-run census of every command code reaching mtumodel (trial exl-h, 2026-09-26) lists 116 distinct codes; 46 at IUA 10/12 have no survey name, and almost all are high-volume outputs (13,832 each) with no receive. Two are rare: 532aaa=8 and 632aaa=8 -- four strings, twice -- matching the universal left=1 events seen in every trial (e.g. exl-a at 56.386, all eight buses: commander at FIOHFE38+01fe, the three listeners at FIOHFE38+021a, every computer). Correction to an earlier entry: the MDM RETURN WORD read is 531555/631555 in this build (96 each), which FF_MDM_READS already answers -- NOT FIOMDMRT (531c20), which never appears in the run at all. The OI301700 and OI340600 BCEEQU both give FIOMDMRT=X 00031C20 and neither carries 12AAA, so these commands are built at run time. FIX, behind YAGPC_FC_ANSWER_ALL=1: answer any read addressed to the FF/FA MDMs that a BCE has armed a receive for, named or not, with the armed length; every unnamed armed read is logged once (UNANSWERED). On the vehicle the MDM answers, or the orbiter would lose every string twice a run. PREDICTION: FIOBY51-53 and FIOBY51L-53L stay live on every computer. Trials aa-a/b/c test it.  THE FIX IS REFUTED, trial aa-a (2026-09-26, YAGPC_FC_ANSWER_ALL=1 with the lock, WITHOUT echo expiry) -- and the switch has been REMOVED; only the once-per-code UNANSWERED log remains. The timing unit was still bypassed on all four computers. The run logged only 632aaa as unanswered (532aaa never appeared as an unnamed armed read, so the pairing stated above was incomplete). Decisive detail: the universal one-word event still happened at 55.504 on buses 20-23, but now ONLY THE THREE LISTENERS erred (FIOHFE38+0038 etc., GPCs 234/124/123/134) -- the answered commander did not -- and three of four is still a commfault, so the string is still bypassed. So the loss is in the LISTENERS one-word receive, not in whether the commander is answered. The three deaths in that trial were the FA stale-echo kind (left=34 at 1d6dc) because echo expiry was off, and say nothing about answer-all. DO NOT RETEST.  REOPENED -- the refutation above was drawn from a CONFOUNDED trial and is withdrawn. Seen from both ends in trial ringerr (YAGPC_RING_ON_ERR=20-23, lock and echo expiry on, answering OFF): at 56.384213 commander GPC3 armed a one-word receive at FIOHFE38+009c and issued 532aaa on bus 22; listeners GPC1, GPC2, GPC4, parked at FIOHFE38+00c4 awaiting an IUA-10 sync, took its echo as that sync and waited for one word; the model queued NOTHING (owed=00, no m word to anyone) and logged UNANSWERED 532aaa armed=1 and 632aaa armed=1; at 56.386223 -- exactly 2.0 ms later -- ALL FOUR computers errored together (cause 0x40). So the model declines a read EVERY computer has armed for, only because the survey has no name for it. Trial aa-a answered it but ran WITHOUT echo expiry, so the listener errors it showed were the stale-echo kind and cannot be charged to the answering. Reinstated as YAGPC_FC_ANSWER_UNNAMED=1, to be judged TOGETHER with echo expiry and the lock.  FIXED ON THE FLOWN CONFIGURATION, trials all-a, all-b, all-c (2026-09-26): restored 4-GPC 3-CRT OPS 0 fixture, OPS 3 0 1 PRO replayed, held 150 s, with YAGPC_FC_MDM=1 YAGPC_FC_SENSORS=1 plus YAGPC_FC_ANSWER_UNNAMED=1 YAGPC_MTU_ECHO_EXPIRE=1 YAGPC_MTU_LOCKED=1. In ALL THREE, mtuscore reports FIOBY51-53 and FIOBY51L-53L ALL LIVE on GPC1, GPC2, GPC3 and GPC4 -- the timing unit being read by every computer after the transition -- with zero CAM votes and zero FCMSFAIL. Against that: in every one of roughly twenty earlier runs and trials on this configuration today (fix4, base-a..c, iuar-a/b, fix-a, ex-a/b, exl-a..l, aa-a, ringerr) the unit was bypassed on the redundant set. The one partial exception, ex-a, kept FIOBY53 on three computers. One residual observation: all-a took a single left=31 NSP-listener error on bus 20 at 140 s (the parked-listener mechanism of #247) -- one error, below FIOERRLC s threshold of two, so no death. STILL TO DO before this becomes a default: longer holds, more replicates, a full from-IPL run, and the owner s watched acceptance run.  CONFIRMED ON A FULL FROM-IPL RUN, full4, 2026-09-26: four computers, three CRTs, 4gpc-g3-startup.script from IPL through OPS 3 0 1 PRO, with YAGPC_FC_MDM=1 YAGPC_FC_SENSORS=1 YAGPC_FC_ANSWER_UNNAMED=1 YAGPC_MTU_ECHO_EXPIRE=1 YAGPC_MTU_LOCKED=1, held 2500 s. Seven captures at 2, 7, 12, 17, 22, 27 and 32 minutes after the OPS request: mtuscore reports FIOBY51-53 and FIOBY51L-53L ALL LIVE on all four computers at every one. cam.log: zero votes. Zero FCMSFAIL. ONE error termination in the entire run (GPC4, bus 20, FIONSPPG, left=31, at 1606.6 s -- the residual parked-listener mechanism of #247, a single error, below FIOERRLC s threshold of two); fix4, the same configuration without these switches, had 66 error terminations in its first seven minutes and the unit bypassed on all four within 60 s of the transition. The lock: 0 collisions in 1,000,865,960 model calls. Echo expiry withdrew 585,264 stale echoes over the run.

- **#253** *(emulator)* A SECOND, DIFFERENT RESIDUAL IN THE FOUR-COMPUTER OPS 201 CONFIGURATION: EXECUTION DIVERGENCE WITH NO BUS ERROR AT ALL. One computer alone reaches a sync point its peers do not, and is voted out. With the MDM answering now error-free, a redundant-set member can only take a different path if it was given different INPUT DATA -- the leading hypothesis is a listener that lost a transfer (for instance an echo withdrawn under the 5 ms rule) never updating its buffer and computing from stale values such as the previous cycle s timing-unit time, which orbit (G2) software leans on hardest. UNTESTED.
  - evidence: Run s201-f1, 2026-09-27, no environment (all defaults including YAGPC_FC_ANY_IUA), 4gpc-startup.script to OPS 201, held 1200 s: ZERO error terminations in the whole run, yet at shared 1001.857 GPC3 entered FCMSFAIL from FCMSSYNC+0086 (the SVC sync) naming GPCs 1, 2 and 4, and 6 ms later GPCs 1, 2 and 4 voted GPC3 out from FCMTSYNC+0029 (cam 13/23/33/43 at t=1009.5). s201-all1 earlier had the same shape at a different sync point (GPC1 alone at FCMISYNC, naming all three peers). Tally for this configuration with the fixes: 5 of 7 clean. The other configurations are clean on the current defaults (acid-f1 and g3-f1, zero errors). NEXT: the OPS 201 configuration with YAGPC_MTU_RING, YAGPC_BCE_RING and YAGPC_SYNC_HISTORY at a 2 s window, to see what the diverging computer was fed.  THE VEHICLE CLOCK IS A RUN-TO-RUN INPUT, and the failure sits on a minute rollover. A fresh yaGPC2 anchors GMT to the host clock at start (ageharness.c:292, time(NULL)); simulatePASS.py pins it with --date-time-epoch ONLY on a resume. So every fresh run presents the flight software with a different GMT, with no change to options or environment. s201-f1 s anchor, recovered exactly: its last timing-unit reply lastTime 9c07 6c20 00f8 decodes (mtumodel.c BCD layout) to day 270, 07:36:08.031 GMT; its end capture was at shared 1550.387 s; time(NULL) is whole seconds and the unit is read every 960 ms, so the anchor is 1790493018 (02:10:18 CDT, when that pair launched). THE FAILURE (shared 1001.857) WAS THEREFORE AT GMT 07:27:00.857 -- 0.86 s after a minute rollover, i.e. about the first timing-unit read after it. Suggestive, not proven: about 25 rollovers happen per run and only this one failed. TESTING: runs pinned with --yagpc-extra "--date-time-epoch 1790493018", which puts every minute rollover at the same simulated time as in s201-f1, each paired with an unpinned run so the ordinary hunt continues.  TORN-TIME HYPOTHESIS (user, 2026-09-27): a time reading arriving mid-conversion splits MM from SS. MODEL SIDE EXCLUDED for s201-f1: mtu_fill_time builds dyhr/mnsc/msec from ONE gmtime_r of one clock value per command, identical for every reader; zero ECHOX withdrawals of any timing-unit (524c..) read in the whole run (all 32930-56587 withdrawals per GPC are 520002, the rest 620040); mtu carry report '31 carried, 0 dropped', and a carry holds only the SAME reply's words. So no GPC was handed words from two replies. GPC-SIDE still open (FPMMTURM TMP every 960 ms computes a new OFST from the buffer; a conversion racing the next DMA into TFCMMTU1). WHAT THE SYNC STATE SAYS: FCMTSYNC is the TIMER-INTERRUPT sync (every PC2 interrupt except SSIP, code issued by FPMIHPC2), not time agreement; FCMSSYNC is the SVC sync. So GPCs 1,2,4 had taken a PC2 timer interrupt and GPC3 had not -- it went on to an SVC sync instead. That is a divergence in WHEN GPC3's timer fired, which a bad time offset in GPC3 would produce, but so would any skew in its interval timer or its TQE chain. GPC3 r4=0000000b at FCMSFAIL entry (plausibly the SVC number from FCMSVCNM; not confirmed).  MECHANISM FOUND 2026-09-27, from the flight software's OWN sync trace. FCMTRACE keeps a 50-entry cyclic table (FCMTRCLG, physical 1c99c-1cb2b, 8 halfwords an entry: time, old PSW, detail, id/SVC number) and FCMSFAIL FREEZES it (STOP TRACE, r0 bit 2 clear), so an end-of-run memory capture still holds the 50 events before the failure. Decoder: scratchpad fcmtrace.py/fcmcmp.py. BOTH FAILURES ARE ONE COMPUTER REPLAYING ONE SVC. Run d4b (a second instance, GPC4, shared 903.570, GMT 10:08:13, not near a minute rollover): peers' sequence after the SSIP is SVC21 A1AIESIP, SVC44 #CGOAGNC+0038, SVC21 27db3; GPC4's is SVC21, SVC44 #CGOAGNC+000e AGAIN (it had already traced it 0.29 ms before the SSIP), SVC44 +0038, and then SVC21 27db3 ALONE, timed out, FCMSFAIL. SYNCHIST confirms 'svc nia=1411e' executed twice by GPC4 and once by GPC1. s201-f1: GPC3 traced SVC16 $0DCICYC+00fd four times against its peers' three, then SVC21 $0DCICYC+05a7 alone. The sync code does not say WHICH SVC, so a computer one SVC behind keeps handshaking against its neighbours' next one until it issues one nobody answers -- which is why it looked like arriving '4 ms early' (#190) and like work divergence. WHY IT REPLAYS: FCMSSYNC reads the lines again FCMST5=87 us after first seeing the set in step and holds its own code until FCMST3=182 us, so the design margin is 95 us less one pass of its polling loop (19 instructions). In d4b GPC1 dropped its code at 903.560152 on its own clock and GPC4 received that at 903.560127 on ITS clock, while GPC2's SVC reached GPC4 25 us AFTER GPC2 sent it: delivery is immediate in wall time but the simulated clocks differ by the barrier delta. Measured over 18,000 deliveries in d4b's history: receiver clock minus sender clock p1 -24 us, p50 +1..+12, p99 +33. GPC4's second read therefore fell after GPC1's code had gone; it stayed in the loop until the SSIP interrupt took it out (old PSW FCMSSYNC+0046, the loop's interrupt window) and re-executed the SVC afterwards. FIX UNDER TEST: vehicle_route_out stamps each change with the sender's shared-frame time plus syncLagUs (2 x barrier delta = 50 us; YAGPC_SYNC_LAG_US, 0 = old behaviour) and the receiver takes it in vehicle_barrier_wait when its own clock reaches the stamp. TORN TIME (user's hypothesis) NOT SEEN: run e2p, pinned to s201-f1's epoch, YAGPC_WATCHHW on TFCMOFST (09c98): 1405 time-management cycles across 25 minute rollovers, largest step 1216 us in any computer, the four offsets within 125 us of each other throughout; and e1p/e2p both passed the 07:27:00 rollover clean. RATE before the fix, OPS 201 on the 2026-09-26 defaults: 2 of this kind in 16 runs.  A FIXED LAG ON THE SYNC LINES IS REFUTED (run g1a, 2026-09-27, YAGPC_SYNC_LAG_US effectively 50): the set lost GPC2 at shared 364.606 and then GPC3 at 476.8 and GPC4 at 547.5, nine FCMSFAIL entries in ten minutes, all from FCMSSYNC+0086 -- the same replay, mirrored. GPC2 raised its SVC code LAST, saw the set in step at once and completed SVC 1411e; GPCs 1, 3 and 4 were still in the loop waiting for GPC2's code (50 us on the wire), took the SSIP timer interrupt in the loop's interrupt window, and re-executed 1411e afterwards, so GPC2 was one SVC AHEAD. The flight software's second look (FCMST5, 87 us) is what is meant to catch this: the last computer to arrive must see that its neighbours dropped their codes for the interrupt. With the neighbours' drop 50 us late and their wait for it 50 us longer, 2 x lag + one loop pass exceeds 87 us. So the lines must have NO added delay; do not retry a lag. REPLACED BY: stamp at the sender's own time, deliver when the receiver's clock reaches the stamp, and gate every READ DISCRETE INPUT A (PC command 0818) until no other computer's clock is behind the reader's (vehicle_sync_read_gate, iop.c) -- YAGPC_SYNC_STAMPED, on by default, 0 for the old delivery.  THE STRESS TEST AND WHAT IT FOUND (2026-09-27). YAGPC_BARRIER_US loosened from 25 makes the failure that took 2.6 hours on average arrive in two minutes: the committed build b235b583a fails at cam t=109 s at 200 us (sold1) and at 116, 110 and 173 s at 100 us (yold100-1..3), always shortly after the second or third computer joins. THE STAMPED DELIVERY ALONE STILL FAILED at 200 us, 4 runs of 4 (sfix1 128 s, t200 186 s, u200 132 s, w200 184 s), and at 100 us (t100, 350 s): vehicle_refresh_lines -- the periodic re-send that keeps the lines from going stale -- wrote the sender's PRESENT code straight into the neighbour, bypassing the stamps. Run w200, shown by YAGPC_SYNC_READS and YAGPC_SYNC_STAMP_TRACE: GPC2 read 2b000009 (both neighbours 001) at +51 us, then 2b000889 at +150 us with NO stamped change taken in between; GPC3 did not raise null until +226 us on its own clock. GPC2's second look failed, its I/O sync timed out, and the set broke. FIXED by sending every write to a neighbour's lines through one stamped path (line_put). RESULT: build fix5 CLEAN for 956 s wall at 200 us (x200) and at 100 us (x100), 0 votes, 0 error terminations, four computers in RUN, script complete. INSTRUMENT NOTE: SYNCHIST's time for a code HEARD was the listener's clock as last noted per instruction and ran up to about 100 us stale; lag_deliver now notes the clock first. Believe STAMP take/put over SYNCHIST where they differ in older logs.  VERIFIED ON THE FINAL BUILD (fix5 = the committed tree), 2026-09-27, normal 25 us barrier, no environment: runs k1a and k1b 0 votes, 0 FCMSFAIL, 0 error terminations, timing unit live on all four computers, rate 0.987 (1550 s simulated in 1570 s wall, unchanged); earlier h1a-h3b 6 of 6 clean on the build before the refresh fix. Headless gate exact: mmu1 56/431/220731/220011/720, formatFills 8, one MODE: IPL, GPC MEMORY. The read gate waited 3.4-5.6 million times a run for 63-91 s of wall time in all, none gave up. NOT CLAIMED: that sync loss is eliminated -- 400 us still fails through the bus model (#254).

- **#254** *(emulator)* A LISTENER THAT HAS NOT YET LOOKED LOSES THE COMMAND WHEN THE NEXT ONE IS ISSUED, and with it its place in a multi-receive listen program. mtumodel keeps ONE command per bus (echoCmd) and a new command replaces it. FIOMTUPG's commander issues 400a08, 526420, 525000, 526c7f within 580 us (the last three within 297 us) every 160 ms on the timing buses, and the listeners take them with receives of 1, 1 and 32 words (BCE pc 1cc2e, 1cc2f, 1cc32). A real BCE cannot miss a word while armed; ours looks when its thread next runs it.
  - evidence: Run e2u 2026-09-27 (OPS 201 script, 4 GPC, base 9000, defaults of b235b583a): GPC4 alone, ONE error (ERRTERM bce=20 pc=1cc32 left=31 shared 352.511339), FIOERRLC force-fail-self 6 ms later (r0=8bc40000 r7=9dc70031), peers voted from FCMISYNC+00bb. BCE/MTU rings, bus 20: in the cycle at 352.351 GPC4 armed 1cc2e at 352351242.7, did not look again until 352351608.1 (365 us), and took 525000 there -- 526420, issued 352351471.5 and replaced 132 us later, never reached it. Its second receive then took 526c7f and one word, its third (32 words, Listen Mode, no time-out) waited a whole cycle, took the NEXT 526420 as its command at 352511187.6, one data word, and was handed 525000's sync at 352511339.3 where it expected data. GPC2 and GPC3 took 526420 63 and 84 us after issue in the same cycle. FIX UNDER TEST: mtu_carry_take also carries the untaken command sync, in front of its words, for a reader that looked at the bus within MTU_LISTENING_US (1000 us) before the command was issued; the backlog is bounded at MTU_CARRY_MAX words and dropped MTU_ECHO_STALE (5 ms) after the last addition (mtu_note_look), so #210's buried listener cannot recur.  THE CARRY IS A PATCH AND 400 us BREAKS IT (run y400, build fix5, YAGPC_BARRIER_US=400): GPC3 ERRTERM bce=20 pc=1cc32 left=31 at shared 343.394534, the same signature, FIOERRLC force-fail-self 6 ms later. In the cycle before, GPC3 armed 1cc2e at 343233939.5 on ITS clock; GPC1, at least 226 us ahead, issued 526420 at 343234180.5 and 525000 at 343234312.5 on its own; GPC3 was handed 525000 (carried, ring kind k) at its clock 343234086.3 -- 226 us BEFORE that command was issued in simulated time -- and never saw 526420, because its first look after arming came, in WALL time, after both had been issued, so it did not count as listening. WHAT IS WRONG IN PRINCIPLE: the model decides who gets a word by the wall-clock order of calls. In simulated time GPC3 was armed 241 us before 526420 and must get it. At 200 us the carry held (60,723 commands carried in x200, no error); at 100 us 357. DESIGN FOR THE REAL FIX, not yet written: a wire LOG per bus -- every word with its wire time, command syncs included -- and a cursor per reader; a word is available to a reader when the reader's clock reaches its wire time, never before; a receive armed straight after another on the same bus continues from the last word taken, not from the reader's (possibly late) clock; a first arm takes words whose wire time is at or after the arm. The arm has to be TOLD to the model (iop.c knows it, the 'A' ring event), not inferred from polling. Same rule then for iccmodel.c.  REPLACED BY THE WIRE MODEL, default on 2026-09-27 (YAGPC_MTU_WIRELOG=0 restores the per-transaction delivery). One ring of 2048 words per bus -- word, wire time, issuer -- with one write position and one read position per computer; a word is there for a computer when ITS clock reaches the word's wire time, and stays until taken; a fresh listen arm (iop_arm_hook -> mtumodel_note_arm) starts at the words at or after the arm; a command's reply is appended once for everybody. Results: 5 of 5 full runs clean at 25 us, 18 of 20 at 200 us (ws1: GPC2 failed while still in STANDBY, not explained; xa2: display bus 6 answered late during a four-vehicle overload, rate 0.943); the delivery it replaces lost a computer at 400 us (y400). The 7 'lost to a reader more than 2048 behind' per run were all a computer's FIRST look at bus 20, 21 or 22 at the OPS 2 transition (run ov1, WLOVERRUN lines, every one 'none of it was for this reader'); fixed by starting a fresh arm at the present end. Final build fin3/fin4: 0 votes, 0 errors, timing unit live on all four, rate 0.993 from GPC 1's IPL, 0 overruns.
