- **#256** *(emulator)* A FOURTH COMPUTER JOINING THE COMMON SET COMES OUT TWO MINOR CYCLES (80 ms) OUT OF PHASE AND FAILS THE OTHER THREE, when a crew-keyed bus configuration change is processed in the SAME 40 ms minor cycle as its clock initialisation. Seen 4 times in about 27 runs through the join on builds with the stamped sync delivery (52480e5bf and after), at shared 203-205 s of examples/4gpc-startup.script; NOT YET KNOWN whether the stamped delivery is needed for it -- the committed-before build ran the join 24 times without it, which at a rate near 1 in 10 is only weak evidence.
  - evidence: Runs q25-3 (shared 204.211), join1 (204.124), z400-3 (205.1, barrier 400), jfix-2 (203.3). GPC4, 43.88 s after leaving reset, enters FCMSFAIL from FCMCSYNC+00bd (r7=8c4be031, r0=801c0001: common set, GPCs 1 2 3). ITS CLOCK IS RIGHT: on the flight software's own trace GPC4's and GPC1's last 40 ms timer entries are stamped 0.5 ms (join1) and 1.6 ms (q25-3) apart. WHAT IS WRONG IS WHICH MINOR CYCLE IT TAKES FOR THE SIP: its SIP codes go out 160 ms apart at -244.9 and -84.9 ms, each about 2 ms before the set's, and then at -3.9 ms, 81 ms after the last, when the set's next is due at +77. FCMTRACE, failing (join1): timer interrupt, then A2ARDCSB (ARD_CS_BUS_CHG, the common-set bus configuration change AIE_SIP runs for a crew input passed by ICC) SVC 44/13/36/44 at +0.9..1.8 ms, SVC 5 A4AIESIP+010a, SVC 32 A2AIBGPC+0053 (FPMMTURM secondary clock initialisation) at +3.43 ms; then on the new clock SIX timer entries inside 1.05 ms, TQE flags 8001, 000d, 0002, 0004, 0001, 0001. Clean (jc-3): the same minor cycle WITHOUT the A2ARDCSB sequence, SVC 32 at +2.06 ms; then timer entries 8001, and 0004 1.9 ms later, 0002 40 ms after that, 0001 40 ms after that -- one per minor cycle. The crew script keys an NBAT ITEM every 8 s from 13 s after each computer goes to RUN (4gpc-startup.script lines 54-67), so one lands near GPC4's initialisation (about 43.4-43.9 s after its RUN) and whether it falls in that one minor cycle is wall-clock chance; extra tracing, which slows the vehicle slightly, moved it away (0 in 36 runs with YAGPC_SYNC_STAMP_TRACE or YAGPC_RECORD_AT). AIBGPCLO's own history has DR 28365 'POTENTIAL OFFSET BETWEEN SIP CYCLE AND TSIP' and a warning not to put anything on the TQE chain during initialisation before AIESIP is scheduled, so this may be a hazard of the flight software that a crew avoids by not keying during a computer's initialisation -- NOT ESTABLISHED. INSTRUMENTS ADDED: YAGPC_RECORD_AT=<shared s> writes the failure record in a run where nothing failed; YAGPC_ICCSYT=8120 adds each computer's copies of the sync times (TICCSYTM/TH at +45/+46 of the ICC buffers, table FCMICCAD at 08120).  IT IS A DOCUMENTED HAZARD OF THE FLIGHT SOFTWARE WITH A CREW WORKAROUND, AND OUR CREW SCRIPT BROKE THE RULE (user's suggestion, 2026-09-27). PASS Program Note 42433 rev B 02/22/90 (DR46617, DR30138), 'POTENTIAL F-T-S DUE TO ICC CONTENTION AT NEW GPC START-UP', STS 1 and subs: 'IF ANY OF THE FOLLOWING ACTIVITIES OCCUR IN THE EXISTING CS DURING ICC INITIALIZATION OF A NEW GPC, THE RESULTING ICC DATA COULD BE OVERLAID BY THE INITIALIZATION ICC DATA. THIS COULD LEAD TO A F-T-S AT CS FORMATION OR LATER AT RS FORMATION' -- the list includes 'E. GPC/CRT KEY, BFC CRT SELECT SWITCH, OR BFC CRT DISP SWITCH' and 'I. MODING ANOTHER GPC FROM STBY TO RUN OR FROM RUN TO STBY'; USER RESPONSE: not 'DURING THE TIME SPAN BEGINNING APPROXIMATELY 10 SECONDS BEFORE MODING A SECONDARY GPC TO RUN UNTIL 10 SECONDS AFTER PLACING THE MODE SWITCH TO RUN OR UNTIL THE OUTPUT TALKBACK GOES GRAY'. Program Note 37536 forbids keyboard functions on the system SPECs (TM, GPC MEMORY, DPS UTILITY) during 'GPC INITIALIZATION TO CS (STBY TO RUN)'. Note 55318 describes a related common-set F-T-S of a non-RS member from bus reconfiguration requests. The PASS User's Guide OI32 (USA002869) section 8.1.1 lists them. examples/ipl-one-gpc.script did 'crt 0' -- which IS the CRT select switch, discrete input B (discretePanel.py _crtSelectChanged) -- 2 s BEFORE 'mode RUN' and 'source OFF' 2 s after; panel.log of join1: crt 0 at 207.6, mode RUN 209.6, first vote 213.2 wall, i.e. the failure is about 1 s after the mode switch. So the A2ARDCSB bus change GPC4 processed in its initialisation cycle is that switch change. CORRECTED: the script now leaves 12 s either side of 'mode RUN'. CORRECTION TO THIS ENTRY'S CLAIM: the NBAT ITEM entries are not the trigger -- the first of them comes 13 s after RUN, after the failure. STILL NOT KNOWN whether the stamped sync delivery changes how often the hazard is hit; with the rule observed the question may not matter.  FIXED, 2026-09-28, with two changes in place that these runs do not separate: the crew scripts keeping 12 s clear of every mode RUN (95bd3b1d7, Program Note 42433) and the MTU's 0.125 ms time (fc482e01a, #259), since both failures turned on where a join fell against the set's minor-cycle boundaries. MEASURED: 20 four-computer start-ups on the fixed build (cyc1, cyc2, cyA1-4, cyB1-4, cyC1-4, cyD1-4, m8full, mtut1), every one of them joining GPC3 and GPC4, with NO vote before the first deliberate excursion -- against 4 in about 27 before, P(0 in 20) ~ 4%.

- **#257** *(emulator)* A COMPUTER LEAVING HALT COULD READ WORDS THAT REACHED THE BUSES WHILE IT WAS HALTED. In HALT it executes nothing, but the bus models went on queueing for it everything the other computers put on the wire -- up to 16384 words a display bus (bcenet_framer recvQueue), 2048 per intercomputer bus (iccmodel q), the mass memory's listener tap, the timing unit's per-reader state -- and HALT -> STBY only reset the CPU (run.c), so the first receives after it could take them. The IPL's system reset cleared the IOP but drained only the boot bus.
  - evidence: batchrunner_clear_input (run.c) empties every model's input for the computer at HALT -> STBY and at its IPL, and clears the 24 MIA receive latches too (user's direction: with every queue behind it empty, a word in the latch can only have arrived while the computer was not executing). Measured in runs fin1..fin4: 855-898 words discarded at each HALT -> STBY, up to 48,816 at an IPL, mostly display traffic. Each model gained a *_clear_input(model, gpcId).
  - files `src/run.c,src/bcenet_framer.c,src/iccmodel.c,src/mmumodel.c,src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `stale input after HALT,queued while halted,MIA latch` &middot; run `clr1,fin1,fin2,fin3,fin4`
- **#258** *(method)* DEBUGGING OUTPUT IS OFF BY DEFAULT (user's rule for production software), and a gate on a message must never gate the work around it.
  - evidence: YAGPC_FAILURE_RECORD=1 turns on the whole failure record (bus rings, sync history, the dump at a failure); YAGPC_MTU_DIAG=1 the timing unit model's live ECHOX/DECLINED/UNANSWERED lines (one 25-minute run had written 172,707 ECHOX lines). THE MISTAKE MADE DOING IT: the UNANSWERED message was gated by wrapping its whole block, which also ANSWERS the unnamed flight-critical reads -- in runs fin1 and fin2 every computer took 16 errors together on buses 14-17 at the OPS 2 transition and bypassed all three timing-unit accumulators (FIOBY51..53), with 0 votes and the rate normal, so a check on votes alone would have passed it. Fixed by gating the print only (fin3, fin4 clean).
  - env `YAGPC_FAILURE_RECORD,YAGPC_MTU_DIAG` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/iop.c,src/discretes.c,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `timing unit bypassed,FIOBY51,all computers err together on 14-17` &middot; run `fin1,fin2,fin3,fin4`
- **#259** *(emulator)* THE SECOND COMPUTER TO JOIN THE COMMON SET TAKES ITS FIRST SIP IN THE WRONG MINOR CYCLE ABOUT ONE JOIN IN 30-180, so its SIP cycle count is one ahead of the set's, it raises the major-cycle sync (110 SSIP) a minor cycle before the others, times out, and the two vote each other. Not a crew-rule problem (no listed action within 10 s), not the clock: after the join GPC2's software clock agrees with GPC1's within about 20 us in failing and clean runs alike. Suspected cause: the intercomputer model hands a word to a receiver as soon as the sender's thread writes it, not when the receiver's clock reaches it (lags as low as -190 us), so WHEN the joining computer routes the set's first ICC message relative to its own clock re-initialisation varies from run to run.
  - evidence: Runs ws1 (200 us, 2026-09-27), na6 (25 us, overnight soak of 3205dd84a), kc21 (join hunt without probe, 1 of 63): GPC2 fail vote at shared 125.408-125.43, GPC1 and GPC2 vote each other (cam 21 and 12). FCMTRACE of GPC2 after the secondary clock initialisation (AIBGPCLO: TIME_MGT(AIBV_INIT_SECOND_CLK) then SCHEDULE AIE_SIP AT (CZ1V_A_TSIP PHASE_AIE) REPEAT EVERY TIME_AIE): timer entries with TQE flags 8001, 0004, 0002, 0001. CLEAN (ja1, ja10, ja12, ja13): 0004 fires AT the set's minor-cycle boundary (+11..+14 us from GPC1's), 1.9 ms after 8001, and 0002 (the rescheduled SIP) 40 ms later. FAILING (na6, kc21, identical to 10 us): 0004 fires 0.10 ms after 8001, 2.14 ms BEFORE the boundary, and 0002 at the boundary 2.26 ms after -- one minor cycle early. AIESIP's handling of the new member's message 1 is 'CZ2V_SIPCYCLE = CZ2V_SIPCYCLE + 2 ... CANCEL AIE_SIP' (SRN 068200), which assumes the message is routed a fixed two minor cycles after it was sent; a SIP run in the wrong minor cycle leaves the count one off. Rates: 0 of 120 joins with the FPMISSIP probe landmark (1c429) and YAGPC_RECORD_AT (hunt ja/jb/jc), 1 of 63 without (ka/kb/kc), 2 of about 55 full or stress runs through the join. AIBGPCLO history has DR 28365 'POTENTIAL OFFSET BETWEEN SIP CYCLE AND TSIP' (1981). NEXT: deliver ICC words by simulated time as the wire model does for buses 14-23.  THE ICC TIME GATE IS REFUTED AS THE FIX (2026-09-28): with intercomputer words delivered only when the receiver's clock reaches their send time (YAGPC_ICC_TIMEGATE, iccmodel.c, not committed), run gk19 failed at the same point with the same signature -- GPC2 timer 8001, then 0004 0.10 ms later, then 0002 2.23 ms after 8001 -- after 63 clean gated joins; baseline without the gate 1 in 101 (ka1-40, kb1-40, kc1-21). A full four-GPC run with the gate (gfull1) was clean, rate 0.993, so the gate breaks nothing, but it does not decide this. The cause is inside the joining computer's own sequence between its clock initialisation and its first SIP, not in when the set's message reaches it.  THE TIMER ENTRIES DECODED (TFTQE, FPMIHPC2.asm TTQEFLGS: low 4 bits the TQE type -- 0001 SSIP, 0010 WAIT, 0100 SCHEDULE AT; 8000 a status bit). The join sequence in AIBGPCLO is the provisional AIE_SIP (scheduled 'IN (.31767-RUNTIME)') routing the set's first message, then TIME_MGT(AIBV_INIT_SECOND_CLK), then 'SCHEDULE AIE_SIP AT (CZ1V_A_TSIP PHASE_AIE) REPEAT EVERY TIME_AIE', then 'WAIT 0.040'. In the trace: 8001 = the provisional SIP firing; 0004 = the SCHEDULE AT taking effect; 0002 = the WAIT expiring; 0001 = the regular SIP. IN EVERY RUN the provisional SIP fires about 2 ms before the set's next minor-cycle boundary -- 1.89 ms in clean joins, 2.2 ms in failing ones -- and the SCHEDULE AT then resolves to the coming boundary (clean) or to one already passed, so it runs at once (failing). So the choice flips at about 2 ms before the boundary and our emulation puts this event on that edge every time; run-to-run variation of a few hundred microseconds decides it. NEXT: why the provisional SIP lands 2 ms before a boundary -- its time is set from GPC2's clock before re-initialisation ('.31767-RUNTIME') and the size of the jump (prime's CS sync time minus own), and a real computer presumably lands well clear of the edge; compare with the instruction timing of the path from the provisional SIP to the SCHEDULE.  CAUSE FOUND (2026-09-28), AND THE EARLIER TIMER DECODE CORRECTED. TQE flag 8000 is CANCELLED (TFTQE, FPMTMENQ.asm: status 10.. = cancelled), so '8001' is NOT the provisional SIP firing: it is the provisional AIE_SIP's next repeat element, SSIP-typed because AIBGPCLO sets SUBBIT$10(TFCMFLAG) before scheduling it, left on the chain by 'CANCEL AIE_SIP' and still carrying an expiry in GPC2's PRE-JUMP clock frame. TIME_MGT(AIBV_INIT_SECOND_CLK) then moves the clock about 880 s forward, so that stale element sorts ahead of every new one and FPMADTQE never reloads PC2 for a newcomer (it calls FPMITUPD only for a new TOP element). THE RULE (FPMTMENQ): SCHEDULE AT with REPEAT EVERY whose AT time is already reached takes phased scheduling (FPMCMPAT) to the next period; one still in the future is queued as is. CLEAN joins issue the SCHEDULE (SVC 1, AIBGPC+005f) 0.05-0.11 ms AFTER the set's boundary: phased to the next boundary, 40 ms on, after the stale element has expired (1.9 ms before), so PC2 is set right. FAILING joins issue it 0.27-0.30 ms BEFORE the boundary: queued at that boundary behind the stale element, fired only when the stale element expires 38 ms later (then 0.10 ms), an extra SIP -- SIPCYCLE one ahead. Measured in gk19, kc21, na6, ws1 (fail) and ja1, ja10, ja12, ja13 (clean), every one. WHY THE SCHEDULE LANDS ON EITHER SIDE: GPC2's first provisional SIP comes a CONSTANT 318.15-318.19 ms after the GPC1 SSIP edge FCMASYNC caught (detection delay is not the variable -- the 0.13/0.44 ms figures above were inferred, not measured, and are withdrawn). The variable is the SET'S GRID: GPC1's SSIP phase on the shared clock walked a sawtooth of +1/3, +1/3, -2/3 ms every 0.96 s (period 2.88 s, spread about 1.0 ms, largest step 0.69 ms, in every run looked at), and a step between the caught edge and the SCHEDULE moved the boundary by more than the 0.4 ms margin. 0.96 s is FPMMTURM's period ('VALIDATING THE TIME SOURCE EVERY 960 MILLISECONDS'). ROOT CAUSE: src/mtumodel.c mtu_fill_time truncated the unit's time to WHOLE MILLISECONDS and multiplied by 8 into a field that is in 0.125 ms units; FPMMTUFX uses all thirteen bits ('MH R4,FPM125'). FIX: report eighths. MEASURED (runs m8a, m8b, m8c, yaGPC2-mtu8, ICC gate off): GPC1 SSIP phase spread 156-166 us (was 962-1026), largest step 106-127 us (was 682-688), three joins clean. Soak m8soak (120 joins plus one full four-GPC run) under way.  FIXED in fc482e01a (2026-09-28): 312 joins with no failure on the fixed build (92 IPL-and-join runs m8a-c and m8soak lanes p/q/r; 220 STBY->RUN rejoins in cycle runs cyc1, cyc2, cyA1-3, cyB1-3, cyC1-2, cyD1-2), against about 1 in 100 before -- P(0 in 312 | 1%) ~ 4%. Full four-GPC OPS 201 run m8full clean: 0 votes, MTU live 4/4, rate 0.993. The ICC time gate (iccmodel.c) was OFF throughout (YAGPC_ICC_TIMEGATE=0) and stays uncommitted.
  - addrs `1c429` &middot; csects `AIBGPCLO,AIESIP,FPMMTURM,FCMCSYNC,FPMIHPC2  AIBGPCLO,FPMIHPC2  FPMTMENQ,FPMCMPAT,FPMITUPD,FPMADTQE,FPMMTUFX,FPMMTURM,FCMASYNC` &middot; procs `ICC` &middot; config `SSW` &middot; env `YAGPC_LANDMARKS,YAGPC_ICCSYT,YAGPC_RECORD_AT  YAGPC_ICC_TIMEGATE` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c  src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `join out of phase,SIP one minor cycle early,second computer,GPC1 and GPC2 vote each other,shared 125.43  SCHEDULE AT resolves to a past boundary,provisional SIP 2 ms before the boundary  SSIP` &middot; tsec `125` &middot; run `ws1,na6,kc21,ja1,ja10,ja12,ja13  gk19,gfull1  gk19,kc21,na6,ws1,ja1,ja10,ja12,ja13,m8a,m8b,m8c  m8soak,m8full,cyA1,cyA2,cyA3,cyB1,cyB2,cyB3,cyC1,cyC2,cyD1,cyD2`

## Latent (real, but unreachable here)

- **#53** *(emulator)* exec_DLY (the MSC's @DLY) is a NO-OP: it ignores its count and just advances the PC. The POO: 'This instruction performs no operation other than delaying the execution of the next instruction. The time period delayed is indicated by the 18-bit EC, with a resolution of 2 microseconds. The maximum delay is roughly .54 sec.' LATENT: the PASS flight software never uses @DLY -- zero occurrences in SSSRC -- so it has never mattered, but the implementation is wrong if anything ever does.
  - evidence: src/iop_msc_instr.c exec_DLY is 'iop_incr_nia(t, 1);' and nothing else. Not to be confused with the BCE's #DLY/#DLYI, which ARE implemented (iop_bce_delay). WHY IT CAME UP, and the answer it produced: asked why the flight software builds a delay out of @RAW when @DLY exists, the answer is not 'the MSC has no delay instruction' -- it has one and never uses it. It is that FIOMDLY and FIOMCKIO must be THE SAME instruction. The disassembly of the linked monitor shows 1cb6c d800 and 1cb70 d800, byte-identical @RAW 0(1); the only difference between 'delay' and 'check for I/O complete' is the accumulator loaded first, -1 versus FIOMBCEM. Because @RAW has two exits -- +2 halfwords when met, +1 on time out -- the pair is a state machine: FIOMDLY times out and falls through into FIOMCKIO, which checks and branches to FIOMTOUT. @DLY has one exit and no condition and cannot do that. So the MSC bit is not a trick to fake a delay, it is the parameter selecting which behaviour of one instruction you get -- which is a further reason the bit must participate (entry 2).
  - addrs `1cb6c-1cb70` &middot; csects `FIOMDLY,FIOMCKIO` &middot; instrs `@DLY,@RAW` &middot; procs `MSC` &middot; files `src/iop_msc_instr.c` &middot; doc `IBM-6246556A @DLY DESCRIPTION`

## Refuted -- DO NOT RETEST

- **#3** *(iop)* The MSC is being clobbered by LOAD MSC BUSY arriving while it is already busy.
  - evidence: By design. TCVTMSC=-1 is 'BUSY BUT INTERRUPTABLE' and FIOSTMSC waits DO UNTIL=(...,NP), which -1 satisfies. The 186 'clobbers' are the intended interrupt path.
  - addrs `1a2-1a2` &middot; csects `FIOSTMSC,FIOPDISP` &middot; instrs `LOAD MSC BUSY` &middot; procs `MSC` &middot; symptom `TCVTMSC latched ffffffff` &middot; run `v51a/v51b`
- **#4** *(iop)* The MSC executes too slowly, so FCOS's next dispatch catches it mid-program.
  - evidence: IOP_PASS_US=0.1 cut clobbers 18x and phase 18 still did not load. Also sio ~= busyset at every second, so when started the MSC does issue its SIO.
  - csects `FIOSTMSC` &middot; procs `MSC` &middot; env `YAGPC_IOP_PASS_US` &middot; run `v51b/v71a`
- **#5** *(iop)* Completions are lost: FIOCMPLT / the @INT path never runs for DK transactions.
  - evidence: MSCINT fires 34-37/s throughout the stall, so completions ARE raised. The failure is dispatch-side, not completion-side.
  - csects `FIOCMPLT,FIOPDISP` &middot; instrs `@INT` &middot; procs `MSC,BCE6,BCE7,BCE8` &middot; symptom `DK buses idle` &middot; run `v64a`
- **#6** *(iop)* TCVTBCEB is stuck because XST double-frees the bus mask.
  - evidence: The mask alternates healthily (FIOPDISP+8 sets, FIOCMPLT+416 clears) and drains to 0000. No double-free.
  - addrs `152-152` &middot; csects `FIOCMPLT,FIOPDISP` &middot; env `YAGPC_WATCHHW` &middot; symptom `TCVTBCEB stuck busy` &middot; run `v66a`
- **#7** *(iop)* DMA contention starves the DK buses while mass memory transfers.
  - evidence: dmaBurst defaults true, so iop_exec_dma_queue drains the whole queue every step. The queue never backs up.
  - procs `BCE6,BCE7,BCE8` &middot; env `dmaBurst` &middot; files `src/iop.c`
- **#8** *(iop)* Mass memory head-blocks ordinary I/O in the active queue.
  - evidence: Separate queues (TCVTIOA vs TCVTMMA), and the ordinary queue is only ever 3 deep. BCE20/BCE24 are serviced normally throughout.
  - addrs `144-144,151-151` &middot; csects `FIOPDISP` &middot; procs `BCE18,BCE20,BCE24` &middot; run `v62a`
- **#9** *(iop)* The IOQE pool leaks entries over time.
  - evidence: Free list is a full 25 from t=150 to t=389, then drains in 5 s and RECOVERS to 24. A spike, not a leak.
  - addrs `14d-14d,90b2-9273` &middot; csects `FIOSVC` &middot; env `YAGPC_IOQEDEPTH` &middot; symptom `pool drains` &middot; run `v62a`
- **#10** *(iop)* DK transfers are ~10x too slow per word (1385 us/word).
  - evidence: Measurement artifact: YAGPC_DKRATE times XMIT_CMD to the NEXT XMIT_CMD and divides by words, i.e. the inter-command GAP, not transfer duration. Figures void.
  - procs `BCE6,BCE7,BCE8` &middot; env `YAGPC_DKRATE` &middot; files `src/run.c` &middot; run `v68a`
- **#11** *(iop)* The DK bus holds are message timeouts.
  - evidence: Zero RECV timeouts on buses 6/7/8 across the window; all 40 timeouts are BCE20, a bus with no model attached.
  - instrs `#RDLI` &middot; procs `BCE6,BCE7,BCE8,BCE20` &middot; env `YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE` &middot; run `v69a`

- **#27** *(iop)* RESOLVED, not by the reading it was written for. The question was whether a DK transaction is dispatched inside a bus-6 silence -- i.e. whether FCOS dispatches and the MSC starts the BCE yet nothing reaches the wire (service starvation live), or nothing is dispatched at all (fault upstream in dispatch). NEITHER: the transaction IS dispatched, the BCE DOES run, and it FINISHES in ~0.3 ms. The silence is the wire being legitimately idle after the work is done, while the completion takes ~1053 ms to arrive.
  - evidence: See entry 22. w3's BWTRACE shows #WAT at +0.29 ms into a 1052.64 ms hold, identical to a 0.59 ms hold. Note the earlier v91a analysis of this question was inconclusive because the SIO it found 'inside' each silence sat 0.02-0.03 ms before the silence ENDED -- it was the NEXT transaction's dispatch, not one inside the gap.

- **#30** *(method)* Bus 18's multi-second continuous activity during the stall (921,749 events, 292,074 RECV_POLL over 8.54 s, vs 1,833 events / 9 polls for a normal transfer) is a runaway BCE read loop.
  - evidence: NOT an anomaly. In the bus log RECV_POLL/RECV_WORD are emitted once per 16.5 us wheel revolution while a receive is pending, so the counts measure SERVICE TICKS, not words -- 1822 events over 30.13 ms is exactly one per revolution. Each bus-18 burst matches its own MMU READY->0..READY->1 interval exactly (26 blocks: 651 ms ready-gap vs 654 ms burst; 123 blocks: 3109 ms; 115 blocks: 2906 ms; ~25 ms/block throughout). The bus-18 activity is a large read taking the time a large read takes. Do not read the bus-log event counts as word counts.
  - procs `BCE18` &middot; tape `v36` &middot; env `YAGPC_BUSLOG` &middot; files `src/run.c,gpc-buslog.py` &middot; tsec `390-401` &middot; run `v91a`
- **#36** *(method)* RETRACTED (was: 'make while a run is in flight kills it'). It does NOT. v93a and v94a were still running normally after the rebuild -- clang unlinks and recreates the output, so a running process keeps its old inode. They looked dead because gpc.log is EMPTY UNTIL EXIT (the SIMULATED TIME line is written on stop) and because a ps|grep check returned 0 spuriously.
  - evidence: Both were found alive minutes later, at 12:40 and 04:56 elapsed, and on SIGINT wrote SIMULATED TIME 584.5 s and 301.6 s. What IS true and did cost time: (1) a nohup'd headless-gpcmem.sh can be orphaned so its 'sleep SECS; kill -INT' never fires, leaving an emulator running indefinitely and its port base held, which makes the next launch fail with 'a run is already on port base N'; (2) an empty gpc.log means STILL RUNNING, not crashed -- judge progress from deu.log; (3) a scanner using re.search(r't=([0-9.]+)') silently matches the 't=0' inside 'prot=0' and reports 0, so anchor it as \\st=.
  - files `pass-run/headless-gpcmem.sh` &middot; run `v93a,v94a`
- **#39** *(iop)* REFUTED as a mechanism -- the MSC does NOT park in an 8.65 s wait. The 0x3ffff arm is real and correctly identified, but harmless: it is never waited out.
  - evidence: w4, 7932 arms of pc=1cb70 with count=262143. Time from each such arm to the MSC's NEXT repeat arm of any kind: median 33.7 ms, p90 40.0 ms, max 1621 ms, and only 3 of 7932 over 100 ms. So the monitor keeps cycling roughly every 34 ms and the 8.65 s count is not the source of the ~1053 ms completion latency. This is what the 'by design' reading predicts: if FIOMDLY's @RAW is met then every BCE is idle, so FIOMBCEM's buses are idle too and FIOMNTR6's @RAW is satisfied at once through its own +2 exit, whatever count it armed. The met-exit skipping '@LI 0' is therefore NOT a defect, and neither the mask (entry 2, refuted twice) nor this count explains the holds. WHAT THE NUMBERS NOW POINT AT, arithmetic fit only and NOT yet tested: FIOMNTR0 takes its monitor mask from the TOP ACTIVE IOQE (TCVTMIOA, 'GET I/O MONITOR MASK FROM THE TOP ACTIVE IOQE'), so the MSC watches one request at a time; at ~34 ms per monitor cycle a request 31 places down the queue would not be looked at for ~1053 ms, and 1053/34 = 31. That would make the hold a QUEUE DEPTH effect rather than a lost completion, and would move the question back to why so many IOQEs are outstanding -- the DEU fill storm -- rather than to the MSC or the BCE. Test it by recording queue depth (YAGPC_IOQEDEPTH exists) against hold length in the same run.
  - addrs `1cb6a-1cb71,14d-14d` &middot; csects `FIOMNTR,FIOMCNTL,FIOCMPLT` &middot; instrs `@RAW,@LI,@XAX,@L` &middot; procs `MSC` &middot; env `YAGPC_REPEATTRACE,YAGPC_IOQEDEPTH` &middot; files `src/iop_msc_instr.c,SSSRC/FIOMCNTL.asm` &middot; symptom `1053 ms DK holds,completion latency` &middot; tsec `380-400` &middot; doc `FIOMCNTL.asm FIOMDLY/FIOMCKIO comments` &middot; run `w4`
- **#40** *(iop)* WITHDRAWN in part. The claim that FIOMDLY and FIOMCKIO 'must be the same instruction' was over-stated: they sit at different addresses (1cb6c, 1cb70) and nothing requires identical encodings. The fall-through works because @RAW's time-out exit advances +1 into whatever follows. Both disassemble to d800 because both use displacement 0 with indexing -- a consequence, not a requirement. WHAT STANDS: exec_DLY is a no-op, and the POO documents @DLY as a real delay (18-bit EC, 2 us resolution, max ~0.54 s). LATENT: PASS uses @DLY nowhere.
  - evidence: src/iop_msc_instr.c exec_DLY is 'iop_incr_nia(t, 1);' and nothing else; the BCE's #DLY/#DLYI are separate and ARE implemented. Instruction census over SSSRC: @RAW 4 uses, @RNW 0, @RNI 0, @RAI 0, @DLY 0. So PASS uses exactly one repeat instruction for both the delay and the I/O-complete check, and the MSC bit is what distinguishes them. The POO also documents a pure-delay alternative the software does NOT take: 'A zero accumulator will never allow an @RNI or @RNW to reach their repeat conditions. Consequently, each repeat will loop until the maximum repeat count is reached.' Why @RAW was preferred over @RNW-with-zero or @DLY is not recorded in the source and should not be guessed at. NOTE on the two POO format lines: 'I=0 Count / @RAW COUNT' and 'I=1 X + Count / @RAW COUNT (1)' are the non-indexed and indexed forms of the COUNT, not a delay variant -- both still test the condition. FIOMDLY uses the indexed form because its count is computed at run time from TCVTMTTG and moved to the index register by @XAX, which a literal displacement could not carry.
  - addrs `1be4b-1be4b,14d-14d` &middot; csects `FIOMNTR,FIOSVC` &middot; instrs `@DLY,@RAW,@RNW,@RNI,@RAI` &middot; procs `MSC,BCE6` &middot; env `YAGPC_WATCHHW,YAGPC_PGMTRACE` &middot; tsec `390-397` &middot; run `w3,v91a`
- **#43** *(flight-sw)* The store-protect fault comes from an overlay's re-protection landing on memory something else still needs to write -- i.e. FIOMGCV's asynchronous verify-and-reprotect racing another writer.
  - evidence: Testable and tested against #PFCMGPT, negative. NONE of the addresses in the fault is inside any phase's load block: 080ce (the FPMSVCEP sentinel), 080d6 (the lastProt address) and 1be4b (the faulting instruction in FIOSVC) are all outside every overlay region, so their protection is part of the resident image and no overlay re-protected them. The IOQE pool itself (090b2..09273) is likewise covered by NO load block of any phase, so an overlay cannot corrupt or re-protect it. FPMIDLE, FIOCMPLT and FIOMGCV are also outside all overlay regions. The only I/O control structures any overlay covers are TCVTIOFP (0x14d) and TCVTBCEB (0x152), and only through phase 10's LB1 (00000..03c21) -- phase 10 is the IPL phase, loaded long before the transition. THE NON-RACE VERSION OF THE SAME IDEA IS NOT REFUTED AND IS THE LEADING HYPOTHESIS: see the entry on FIOMGCV's PCT.
  - addrs `080ce-08100,090b2-09273,1be4b-1be4b` &middot; csects `FIOMGCV,FIOSVC,FPMSVCEP` &middot; phases `8` &middot; run `w1`
- **#47** *(iop)* The DK completion latency is TCVTMIOA (the active-IOQE head) STALLING -- the head sitting on one entry far longer than that entry's I/O takes, so the defect would be in what advances it (FIOMGCMP/FIOCMPLT dequeuing).
  - evidence: REFUTED by direct measurement. Run z6, YAGPC_WATCHHW=146-152 covering TCVTMIOA (0x146) and TCVTBCEB (0x152) in one run so head changes correlate with bus-6 holds. Inside the 1053 ms hold at 391.833 the head changed 69 TIMES and the longest interval with it unchanged was 35.2 ms; the 2101 ms hold shows 135 changes, same 35 ms ceiling. The head is not stuck and advances briskly throughout. WHAT IS TRUE INSTEAD: the head cycles among a handful of IOQEs and bus 6's entry is almost never selected -- one check of bus 6 against 69 head changes. AND ONE ENTRY DOMINATES: address 9068 is at the head 33 of 69 times in that hold, 33 of 70 in the next, and 65 of 135 in the 2101 ms one -- about 48% consistently -- while the others (905a, 90c4, 90fa, 910c, 9130, 9166, 9178, 918a, 91d2, 922c) rotate. IOQE stride appears to be 18 halfwords. SO THE REMAINING QUESTION IS NARROWER: not why the head is slow, but why one IOQE keeps being re-selected as head while bus 6's waits. Note this is still distinct from the refuted queue-DEPTH idea, which turned on how many entries exist.
  - addrs `146-146,152-152,9068-9068,905a-922c` &middot; csects `FIOMNTR,FIOMGCMP,FIOCMPLT` &middot; procs `MSC,BCE6` &middot; env `YAGPC_WATCHHW` &middot; tsec `390-396` &middot; run `z6`
- **#51** *(emulator)* Porting upstream's coordinated set (zero receive-timeout floor + 33 us MIA receive pacing + @RAW keeping accumulator bit 0) fixes the DK holds. The reasoning was that bit 0 alone had been catastrophic because it was one item of an interdependent set.
  - evidence: REFUTED by run u1, YAGPC_IOP_UPSTREAM=1, full length. The set regresses EXACTLY as the mask alone did: IPL is healthy (all reads through 228 blocks at 128.9 s and 38 at 136.5 s), then the IOQE sentinel fault PGMCHK 0007 at=1be4b lastProt=080d6 fires at t=231.19 and 232.03 -- against x2's 230.9 for the mask alone -- no transition, no overlay landmarks, and the bus-6 hold distribution goes to 15 intervals with a 2166 ms MEDIAN and 13 over 300 ms, against a baseline of 804 intervals at 0.85 ms with 5 over 300 ms. So the pacing and the zero floor do NOT rescue bit 0, and entry 2's refutation of the mask stands on its own rather than being an artifact of isolation. WHAT IS STILL WORTH TESTING, and is now split out behind YAGPC_MIA_PACE (the zero floor needs no flag, YAGPC_RECV_FLOOR_US=0 does it): the pacing and the floor WITHOUT the mask change (run u2). Those two are independently justified -- FCMINIOP programs per-bus MTOs that our 2 ms floor overrides, and a real MIA presents one word per bus word time -- and they may help even though bit 0 does not. IF u2 IS ALSO BAD, the remaining items of 818df88 are load-bearing here and the port must be wider: the sector replacing the high-order bit of a 16-bit address, SRS/SI keeping the low bit of the base, SVC saving the sector in old-PSW bits 40-43, BALR/SCAL taking the link before the branch address, ISPB M1=001/011 pairing (EA, EA+1), @LH sign-extending, and the IOP stepping on elapsed time.
  - instrs `@RAW` &middot; procs `MSC,BCE6` &middot; env `YAGPC_IOP_UPSTREAM,YAGPC_MIA_PACE,YAGPC_RECV_FLOOR_US` &middot; files `src/iop.c,src/iop.h,src/iop_msc_instr.c` &middot; tsec `230-232` &middot; doc `nsts-sim-gpc 818df88` &middot; run `u1,u2,x2`
- **#52** *(emulator)* The ~1053 ms DK holds are caused by our receive timing -- the 2 ms RECV_TIMEOUT_FLOOR_US overriding the per-bus MTOs FCMINIOP programs, and/or the absence of 33 us MIA receive pacing.
  - evidence: REFUTED by run u2: YAGPC_MIA_PACE=1 with YAGPC_RECV_FLOOR_US=0, i.e. both corrections WITHOUT the @RAW mask change. The run is valid (transition at 390.55/392.23/397.87, phase 3's overlay completes at 391.489) and the hold distribution is unchanged from baseline: 804 intervals with 5 over 300 ms and a 4263.80 ms maximum, against baseline's 804 / 5 / 4264. Only the median moves, 0.85 -> 1.36 ms, which is the pacing showing up on short transfers as it should. Phase 8's overlay still never completes, cksum-verify still fires twice (both for phase 3's transaction), and the sentinel fault still lands at 396.91. SO THE TWO CORRECTIONS ARE SAFE AND FAITHFUL BUT ARE NOT THE FIX. They are independently justified -- FCMINIOP programs per-bus MTOs (33 us most buses, 49.5 us bus 24, 5 ms DK, 1.959936 s mass memory) that a 2 ms floor overrides 40-60x, and nsts-sim-gpc 818df88 makes both corrections -- so they are worth keeping on their own merits, but they do not touch the holds. CONTRAST with the @RAW mask, which is ALSO documented as required and which does NOT leave the machine standing (entry 2).
  - procs `BCE6,BCE24,MSC` &middot; env `YAGPC_MIA_PACE,YAGPC_RECV_FLOOR_US` &middot; files `src/iop.c,SSSRC/FCMINIOP.asm` &middot; doc `nsts-sim-gpc 818df88` &middot; run `u1,u2`
- **#66** *(flight-sw)* The zero entries in the SVC entry-point table FPMSVCEP (SVCs 0, 10, 11, 23, 28, 29; the 'unresolved FSVC00nn' relocations of every link) are a link defect.
  - evidence: All eight DASS dumps have 0000 in exactly those six entries too; the other entries match ours exactly. They are unused SVC numbers. A machine that issues one of them branches to 0 through FPMSVC (LA R2,X'80CE' / LH R2,0(R4,R2) / BAL R7,0(R2)), which is what OPS 901 on v38 does at 274.6 s -- so the question is why an unused SVC is issued, not the table.
  - addrs `80ce-80f5,1b13a-1b15a` &middot; csects `FPMSVCEP,FPMSVC` &middot; tape `v38` &middot; run `R901`
- **#69** *(emulator)* exec_BCR is wrong because 'BR 7' (BCR 7,R7) does not branch when CC=2. THE BCR IS CORRECT -- do not 'fix' it.
  - evidence: AP-101S instruction set sec. 5.3: 'bit 5 tests for a code equal 00, bit 6 tests for a code equal 11, and bit 7 tests for a code equal 01... M1 = 111 always branches' -- and the programming-notes table shows every instruction class producing only 00, 11 or 01. CC=10 arises only from the documented CC ANOMALY (Figure 2-20 note '##'): a MASKED DMA STORE PROTECT interrupt 'will set the condition code (CC) to a binary 10... This can result in erroneous GPC operation of an instruction which tries to utilize the CC... before they are set by another instruction'. Our two psw_set_cc(...,2) sites are exactly that note (cc_anomaly and cpu_signal_dma_protect_violation). MEASURED in L901 (v39, OPS 901, landmark stop at FPMIHIM t=285.438): R7 = a0ac0031, the CORRECT return into FIOLGERR, and FP$GMTIM's saved R7 is the same; the Instruction Monitor old PSW at 0x0070 is all zero, so NO interrupt was taken; PSW1 = acf88031, CC=2. So FPMGMTIM's closing c7e7 (BCR 7,R7) fell through to the next halfword, which is FPMIHIM+0 -- as the real machine would. The defect is upstream: something DMAs into protected storage.
  - addrs `1acf7,1acf8,932e,70-77` &middot; csects `FPMGMTIM,FPMIHIM,FIOLGERR` &middot; instrs `BCR,BC` &middot; tape `v39` &middot; doc `AP-101S-instruction-set.txt sec 5.3; Figure 2-20 note ##` &middot; run `L901`
- **#91** *(emulator)* The inter-GPC sync codes reaching a neighbour by UDP could not meet FCOS's 3.85 ms timeout. SETTLED BY REMOVING THE PATH, not by measuring it: the rotated bits now go straight into the neighbour's register from the sending machine's thread (discretes_apply_external), synchronously within the sender's call, so there is no delivery latency left to miss a deadline. The datagram is still sent, for external monitors only.
  - evidence: A WARNING ABOUT THE MEASUREMENT THAT WAS ATTEMPTED FIRST, so nobody repeats it: pairing a receive-side trace against a transmit-side trace by (sender, code) IS NOT A LATENCY MEASUREMENT. The sync code alternates between just two values, null and SSIP, thousands of times a run, so an arriving trace pairs against whichever recent transmission happens to carry the same code. That instrument produced '28.7% late' before the fix and '81.6% late' after it -- both artefacts, and the second is what an instrument looks like when it is measuring itself. A sound version needs a per-transition sequence number carried from sender to receiver, not a code match. What is true by construction: vehicle_route_out now calls discretes_apply_external in the sender's thread before returning, so the neighbour's register holds the new bits before the sending instruction completes, and the generation bump invalidates the receiver's overlay cache immediately. Any remaining delay is the receiving CPU's own interval between reads of discrete input A, which is a different quantity from the one this entry was about.
  - csects `FCMCSYNC,FCMTSYNC,FCMSSYNC,FCMISYNC,FCMSFAIL,FCMSMASK,FCMASYNC` &middot; env `YAGPC_BARRIER_US` &middot; files `src/discretes.c,src/vehicle.c` &middot; symptom `fail to sync,GPC voted out,3.85 ms timeout`
- **#103** *(emulator)* WRONG DIRECTION -- DO NOT REUSE. This entry said the OPS request cannot reach a second computer because the intercomputer bus is write-only, making ICC receive the blocker. The flight software says the dependency runs the OTHER WAY: the ICC is DOWNSTREAM of the common set, not upstream of it. SSSRC/FIOICCPG.asm has one GPC run FIOSICCM, the 'SSIP ICC COMMANDER PROGRAM (WRITE IT OUT)', while the others run FIOSICLS, the 'SSIP ICC LISTENER PROGRAM' -- and the listener issues NO COMMAND WORD AT ALL, just #RDLI to read the data. So the ICC is a broadcast the listeners simply receive, those programs are dispatched as part of SSIP sync processing, and SSIP sync processing is something only a COMMON SET MEMBER does. A computer that is in no set never runs the listener, which is why ours never reads.
  - evidence: FIOICCPG.asm:76-93. FIOSICCM: #LBR@ FIOBRE 'THIS GPC BUFFER', #DLYI 0 'WAIT FOR LISTENER', #MOUT 0,SIPICCNT-1 'WRITE OUT OUR BUFFER'. FIOSICLS: #LBR@ FIOBRE 'BUFFER FOR INPUT GPC', #RDLI SIPICCNT-1 'READ THE DATA'. SIPICCNT is 124 words, so neither is the 31-word transfer this entry decoded -- those four reads belong to something else and are recorded in #107. THE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE EMULATOR: building an ICC that answers read commands was solving a problem the software does not have. What the wire has to do is deliver a broadcast to listeners, which is what iccmodel.c already does; what is missing is upstream, in the common set. SO THE BLOCKER IS BACK WHERE #106 PUT IT: the set never forms on the DISCRETES, TCVTCSSM stays zero, and FCMSMASK is called once per machine at load with an empty mask.
  - csects `DM6OPS,CZ2COMMO,ARCGPC` &middot; procs `BCE24` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `ICC,intercomputer bus,recvWords 0,redundant set,DM6OPS targets` &middot; run `rs11`
- **#107** *(emulator)* THERE IS NO ICC READ PATH, AND THERE SHOULD NOT BE.  A GPC BROADCASTS its own 124-word ICC buffer on its own bus and the other four LISTEN PASSIVELY; a listener puts no command word on the wire at all.  The absence of read commands on the intercomputer buses is CORRECT, not a symptom, and the model does not break anything by not implementing one.  The four 'reads' in the original evidence are not ICC traffic: they are PCMMU transfers on bus 24, which is a DIFFERENT BUS from the ICC buses, and this entry's own 'procs BCE24' was the mis-attribution that made them look like ICC.
  - evidence: Read out of the flight source 2026-09-22, every citation checked against ~/workspace/PFS/OI340600 (read-only) rather than taken on report.  SSSRC/FIOICCPG.asm is the whole set of ICC bus programs: the listener FIOSICLS is '#LBR@ FIOBRE / #RDLI SIPICCNT-1 / FIOWAIT' -- three instructions, no command -- and the only two command-issuing instructions in the entire file are the commander's '#MOUTC 5,SIPICCNT-1' and '#MOUTC 5,IPRCNT-1', which are exactly the writes we trace (iua=5 func=0x000 count=123, and count=8 for IPR).  MLIB80/FIOADCMC.asm gives five commander and five listener PC entries for buses 1-5 and no third kind.  SSSRC/FIOCBLKS.asm FIOBRE demultiplexes the deposit by BUS NUMBER, which is the SENDER's GPC id, so a receiver needs no addressing and hence no command; FIOCBLKS.asm:1272 starts all five ICC BCEs in one transaction, one talker and four listeners.  AIESIP does no I/O: it walks the five in-memory copies with %NAMECOPY and short-circuits self, since FIOSICCM transmits out of this GPC's own buffer in place.  FCMLINIT's IPL duplicate-id check listens with '#LBR 0 / #RDLI 1' -- 'IF YOU RECEIVE ANYTHING' -- which only works on unsolicited traffic.  THE BUS NUMBERING, which is where this entry went wrong: MLIB80/FIOADCMC.asm:93 'FIOBUS1 EQU 2  ICC BUS 1' and :99 'FIOBUS24 EQU 48  PMU BUS 24'.  The cmd=6a401f/6a441f/6a481f/6a4c1f quartet is SSSRC/FIOPDSMU.asm:231-237 building PCMMU command words -- 'SLL R6,5 / OHI R6,X0001F / AHI R6,32' -- so RAM 512<<5|0x1F=0x401F, then 544, 576, 608: a 128-word PCMMU RAM read in four 32-word pieces, from the SM application's own table, which is why it appeared only in an SM phase.  SSSRC/FIOHISAM.asm:299 'IUA1 EQU X0D' is the PCMMU's IUA, and its commands there are #MOUT/#MOUTC WRITES despite the same mode nibble, so direction cannot be read off the function field.  Applying the BCE decode iua=(cmd>>19)&0x1f to a two-halfword PCMMU command is what produced 'iua=13'.  DO NOT REIMPLEMENT AN ICC READ.  If the other four buffers are ever seen not filling, the place to look is the listen/receive path -- receiver enable via FCMBUSCM/FCMERPC, the #RDLI base from FIOBRE, and bus-to-bus delivery of the transmitted words -- not a missing read command.
  - addrs `196ba-196ba` &middot; csects `FIOICCPG,FIOADCMC,FIOCBLKS,AIESIP,FCMLINIT,FCMBUSCM,FIOPDSMU,FIOHISAM,FCMBUSPC` &middot; procs `ICC1,ICC2,ICC3,ICC4,ICC5,BCE24,PMU` &middot; env `YAGPC_ICCTRACE` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c` &middot; symptom `ICC read,four reads of 31 words,iua 13,func 120-126,recvWords 0` &middot; doc `PASS OI340600 flight source (SSSRC/FIOICCPG.asm, MLIB80/FIOADCMC.asm)` &middot; run `icc3`
- **#114** *(goal)* THE TWO COMPUTERS DO NOT DO THE SAME I/O, BY A FACTOR OF SEVENTY, which is why the I/O-complete sync can never be met. In one 900 s run GPC1 enters FCMISYNC 34,432 times and GPC2 enters it 487 times. A barrier that every member must reach cannot be reached by a computer that arrives at it seventy times less often, so the set is voted apart on the first one after admission.
  - evidence: Run io1, tracing FCMISYNC's entry address 0x18e94 on both machines: 34432 entries for gpc=1 against 487 for gpc=2. The cause is not subtle -- GPC1 commands the display (1848 DEU commands in the equivalent single-GPC run), the mass memory and the timing unit, while GPC2 sits in GPC MEMORY commanding essentially nothing. AND THIS IS CIRCULAR IN THE EMULATOR AS IT STANDS: a computer needs bus assignments to do comparable I/O, the assignments come from the NBAT, and the NBAT takes effect at an OPS transition that the computer can only join if it is in the set. The real vehicle breaks the circle because the NBAT's assignments apply to every TARGET GPC as the transition runs, not only to the one that typed it. NOTE WHAT IS ALREADY EXCLUDED so this is not re-litigated: the barrier keeps the clocks within 200 us with zero abandoned holds (#113), the sync code and its timing windows are correct (#109), and handing GPC2 a display unit of its own does not change the ratio or the outcome (#113). The disagreement is in HOW MUCH I/O each computer does, not in when either of them does it. addrs 18e94-18e94 | csects FCMISYNC,ARCGPC,DM6OPS | procs BCE6,BCE18,BCE20 | symptom I/O complete sync,70 to 1,different I/O,set voted apart,redundant set | run io1 ANSWERED FROM THE SOURCE, so it need not be guessed: FCMISYNC.asm:33-36 says it 'SYNCHRONIZES GPCS IN THE COMMON OR REDUNDANT SETS FOR I/O COMPLETION (IOC) INTERRUPTS'. So a common-set member IS meant to take part, and GPC2's inclusion is correct rather than something the emulator invented. That makes the 70-to-1 disparity the thing to explain, and it points back at how much I/O OUR GPC1 generates rather than at how little GPC2 does -- 34,432 entries in 900 s is 38 I/O completions a second for a computer sitting on the GPC MEMORY display. Ledger #22 measured a DK hold as 1052 ms of pure dispatch latency with the BCE finishing in 0.3 ms, and #49 measured bus 24 taking more transactions than any modelled bus; either would inflate completions on the busy computer. Counting FCMISYNC entries PER BUS is the measurement that separates them, and it has not been made. addrs 18e94-18e94 | csects FCMISYNC,ARCGPC,DM6OPS | procs BCE6,BCE18,BCE20 | symptom I/O complete sync,70 to 1,different I/O,set voted apart,redundant set | run io1  SUPERSEDED BY #117, WHICH MEASURED THE BARRIERS RATHER THAN THE ENTRIES. The 34,432-against-487 figure is real and reproducible (34,428 against 454 in run ms2), but it counts CALLS TO FCMISYNC, and a call is not a barrier. FCMISYNC picks its mask from TIOQSYNT in the requesting IOQE; the redundant-set mask is zero on both machines, so every one of those passes takes the `R3 = 0` exit at 0x18eb0 and waits for nobody. The passes that actually enter the spin number FOUR ON EACH MACHINE. The two computers therefore reach the same number of I/O-complete barriers, and "a barrier that every member must reach cannot be reached by a computer that arrives at it seventy times less often" does not describe this failure. DO NOT RE-ADOPT the entry-count disparity as the cause.
  - addrs `18e94-18e94` &middot; csects `FCMISYNC,ARCGPC,DM6OPS` &middot; procs `BCE6,BCE18,BCE20` &middot; symptom `I/O complete sync,70 to 1,different I/O,set voted apart,redundant set` &middot; run `io1`
- **#116** *(emulator)* ATTACHING MORE DISPLAY UNITS DOES NOT FIX THE I/O INFLATION -- IT MAKES IT WORSE. The idea was that PASS drives four display units and modelling one leaves the DK handler talking to units that never answer, so giving it DK2, DK3 and DK4 would bring the busy computer's I/O down. Tested: GPC1's total transactions ROSE, and buses that had been silent opened up with millions of their own.
  - evidence: Run bc2, identical to bc1 but with --deu-bus 7,8,9. Bus 7 did fall as predicted, 561,010 to 161,916, so attaching a unit really does slow its bus. But bus 8 barely moved (561,332 to 522,167) and the software, now getting further -- mmu1 reads 1040 blocks against 749 -- opened buses that had had NOTHING before: bus 14 = 3,214,762 and bus 15 = 3,211,341 where both were absent from the bc1 census entirely, bus 18 = 3,212,932 (was 2,153,932), bus 20 = 4,750,010 (was 541,708), bus 21 = 2,753,097 (was 17,976), bus 24 = 2,534,724 (was 2,036,905). GPC2 is unchanged at bus 19 = 1,022,695 and bus 6 = 11,316, so the gap WIDENED. THE LESSON, and it is the useful part: the inflation is not about which peripherals exist but about what an unserved bus COSTS, and every new bus the software reaches inherits the same defect. So the fix has to be in the bus timing itself -- #115's proposal -- and not in the vehicle's inventory. Do not retry this by adding models.
  - procs `BCE7,BCE8,BCE14,BCE15,BCE20,BCE21,BCE24` &middot; env `--deu-bus,YAGPC_BUSCENSUS` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `unmodelled bus,inflated I/O,more DEUs,fail to sync` &middot; run `bc1,bc2`
- **#125** *(emulator)* THE SIMULATED-TIME BARRIER IS NOT WHY THE SET BREAKS, and tightening it does not help. The handshake pulse is 143 us wide and the barrier's default tolerance is 200 us, so a reader may in principle step over a pulse entirely -- a clean quantitative story, and wrong. At 25 us, where the permitted drift is a sixth of the pulse, the set is voted apart exactly as before. DO NOT RETUNE THE BARRIER AGAINST THE PULSE WIDTH; the skew that matters is not simulated-time drift.
  - evidence: Sweep of YAGPC_BARRIER_US over three 400 s two-computer runs, otherwise identical (runs sf-short, bar25, bar5). 200 us (default): 3 fail-votes, mmu2 432 blocks, 8 ICC commands per bus, barrier held 457,494 times for 128.1 s, 0 abandoned. 25 us: 3 FAIL-VOTES STILL, mmu2 432 blocks, only 2 ICC commands per bus, barrier held 2,742,259 times for 322.9 s of a 400 s run. 5 us: 0 fail-votes BUT THAT IS NOT A PASS -- mmu2 read 160 blocks of 432, so GPC2 never finished loading PASS and never reached a sync at all; the barrier held 2,082,125 times for 224.2 s. So the only run without a vote is the one that never got far enough to have one, and the only effect of tightening the barrier is to spend the run waiting. WHAT THE FAILING WAIT ACTUALLY SEES, traced instruction by instruction on GPC2 (run sp4, YAGPC_RANGETRACE over 0x18ece-0x18f10): every one of the ~96 samples in the 3.85 ms spin reads R3=0x27000111, GPC1 sitting at 111 null, sampled every 39-40 us. FCMADIS contributes 0x0000f000. The masked term R2=0x111 and the comparison at 0x18f07 gives R4=0 on EVERY iteration -- the condition is satisfied throughout -- and the loop is left not by the condition but by the timer at 0x18f0e. The clock read by ICR counts DOWN at one count per microsecond; R1 = start - FCMSNTO2, the difference goes negative after 3807 us, and the second phase at 0x18f32 then re-reads the discretes, finds no NEW neighbour bit, and falls to 0x18f44 BAL FCMSFINT and 0x18f4d BAL FCMSFAIL. So GPC1 does not raise the I/O-complete code at any point during GPC2's whole 3.85 ms wait; the SYNC trace shows its 001 lasting 143 us and ending BEFORE that wait began. GPC2 arrives late by more than a pulse width, and at a barrier delta of 25 us that lateness cannot be simulated-time drift. WHAT IS LEFT: the emulator's own completion-dispatch latency, #22 -- a hold that is entirely the gap between the BCE finishing its bus program in 0.3 ms and FCOS learning about it. #115 predicted this order of work and this measurement confirms it.  CORRECTED 2026-09-13 by #126: 'GPC2 arrives late by more than a pulse width' generalised from ONE handshake and is withdrawn. A swarm of eight runs shows no systematic lateness, and at the failing handshake both computers raise I/O-complete within 13-143 us of each other; the failure is the voter not recognising a present partner. The sweep result itself stands -- 25 us still failed -- but the pointer to #22 that followed from 'late' is unsupported.
  - addrs `18ece-18f10,18f44-18f4f` &middot; csects `FCMISYNC,FCMSFINT,FCMSFAIL` &middot; env `YAGPC_BARRIER_US,YAGPC_RANGETRACE,YAGPC_SYNCTRACE` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c,src/vehicle.h` &middot; symptom `fail to sync,barrier delta,pulse width,I/O complete sync,arrives late,completion latency` &middot; run `sf-short,sp4,bar25,bar5`

NEXT: rerun with YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE/ERRTERM/TIMEOUT traces on buses 20-22 to see GPC4's bad transaction; check the display-bus owner against the NBAT.

- **#155** *(emulator)* A COMPUTER THAT LEAVES THE REDUNDANT SET CANNOT BE GIVEN A CRT OR DRIVEN BACK INTO G2, AND THE CAUSE IS UPSTREAM OF THE DISPLAY PATH -- either the COMMON SET never forms for it, or it does not process inbound ICC at all. THE MECHANISM THAT MAKES IT MATTER, from the source: AIESIP filters inbound ICC messages on set membership -- when the common set EQUALS the redundant set a GPC processes all messages IT sent plus only GST (table index 1), annunciation (3) and PMU (7) from other GPCs; when CS != RS it processes ALL of them. A GPC/CRT message is HEX'817E', index 11 in CZ2V_ICC_MSG_TAB, so a computer whose CS equals its RS DISCARDS one sent by another GPC. That also explains how the key normally works and that it is NOT a transfer: inside a redundant set every member runs the same keystroke through DM1KEY and GENERATES THE MESSAGE ITSELF, the 'sent by this GPC' case. REFUTED, so do not retry it: the obvious explanation -- that a LONE out-of-set computer has no partner, so CS = RS = itself -- is NOT the whole story. Taking TWO computers out together, which gives them an obvious partner to form a common set with, changed nothing at all. LOOK AT COMMON-SET FORMATION (FCMASYNC, FCMCSYNC) AND AT WHETHER AN OUT-OF-SET GPC RUNS AIESIP AT ALL -- not at the DEU or display path. An OPS 0 computer drives a CRT perfectly well when CONFIGURED to own one: headless-gpcmem.sh boots a single GPC with no set anywhere and logs 1816 DEU commands and 575 polls, so 'a GPC in OPS 0 cannot drive a display' is FALSE.
  - evidence: Source read 2026-09-16: AIESIP.hal 056816DB-056836DB for the filter rule, CZ2COMMO.hal 027900DX-029220DL for CZ2V_ICC_MSG_TAB (HEX'817E',HEX'1502',HEX'4206' is the 11th entry; col 2 bits 9-16 = 2 halfwords, col 3 bits 13-16 = filter type 6), DM1KEY.hal 016600AA for DM1B_GPC_CRT_ICC_MSG = HEX'817E', DIMICC.hal for the collector, which only appends to CZ1B_ICC_MSG_BUF for AIESIP to ship later inside an ordinary buffer write. DETERMINISTIC REPRO, needing no fault and no waiting: bring a four-GPC set up to OPS 201, mode one computer RUN -> STBY -> RUN so it leaves the set and re-initializes into OPS 0, then GPC/CRT it a display. Done twice with GPC4 (--gpcs 1,2,3,4 --bce-network, port base 6900); the second time under YAGPC_ICCTRACE with the cap raised from 24 to 20,000. The entry was typed on KYBD2, so GPC2 processed it while GPC1 owned CRT1; afterwards CRT1 still read GPC1 and SPEC 6 PRO reported GPC4 not synced, in RUN, OPS 0, DRIVING NO DATABUSES. ICC trace: 14,087 ICCCMD lines (GPC1 3920, GPC2 3920, GPC3 3406, GPC4 2841), EVERY one iua=5 func=0x000 count 123 or 8 -- all writes, no reads anywhere -- and the last line of the trace is gpc=4, so the out-of-set computer's ICC transmit path is alive and #107's broken read path is not implicated. ICC buses are 1-5, one per GPC (YAGPC_ICC_BUS_FIRST/LAST in iccmodel.h), not bus 24. EARLIER, TWO COMPUTERS REACHED THE SAME STATE BY FAILING, AND BOTH TRIED TO FORM A COMMON SET: GPC3, voted out after missing a 101 timer sync, sat issuing 110 SSIP -- common-set sync -- about every 160 ms while standalone, and briefly succeeded after OPS 201 PRO (all four issued 100 SVC together at t=812505.035 and 001 I/O complete at t=812508.0316, the fail votes cleared and every cam.py lamp went OFF) before falling silent ~3 s later in OPS 0. GPC1, voted out later, ran at 200 sync issues per 8 s against 4542-4548 for the others and OPS 201 PRO left that rate UNCHANGED. All four emulator threads held equal CPU throughout (339-344 ticks per 4 s): these computers are executing, not blocked. This is Stage 4 ground in the multi-GPC plan. THE PAIRING TEST, which refutes the lone-computer explanation: with the set up, GPC3 was taken RUN -> STBY -> RUN and then GPC4 the same, so TWO computers sat outside the redundant set at once and had an obvious partner to form a common set with. SPEC 6 PRO showed BOTH out of sync, in RUN, OPS 0, driving no databuses -- unchanged from one being out -- and GPC/CRT 3 1 EXEC from KYBD2 then left CRT1 on GPC1, exactly as with one. Giving the stranded computer a partner changes nothing, so CS != RS is not reached that way and the cause is upstream of AIESIP's filter. NEXT RUN should carry YAGPC_SYNCTRACE=1 together with YAGPC_ICCTRACE=1 and YAGPC_ICCTRACE_MAX=0 -- 20,000 lines lasts only about 13 minutes at ~374 commands per GPC per minute and was exhausted before this test -- and should ask whether two out-of-set computers exchange sync codes WITH EACH OTHER. All four transmit ICC at the same rate regardless of membership (374/374/375/374 per minute), so ICC participation itself is not the discriminator. TWO MORE HYPOTHESES KILLED, AND SPEC 6 IS NOT A WITNESS. With sync tracing on and BOTH GPC3 and GPC4 outside the redundant set, every SSIP round in 20 s had ALL FOUR computers in it (125 rounds, 125 of them four-party), GPC3 and GPC4 landing within a median 101 us and max 203 us of each other -- the same order as a healthy set's publication spread, so this is real synchronisation, not coincidence at 6.25 Hz. THE COMMON SET DOES FORM, and it includes the computers that left the redundant set, so 'the common set never forms' is REFUTED alongside the pairing idea. CS != RS therefore holds for them and AIESIP's filter should pass everything. Out of the set they issue ONLY SSIP, I/O complete and null -- no SVC, no timer, no IPR -- which is what a common-set-but-not-redundant-set member SHOULD look like, since timer and synchronising SVC are redundant-set business. They are also plainly doing bus I/O (125 I/O completes in 20 s). CRITICAL CAVEAT ON ALL SPEC 6 EVIDENCE IN THIS ENTRY: ARAGPCSW builds the crew's view of other computers from CZ2B_DIA_RM1/RM2, redundancy-managed by CMR(ARAV_SNIN) from CZ2B_DIA1, a PER-GPC array distributed over the ICC. So what SPEC 6 says about ANOTHER computer is only as good as what the ICC delivered to the reporting one. 'Driving no databuses' and even 'in OPS 0' may be the reporting GPC's IGNORANCE rather than the other computer's state -- do not treat them as established facts about the out-of-set computer. PRIME SUSPECT IS #107 after all: both remaining failures -- one GPC seeing another's status, and acting on another's GPC/CRT message -- require READING A PEER'S ICC BUFFER, which is the path #107 says is broken. IDP3/IDP4 REASSIGNMENT REFUTED, and an out-of-set computer's ONLY I/O is its own ICC transmission. Fresh MEDS2 instances were attached to the live run on idp3 and idp4 (MEDS2.py --port-base 6900 crt3 idp3) without restarting anything: both MDUs sat at MDU IS AUTONOMOUS, the state for a lost IDP heartbeat, so nobody was driving those displays. Measured over one 30 s window with the unlimited ICC trace, per GPC: ICC commands 187 for all four, against I/O-complete syncs of 2720 (GPC1), 2719 (GPC2), 188 (GPC3), 188 (GPC4). So the two out-of-set computers do ONE I/O completion per ICC command and nothing else, while the two in the set do ~2530 other transactions on top. They are not mis-assigned and not wedged: they have no bus assignments at all. NOTE THIS PARTLY RESTORES SPEC 6 on one point -- 'driving no databuses' was LITERALLY TRUE, confirmed independently -- so the caveat above about SPEC 6 reporting another computer's state over the ICC still stands in general, but this particular reading checked out. CORRECTION 2026-09-17: the claim once made here that the per-GPC per-bus census cannot work under --bce-network was WRONG. busCount is incremented on that path; the census print in vehicle_free is simply gated on the environment variable YAGPC_BUSCENSUS. Set it and the run ends with 'vehicle: bus NN transactions: GPC1=... GPC2=...' for every bus that carried anything -- which IS the way to answer 'which buses is this computer using', including whether the launch data buses 12 and 13 carry the GPC-to-GPC overlay traffic of #157. REEVALUATED 2026-09-16 LATE: REJOINING SYNC IS SOLVED, REGAINING FUNCTION IS NOT, AND THEY ARE DIFFERENT PROBLEMS. STBY -> RUN drives 000 via FCMSWMON and the computer rejoins the COMMON set completely -- it takes part in every SSIP round with all four. What it cannot do is regain REDUNDANT-set membership: it has no bus assignments, does no I/O but its own ICC, and cannot load an OPS overlay. So the blockage is not in the sync machinery at all but in CONFIGURATION DISTRIBUTION. A LIKELY CONTRIBUTOR IS OUR OWN NBAT, not the emulator (user's hypothesis, 2026-09-16): examples/4gpc-startup.script enters ITEM 1 +2 (MC 2), ITEM 2-5 for the target set 1,2,3,4, ITEM 7-10 giving FC strings 1-4 to GPC 1-4, ITEM 11 +1 (PL to GPC1), ITEM 12 +1 and 13 +2 (CRT1 to GPC1, CRT2 to GPC2), ITEM 18 +1 and 19 +2 (MM1 to GPC1, MM2 to GPC2). GPC3 AND GPC4 ARE THEREFORE GIVEN ONE BUS EACH -- an FC string -- AND NOTHING ELSE: no CRT, no mass memory, no payload, no launch data bus. Inside the set they get mass-memory data as listeners on GPC1's or GPC2's bus; outside it they have no MM bus to reload an OPS overlay from, which would explain why GPC3 settled into OPS 0 after being briefly readmitted. That may be CORRECT flight behaviour, making our configuration -- not a defect -- the reason recovery is impossible. TO TEST: give GPC3 and GPC4 a CRT and an MM in the NBAT (ITEM 14/15 and a shared or alternate MM assignment) and repeat the RUN -> STBY -> RUN repro; if the computer then reloads and rejoins, the emulator was never at fault here.  LIKELY NOT A DEFECT -- the IDP MAJOR FUNCTION switch decides it. Owner s live run 2026-09-26 (four computers, three CRTs, from IPL, with YAGPC_FC_MDM, FC_SENSORS, FC_ANSWER_UNNAMED, MTU_ECHO_EXPIRE and MTU_LOCKED; OPS 3 then OPS 201, so GPCs 3 and 4 were RELEASED to OPS 0 by the transition -- G2 is a two-GPC set, GPC SEL 1,2, per the STS-134 Post Insertion table in #156): with every IDP/CRT MAJ FUNC switch in GNC, GPC/CRT would not attach a CRT to GPC 3 or 4; with CRT2 s switch moved to PL or SM, GPC/CRT DID attach CRT2 to GPC 3. The key handler has no switch test of its own -- OI340600 DM1KEY.hal case 4 (046300-048400) only checks the GPC and DK numbers, marks the DEU offline for the set if the target is not in it (CDMB_RSALL), and sends the ICC assignment -- so the switch acts on the claiming side: a display whose MAJ FUNC names the major function the redundant set runs is kept by that set. None of the earlier tests above recorded switch positions, and they would have been in GNC. SCOPE: seen for a computer released by an OPS transition; the voted-out and STBY/RUN cases recorded above have not been retested with the switch moved, and should be before anyone reopens this.
  - csects `AIESIP,DM1KEY,DIM_ICC_COLLECTOR,CZ2V_ICC_MSG_TAB,FCMASYNC,FCMCSYNC,ARCGPC,FCMSWMON` &middot; procs `BCE24,DK6,DK7,ICC` &middot; tape `v44boot` &middot; env `YAGPC_ICCTRACE,YAGPC_SYNCTRACE` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c,src/run.c,src/vehicle.c,SSSRC/AIESIP.hal,SSSRC/DM1KEY.hal,SSSRC/DIMICC.hal,SSSRC/CZ2COMMO.hal` &middot; symptom `GPC/CRT ignored by target,GPC in OPS 0 drives no CRT,driving no databuses,voted out GPC will not rejoin,CAM clears but GPC stays out,common set not formed,SSIP issued alone,ICC message filtered,CS equals RS`
- **#169** *(method)* PORTING MEDS2.py TO C IS NOT JUSTIFIED BY PERFORMANCE -- but NOT for the reason first given. The emulator DOES stop and wait in wall-clock time for a display reply, so MEDS's LATENCY gates it directly whatever MEDS's CPU use is; arguing from 'MEDS runs at 5% CPU, therefore irrelevant' confuses throughput with latency and is wrong. Measured, the waiting costs 0.8% of a run.
  - evidence: iop.c:1965 is explicit: 'A PEER IN ANOTHER PROCESS IS LATE IN WALL TIME, NOT IN SIMULATED TIME' -- once a bus reply is more than PEER_HOLD_AFTER_US (500 us) overdue, bcenet_framer_peer_wait holds the machine until it arrives, and rtpacer_note_peer_hold counts it. MEASURED with YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE (which costs nothing per instruction, unlike YAGPC_PACETRACE, whose four clock reads per step would slow the emulator and so give MEDS more time, understating the very thing being measured), one GPC three displays, 428 s: 808 holds totalling 3.55 s = 0.8% OF THE RUN; per hold min 0.01 ms, MEDIAN 4.92 ms, max 87.29 ms. So MEDS is VERY late when it is late -- a real display unit answers in tens of microseconds -- but rarely enough that the total is under one per cent. WHY IT IS LATE: MEDS2.py drains its socket from the Qt event loop (QSocketNotifier -> _readable), so while it is painting it is not reading, and a reply waits behind a paint. THE CHEAP FIX IF IT EVER MATTERS is a dedicated receive thread decoupling drain from paint -- about 50 lines, in Python, since the GIL is released during the blocking recv and during Qt's painting -- NOT an 11898-line rewrite. SCALING: at rate 0.2 this is 0.8%; at real time the bus traffic is ~5x denser so holds might cost ~4%, still not a case for a port. PORTABILITY IS NO LONGER THE OBJECTION IT WAS: the GUI is Qt, so a C++ port keeps the same toolkit and platform behaviour, and .github/workflows/ci.yml already builds on macos-latest, so the platforms the user does not own are covered by CI.
  - env `YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE` &middot; files `discretePanel/MEDS2.py,src/iop.c,src/rtpacer.c` &middot; symptom `peer hold,display latency,MEDS slow,port to C`
- **#171** *(method)* THE BARRIER DELTA CANNOT SAFELY BE RAISED ABOVE 50 us, and PROCESS-WIDE CPU PINNING MAKES THINGS WORSE. Raising YAGPC_BARRIER_US looked like a 20% saving until the CAM was checked: at 100, 150 and 200 us a fail-vote is left STANDING. 25 and 50 us clear their votes, and 50 buys only about 10 points, which is inside run-to-run spread. CHECK THE CAM, NOT THE EMULATOR LOG: yaGPC2.log never prints 'fail vote' or 'fail to sync', so grepping it for those reports zero every time and is not a check at all.
  - evidence: Two GPCs to OPS 201 on a 2011 i7-3930K, CPU sampled for 40-60 s once settled, CAM read from cam.log. delta 25: 213%, both votes cleared. delta 50: 203-210%, all cleared, barrier 8.59M holds / 109.3 s / 8.55M spin-released / 50634 sleeps. delta 100: 169%, STANDING VOTE GPC2->GPC1. delta 150: 189%, STANDING VOTE GPC1->GPC2. delta 200: 170%, STANDING VOTE GPC2->GPC1. The CPU figures are NOT monotonic in delta (100 gives 169% but 150 gives 189%) and identical configurations differ by 7 points between runs, so single runs cannot resolve the effect anyway. THE SPIN IS NOT WASTE: at delta 50 the average hold is 12.7 us and 99.6% release during the spin, while an OS sleep cannot resolve anything under ~50 us -- so sleeping instead would cost more. About 11% of thread time goes to barrier holds at delta 50 and that looks close to irreducible without loosening delta. PINNING, AS TESTED, IS HARMFUL AND THE TEST WAS WRONG: 'taskset -c 0,2,4,6,8,10' restricts the WHOLE simulation to six CPUs -- three MEDS, panel, CAM and the transport thread as well as the two GPC threads -- so the display clients then compete with the computers instead of using the other six logical CPUs. Result at delta 50: holds 8.59M -> 31.9M, hold time 109 s -> 224 s, and a STANDING VOTE where the unpinned run had none. Steps went 378M/336M (balanced) to 256M/966M, a 3.8x imbalance -- a dropped-out computer spinning, not work done, which is also why TOTAL STEPS ROSE WHILE PROGRESS FELL. A correct test would pin ONLY the two GPC threads to distinct physical cores, which needs per-thread affinity, not a process-wide mask.
  - env `YAGPC_BARRIER_US` &middot; files `discretePanel/simulatePASS.py,src/vehicle.c` &middot; symptom `standing vote,CAM,barrier spin,taskset,hyperthread`
- **#181** *(flight-sw)* 'GPC MEMORY 1' ON CRT2 DURING AND AFTER AN IPL IS NOT A BUG -- it names the GPC that really is driving CRT2, and the assignment itself is what the vehicle's own rule prescribes. DPS Workbook USA005350 Rev B, DK Assignment: 'the GPC currently in control of that IDP transfers control to the GPC specified for that IDP (CRT) in the active NBAT for that major function. IF NO GPC IS SPECIFIED FOR THAT IDP, THE LOWEST NUMBERED GPC IN THAT MAJOR FUNCTION ASSUMES CONTROL.' During an IPL no NBAT entry exists for CRT2, so CRT2 falls to GPC1. The digit is the commanding GPC (DPS Dictionary, GPCIPL MENU note 3: 'The GPC driving the display is indicated'). Consistent with the owner's own observation that OPS 000 PRO from OPS 201 shows GPC MEMORY 2: by then the NBAT names GPC2 for CRT2. The GPCIPL MENU digit tracks 2,3,4 because the BFC CRT select routes the CRT to the GPC being IPLed.
  - evidence: Measured with YAGPC_XMITENA_TRACE over a 4-GPC 2-CRT IPL: GPC1 holds the MIA transmitters for BCE6 (CRT1) and BCE7 (CRT2) from t=57 s and drops BCE7 only at the OPS 201 transition (t=423 s); GPC2/3/4 never enable a display transmitter during the IPL. In a 2-GPC run stopped in OPS 0 (examples/2gpc-ops0-gpccrt.script) CRT2 reads 'GPC MEMORY' with GPC1 commanding, and after GPC/CRT 2 2 EXEC it reads '0001/ / GPC MEMORY 2'.
  - csects `FCMBMASK,FCMBUSCM` &middot; procs `BCE6,BCE7` &middot; env `YAGPC_XMITENA_TRACE` &middot; symptom `GPC MEMORY 1 on CRT2,wrong GPC number on display,CRT2 shows GPC1` &middot; doc `DPS Workbook USA005350 Rev B (DK Assignment); DPS Dictionary Rev J (GPCIPL MENU)` &middot; run `dknum,gpccrt`

I claimed a bus reassignment reaches only the computer that owns the keyboard, because no GPC but GPC1 was ever seen enabling a display transmitter. The trace pads its verb to 'ENABLE ' with two spaces and my parser required one, so it matched only DISABLE lines -- 539 of 678 lines dropped in silence, and every 'never enables' statement came from that. WHAT THE TRACE ACTUALLY SHOWS (same logs, correct parser): GPC2 DOES take CRT2, enabling its transmitter inside FCMBUSCM's enable path (nia=185e4) at t=50.0 s in the GPC/CRT run, t=82.8 s in the NBAT-first run and t=340.9 s in the 4-GPC run -- and GPC1 does NOT release it until much later (t=103.3 s at the GPC/CRT entry, t=234.9 s and t=423.1 s at the OPS transition). So for a long stretch BOTH computers command CRT2. Superseded by #184, which states that as the defect to chase.

- **#186** *(emulator)* REFUTED, AND IT WAS MY OWN CLOCK ERROR BOTH TIMES.  The claim was that several GPCs' bootstraps talk on the display buses at once, before any of them is IPLed -- GPC2, GPC3 and GPC4 all commanding BCE7 from t=13.7 s while GPC1 is being loaded.  Measured on the VEHICLE'S SHARED CLOCK, no such thing happens: bus 7 carries no traffic at all before t=59.6, and each machine's bootstrap talks only AFTER ITS OWN IPL.  The number 13.697 is not an event time at all -- it is the fixed offset from a machine leaving reset to its bootstrap's first display-bus command, so EVERY machine prints t=13.697 on its own clock no matter when in the run it was loaded.  Reading those four identical numbers as one instant is what produced both wrong findings here: first 'machines in HALT transmit', then 'three bootstraps at once'.  There is no defect.  A bootstrap talking on DK1 after its own IPL is run.c's documented behaviour.
  - evidence: Run 4gpc2 (4 GPCs, 2 CRTs, corrected subtitled script, YAGPC_CMDTRACE=6,7, 9400 command words, 0 parser rejects).  First command on bus 7 per machine, own clock vs shared: gpc1 13.697/59.550, gpc2 13.697/97.965, gpc3 13.697/183.788, gpc4 13.697/269.679 -- identical on the own clock, 210 s apart on the shared one, each following that machine's own MODE: RUN.  Bus 6: gpc1 only, 13.697/13.697 (it is the machine whose reset release defines the shared origin, which is why the two agree for it alone and made the artifact look like data).  Shared-clock seconds with two commanders on bus 6 or bus 7: 0 of 627 and 0 of 447.
