- **#191** *(emulator)* LEADING HYPOTHESIS FOR #190: THE IDLE CATCH-UP CAP IS LARGER THAN FCOS'S SYNC TIMEOUT.  rtpacer.c caps one wait-state repayment at IDLE_CATCHUP_MAX_NS = 5 ms of simulated time, and its own comment says that 'stays well inside a bus receive timeout' -- which is true of the 5.0 ms MTO on a display bus and FALSE of the tightest deadline the flight software measures in simulated time, FCMOS's sync timeout of 3.85 ms (FCMIT3 and friends, #148).  So a single repayment pass can carry a waiting computer's clock further than the interval in which its peers' sync codes must appear, and the next comparison it makes finds all of them late at once -- which is exactly #190's signature: the victim fails ALL THREE peers from FCMISYNC+0x0BB and the peers then fail it from FCMCSYNC.  THE CORRELATION IS THERE IN THE FAILING RUN: capped repayments per computer were GPC1 15, GPC2 25, GPC3 20, GPC4 54 -- and GPC4 is the computer that was voted out, with more than twice any other and the LEAST running time, so it is not a duration effect.  NOT PROVEN, and one thing argues against it: cpu_advance_idle_ns stops at the next timer expiry and at the instant the wait clears, so the jump cannot skip an interrupt -- the repayment would have to land immediately BEFORE a sync wait rather than during one.  What settles it is whether a capped repayment coincides with the vote.  REFUTED THE SAME DAY, BY ITS OWN INSTRUMENT -- DO NOT RETEST.  With YAGPC_IDLE_CATCHUP_TRACE on (it prints every repayment over 1 ms), a full 3-CRT run makes 23,644 such repayments and EVERY ONE OF THEM falls between t=8.90 and t=18.98 -- the IPL window -- with NONE AT ALL after t=19, while the run continues to t=595 and the votes land at t=475-486.  The 273 repayments that exceeded FCOS's 3.85 ms sync timeout, and the 153 that hit the 5 ms cap, are all in that same first ten seconds of bootstrap.  So the oversized jump this entry proposed does not happen anywhere near the failure; the correlation with capped counts in #190's failing run was a START-UP artefact of which machine IPLed when, not a mechanism.  The cap being larger than the sync timeout is still wrong in principle and YAGPC_IDLE_CATCHUP_MS is kept so it can be tuned, but CHANGING IT CANNOT FIX #190 and a series at a reduced cap would have been two hours spent proving that.
  - evidence: Run g3crt3 with YAGPC_PACETRACE, the run in which GPC4 was voted out at shared t~478: per-computer pacer lines at exit, 'idle: 8532 calls 0.14s wall, capped 15', GPC2 10982/25, GPC3 10887/20, GPC4 10430/54.  Rebase write-offs 10.6/63.7/116.9/169.9 s, which track IPL order and so are probably start-up resyncs rather than part of this.  Constants: IDLE_CATCHUP_MAX_NS 5000000.0 at rtpacer.c:30; FCOS sync timeout 3.85 ms; DK bus MTO 303 = 5.0 ms (iop.c).  NEXT, in order: (1) YAGPC_IDLE_CATCHUP_TRACE at the default cap, to see whether a capped repayment lands at the failing instant -- the trace prints any pass repaying more than 1 ms; (2) YAGPC_IDLE_CATCHUP_MS=1 for a run series, to see whether the failure rate falls.  Both hooks are in the tree; the cap's DEFAULT is unchanged at 5 ms so the baseline stays comparable.
  - addrs `18f4f,18c4b` &middot; csects `FCMISYNC,FCMCSYNC,FCMSFAIL` &middot; env `YAGPC_IDLE_CATCHUP_MS,YAGPC_IDLE_CATCHUP_TRACE,YAGPC_PACETRACE` &middot; files `src/rtpacer.c` &middot; symptom `victim fails all peers,sync timeout,idle catch-up,simulated time jump,3-CRT vote` &middot; run `g3crt3,rate-1,trace-1`
- **#194** *(flight-sw)* THE 3.85 ms SYNC TIMEOUT IS QUOTED EVERYWHERE AND DERIVED NOWHERE, AND THE CODE SUGGESTS A MUCH LONGER ONE.  The sync loops time themselves with a BIT TEST, not a duration: FCMISYNC reads the PC1 timer ('ICR R4,R4'), subtracts the start ('SR R4,R1'), masks with FCMBIT16 and branches on zero.  FCMBIT16 is A(X'8000') and its own comment calls it the SYNC ON SYNC FAIL LOOP TIME MASK; the same constant is used by FCMISYNC, FCMCSYNC, FCMTSYNC, FCMSSYNC, FCMASYNC, FCMSFAIL and FCMBOOT.  X'8000' is 32768 ticks, and THIS EMULATOR TICKS THE INTERVAL TIMERS ONCE PER MICROSECOND (cpu.c, 'one tick is the timers' own resolution'), which makes that loop time out at 32.8 ms -- not 3.85.  Nothing in the ledger or the source comments derives 3.85 from anything; the nearest measurement is #113's 4.33 ms between a computer being admitted to the common set and being voted out again.  WHY IT MATTERS RIGHT NOW: #190's proposed mechanism was that three machines held an SVC phase 4.7 ms while the fourth timed out at 3.85 ms.  If the effective window is 32.8 ms that explanation is WRONG and the victim failed its peers for some other reason inside FCMISYNC.  Settle the number before anything is built on it -- and before widening any window by conditional assembly, which the owner proposed as a workaround for our microsecond-level timing: note that because the test is a MASK, the cheap change is a power of two (X'10000' doubles every sync window at once), and an arbitrary factor means replacing the mask test with a compare in seven routines.  SETTLED 2026-09-21, AND THE DOUBT WAS MINE: the window IS 3.85 ms, stated in the source as a plain constant.  FCMCBLKS.asm:686 'FCMSNTO2 DC F'3850'  3.85 MILLISEC IN MICROSECONDS', and FCMISYNC, FCMCSYNC and FCMSSYNC each form their deadline with 'ICR R1,R7' then 'S R1,FCMSNTO2' -- now MINUS the window, a down-counting microsecond timer.  FCMBIT16 is then only a SIGN TEST on the remaining time ('N R1,FCMBIT16  TEST FOR DIF LESS THAN ZERO', FCMISYNC.asm:303), not a count of 32768 ticks, which is where my arithmetic went wrong.  The PC1 timer does tick once per microsecond, so this emulator's timer model is right too.  CONSEQUENCE FOR THE WORKAROUND: widening is a ONE-WORD EDIT TO ANY VALUE -- F'3850' to F'12000' gives 12 ms -- NOT restricted to powers of two as this entry first said.  SECOND WINDOW worth knowing before touching either: FCMSYNTO DC F'440', .44 ms, which is only about five times our p99 arrival spread of 92 us.  And #190's arrival-skew measurement now fits exactly: the window is 3.85 ms and the excursions that break the set are 4 to 6 ms.
  - evidence: FCMCBLKS.asm:543 'FCMBIT16 DC A(X'8000') SYNC ON SYNC FAIL LOOP TIME MASK'; FCMISYNC.asm:271-275 the ICR/SR/N/BZ sequence; grep shows FCMBIT16 referenced by FCMASYNC, FCMBOOT, FCMCBLKS, FCMCSYNC, FCMINSSL, FCMISYNC, FCMNINIT, FCMSFAIL, FCMSSYNC, FCMTSYNC.  Timer resolution: cpu.c cpu_tick advances 1.0 us per tick and tick_counter decrements by whole microseconds.  Related constants, all MINIMUM durations rather than timeouts: FCMNOISE 10, FCMMISKW 200 (max interrupt skew), FCMIT5/FCMST5 87, FCMIT3 188 (min ISYNC), FCMIPRT3 248 (min ISYNC with IPR), FCMCTT3 103 (min CSYNC/TSYNC), FCMST3 182 (min SSYNC).  The 3.85 ms figure appears in CAUSES.md #91/#113/#163 and in src comments (discretes.c three times, rtpacer.c twice) with no derivation behind any of them.  NEXT: the sync-history ring now records each event's SIMULATED time as well as wall (discretes_note_sim_us, one store per instruction), so the next captured failure measures the victim's wait-to-fail interval in the clock the timeout actually uses.
  - addrs `18f4f,18c4b` &middot; csects `FCMISYNC,FCMCSYNC,FCMTSYNC,FCMSSYNC,FCMASYNC,FCMSFAIL` &middot; files `src/cpu.c,src/discretes.c` &middot; symptom `sync timeout value,3.85 ms,FCMBIT16,window too tight,conditional assembly workaround` &middot; doc `FCMCBLKS.asm:684-686 (FCMSYNTO 440, FCMSNTO2 3850); FCMISYNC.asm:186-187,303`
- **#195** *(flight-sw)* Raising the FCOS T3 sync holds (FCMIT3/FCMIPRT3/FCMCTT3/FCMST3) to ~1 ms cures the 4-GPC 3-CRT redundant-set vote (#190) by giving a late machine time to see its peers' codes
  - evidence: Soak with YAGPC_SYNC_T3_US=1000 (patch confirmed applied, syncT3Applied) on the g3-3crt-ops301-wide restore: 4 fail votes in 2.72 h of flying against ~4.1 expected from the 3.85 ms baseline rate (~1 per 40 min).  No change.  Same class as widening FCMSNTO2 to 12 ms (7/8 clean vs 7/8 at 3.85).  Neither window length matters, which says the late machine is not slightly late but is DOING SOMETHING DIFFERENT.  Decode of soak7500-1 (ring with BCE io completions): all four said 101 timer at ~-5.2 ms; GPC1/2/3 then went 111 null ~-4.26 ms and stayed; GPC4 alone went null then 100 SVC at -4.134 ms, waited 4.1 ms and failed 1,2,3 from FCMSSYNC+0x86 (0x1979c, the #148 address), r0=001c0001; the others failed GPC4 5.1 ms later from FCMISYNC+0xBB 0x18f4f.  GPC4's io: BCE20-22 done -4.85 ms, BCE14-16 done -4.77 ms, but its OWN FC string BCE17 done only at -0.247 ms, ~4.5 ms after the other three.  Lead: late completion of a GPC's own FC-bus transfer (BCE14-17) making its software issue an SVC its peers did not.  Failures clustered on vehicle port base 7500 (3 of 3 there; 7700 1/3; 7900, 8100 0/6 each).
  - addrs `1979c,18f4f,1948c,081fa-08201` &middot; csects `FCMSSYNC,FCMISYNC,FCMSFAIL` &middot; procs `BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17,BCE20,BCE21,BCE22` &middot; config `G3` &middot; env `YAGPC_SYNC_T3_US,YAGPC_SYNC_HISTORY` &middot; symptom `GPC4 voted out,three CRTs,4-GPC 3-CRT,SVC sync one machine only,late FC bus completion` &middot; run `soak7500-1,soak7500-2,soak7500-3,soak7700-1`
- **#200** *(emulator)* OPS 2 0 1 PRO transitions of a 4-GPC set (from a restored G3 vehicle, via OPS 000) can lose sync: 2 of 3 so far drew fail votes.  All the post-#196 sync-loss testing (7.4 h) was steady OPS 301 flight with NO OPS transitions, so transitions are untested ground.
  - evidence: DOES NOT REPRODUCE ON THE CURRENT BUILD, and the earlier evidence predates #199's fix.  Three parallel repeats of the same sequence that drew the votes -- restore g3-3crt-ops301-idp3 as 4 GPCs / 3 CRTs, then KB2 OPS 0 0 0 PRO, KB2 OPS 2 0 1 PRO, KB2 OPS 0 0 0 PRO, KB1 OPS 3 0 1 PRO, KB1 SPEC 0 PRO, 6 min hold (ops200-7500/7700/7900, 2026-09-22) -- all CLEAN: 0 votes, 0 fail-to-sync, 0 BCE receive timeouts, 0 error terminations, pacing 96.1%.  The transitions really happened, which is the check that matters: mmu1 10 commands, 291 blocks read, 149000 words out, 149000 taken, 0 lost, and 184934 words carried GPC-to-GPC on the mmu1 bus.  A fourth run (iccgate-7500) after the #193/#201 ICC fixes was CLEAN too.  The failing ops3-B run was taken BEFORE #199 put a GPC on the mass-memory buses, which is the path this entry implicated; and ten repeats of the bare 301->201 transition (D.script, ops2r-7500/7900 x5) had already drawn 0 votes, so the OPS 000 step was never the discriminator it appeared to be.  Retest only with new evidence -- a vote seen on a build at or after commit be386fb3d.
  - procs `MMU1,MMU2,BCE18,BCE19` &middot; config `G2,G3` &middot; symptom `fail votes during OPS transition,GPC2 voted out,GPC1 GPC2 mutual vote,I/O ERROR MMU1,I/O ERROR MMU2,OPS 201,4-GPC` &middot; run `ops200-7500,ops200-7700,ops200-7900,iccgate-7500,ops3-B`
- **#221** *(emulator)* ANSWERING THE OTHER UNITS ON BUSES 14-17 -- FIOFAIC5 at IUA 17, 23 and 24 and FIOHI1EI at IUA 17 -- IS A NET LOSS, even though it removes exactly the failures it was aimed at. The model gated its answers on iua == 10 || iua == 12, and YAGPC_FC_LEARN showed the reads still unanswered on those buses were FIOFAIC5 (32 words) and FIOHI1EI (6 words) at IUAs the gate excluded, while fc_ours() already accepted FIOFAIC by name. Relaxing the gate under YAGPC_FC_SENSORS did remove them -- and cost more than it gained. REVERTED.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26, two five-computer ascents identical but for the change, both with YAGPC_FC_LEARN and NEITHER with YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE (which is itself decisive, see #216).  discretePanel/learn2, IUA 10/12 only: 65 error terminations on buses 14-17 (34 with left=1, 29 with left=32, 2 with left=6) and THE TIMING UNIT ALIVE, 4/6/6/6 slots on GPC1-GPC4.  discretePanel/allunits, all IUAs: 75 error terminations (52 left=1, 9 left=6, 9 left=2, 5 left=34) and THE TIMING UNIT BYPASSED on all four members, with four votes against GPC4 at t=866.9.  THE AIMED-AT FAILURES DID GO: the 29 left=32 terminations at FIOMFEG9's FIOBY27L, FIOBY28L and FIOBY29L fell to ZERO, which confirms the diagnosis -- they were waiting for FIOFAIC5's 32 words.  But left=1 rose from 34 to 52 and left=2, left=6 and left=34 appeared, which is #210's 'ANSWERING PEELS LAYERS' arriving with a net loss rather than a net gain.  A CAUTION ABOUT THE ARITHMETIC: an earlier reading of this called it '162 to 75, a 54% improvement' by comparing against the discretePanel/mask run, which carries YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE and is therefore not a valid baseline for anything (#216).  The like-for-like baseline is learn2 and the change is 65 to 75, WORSE.  SIDE EFFECT WORTH KEEPING: unanswered receives on bus 24 fell from 14,001 to 1,278 (#220), so that device is reachable this way -- but not at this price.  A NARROWER FORM WAS TRIED AND IS ALSO REFUTED, AND IT EXPOSED THAT THESE SINGLE RUNS ARE NOT TRUSTWORTHY.  The second attempt tested the NAME first and the IUA second -- a command whose symbol fc_ours_strict() accepts is answered wherever it lives, anything else still has to be at IUA 10 or 12 -- which opens exactly SEVEN commands (FIOFAIC5 at 17, 23, 24 and FIOMDMRT at 6, 9, 29, 30) instead of the whole survey.  It is therefore a strict SUBSET of the all-IUA form.  It measured WORSE THAN BOTH: 155 error terminations on buses 14-17 against allunits' 75 and learn2's 65, 37 distinct failing elements against 17 and 15, SIXTEEN held by a single computer against 5 and 8, and seven votes.  AND THE FAILURES IT WAS AIMED AT DID NOT CLEAR: left=32 was 46, where the broader form drove the same 29 to ZERO.  A SUBSET THAT BEHAVES WORSE THAN ITS SUPERSET MEANS THESE ARE NOT MEASUREMENTS OF WHAT THEY APPEAR TO MEASURE.  It is not logging volume -- learn2 wrote 14,078 FCLEN lines and kept the timing unit alive, byname wrote 1,338 and lost it, which is the opposite of the RECVWORD effect in #216.  Something else about this configuration varies run to run and has not been isolated, so the numbers in this entry and in #221's first half should be treated as one sample each, not as characteristic.  WHAT THIS DOES NOT OVERTURN: the asymmetry measurement in learn2 stands on its own structure rather than on counts -- 8 of 15 failing elements held by ONE computer, and always the one COMMANDING that bus (bus 14 GPC1, 15 GPC2, 16 GPC3, 17 GPC4), because a commander runs FIOEL27C where the listeners run FIOBY27L/28L/29L.  One transaction, different program addresses, so an unanswered read gives the commander an error its peers cannot have.  That is FIOGPCWE == 1 by construction and is not a delivery defect any model change can remove: only making the read SUCCEED removes it.  THAT 'NOT TRUSTWORTHY' IS TOO STRONG AND IS NARROWED HERE.  Sorted by what was actually different, the eight runs of this configuration are CONSISTENT: with the committed code and no YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE the timing unit is ALIVE three times out of three (sensors, sensors2, learn2); with RECVWORD it is BYPASSED three times out of three (fa, carry, mask); and with either IUA extension it is BYPASSED two times out of two (allunits, byname).  So the extensions really do cause the loss -- that direction is 2 of 2 against a 3 of 3 baseline -- and what remains anomalous is only that the narrower form scored worse than the broader one on the error COUNTS, not that the outcome is random.  Treat the counts as one sample each; treat the ALIVE/BYPASSED outcome as reliable.   AND THE BOUNDARY #210 DREW IS WHAT EXPLAINS IT.  FIOFAIC5 is an aft-MDM CHANNEL read -- sensor data, not interface state -- so answering it with zeros is inventing what a sensor saw, which is precisely the line #210 drew from many earlier runs: 'a device model may truthfully report an unpowered box; it may not invent channel data, because the flight software acts on it and two computers then disagree'.  Both of tonight's extensions crossed that line and both cost the set, which is the third and fourth independent confirmation of it.  The reads still unanswered on buses 14-17 in the baseline are channel reads of boxes this vehicle does not have, and they cannot be faked without paying for it.  AND THE ASYMMETRY ARGUMENT THIS ENTRY LEANS ON IS WITHDRAWN TOO -- see #216.  FIOGPCWE is computed per BUS from a mask of BCEs, not per element, so 'a commander runs FIOEL27C where its listeners run FIOBY27L' does not create an asymmetry at all: same bus, same bit, counted as universal.  Measured, buses 14-17 have all four computers erring in every run.  What these two refutations still establish stands -- both forms of widening coverage cost the set and the timing unit -- but the REASON offered for it was wrong.
  - csects `FIOFAIC5,FIOHI1EI,FIOMFEG9,FIOBY27L,FIOHFE89` &middot; procs `BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17,BCE23,BCE24` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_FC_SENSORS,YAGPC_FC_LEARN` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `peeling layers,net worse,timing unit bypassed,left=32 cleared` &middot; doc `fc-bus-survey: 88401f FIOFAIC5 32 words; 884005 FIOHI1EI 6 words` &middot; run `learn2,allunits`
- **#222** *(emulator)* THE ARMED COUNT IS NOT A RELIABLE TEST FOR 'THIS IS A READ', so a coverage rule built on it is LESS complete than the survey it replaces -- not more. The idea was sound on its face: #210 says partial coverage is what breaks a redundant set (answer some devices and the residue is asymmetric, FIOGPCWE is 1, FIOERRLC self-fails), and zeros do not diverge, so answering EVERY read with the length the commanding BCE armed for should leave no residue at all -- and should exclude the two things that must never be answered, the listen command at IUA 8 and FIOHIBAD, by construction, since neither arms a receive. It does exclude those. It also excludes a great many genuine reads, because iop_bce_armed_words is only set when exec_MIN issues the command: a read whose receive was armed by a separate instruction has no stash and reports -1.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26 (YAGPC_FC_ALL, since reverted) on the same five-computer ascent as the baseline, YAGPC_FC_MDM=1 and YAGPC_FC_LEARN on both.  Baseline (discretePanel/learn2): 65 error terminations on buses 14-17, 15 distinct failing elements, 8 of them held by ONE computer, 67 unanswered receives on the flight-critical buses.  With the armed-count rule (discretePanel/fcall): 136, 35, 21 and 141 -- every figure worse, and NINE votes.  THE PROOF THAT IT IS THE ARMED COUNT AND NOT THE IDEA: the reads left unanswered include 632aaa and 631c20 at IUA 12 and 526c05 and 525000 (FIONSPDR) at IUA 10, which the BASELINE answers.  They are at the two IUAs the model has always covered, so nothing about coverage policy excluded them; they were excluded by m_armed < 0.  The baseline's own expression says as much and was written knowing it -- 'int n = (m_armed >= 0) ? m_armed : surveyed' -- with the survey as the fallback for exactly this case.  Requiring the stash threw the fallback away.  CONSEQUENCE FOR #212, which proposed answering only when m_armed >= 0 as a hardening against replying to a command that armed nothing: that hardening would ALSO silence these reads, so it must not be applied as stated.  The narrower form -- refuse only when the symbol also appears in the no-receive table -- is still open.  THE LIKELY REASON m_armed IS -1 ON THOSE READS, and it is checkable.  FIONSPPG issues its reads as '#MIN 0,0' followed by a bare '#MINC FIOFFIUA,FIONSP2P' (#146, #210).  exec_MIN stashes armingWords and issues the #MIN's OWN command, then iop_bce_receive arms the receive and CLEARS the stash to -1 -- so when the #MINC carrying the real command arrives, armingWords is already spent.  iop_bce_armed_words is meant to fall back to recvCount when recvActive, which should cover exactly this, so either that fallback is not being reached or the receive is not active at that moment.  That is one fprintf to settle and it was not done tonight.  IT ALSO EXPLAINS THE SHAPE of what went unanswered: 525000 is FIONSPDR and 526c05 is FIOHI1C2, both at IUA 10, both answered by the baseline through the survey fallback and both silent under the armed-count rule.  AND IT BOUNDS WHAT A SURVEY CAN EVER DO: 532aaa is not in the OI301700 source at all -- no EQU, count field 170, function 195 -- so it is built at run time and no parser will ever recover it.  Complete coverage therefore cannot come from the survey alone NOR from the armed count alone; it needs the armed count to work for #MIN/#MINC pairs, which is the one thing left to check.
  - procs `BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_FC_ALL,YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_FC_LEARN` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/iop.c,src/iop_bce_instr.c` &middot; symptom `coverage worse,armed count -1,partial coverage,asymmetric residue` &middot; doc `#210 partial coverage is worse than none; #212 the armed-count hardening` &middot; run `learn2,fcall`
- **#224** *(emulator)* BLOCKING A DISABLED TRANSMITTER'S COMMAND FROM REACHING THE DEVICE MODEL DOES NOT FIX THE LISTENER FAILURES -- IT MAKES THEM WORSE. The reasoning was sound and the measurement behind it stands: a Listen-Mode BCE is one whose transmitter is disabled (BCE PoO 4.1), FIOHFEPG's listener element is literally '#CMDI FIOFFIUA,FIOMDMRT  SET IUAR FOR LISTENER' followed by '#RDLI 0', and on real hardware that instruction is local and puts nothing on the wire -- while mia_xmit_cmd forwards it to the servicer unconditionally, so the model makes the listener the commander of that bus and clears every reader's pending answer. Guarding the servicer call on iop_proc_get(regXmitEna) nevertheless increases the very failures it targets.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-26 on the five-computer ascent, YAGPC_FC_MDM=1 YAGPC_FC_SENSORS=1, ERRTERM on all eight flight-critical buses so the comparison is like for like (discretePanel/sensors2 against discretePanel/xmit8).  Baseline: 121 error terminations, 86 on buses 14-17, 35 on 20-23, 24 at FIOBYLR5-8/FIOBYRR5.  With the guard: 112 total, 62 on 14-17 (better), 50 on 20-23 (worse), and THIRTY-EIGHT at FIOBYLR5-8/FIOBYRR5 against 24 -- the target group up by half.  So the model evidently depends on seeing that command somewhere not yet traced; removing it does not restore the listener's answer.  THE MEASUREMENT THAT MOTIVATED IT IS STILL GOOD and is the most precise thing known about the vote: binned in PANEL seconds (clock alignment validated two ways -- the panel's reset-release stamps and a shared wire event agreeing to 0.3 s), 1.5 s before a vote EVERY flight-critical bus shows the three LISTENERS erring at FIOBYLR5-8 with one word still wanted and the COMMANDER not erring -- bus 20 held by GPC2,3,4 and not GPC1, 21 by 1,3,4 not GPC2, 22 by 1,2,4 not GPC3, 23 by 1,2,3 not GPC4 -- while the commander's own FIOMDMRT read succeeded 399 times in that run.  A defect that hurts listeners specifically is real; this is not its remedy.  A CAUTION ON COMPARING RUNS: a first attempt compared against learn2, which traces only buses 14-17, where FIOBYLR5-8 do not live, so both arms read zero and the change looked neutral.  Match the trace masks before comparing.
  - addrs `1d820,1d866,1d8ac,1d8f2,1d7f8` &middot; csects `FIOHFE89,FIOHFEPG,FIOELLR6,FIOELRR5,FIOMDMRT` &middot; instrs `#CMDI,#RDLI,#MIN,#MINC` &middot; procs `BCE20,BCE21,BCE22,BCE23` &middot; config `G9` &middot; env `YAGPC_FC_MDM,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS,YAGPC_ERRTERM_TRACE` &middot; files `src/iop.c,src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `listener fails commander succeeds,left=1,IUAR set by listener,transmitter disabled` &middot; doc `BCE PoO 4.1 Listen Mode; FIOHFEPG FIOELLR6` &middot; run `sensors2,xmit8,xmit`
- **#235** *(emulator)* THE INTER-COMPUTER CHANNEL DROPS AND BACKLOGS ENORMOUS NUMBERS OF WORDS, AND THE COMPUTERS WHOSE ICC READS FALL BEHIND ARE EXACTLY THE ONES THAT DIE. FIOGPCWE -- NUMBER OF GPCS WITH ERROR -- is computed in FIOCMPLT from the per-BCE error masks the computers exchange over the ICC. A computer whose ICC receive is chronically backlogged is reading STALE peer error masks, so a UNIVERSAL I/O error looks to it like an error only IT had: FIOGPCWE=1, FIOGPCID=self, and FIOERRLC force-fails it to sync. That is a defect in the channel, not an asymmetry in the devices, and it explains the self-fail without requiring any real per-computer difference in the bus models.
  - evidence: Run idlestep, five computers, shutdown report. TOTALS: xmitWords 2023873, recvWords 5899290, pending 18462, DROPPED 825461. PER READER, the pattern is not latency -- every lag histogram is <1 ms, min -27 max 135 us -- it is VOLUME. GPC1 reading bus 2: 3487 transfers of which 1123 found older words still queued, backlog 2,283,000 words. GPC1 on bus 3: 1030 of 2793, 2,093,000 words. GPC1 on bus 5: 1084 of 1171, 2,204,264 words. GPC4 on bus 2: 918 of 2304, 1,863,000. GPC4 on bus 3: 825 of 2304, 1,673,000. GPC4 on bus 5: 890 of 1365, 1,806,000. GPC2 and GPC3 are by contrast CLEAN on most buses: 0 new commands found older words still queued. Per-bus drops: bus 1 25731, bus 2 278882, bus 3 237478, bus 4 30889, bus 5 252481. Every bus also shows ~2048 words expired unread (not taken within 10000 us). THE CORRELATION WITH DEATH IS EXACT IN THIS RUN: GPC1 (worst backlog) self-failed from FIOERRLC at shared=725.420626; GPC4 (second worst) was voted out at 755.185580; GPC3 (92 stale transfers, mild) self-failed at 880.940474; GPC2, the cleanest reader, never failed and cast every vote. See #234 for the FCMSFAIL chain and #136 for the shared-device-model statement of the same family.  THE LINK TO FIOGPCWE IS CONFIRMED AT THE SOURCE, not inferred. FIOCMPLT.asm:1472-1500 (BCELOOP): for each BCE 1-5 it zeroes FIOGPCWE, then loops GPC 1-5, takes A(ICC BUFFER) THIS GPC from the FIOICBSV buffer table, loads MASK OF BCES WITH ERROR THIS GPC out of that buffer, shifts by the BCE number, and on a set bit does AHI R4,1 / STH R4,FIOGPCWE / STH R5,FIOGPCID. So FIOGPCWE is literally a count over the peers ICC BUFFERS. AND THE GUARD MAKES IT WORSE, CORRECTLY: a GPC is included only if its bus mask says it is in the set AND FIOBMASK (MASK OF ICC RETRY BUSES IN ERROR) says the ICC was received error free on retry, OR it is selfs own buffer (CH R5,EQ,TFCMID). A peer whose ICC receive is broken is therefore EXCLUDED from the count, which drives the count DOWN toward 1 -- and 1 with FIOGPCID=self is exactly FIOERRLCs force-fail-self condition. Self is always counted because self is exempt from the guard. The flight software is behaving correctly against a channel that is not delivering.  FIGURES RESTATED PRECISELY, because the million-word numbers above are NOT words lost -- staleWords accumulates the queue DEPTH at each sender command (iccmodel.c, staleAtCmd/staleWords in GPC_SVC_XMIT_CMD), so the right statistic is the mean depth. It is devastating: bus2/GPC1 2033.5, bus5/GPC1 2033.5, bus2/GPC4 2030.2, bus5/GPC4 2030.3, bus3/GPC1 2032.2, bus3/GPC4 2028.1, bus4/GPC1 1968.3, bus2/GPC3 and bus5/GPC3 1876.6 -- ALL OUT OF ICC_QUEUE = 2048. The queues are not lagging, they are SATURATED at 99.2 percent of the bound, so every arriving word drops the oldest one. By contrast GPC5 sits at mean depth 17.5 on every bus and GPC2 never went stale on any bus at all. THE CORRELATION WITH DEATH IS EXACT AND COMPLETE IN THIS RUN: the three computers with saturated queues -- GPC1, GPC4, GPC3 -- are precisely the three that self-failed or were voted out (725.42, 755.19, 880.94), and the two with healthy queues -- GPC2 at zero and GPC5 at 17.5 -- are precisely the two that survived, with GPC2 casting every vote. WORDS ACTUALLY DROPPED, which is a different and smaller number: bus 1 25731, bus 2 278882, bus 3 237478, bus 4 30889, bus 5 252481, total 825461.  CAUSAL DIRECTION IS NOT YET ESTABLISHED AND MUST NOT BE ASSUMED. iccmodel.c s own note on the overflow policy records that the only receiver ever observed to fill a queue was GPC4 AFTER it had been voted out and stopped reading. A computer that has been failed out stops draining its ICC queues, so saturation is exactly what a DEAD computer looks like -- and the shutdown report is cumulative, so it cannot separate "saturated, therefore voted out" from "voted out, therefore saturated". The perfect correlation in this entry is consistent with BOTH. Status dropped from confirmed to open until the order is measured: iccmodel.c now records firstFullUs[bus][gpc], the shared time each queue first reached the bound, which compares directly against the FCMSFAIL landmark times. The channel is a cause only if a queue saturates BEFORE its reader is failed.  CAUSAL DIRECTION SETTLED IN ONE DIRECTION AT LEAST: SATURATION IS NOT AFTERMATH. Run errwho, 2026-09-26, with firstFullUs recording the shared time each queue first reached the bound. GPC3 s queues on buses 1 and 2 were full from shared t=272.158, GPC4 s on buses 1, 2 and 3 from t=383.359, GPC5 s on buses 1-4 from t=528.479. THE FIRST FCMSFAIL IN THE WHOLE RUN was at t=759.655704. So GPC4 was saturated 376 s and GPC3 487 s BEFORE any computer had failed anything -- neither can be a consequence of having been voted out. AND THE DEATHS LINE UP WITH IT: at 759.6557 GPC1 and GPC2 both voted GPC4 out of the redundant set from FCMTSYNC+0029 (r0=00020001) and out of the common set at 759.6818; at 759.9757 GPC1 voted GPC3 out (00040001), common set at 760.0019. Those two victims are exactly the two redundant-set members with long-standing saturation. GPC1, the lone survivor, NEVER saturated a queue on any bus. TWO QUALIFICATIONS, so this is not overclaimed: GPC5 was saturated from 528 s and was NOT voted out, but GPC5 runs in the common set rather than the redundant set and is not subject to these votes; and GPC2 died at 759.8086 from FIOERRLC+0313 with NO prior saturation (its queues first filled at 762.4, after its death), so a clean channel does not make a computer safe. The claim that survives is the weaker one: saturation precedes the VOTES, by hundreds of seconds, and is not explained away as aftermath.  REFUTED, run iccage, 2026-09-26. DO NOT RETEST. Every queue saturation has a benign explanation and none of them is a channel defect. THE THREE EARLY ONES ARE THE STAGGERED IPL: 5gpc-3crt-subtitled.script IPLs the computers one at a time, and firstFullUs for GPC3/GPC4/GPC5 (shared 272.18 / 383.22 / 528.18) lands at each machine s OWN IPL -- the panel log has GPC3 s IPL switch at wall 324.2 and GPC4 s at 435.3, and with the run at about 0.85 of real time those map to shared 272 and 383. A computer becomes "present" the moment it first touches the bus and words then queue for it, but it does not READ until later, so the queue fills once in between. iccmodel.c ALREADY DOCUMENTS THIS as expected -- "a receiver s first read flushes the whole queue by age anyway (each one was measured discarding exactly 2048 words expired unread)" -- and the expired counter confirms it: EXACTLY 2048 words per reader, once, which is one queue flushed at the first read and nothing afterwards. THE FOURTH IS A DEAD COMPUTER: GPC2 s queues first filled at shared 719.536, and GPC3 had already voted GPC2 out at shared 716.981 -- 2.56 s EARLIER. That is the "voted out, therefore stopped reading, therefore saturated" case this file s overflow note warned about. THE HEAD-AGE FIGURES FIT THE SAME STORY: head age at a drop was min 2.40 s, mean 2.62 s, max 2.72 s uniformly, which is simply 2048 words at the arrival rate -- the age of a queue nobody has read yet, not evidence of a broken expiry. THE ORIGINAL PERFECT CORRELATION IN RUN idlestep WAS THE CONFOUND, exactly as suspected when this was downgraded from confirmed: the computers that die stop reading, and IPL order put the rest in the same column by coincidence.
  - addrs `08d83,08d84,08d86` &middot; csects `FIOCMPLT,FIOERRLC,FCMSFAIL` &middot; procs `ICC,BCE1,BCE2,BCE3,BCE4,BCE5` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c` &middot; symptom `fail to sync,vote out,words dropped,older words still queued` &middot; run `idlestep`
- **#236** *(emulator)* THE ICC RECEIVE QUEUES ARE SATURATED BECAUSE THEIR EXPIRY IS BLOCKED AT THE HEAD BY A WORD THAT CAN NEVER EXPIRE. iccmodel.c stamps each broadcast word with the SENDERs shared time, m->sharedUs[from], which is initialised to -1.0 and only set when that computer first reports its time. Any word broadcast before its sender has reported carries at = -1.0, and the expiry loop does "if (at < 0.0 || sharedUs[rx] - at <= expireUs) break" -- so a negative stamp at the head stops the loop and every word behind it is unreachable, however old. The queue then only ever loses words to the overflow policy, which drops the oldest one per arrival, and it sits pinned at the bound.
  - evidence: PREDICTED FROM ARITHMETIC BEFORE MEASURING, and recorded first so the measurement can refute it. The run idlestep figures are not self-consistent under any healthy reading: mean queue depth is 2028-2034 of ICC_QUEUE 2048, yet expired[] shows only about 2048 words per reader -- ONE flush, at the first read -- and nothing after. For a full 2048-word queue to hold nothing older than the 10 ms expiry, words would have to arrive at 204 per ms, which over the 900 s run is 184 million words on one bus; the bus actually carried about 1.2 million. The only way both numbers can be true is if THE EXPIRY NEVER RAN after the first flush. Two counters now answer it directly: unstamped[bus][gpc] counts words queued while their senders shared time was still negative, and expiryBlocked[bus][gpc] counts the times the loop broke on at < 0. If both stay zero this is refuted and the saturation has another cause. NOTE the lag histogram is NOT evidence of health here and must not be quoted as such: it is computed only at the last word of a transfer, against sentAtUs indexed by a 64-entry ring.  WHY THE FIX IS PRINCIPLED WHATEVER THE COUNTERS SAY. This queue models a WIRE, and a wire has no memory: a word nobody took is gone, which is exactly what expireUs implements. A receiver only takes ICC words while its BCE is armed to listen, so words broadcast at any other moment SHOULD vanish. An expiry that can be blocked at the head therefore does not merely leak -- it converts the wire into an unbounded buffer, which is the opposite of the thing being modelled, and the overflow policy (drop the oldest) is then the only thing bounding it at all. So "never let the expiry be blocked" is a fidelity requirement and not a workaround, and it holds even if this particular blocked-head mechanism turns out not to be what saturated these queues.  REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT, run errwho, 2026-09-26. The two counters were added exactly to settle this and both are ZERO on every bus and every reader -- not one word was ever queued while its sender lacked a shared time, so the expiry was never blocked at the head by an unstamped word. The arithmetic that motivated this (a full 2048-word queue cannot hold only sub-10-ms words at the observed traffic rate) still stands and still needs an explanation, but this is not it. DO NOT RETEST. The fidelity argument recorded above -- that a blocked expiry would turn a wire into an unbounded buffer -- remains a reason to keep the guard defensive, but it is not a live defect.
  - procs `ICC` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c` &middot; symptom `queue saturated,words dropped,expiry never runs` &middot; run `idlestep`
- **#239** *(emulator)* A QUEUE IS ONLY AGED WHEN ITS OWN READER POLLS THAT PARTICULAR BUS, SO A READER THAT STOPS POLLING A BUS NEVER EXPIRES ANYTHING ON IT AND THE QUEUE GROWS TO THE BOUND. iccmodel.c runs the expiry inside the GPC_SVC_RECV_POLL / GPC_SVC_RECV_WORD case, and it touches only q[bus][gpcId] -- the one bus being serviced by the one computer doing the servicing. icc_broadcast, by contrast, queues every word to EVERY present computer unconditionally. So the model keeps putting words into a queue nobody is listening to, and ages that queue only if and when its reader comes back. A WIRE DOES NOT WORK THAT WAY: a word nobody took is gone whether or not the computer that ignored it ever listens again, which is the whole point expireUs exists to model.
  - evidence: PREDICTED FROM ARITHMETIC, recorded before the measurement that tests it. Run errwho: bus 1 carried about 390,000 broadcast words in 1000 s, which is 0.39 words per ms to each reader. For its 2048-word queue to be full of words younger than the 10 ms expiry, they would have to arrive at 204 per ms -- five hundred times the actual rate. So the head MUST be old, and the expiry must not be reaching it. The other half of the picture agrees: every reader expired EXACTLY 2048 words, once, and then never again, while its queue sat at the bound -- one flush at its first read on that bus, and nothing afterwards, which is what "the expiry only runs when this reader polls this bus" produces. At shutdown buses 1 and 5 each showed 6144 pending, exactly three full queues, while buses 2, 3 and 4 showed 145, 415 and 10. THE TEST: fullHeadAgeMin/mean/max now record the head word s age at the moment a word is dropped for want of room. If the mean age is far past 10000 us this is confirmed; if it is under 10 ms the arithmetic is wrong somewhere else. THE FIX IF CONFIRMED: age the destination queue in icc_broadcast, before appending, so a queue is aged by the passage of time rather than by its reader s attention.  ONE READING ALREADY EXCLUDED: it is not that a bus is never read at all. In run errwho every reader read every peer bus -- bus 1 was read 3711/3018/2320/2237 times by GPC2/3/4/5, bus 5 was read 2232/1381/1382/1380 by GPC1/2/3/4, and so on for all five. What readers do is take FEWER transfers than were sent: bus 1 was commanded 4563 times and GPC5 took 2237 of them, 49 percent. So the expiry is being reached on every bus by every reader; the question is why it then leaves the queue at the bound. CUMULATIVE COUNTERS ARE CONFOUNDED BY DEATH and must not be compared naively: GPC4 was voted out at 759.66 of a 1000 s run and stopped transmitting, so its 2320 commands against GPC1 s 4563 measures how long each lived at least as much as how fast each ran.  REFUTED ALONG WITH #235, and its premise was wrong rather than its reasoning. The queues do NOT grow to the bound and stay there because a reader stops attending to a bus: they fill ONCE, before that reader s first read on that bus, and the first read then flushes the lot -- which is why expired[] is exactly 2048 per reader and never more. The arithmetic that motivated this entry ("2048 words younger than 10 ms would need 500x the traffic") was right, and the resolution is that the words are NOT younger than 10 ms: they are about 2.6 s old, they accumulated before anyone was listening, and the expiry removes them in one go the moment the reader arrives. The proposed fix -- ageing the destination queue inside icc_broadcast -- would make the pre-read fill tidier but changes no outcome, and it is not worth perturbing a model whose default configuration the owner has validated clean. DO NOT RETEST.
  - procs `ICC` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c` &middot; symptom `queue saturated,words dropped,expiry runs once` &middot; run `errwho,idlestep`
- **#243** *(emulator)* RELEASING THE REPLY ATOMICALLY AT THE DOCUMENTED 33 US RATE (YAGPC_MTU_ATOMIC) HELPS BUT DOES NOT FIX IT, and the reason exposes the real constraint: with atomic release a 21-word reply is not available until wireUs + 693 us, while #217 measured the NEXT command arriving at a median of 561 us -- so the reply is cut off before it ever becomes available, and the reader gets nothing rather than part.
  - evidence: Run atomic, 2026-09-26, same fixture and baseline as iccage. ERROR TERMINATIONS 151 -> 85, so better than the baseline but far worse than the 17 the fast wire gave. STILL EXACTLY ONE DEATH: GPC3 self-failed from FIOERRLC+0313 at shared 735.242006 and was voted out by GPC1, GPC4 and GPC2 from FCMISYNC+00bb and by all four from FCMCSYNC. The timing unit was as healthy as under the fast wire -- timeReads 2607 against 2609, bus commands 53,472 against 817,178 in the baseline. THE DECISIVE OBSERVATION ACROSS ALL THREE RUNS: 151, 85 and 17 error terminations each cost PRECISELY ONE COMPUTER. The death is therefore not a function of how MANY errors there are, and driving the count down is not the route to a fix. FIOGPCWE is counted per BCE and FIOERRLC force-fails only on a count of EXACTLY ONE, so an error that catches all three listeners together is harmless while one that catches a single reader is fatal. THE FIX MUST MAKE THE ERRORS SYMMETRIC, NOT RARE. That is now being measured directly: the model counts, at every command, whether the reader still owed words was the COMMANDER alone, the listeners only, or both, and how many readers at once.
  - env `YAGPC_MTU_ATOMIC,YAGPC_MTU_WORD_US,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `sync error,fail to sync` &middot; run `atomic,wire10,iccage`
- **#245** *(emulator)* THE FATAL ASYMMETRIC ERROR IS A LISTENER WHOSE IUAR-SETTING #CMDI IS MODELLED AS A BUS TRANSACTION. Every flight-critical listen entry point begins "#CMDI FIOxxIUA,FIOMDMRT  SET IUAR FOR LISTENER" then "#RDLI n" -- a command instruction executed only to load the listener s own IUA register. mtumodel.c treats every command as a transaction: issuer becomes commander, every reader s pending reply is cleared, the wire clock restarts, and the command is echoed to everyone. A listener whose #CMDI lands after the real commander s read therefore destroys its own copy of the reply and waits in #RDLI for a sync that has already passed; and the stray echo arrives at the REAL commander as a command sync in the middle of its receive.
  - evidence: Run whocut (5 GPCs, baseline configuration) converted all 184 error terminations to the shared clock and clustered them per BCE within 3 ms: 31 clusters caught all four set members together and 16 caught three -- symmetric, which FIOERRLC tolerates -- while 10 caught ONE computer. The single-computer errors in the second before the death were all the same transaction: FIOMFE02+017c, +01a0, +01c4, +01e8 (one instruction per bus 14-17, 0x24 apart), a 34-word receive, and those four instructions NEVER appear in a symmetric cluster. 760.211774 GPC4 alone on bus 14 with left=34 -- NONE of the 34 words -- and 10 ms later, at 760.221814, GPC4 self-failed from FIOERRLC. The flight source (OI301700 FIOMFE02.asm 640-660) gives the commander path as "#CMDI FIOLMIUA,FIOFAIUA*256+10 LISTEN COMMAND / delays / #MIN 0,33 / #MINC FIOFAIUA,FIOFAIC1 RECEIVE DATA" and the listener path FIOEL23L as "#CMDI FIOFAIUA,FIOMDMRT SET IUAR FOR LISTENER / #RDLI 33". NOT A RETEST OF #224, and the difference matters: #224 blocked the servicer call for ANY command from a BCE with transmit-enable off -- which it records the model evidently depends on somewhere -- and judged it by total error counts on single runs, the metric #243 shows is blind to this fault (two runs of the IDENTICAL configuration gave 151 and 184). This change recognises only the idiom: named FIOMDMRT by the survey AND no receive armed by the issuing BCE, read from the thread s own record (tlArmed) rather than the shared scalar #244 shows can be overwritten. Behind YAGPC_MTU_IUAR=1. PREDICTION: the single-computer clusters at FIOMFE02+017c..+01e8 disappear, and the death with them. Judge by deaths and single-computer clusters over several runs, never by totals.  FIRST TRIAL WAS INERT, NOT A TEST -- do not read it as a refutation. Trial iuar-a (restore of the OPS 0 fixture pre4-snap, only OPS 3 0 1 PRO replayed, YAGPC_MTU_IUAR=1) ended with the timing unit bypassed on all four computers exactly like the baseline base-a -- but its report has NO "IUAR-setting command(s) kept off the wire" line, i.e. the switch matched ZERO commands. Cause: the test required the issuing BCE s armed count to be 0, and iop_bce_armed_words returns -1, not 0, when the BCE has neither a stashed length nor an active receive (iop.c:1679-1717), which is precisely the state before a listener s #RDLI. Corrected to armed <= 0, and the model now reports every FIOMDMRT command binned by the issuer s armed count (unset/0/1/2-7/8+/negative) so the next trial shows what the idiom actually looks like rather than what was assumed. The fixture itself is validated: base-a reproduced the MTU bypass from a restore with zero votes.  REFUTED BY THE MODEL S OWN EVENT RING, trial fix-a, 2026-09-26. With the armed-count test corrected (<= 0), the new histogram of every FIOMDMRT command reaching the model read ZERO in every bin -- unset, 0, 1, 2-7, 8+, negative -- against 80,721 IUA-8 listen commands, so no FIOMDMRT command reaches mtumodel at all. The YAGPC_MTU_RING dump taken at the trial s FCMSFAIL (777 events) confirms it independently: every command code on buses 14-23 appears exactly once from each of GPC1-4 -- each computer commanding its own bus -- and NONE appears from the other three computers on the same bus, which is what a listener s IUAR-setting #CMDI would look like. Whatever #224 observed ("mia_xmit_cmd forwards it to the servicer unconditionally") is not true of the current build, and the listener s #CMDI is not the mechanism for either fault. DO NOT RETEST. The source observations stand -- both listeners really do use the #CMDI FIOMDMRT / #RDLI idiom -- but the model never sees the #CMDI.
  - addrs `1d6dc,1d700,1d724,1d748` &middot; csects `FIOMFE02,FIOMFEPG,FIOHFEPG,FIOERRLC` &middot; instrs `#CMDI,#RDLI,#MINC` &middot; procs `BCE14,BCE15,BCE16,BCE17` &middot; env `YAGPC_MTU_IUAR,YAGPC_FC_SENSORS` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c` &middot; symptom `left=34,one computer alone,fail to sync,IUAR set by listener` &middot; doc `FIOMFE02.asm 640-660` &middot; run `whocut`

## Superseded

- **#61** *(method)* BUILD PROVENANCE IS NOT KEPT: the tape that a run used cannot be traced back to the tree that made it. Staging is reused in place, so c80boot/ and c80src/ hold only what the LAST build left; 36 numbered volumes were cut across 09-08/09-09 and only the .mmv of each survives. Do not assume a phase*/ or c80boot/ artifact corresponds to any particular tape -- CHECK, by fingerprinting a few halfwords.
  - evidence: WRONG AS STATED: v36's own staging tree survives at /tmp/claude-1000/c80v36 (09-09 14:53), with a .lnk101.repro.json per phase recording every input and its md5; I searched ~/pass-build and c80boot but never c80v*. The real provenance gaps were different: the build script lived only in /tmp/claude-1000/buildtape.sh, several inputs had no recipe, one object (AIGDEU) had drifted, and dfg was an uncommitted working tree. All resolved by yaGPC2/tapebuild/ -- see the entry recording the from-source build.

- **#104** *(goal)* SUPERSEDED by #106, #111 and #112. This said two computers load and sync but never form a set, and that the cause was not delivery latency. The second half stands; the first stopped being true once the stale-level defect (#110) was fixed -- the set DOES form now, and is voted apart 4.33 ms later. Read #111 for the state and #112 for which sync fails.
  - evidence: Run rs12, identical script to rs9 but with discretes_apply_external in place: keys 15 of 15 delivered, mmu1 71/749, mmu2 59/432 -- the same figures as rs9, rs10 and rs11 before the change. So the socket was never the reason. TWO THINGS THE RUN DOES SAY. First, ONLY null and SSIP ever appear on the lines -- 6732 nulls and 6730 SSIPs across both machines, and NOT ONE timer (101), SVC (100), IPR (010) or I/O-complete (001) code. In a redundant set every timer tick and synchronising SVC is a barrier, so their total absence means neither machine believes it is in a set with anybody; they are each running their own SSIP cycle. Second, the intercomputer bus is still write-only: recvPolls 10 and recvCalls 0 against 57730 command words and 1477888 data words in 900 s, which is #103 unchanged. WHAT TO DO NEXT, and it is a measurement not a guess: CZ2B_CS and CZ2B_RS are the last two fields of the CZ2V_GST structure in CZ2COMMO.hal, one entry per GPC, and CZ2B_RS is literally the redundant-set mask -- reading either would settle in one run whether the common set forms. Getting at it needs the address, which needs a link map we do not currently produce for the whole image; the GPC MEMORY display's own READ/WRITE section (ITEM 28+addr shows a location in the ACTUAL column) is the alternative and needs no new tooling.
  - csects `CZ2COMMO,FCMASYNC,FCMCSYNC,DM6OPS` &middot; tape `v44` &middot; symptom `no common set,no redundant set,only SSIP and null,GPC2 not in OPS 2` &middot; run `rs12`
- **#149** *(emulator)* SUPERSEDED BY #148, which is the same thing seen from outside. A computer stops issuing sync codes and the others vote it out; it keeps RUN(READY) and I/O ACTIVE, still hears its neighbours, still burns a core, and holds the NULL code 111 (not 000 dead/halt/standby) for hours. With YAGPC_SYNCTRACE the cause is visible: it is #148's IPR-handshake slip. Seen twice, on GPC2 (2026-09-16 06:17) and GPC1 (13:02).
  - evidence: THE SECOND EPISODE, WITH THE TRACE ON, is the useful one. 4-GPC OPS 2 demo run, tape OI340700-v44boot, YAGPC_SYNCTRACE=1. GPC1's last transmission: t=794931.477193 'GPC1 out 111 null', 4.4 ms after a normal SVC sync (out 100 SVC at .472804, seen by all three peers). At .477458-.477483 GPC2, GPC3 and GPC4 each issued 010 IPR and GPC1 issued NONE; the computer fail lamp went on in the same millisecond; the other three returned to null and carried on. GPC1 has not transmitted since (checked 16:08, 3 h later: 0 OUT changes in 6 s against 3452/3451/3453 for the others). IPR traffic is normal and constant: 5096 IPRs in the 400 s before the failure, 1274 from each computer, about three a second each -- so this is one slip in thousands, matching #148's rate of one episode per run. The CAM showed 11 with votes 21, 31, 41 -- correct live behaviour after #150 removed the stale latch, which is how the owner noticed. FIRST EPISODE (no trace): GPC2 at 06:17:38 after the crew took the set to OPS 301, same aftermath, votes cleared at 08:11 when the survivors re-converged while GPC2 stayed silent at null.

- **#158** *(emulator)* A FOUR-GPC REDUNDANT SET FORMS AND HOLDS IN G3 / OPS 301 WITH TWO CRTs, AND A THIRD POWERED IDP BREAKS IT -- killing GPC2 before the set ever forms. This is the first healthy multi-computer set this project has produced. The G3 configuration itself is sound; the earlier reading of this entry, that 'the G3 four-GPC configuration does not form a working set either', IS WRONG AND IS WITHDRAWN -- it was measured with --crts 3 and the third display was the variable, not the OPS. NOTE THE TIMING, because it rules out the obvious explanation: GPC2 dies at about minute 2, while the NBAT that assigns CRT3 to GPC3 is not typed until minute 5. So it is not the ASSIGNMENT, it is the mere PRESENCE of a third powered IDP from the start of the run. That has a documented counterpart on the real vehicle (#99, USA005350 Rev B 3.2.15.1): a PASS GPC moded to RUN automatically takes control of IDPs 1 TO 3 when no DK buses are being commanded by the common set, and 'no check is made to ensure against multiple commanders on the same DK bus ... Dual commanders should be avoided, as it can result in PASS GPCs failing-to-sync'. The three-CRT bus census is consistent with exactly that: GPC1 held bus 8 (DK3) with 127,203 transactions where GPC3 -- the computer the NBAT gives CRT3 to -- had 413.  SUPERSEDED 2026-09-21: the failure THIS entry describes -- a third powered IDP killing GPC2 before the set forms -- was diagnosed and fixed by #159 (power each display only after the NBAT names its commander), and re-measured today it does not happen: displays distribute correctly, rate 0.86-0.99, zero receive timeouts, CPU at 54-58 percent of a core.  A DIFFERENT and INTERMITTENT failure remains in the same configuration and is #190: GPC4, the machine with no display, voted out at t=485.6 seconds after IDP3 comes up.  Do not re-open this entry for that; it is not the same defect.
  - evidence: Run 2026-09-16 21:14, unattended: simulatePASS.py --gpcs 1-4 --crts 3 --script examples/4gpc-g3-startup.script --duration 1200 --no-manager, with YAGPC_SYNCTRACE=1, YAGPC_ICCTRACE=1 and YAGPC_ICCTRACE_MAX=0. The script ran to completion -- all four IPLs (GPCIPL menu 13.5 s after each STANDBY, mode talkback RUN 25.9/28.1/28.8/29.2 s later), all fifteen NBAT entries, and OPS 3 0 1 PRO -- and all three CRTs were still taking fills at the end. Sync issues per GPC per minute: min 1-2 GPC1 alone (684, 668); min 3 GPC1+GPC3 (1300 each); min 4 GPC4 joins (1520/1520/1454); min 5 all four (1080/310/1080/1080); min 6 GPC3 and GPC4 jump to ~4575 each while GPC1 falls to 828 and GPC2 to 0; min 7 GPC1 and GPC2 at ZERO and GPC3/GPC4 at ~5475; min 8 to 20 GPC3 and GPC4 steady at ~666 per minute each and the other two silent throughout. The whole trace is 49,067 events in 1203 s and the log only 14 MB -- a healthy four-computer set writes about a gigabyte every ten minutes -- so no redundant set was running for most of the run. In the last 20 s GPC3 and GPC4 issue only SSIP (56 each), I/O complete (56) and null (112): the common-set-but-not-redundant-set signature of #155. cam.py ended with one lamp lit, 21 -- GPC2 voting against GPC1 -- after several came on and went off during the transition. THE DISCRIMINATING RUN, 2026-09-17: the same script with the third CRT removed (idppower 3 and majfunc 3 dropped, ITEM 14 +0 instead of +3, --crts 2) gave a FULL FOUR-COMPUTER SET. Sync issues per GPC per minute, minutes 7-11: 36319/36322/36320/36308, 36331/36332/36332/36322, 36329/36328/36328/36335, 36308/36303/36298/36301, 36328/36332/36315/36332 -- all four matched to within 30 a minute until the run's own duration stopped it. Bus census: every computer drives its own flight-critical strings, bus 14 GPC1=10,508,421, bus 15 GPC2=10,484,487, bus 16 GPC3=10,484,472, bus 17 GPC4=9,703,086, bus 20 GPC1=8,848,082, bus 21 GPC2=7,905,073, bus 22 GPC3=8,405,812, bus 23 GPC4=6,994,959. GPC2, which in BOTH three-CRT runs never touched buses 15 or 21 at all, is here doing 10.5M and 7.9M transactions on them. TWO EARLIER THREE-CRT RUNS, for contrast: the first lost GPC1 AND GPC2 at the OPS transition and left GPC3+GPC4 idling; the second lost only GPC2, at minute 2, and GPC1+GPC3+GPC4 then formed a healthy THREE-computer set at ~14,400 issues per minute each. In both, GPC2 spun in common-set sync at 1-4 ms intervals -- far faster than GPC1's ~160 ms cadence -- and then stopped dead with no fail vote and no error line. A CORRECTION: the first run's mass-memory word loss (27% on MM1, 71% on MM2, with bus 18/19 contention reported) DID NOT REPRODUCE -- the second three-CRT run lost 2,880 words, 0.3%, with no contention at all, and still lost GPC2 -- so that loss was a symptom of a bad run and not the mechanism.

- **#190** *(emulator)* A 4-GPC 3-CRT SET STILL LOSES A COMPUTER, INTERMITTENTLY, AND IT IS NOT #159's FAILURE.  With #159's ordering in place (ITEM 14 +3 before idppower 3) the displays ARE distributed one per machine and the vehicle runs near real time -- and all four computers still voted GPC4 out at t=485.6 of a 1200 s run.  THE DISPLAY BUSES SHOW NOTHING AT THAT INSTANT: traffic is dead steady at 10 commands per second per bus from t=470 to t=500, with no burst, no gap and no DEU-load spike anywhere in the run -- so whatever fails GPC4 is not visible in display traffic at all.  (An earlier reading of this entry said the vote came seconds after IDP3 was powered; that was inferred from the tail of a log while the run was still going, not measured, and is withdrawn -- the power-up is roughly 90 s earlier, at the OPS transition that hands bus 8 to GPC3 at t=368.)  GPC4 is the machine with NO display.  It keeps executing after the vote; it is failed out, not halted.  EVERY SUSPECT FROM #158/#159 IS EXCLUDED BY MEASUREMENT HERE: rate 0.991/0.947/0.903/0.859 (not 0.42); per-thread CPU 54-58 percent of a core mean and 64-68 percent at the instant of the vote (not 100); ZERO receive timeouts on any bus (not 120 on BCE8); zero barrier abandons; displays held GPC1 bus 6, GPC2 bus 7, GPC3 bus 8 with no second commander at any point after the handover.  INTERMITTENT: an immediately following run of the same script did not vote at all, so a single run proves nothing in either direction and the rate has to be measured before the cause is chased.  THE ONE ASYMMETRY in the failing run: GPC3, the machine that owns CRT3, logged 2726 peer holds of which only 1133 were answered, against GPC2's 929 of 929 and GPC4's 88 of 88.  CAUSE FOUND 2026-09-21, and the earlier SVC-duration reading in this entry is WITHDRAWN (it came from measuring each machine's last event against the last event in ANY machine's ring instead of against its own record -- the same class of error as reading four per-machine clocks as one timeline).  IT IS ARRIVAL SKEW, NOT PHASE LENGTH.  On the shared clock the four computers normally reach a sync point within 35 microseconds of each other (median over 38,879 SVC rounds, p99 92 us).  About once in 39,000 rounds -- roughly once every ten minutes of running -- one arrives 4 to 6 ms late, and THAT is where the failures sit: spreads of 4.870 ms (run full-1) and 4.3 ms (run ring-1).  In full-1 GPC1 announced SVC 4.87 ms before its peers, waited, raised 010 IPR alone, and the three failed it within 8 us of hearing the IPR.  In ring-1 GPC4 announced SVC 4.3 ms early and failed the other three at the instant their SVC arrived.  So the defect is a rare SCHEDULING EXCURSION in our four-thread model, not anything in the flight software, the display path or the buses.  NOT SUFFICIENT ON ITS OWN: the clean run hist-1 contains a 5.6 ms SVC spread and survived it, so which machine is late and what it does next also matters -- an early machine that raises IPR alone is what the peers fail.  WIDENING IS NOT YET TESTED -- an earlier claim here that it removes the vote is WITHDRAWN.  YAGPC_SYNC_WINDOW_US patched FCMSNTO2 from 3850 to 12000 once, at each machine's t=31 s, and read it back as 12000; but a capture of the same run at t=442 s holds 3850 in all four images.  The constant is REWRITTEN after the patch -- almost certainly by the OPS 3 load bringing in a fresh common block -- and the patch was one-shot, so it never re-applied.  Every 'widened' run was therefore at 12 ms only until its OPS transition and at 3.85 ms for the whole period in which failures occur.  The 0-in-11 result stands as measured but is NOT evidence for widening: at best chance against the like-instrumented baseline (ring+full, 3 in 12; p about 0.04 for none in eleven), which after this many series is not remarkable.  The fault test labelled 'wide' likewise ran at 3.85 ms when GPC3 was halted.  FIX: re-apply whenever the word reverts, and log each application with its time, so the reversion itself is measured.  WIDENING FCMSNTO2 IS REFUTED AS THE FIX, and the reason corrects the mechanism.  Tested properly 2026-09-21 by RESTORE (the one-shot patch problem cannot arise, no OPS load follows a resume), four vehicles in parallel, sixteen five-minute holds: 3.85 ms 7 of 8 clean, 12 ms 7 of 8 clean -- identical.  The widened failure, decoded: GPC2/3/4 said 001 I/O complete at -12.82..-12.77 ms and dropped back to 111 null by -12.53; GPC1 said 001 only at -12.274, about 0.5 ms after the others had FINISHED, and then waited 12.27 ms -- the widened timeout -- for a pulse that had already come and gone, before failing all three from FCMISYNC.  So the failure is a MISSED PULSE, not a late arrival within reach of a longer wait, and the governing tolerance is how long each machine HOLDS its code: FCMIT3 'T3 - MINIMUM DURATION FOR ISYNC' (188 us), FCMIPRT3 (248 with IPR), FCMCTT3 (103, CSYNC/TSYNC), FCMST3 (182, SSYNC).  A machine more than about 0.2 ms late misses it outright -- #148's description exactly.  Located in the loaded image by their run of neighbours: FCMNOISE 10 / FCMMISKW 200 / FCMIT5 87 / FCMST5 87 / FCMIT3 188 / FCMIPRT3 248 / FCMCTT3 103 / FCMST3 182 at halfwords 0x081f2..0x08200, unique in the image.  Raising a T3 has a cost widening the timeout did not: every sync then lasts at least that long in simulated time, so it eats into PASS's cycle.
  - evidence: Run g3crt3, 2026-09-21, examples/4gpc-g3-startup.script --crts 3 --duration 1200, port base 7300, nested X :5, YAGPC_PACETRACE + YAGPC_CMDTRACE=6,7,8 + YAGPC_BUSCENSUS, per-thread CPU sampled from /proc/PID/task/*/stat every 2 s.  Votes, from the stamped cam.py (#187) so in-run and teardown are distinguishable: 44, 14, 34, 24 all ON at cam t=485.6, and NOTHING at the teardown -- simulated time ended at 1192.8 s, so these are 707 s before the end.  Barrier: 'held 121872367 times, 1542.491 s total, 0 abandoned (delta 25 us)'.  Per-thread CPU means 58.3/57.8/56.8/54.5 percent of one core, max 99.5, R-state in 322-352 of 601 samples each; at the vote all four sat at 64-68 percent on a 20-core host.  Display possession on the SHARED clock: bus 6 GPC1 11545 commands; bus 7 GPC1 1723 then GPC2 8657; bus 8 GPC1 2702 then GPC3 8603 from t=368; one shared-second with two commanders on each of buses 7 and 8, both at the handover.  Control: run g3sync, same script with YAGPC_SYNCTRACE (2,397,750 lines) and landmark 1948c -- CLEAN, no votes, which is also why the heavy trace cannot be left on.  REFUTED ALREADY, from the clean traced run: that the third display simply exercises #148's IPR-handshake slip more often.  It does not -- the sync conversation of the 3-CRT set is 1661 IPRs per computer in 703 s, 9.4 per second across the set, which is LOWER than #148's own baseline of 5096 in 400 s (12.7/s) in a 2-CRT G2 run.  Full code census per computer over that run, GPC1/2/3/4: null 109417/109066/108409/107741, SVC 55654/55632/55652/55640, I/O complete 33366/33325/32994/32676, timer 14794/14779/14770/14777, SSIP 4081/3753/3422/3092, IPR 1662/1661/1661/1661.  So if this IS #148's mechanism, it is not reached more often here -- the per-handshake probability would have to differ, which is a different claim and needs its own evidence.  WHO DECLARES WHAT, from run rate-1 (landmark 1948c with registers, the light instrument -- it costs nothing until a computer actually fails another).  THE VICTIM DETECTS FIRST AND FAILS EVERYBODY ELSE: GPC1 at shared t=471.319313, r0=000e0001, r7=8f4f2031.  Then the other three, 5.2 ms later at shared 471.32454-471.32458, each r0=80100001, r7=8c4be031.  R7 is psw1, so the caller expands as (BSR<<15)|(nia16&0x7FFF) with BSR in bits 24-27: 8f4f2031 -> 0x18f4f = FCMISYNC+0x0BB (FCMISYNC base 0x18e94, confirmed by #148's own 18fab=FCMISYNC+0117), and 8c4be031 -> 0x18c4b = FCMCSYNC+0x0BD (FCMCSYNC base 0x18b8e).  R0's format is not inferred but READ FROM THE SOURCE, FCMSFAIL.asm:72-80: 'BIT 0 = 0 - REDUNDANT SET SYNC FAILURE / 1 - COMMON SET SYNC FAILURE', 'BITS 11-15 = MASK OF GPC'S (1-5) TO BE FAILED' (IBM numbering, so GPC1=0x00100000, GPC2=0x00080000, GPC3=0x00040000, GPC4=0x00020000).  Therefore 000e0001 reads 'REDUNDANT SET failure, fail GPCs 2, 3 and 4' and 80100001 reads 'COMMON SET failure, fail GPC1' -- and each matches the routine it was called from, FCMISYNC being a redundant-set sync and FCMCSYNC the common-set one.  READ AS ENGLISH: the victim waited in the I/O-complete sync, saw NONE of its three peers in time and failed all three; the peers then failed it from the common-set sync.  NOTE 0x18f4f IS INSIDE #148's OWN RECORDED RANGE 18ece-18f4f, so this is very likely #148's mechanism appearing at ~30x the rate, NOT a new one -- but see the IPR census above, which refutes 'more handshakes' as the reason for the higher rate.  THE SKEW IS NOT CLOCK DIVERGENCE, IT IS WORK DIVERGENCE -- which rules the barrier out as the place to fix it.  vehicle_barrier_wait is called ONCE PER INSTRUCTION (run.c) and again inside the idle loop, so every machine publishes its simulated clock continuously, and any machine more than barDeltaUs ahead of the slowest is held.  With delta 25 us and ZERO abandons recorded, the CLOCKS cannot be 4-6 ms apart.  What the 4.87 ms and 4.3 ms spreads measure is therefore the moment each machine ANNOUNCES its sync code, which is a point in its INSTRUCTION STREAM, not a point on its clock: two computers whose clocks agree to 25 us can still reach the announcement milliseconds apart if one has more work to do first.  SO THE QUESTION BECOMES WHY FOUR COMPUTERS RUNNING THE SAME SOFTWARE DO DIFFERENT AMOUNTS OF WORK, and there is an obvious candidate this vehicle has in abundance: a COMMANDER and a LISTENER on the same bus do not execute the same path.  On real hardware the IOP carries that asymmetry and the CPU does not feel it; if our model puts any of it on the CPU's instruction stream, the machines diverge by construction and the divergence grows with how much I/O is answered -- which matches the observation that the MDM answering (#216) makes the votes more frequent without being their cause.  NOT YET MEASURED: the per-machine instruction count between two consecutive sync announcements, which would settle it directly -- equal counts mean the divergence is elsewhere, unequal ones name it.

- **#198** *(emulator)* Powering IDP3 and pressing IDP LOAD in OPS 0 (before OPS 3 0 1 PRO) STILL does not load it, though CZ2V_POST_IPL is 0 there (ARCGPC.hal:1007 increments it only when an overlay completes).  Suspect DMIMCD's OTHER gate: it examines poll-header bit 16 (IPL request) only IF the FIRST HALFWORD OF THE TRANSACTION STATUS IS NOT ZERO (DMIMCD.hal ~015830).  MEDS2 sets IPL_REQUIRED (0x0001 = HAL bit 16) correctly, but our BCE very likely returns a zero transaction status for such a poll, so PASS's in-OPS DEU loader (AIG_DEU_LOADER) is never scheduled.  IDP1/IDP2 load by a different path, at IPL (AIBGPCLO/GPCIPL).  ALSO: that run (idp3load-1, fixed emulator e46bb0eac) lost GPC4 ~13 s after OPS 3 0 1 PRO: GPC1/2/3 did SVC, IPR, IPR, I/O, I/O then SVC at 1a241 (inside FIOMGCV, the overlay checksum processor) and failed GPC4 from FCMSSYNC+0x86 (r0=00020001); GPC4 did SVC, ONE I/O complete, nothing for 21 ms, then the same SVC.  The three saw two I/O problem reports GPC4 did not -- plausibly IDP3, powered and unloaded, erroring during the transition.  The committed script (IDP3 powered after OPS 301) is unchanged; the experiment is saved as scratchpad 4gpc-g3-startup.idp3-in-ops0.script.
  - evidence: runs/idp3load-1: meds3 POWER ON (after ITEM 1 9) -> poll reply 0x41 [IPL_REQUIRED] -> only func=908 fills; DEU LOAD -> same; no func=916 fill ever.  panelO6 stamps: idppower 3 t=339.3, idpload 3 t=343.3, OPS 3 0 1 PRO t=383.3; FCMSFAIL landmarks GPC1/2/3 shared 396.4943, GPC4 396.5504 (r0=001c0001).
